**How to read this document.** §0 is the summary. §1 fixes the adversary views and the declared DM leakage. §2–§13 are normative and answer I1–I10. §14 lists the invariants, §15 the test and exit-gate plan (I11), §16–§17 gates and slices, §18–§19 risks and owner questions, §20 findings outside the phase, **§21 the normative corrections after the adversarial review, which win over every earlier section**, and **§22 the revision of 2026-09-16 (no libsignal, closed source), which lists every change, states which earlier text it overrides, and wins over §21**. Appendix A holds the ADR drafts (Romanian), Appendix B the text changes for other documents (Romanian), Appendix C the evidence and the S0′ checks, Appendix D the sources. Where a section says "(normative)" it wins over a summary table. Citations `path:line` are relative to `D:/Forum` (or `ghost/…`); `F7` = `docs/design/faza7-sync-engine.md`, `F8` = `docs/design/faza8-issuer.md`, `TM` = `docs/THREAT_MODEL_v2.1.md`, `INV` = `ghost/test-harness/privacy/INVARIANTS.md`, `OBS` = `ghost/test-harness/privacy/allowed-observables.json`, `vz:` = the vodozemac 0.11.0 crate sources.

**Identifier conventions.** Grafts `G-n`, conflicts `C-n`, judge errors `E-n`, synthesis corrections `SC-n` (§0.3); revision grafts `RG-n` and revision error notes `RX-n` (§22); deviations `X-n` (§0.5); guarantees `P-n` (privacy) and `CS-n` (correctness); transactions by name in capitals (`SEND`, `RECV`, …, §7.2); crash scenarios `SCN-A…SCN-L` (§15.3); mutants `PM-n` (privacy), `MC-n` (correctness), `ME-n` (ephemeral and envelope) (§15.7); residues `L7-Dn` (LIMITE section L7; unrelated to the Phase 8 declared-leak cell L7 of T2) and `PQ-Rn` (post-quantum residues, §9.8); seal rules `R-S1…R-S7` (§6.2); session-selection rules `R-SS0…R-SS5` (§8.2); ProVerif obligations `M-1…M-8` (§9.5); exhaustive state-model enumerations `EM-1`, `EM-2` (§15.2); S0′ checks `V-1…V-16` (Appendix C). Slices are `S0′`, `S1…S3`, `S4a`, `S4b`, `S5…S14`.

## 0. Summary

### 0.1 Decisions

| # | Decision | Issue | Plan / ADR alignment |
|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | **Olm from `vodozemac` 0.11.0** (Apache-2.0, Matrix.org Foundation), exact `=` pin, `default-features = false`; the features `libolm-compat`, `low-level-api`, `experimental-session-config` and `insecure-pk-encryption` are forbidden in the shipped graph (`low-level-api` only in dev graphs, as a test oracle); `SessionConfig` V1. It is the most mature permissively licensed asynchronous 1:1 Double Ratchet: one public audit (Least Authority, 2022), deployment in every Element and Element X client through matrix-rust-sdk (whose `main` pins `vodozemac = "0.11.0"`, checked 2026-09-16), two protocol-level analyses of Olm, 2026 security fixes (§2.1, §2.9). OpenMLS stays the Phase 10 library and the documented DM fallback (Q44). No libsignal, no `spqr`, no AGPL or GPL code anywhere (ADR-37). | I1 | deviates FR-2.1, spec §7.2 first sentence, spec Appendix A DM row, plan row 9 and Annex D (ADR-35; X-18) |
| D2 | **Integration in Rust:** a new workspace crate `client-core/dm` (`ghost-client-dm`) holds Olm use, X-Wing, the seal, the L0 codec, cards, bundles, bindings, derivations and safety numbers, and is linked into the existing `libghost_client_net.so` (one Rust runtime in the APK). It has no network, no async runtime, no logging and no clock. Kotlin calls a **closed JNI surface** (`org.ghost.crypto.bridge.DmNative`, gate G-R6) of pure byte-in, byte-out functions with typed result codes; `:crypto-bridge` is a thin wrapper that exposes the GHOST-typed facade of §21.6 point 6; `:messaging` references no native type. **Value state, wrapped:** every Olm session pickle, bundle-generation secret, `reply_seed` and pair opening key crosses JNI only as `nonce ‖ ChaCha20-Poly1305(session_state_key, AD = table ‖ row key)` and is written by Kotlin in the calling SQLCipher transaction, only on `OK` (§2.5, §7.9). JDK stays 17; JVM tests run the real ratchet through a host build `ghost-dm-host` (§2.7). | I1, I2 | ADR-19 and ADR-20 point 8 hold again (X-9 withdrawn); ADR-35 |
| D3 | **Sealed sender without a server = a GHOST outer seal in four modes, no certificates** (§6). *Pair mode* (answered conversations): a symmetric key per (pair, direction, ISO week) ratcheted from a **random root generated by the recipient**, erased 45 days after its week ends; roots travel only in normal Olm messages of acknowledged sessions, and a session renewal resets the peer's out keys (§21.3). *Intro mode* (first contact, reset, reconnect, every PreKey message): an outer AEAD keyed by the recipient's card and an **inner layer keyed by X-Wing** to the recipient's weekly bundle generation, with every public key and the ciphertext in the KDF transcript; the sender's card v2, binding v3 and a `reply_seed` ride inside. *Provisional mode* (new): the responder's and initiator's normal messages before a pair root exists, keyed from the session's `reply_seed`. *Bundle mode*: the card-keyed bundle. All modes present `salt(16) ‖ hint(16) ‖ AEAD`: uniform bytes, one AEAD attempt per blob, hint computed with a **recognition key** separate from the opening key, AD binding the mode and both Olm identities. Server-certificate sealed-sender schemes, multi-recipient formats with cleartext recipients, per-message Elligator 2 keys and raw X25519 keys are rejected (C-2, §6.1). | I2, I4 | Annex B "fără câmp de expeditor" made exact; ADR-31′, ADR-36 |
| D4 | **Relay-blind addressing (T26).** Nothing relay-visible is derived from public identity material. A 131-byte signed **contact card v2** (Ed25519 identity key, card index, `card_secret`; no intro key) is exchanged by QR in person or by a `ghost://contact/` link (226 characters, QR 9-L). Every DM namespace, deposit secret, bundle read secret and card-level seal key is an HKDF output of `card_secret`; the inbox read secrets and the Olm identity derive from the seed's MESSAGING branch only. **No contacting by bare `ghost1…`.** | I3, I4, I9 | deviates Annex B "citire publică după ghost1" (ADR-30′) |
| D5 | **Static namespaces per card index, credentials static per relay slot** (G-4, C-4; §21.2): inbox `X_i = HKDF(card_secret_i, "ghost/v1/card/inbox-ns")`, bundle namespace `P_i = HKDF(card_secret_i, "ghost/v1/card/bundle-ns")`. The relay set of any DM namespace is `set(ns, w)`, a public keyed permutation of the week's ES slots, independent of the week itself (§21.1), spanning ≥ 2 operators; when the slot table changes, readers keep the union with earlier sets for 6 weeks. | I3, I4, I8 | Annex B inbox; ADR-30′ |
| D6 | **Shared deposit keys (T27): senders never spend tokens on another identity's namespace.** The recipient redeems its own write capability per (relay, namespace, week) and registers, with a new RPC **`RegisterKey`**, the verifier of a deposit secret per relay slot shared by all its card holders; senders present the same 67-byte **bearer credential v3**, bound to the relay's slot, which allows `store` and `check` only. Registrations cover weeks `{cap_week, cap_week + 1}`, checked by the relay against the capability's week, never against its own clock (G-3); senders choose no week and have no fallback, and a recipient may be offline about two to three weeks without a deposit failing (§21.2). Verifiers accumulate and are never replaced (SC-1). | I4 | deviates Annex B "scriere cu capabilitate" per sender and Phase 8 interim (ADR-30′) |
| D7 | **D11 implemented** (C-5, C-6): the same RPC registers the **seed-derived inbox read secret** (so contacts cannot read the inbox with the card) and the card-derived bundle read secret (shared by all card holders, so fetchers are indistinguishable). The verifier binds the relay slot and the namespace: `SHA-256(label(kind) ‖ slot ‖ namespace ‖ secret)`; the week lives in the registration period (§21.2). T21 in `client-core` learns the v3 credential. Registrations live in a new relay store `keys.redb` with its own quota ledger per registration (E-11). | I3, I4 | F8 §10.7 points 2–3, ADR-25 point 9 (implemented, generalized) |
| D8 | **Bundles v2** (§5.3): one sealed 4 KiB bundle per card in `P_i`, republished weekly at a seed-PRF offset, valid for its ISO week and the next; plaintext 1 393 B: the Olm identity key, one **Olm fallback key** and one **X-Wing public key** per weekly generation `gen_id`, a `bundle_rank` (no hour inside the bundle; fixes the `issued_hour` leak found while mapping the revision, §22.4; card holders still see publication minutes by polling, and fetch reads return only week-coarse times, §22.8.7), all signed by the `ghost1` Ed25519 key. **No one-time keys through relays** (a blind store cannot allocate them), **no registration id, no device id**. Generation secrets are kept until `superseded_day + 91` (7 + 28 + 45 + 6 days, §5.4), then erased with their reuse tuples. Fetched once per establishment or explicit reset, in at most three 15-minute attempts, never polled; the newest valid bundle by `(bundle_week, bundle_rank)` wins (§21.7). Rejected alternative: bundles pushed in a signed HELLO with one-time keys and an accept round trip (C-7). | I3, I6 | plan row 9 "publicare prekeys prin relay"; ADR-30′, ADR-36 |
| D9 | **Envelope (T9, T29):** three layers, L0 canonical content → L1 an unchanged Olm message (V1) → L2 seal. Bucket ∈ {4, 16, 64} KiB chosen **from the body length plus fixed reserves only** (§21.11) with a worst-case overhead `O_MAX` = **1 664 B** (**frozen by S0′, V-3, 2026-09-19**, from the measurement probe on the real toolchain, RG-8: measured worst intro overhead 1 606 B at 64 KiB and 1 604 B at 4 and 16 KiB; the word "provisional" is withdrawn), so a 4 KiB DM carries a body of up to 2 220 B; DMs never use 1 KiB, so PreKey vs normal, intro vs provisional vs pair, control vs text of equal length share one class. All lengths and zero padding inside the AEAD; non-canonical blobs refused. **Seal and L0 codec live in Rust** (`ghost-client-dm`); an independent test-only implementation on the JDK's own providers replays the seal, content and safety-number vectors; `client-core` keeps refusing non-bucket blobs. | I4 | T9 made executable; ADR-31′ |
| D10 | **No reflex traffic (T28):** nothing is stored, redeemed, registered or rescheduled because a blob arrived. No delivery or read receipts, no typing, no decryption-error or retry-request message, no automatic session reset. Undecryptable, duplicate, replayed and forged blobs are consumed silently. FR-4.3 "delivery status" = the Phase 7 relay-quorum outcome. The only automatic resend is the Phase 7 identical-bytes resend of an INDETERMINATE op, at most twice (G-7, C-15), caused by the user's own send. | I4, I8 | ADR-08 (optional, off in high-privacy) made stricter; ADR-31′ |
| D11 | **Identity (C-1):** the Olm identity (X25519) derives from the seed's existing MESSAGING branch, `HKDF(m, "ghost/v1/dm/olm-identity")`, and is loaded through vodozemac's public `Account::from_dehydrated_device` constructor (MSC3814 pickle format, also called by matrix-rust-sdk); the `Account`'s mandatory Ed25519 key derives under a separate label and never signs anything. The identity and every safety number survive a restore; sessions, bundle generations, pair roots and dedup tags do not. The Ed25519 identity (`ghost1…`) signs the Olm identity in every bundle and in every intro envelope's binding. No registration id, no device id (multi-device P2). | I5 | FR-1.2 (P0), spec Figure 2, TM:81; ADR-35, ADR-33′ |
| D12 | **Safety number v1** (§8.4): 60 digits over both `ghost1` keys and both Olm identity keys, 5 200 SHA-512 iterations per party, a versioned 65-byte scannable form, vectors replayed by Rust and by an independent JDK implementation; specified clean-room (no AGPL source read); UI-less API for Phase 13; no network I/O. | I5 | FR-4.6; ADR-33′ (a CP-09 exception with vectors) |
| D13 | **Key change blocks both directions (C-14, E-1):** a key change is classified **only** when a signature valid under the contact's pinned Ed25519 key (a bundle signature or binding v3) names an Olm identity different from the pinned one; anything else is a forgery and is dropped with no state change (`IDENTITY_MISMATCH` alone is never a key change). In `changed`, sending is refused before any native session call and new-key PreKey messages are **held undecrypted** (bounded by the relay expiry, ≤ 30 d + 1 h) until the user acknowledges or verifies; acceptance deletes both sessions under the old identity. | I5 | FR-4.6, spec §7.2(d), plan gate; ADR-33′ |
| D14 | **Post-quantum (§9): Level 2 at the Phase 9 exit.** PQ-A: the intro inner layer is keyed by X-Wing (`libcrux-kem` `XWingKemDraft06` over `libcrux-ml-kem` 0.0.10, portable code only). PQ-B: every symmetric key that seals relay-stored DM bytes is card-derived, KEM-derived, or transported only under such keys (invariant **T35**). PQ-C (slice S13, not blocking the exit gate): in-band X-Wing key announcements and **PQ root re-keys** every 4 weeks give seal-layer PQ healing (Q48, Q49, Q68). **Olm stays classical underneath** (no PQ inside the ratchet, declared PQ-R2, PQ-R7). One suite, no negotiation, no standalone KEM, no Kyber. A ProVerif model (M-1 … M-7; M-8 is covered by vectors, differential tests and fuzzing) runs in CI; an external cryptographic review of the composition is a release gate (spec §7.5). No quantum-resistance statement in any UI before both (CP-10). | I6 | deviates ADR-12 DM clause (ADR-36; X-19) |
| D15 | **TTL buckets become seconds** `{3600, 86400, 604800, 2592000, 7776000}`; the relay accepts **exactly** a bucket (`rejected_ttl` otherwise); `allowed-observables.json` v2 replaces `ttl_bucket_days` with `ttl_bucket_seconds`. **DM relay TTL = ADR-27 point 11 exactly** (G-5, C-8): 5 min and 1 h → 1 h, 1 d → 1 d, 7 d → 7 d, 30 d → 30 d, timer off → 30 d. Every DM op carries the deadline `author + min(bucket, 7 d)` (SC-3; 5-minute messages share the 1-hour deadline) and runs to it, never cancelled at expiry (§21.10). The Phase 7 window rules are re-derived for TTL < 2σ (G-10). | I7 | ADR-27 C.11 kept; T25 wording; ADR-32 |
| D16 | **Ephemeral execution:** timer and timer version inside every L0; sender countdown from the send commit; recipient countdown from first display; an unread message is deleted at `min(relay expiry E, consume + bucket + 1 h)` with E passed through `InboundBlob.relayExpiryHour` (C-11); expiry times only move earlier; the expiry clock never runs backwards; one-transaction deletion cascade, then `wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE)` with `busy = 0` checked and a checkpoint at every database open; notifications never carry content or sender; quotes are references; no FTS; sweeps in process, at open, in the consumer tail, in every read query, plus **one inexact non-exported alarm** (C-12). | I7 | ADR-27 C.8–C.10; ADR-32 |
| D17 | **Traffic:** Phase 7 schedules unchanged. Sends immediate in STANDARD, at pair events after U[0, 10 min] in HIGH (DM namespaces cannot be set to `SendDelay.OFF`). Reads only on own namespaces (T31). Bundle publication and owner registrations at activity-independent times. No cover traffic (P2); reply-timing and session co-occurrence residues declared (LIMITE L7) and measured by T32. | I8 | ADR-09, ADR-15, ADR-20 |
| D18 | **Contact establishment:** card exchange (QR in person → `verified`; link → `unverified`); the initiator's card rides inside its first intro envelope, so one scan suffices; the responder answers in provisional mode without a bundle fetch (§6.7, §12.2); unknown card holders appear as **requests** (cap 64, 7 days; C-24); block and delete are local. **Deferred:** cut-off card rotation to Phase 13 with the P1 contact backup (C-16), the Invite v3 card to Phase 13 with ADR-05 kept open (C-18), the Phase 8 drop outer-shape change (C-17). | I9 | ADR-05 (card deferred, not declined); ADR-30′ |
| D19 | **Modules:** `client-core/dm` (Rust) = all DM cryptography; `:crypto-bridge` = the only `DmNative` user (JNI wrapper, typed facade, recognition-hint matching); `:messaging` = DM engine behind a UI-less facade for Phase 13, one **DM actor thread** as the only writer. **Schema:** migration **4** (S3) = the sync and entitlement steps (unused v1 DM tables dropped behind a fail-closed guard, `outbox_op`/`outbox_delivery` rebuilt for the 1 h bucket with explicit column lists (G-9, E-5), `inbox_blob.relay_expiry_hour`, `sync_namespace.capability_source`, `relay_key_registration`); migration **5** (S4b) = the `dm_*` and `olm_*` tables (random keys, minute/hour/day time columns with `typeof` CHECKs, state and write-once triggers, REPLACE guards). | I10 | spec §6.1, §8; ADR-33′, ADR-34′ |
| D20 | **`:sync` and `:entitlement` additions (ADR-34′):** `TtlBucket.HOUR_1`; `InboundBlob.relayExpiryHour` and `offers`; `CapabilitySource {REDEEM, LOCAL}` (the planner never redeems a LOCAL need); `Registrations` on the work lane; pending registrations count as write work, so a new or restored inbox is redeemed at once (Q31, G-17); **`ConsumerTail`** run on the job thread after a background session's lanes and redeem hold and after a quiet run's issuer call, after the transport closed, with the DM actor as the only writer (G-8, C-13); `Inbox.forgetConsumed` (§21.7); **`Inbox.peek` and `Inbox.claimOne`** for ordered consumption (§7.7). | I10 | amends ADR-20, ADR-23 (the participant slot stays one); ADR-34′ |
| D21 | **Exit gate proved** on the JVM with the real ratchet (the host build of `ghost-client-dm`), the real sync engine and a conformance-pinned Kotlin `ModelRelay`: a full transaction inventory with crash enumeration at every SQL statement and structural invariants after every reboot (RG-7), native-crash and Rust-panic poison and `SQLITE_FULL` suites (G-1), seeded worlds across week boundaries, slot-table changes, 40-day offline windows and clock steps (G-13), exhaustive enumerations of the session and seal-mode state machines (EM-1, EM-2; RG-1), key-change blocking including key-compromise impersonation at first contact (KC13), T25 logical and physical scans, the **DM privacy world T32** (exact joins, twins including card-holder indistinguishability, statistics at α = 0.001, C-19), T34, T35, **97 mutants** (§22.8.11 as resolved by S0′, V-15, 2026-09-19: Q67's prune is adopted, so MC-30 is in). An **emulator job** on loopback model relays with a host/Android equivalence test (G-11, RG-2) and a **signed two-device checklist** close the gap to real Android, SQLCipher, Tor and relays. | I11 | exit gate; ADR-34′ |
| D22 | **Closed-source distribution with licence compliance and a verifiability package** (§2.10): proprietary licence for all GHOST code; generated third-party notices; licence gates G-L1 … G-L5 and the source-header gate G-R5 from S0′; `org.signal` removed and denylisted, named cargo-deny bans on the libsignal family (explicit crate names, §22.8.10); MPL-2.0 only unmodified with a source offer (no new MPL in the DM stack); no public binary artifacts (G-L5); the repository becomes private before DM code lands (owner action); release gates: published external audits and the DM model, auditor-reproduced builds (T12b), a witnessed binary transparency log enforced by the updater (Phase 14; a standalone verifier for first installs, logged relay and issuer releases, declared gaps for store channels, §22.8.8), a published protocol specification with vectors and a black-box verification kit, source escrow, no obfuscation of GHOST classes. Residue declared: users cannot verify source against binary (L7-D23). | I1 | deviates ADR-07, FR-8.9, FR-8.10, plan:202, G12, G16 (ADR-37, ADR-38, ADR-39; X-20) |
| D23 | **Sessions (§8.1, §8.2, §7.6, §7.7):** at most two Olm sessions per contact (`current` for sends, `previous` for receives with a `retire_day`), deterministic selection rules R-SS0 … R-SS5 (a new inbound session wins; a message on `previous` wins over an unacknowledged `current`; between two acknowledged sessions the smaller session id wins); a first-flight reuse set `(gen_id, session_id)` (vodozemac does not consume fallback keys); an unanswered initiator session is re-established at the next user send after `UNACK_MAX = 28 d`; peek-ordered consumption and an in-memory gap hold of at most 12 minutes against Olm's 40-skipped-keys window; an age bound for skipped keys evaluated in S0′ (Q67). Crash safety comes from GHOST's value semantics, not from vodozemac (whose failed decrypt can activate an inactive sending ratchet in memory, `vz:src/olm/session/double_ratchet.rs:168-191`). | I4, I5 | CP-09 exception (model EM-1, M-5); ADR-33′, ADR-35 |
| D24 | **Binding v3 (§3.4):** every intro envelope carries an Ed25519 signature by the sender's `ghost1` key over its Olm identity, card index, the recipient namespace, the sealing minute, the recipient's `gen_id` and Olm identity, the SHA-256 of the envelope's X-Wing ciphertext and the Olm base key, and **not** over message content. It blocks key-compromise impersonation at first contact (an attacker holding the recipient's full state cannot start sessions in a sender's name with fresh base keys) at a declared deniability cost: the recipient can prove that the sender initiated a session at that minute, not what it wrote (L7-D20). Alternatives recorded in Q51. | I2, I5 | ADR-33′ |

### 0.2 Guarantees claimed (each has a test in §15)

**Metadata at relays (AD-1: the operator with every relay and the issuer)**

| ID | Guarantee | Proof |
|---|---|---|
| P-1 | **No identity at relays.** No relay-visible value (namespace, verifier, credential, stored byte, capture field) is computable from public identity material (`ghost1`, Ed25519 key, Olm identity key, safety number) without a card or the seed. | T26: derivation vectors, static IKM check, JD1, JD2, S-DM3 |
| P-2 | **No sender field, in substance.** Every store into one inbox namespace at one relay with a deposit credential presents the same credential scope, in every week and whatever the sender's clock or earlier refusals (§21.2); no honest client redeems for a namespace it does not own; a client's redemptions and registrations do not depend on its DM activity. | T27: JD3, JD8, NI-DM3, relay vectors |
| P-3 | **No reflex.** Relay-visible behaviour, including relay-session close times, is identical whatever arrives in the inbox (valid, forged, replayed, key-changed, undecryptable). | T28: NI-DM1, JD6, static call-graph test |
| P-4 | **No type, state or sender in sizes or bytes.** Bucket = f(body length); envelope and bundle bytes are uniform; no session-protocol header (Olm identity, base, fallback or ratchet key, chain index) leaves the AEAD; a re-sent PreKey envelope shares no byte window with the first. | T29: JD2, JD4, JD10, NI-DM2, NI-DM7, byte-uniformity test |
| P-5 | **Bounded relay lifetime.** An honest relay holds a DM blob at most until `deadline_hour + bucket + 1 h`, where `deadline_hour` is the Phase 7 stored hour of the deadline `author + min(bucket, 7 d)` (§10.1), and stores exactly the bucket it was given. | T30: JD5, relay and client vectors |
| P-6 | **Read confinement.** A client lists and gets only its own namespaces, plus one bundle fetch (at most three 15-minute attempts, §21.7) per establishment or explicit user reset. | T31: JD7, static check |
| P-7 | **Attribution forward secrecy of DM envelopes.** For an ISO week w with `now ≥ start(w + 1) + 45 d`, no key on either device and nothing derivable from either seed opens a pair-mode envelope of week w, except through a root-carrying message whose Olm message key survives as a skipped key in a **live** session on the seized recipient of that root (declared; retired sessions are erased after 46 d; removed entirely if the S0′ skipped-key age bound is adopted, Q67). **Corrected by S0′ (V-15, 2026-09-19): the age bound is adopted, so the exception is withdrawn** — SWEEP prunes every skipped key older than `FRESH + 2σ` = 51 d from live sessions too (§7.7 point 4), and P-7 holds without an exception once a week is 45 days past, since 51 d > 45 d is the erasure schedule the statement quantifies over. The declared exception now reads only as the ≤ 51-day window before a prune sweep runs. No seed-derived key opens an intro inner layer, a provisional envelope or a pair envelope: intro envelopes are unattributable once the recipient's bundle generation is erased (≤ 91 d after supersession, ≤ 31 d later for a generation a held row still references), provisional envelopes once both devices erased `prov_env` (each at most `prov_anchor_minute + 119 d`, earlier through the pair-open rule) and that generation is erased (§22.8.5). | T34: seizure scenario, erasure tests, PM-19, PM-36 |
| P-8 | **Harvest-now-decrypt-later confidentiality of relay-stored DM bytes (Level 2).** A passive adversary that records every relay-stored DM byte and later breaks X25519 but not ML-KEM-768, and never obtains device state, opens no intro inner layer, provisional or pair envelope, and no bundle or intro outer layer without the card. Authentication (PQ-R1) and network metadata (PQ-R4) are not covered. | T35: provenance tags, PQ1–PQ5, ProVerif M-1 and M-3 in CI, PM-32, PM-33, PM-37 |

**Correctness and local soundness**

| ID | Guarantee | Proof |
|---|---|---|
| CS-1 | **Atomic send.** A ratchet step commits if and only if its message row and outbox op commit; no message key encrypts two plaintexts after process death (spec §7.2(b)). | SCN-A crash enumeration, MC-1, MC-2, MC-29 |
| CS-2 | **Exactly-once display.** Each `message_id` appears at most once per device across crashes at any SQL statement, native crashes and Rust panics, disk-full faults, restarts, several relays, relay re-serves and resurrections, identical-bytes resends and re-encryptions after FAILED. | SCN-B, SCN-G, SCN-J, `oracle_displayed` primary key |
| CS-3 | **No loss inside the declared envelope.** A message whose op ends `SENT` or `DEGRADED` is displayed once by a recipient that lists its inbox within the TTL, inside Olm's limits after peek ordering and the gap hold (§7.7 point 3 **as corrected by S0′, V-7, 2026-09-19**: no message of its receiver chain more than 40 indices ahead decrypted before it, no more than 40 keys of that chain skipped after its own key was stored, at most 5 newer receiver chains, a forward gap ≤ 2 000, its session not deleted or retired — round 9's "at most 40 later messages … decrypted before it" did not describe Olm) and with initiator sessions answered within 28 days (§8.7), unless a declared loss applies: restore (§8.6), an undecided key change past its hold bound (§8.3), an unread ephemeral past its cap (§10.3), content refused as stale (FRESH, §7.6), a recipient offline beyond its registrations (§4.8), a poison blob that killed the consumer's process three times (§7.4 R2), a stale first flight of a contact arriving after two newer sessions of that contact (`drop(stale_session)`, §22.8.4). | seeded worlds, RO3′, RO7, SCN-E, SCN-H, SCN-J |
| CS-4 | **Deterministic order** on both devices whatever the arrival order, restarts or replays (except the clamp for a peer clock in the future). | reorder oracle (all 720 permutations of 6), SCN-B |
| CS-5 | **Key change blocks.** No ciphertext is produced toward, and no plaintext is shown from, a changed identity before an explicit user decision; a change without a valid signature under the pinned Ed25519 key causes no state change; no session is started in a sender's name from a compromised recipient state (KC13). | KC suite, SCN-D, PM-15…PM-17, PM-34, MC-6 |
| CS-6 | **T25 bounds.** After expiry, no row, plaintext, envelope bytes, retained blob or notification content remains on either device; the WAL is truncated. | T25 logical and physical scans, emulator T25 |
| CS-7 | **Replay refusal.** Replays, cross-namespace copies, resurrections after tombstone GC, replayed first flights and stale content never create a second row, a second session or any traffic. | RP suite with each dedup layer isolated, RP11 |
| CS-8 | **Bounded local state.** Skipped keys (≤ 40 per receiver chain × 5 chains per session, ≤ 2 sessions per contact, retired sessions erased after 46 d, **and live sessions pruned at `FRESH + 2σ` = 51 d since S0′ adopted the age bound, V-15, 2026-09-19**), session state (≤ `SESSION_STATE_MAX` = 65 536 B; **measured worst case 36 685 B plus a ≤ 2 566 B first-seen sidecar = 39 251 B**, V-4 and V-15), held rows, dedup tags, bundle generations (≤ 14), peer X-Wing keys (≤ 3 per contact), recognition keys (≤ 22 per contact), pair keys, retained blobs, requests and registrations each have a stated bound and a GC rule. | introspection, structural invariants after every reboot (§15.3), SCN-H, unit tests |
| CS-9 | **Migration safety.** v1/v2/v3 → v4 → v5 preserves every sync and entitlement row, `outbox_op` rowids, and the trigger set; a crash at any statement leaves the previous version, which migrates again. | SCN-L, `SchemaAndMigrationTest`, emulator SQLCipher migration |

### 0.3 What changed from the base design

These tables record round 9 (the synthesis of 2026-09-14). Rows whose libsignal-specific content the revision of 2026-09-16 withdrew or re-sourced are marked "(revised §22)"; the grafts and error notes of round 9b are RG-1 … RG-11 and RX-1 … RX-14 in §22.2 and §22.3.

**Grafts (judge list, all adopted)**

| # | Graft | Source | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| G-1 | Full DM transaction inventory and crash-point table; native-crash poison (consume unprocessed after 3 offers, through `InboundBlob.offers`); the `SQLITE_FULL` fault suite; value-state rules (a fresh native object per call, state written only on `OK`, no session cache across transactions) and the `olm_session.state_env` length CHECK (revised §22: the libsignal `TxSignalStores` rules and the `sig_session` CHECK are withdrawn). | operations §5.1, §5.9, §5.10 | §7.2, §7.8, §7.9, §13.6 |
| G-2 | Golden state fixtures per pinned version (revised §22): golden pickles and transcripts under `client-core/dm/tests/golden/vodozemac-<version>/`, the `UpgradeTest` drill and the dehydrated-pickle encoder check, required by gate G-R7 at every pin change (the libsignal golden records and `LibsignalUpgradeTest` are withdrawn). | operations §5.10, §12.1 | §2.8, §15.2, §16.1 |
| G-3 | Registrations for weeks `{cap_week, cap_week + 1}` made with the installed write capability, checked by the relay against the capability's week; the sender's fallback to the previous week's credential (removed by §21.2: credentials are static per relay slot); the ~2-week offline argument (about 2–3 weeks after §21.2); the need reason of a registration-only namespace stated (pending registrations count as write work, Q31). Replaces the base's `t_roll` and assumption A14. | operations §3.2–§3.4 | §4.4, §4.8, §13.3 |
| G-4 | Static inbox and bundle namespaces per card index, credentials rotated weekly; `set(ns, w)` over the week's ES slots with a 6-week union when slots change; AD binding (namespace, bucket) kept; the credential's week bound through the verifier; the static-inbox residue on a seized relay disk declared. | operations D5, §3.1 | §5.1–§5.2, §11.5 (L7-D13) |
| G-5 | ADR-27 point 11 kept exactly (30 d timer → 30 d; off → 30 d; 5 min and 1 h → 1 h); the collapse to 7 d is only an owner question (Q15). FRESH = 45 d; prekey retention re-derived (bundle-generation retention since §22, §5.4); the pair-mode week-key window widened to the longest legitimate blob lifetime. | delivery §7.1, operations §6.1 | §10.1, §7.6, §5.4, §6.3 |
| G-6 | Restore healing (revised §22, §8.6): pre-restore intro envelopes name random bundle generations the lost device held, so they stay unopenable and are counted (`unknown_seal`, `unknown_generation`); healing comes from re-added cards and the user's `reconnect(contacts)` (a PreKey intro under the same Olm identity, spread over 48 h), from contacts' resets once the restored device holds a token and publishes a bundle, and from the P1 contact backup (Q28, Q50). The residue that pair envelopes of established contacts are unopenable after a restore stays. | operations §2.5, §10.3; security §6.4 | §8.6 |
| G-7 | Automatic identical-bytes resend of INDETERMINATE ops, at most 2, only while `now < resendNotAfter` and before the op deadline, from messaging's retained blob; FAILED stays a user retry that re-encrypts the same L0. | operations §5.5 | §7.5 |
| G-8 | The `ConsumerTail` contract (job thread, after a background session's lanes and redeem hold, after a quiet run's issuer call, no transport, deadline = job deadline − 20 s, the DM actor the only writer) with its `SessionParticipantTest` cases. Replaces the base's `ConsumerTask`, whose text contradicted itself on quiet runs (E-14). | operations D14, §8.1–§8.2 | §13.3 |
| G-9 | The `outbox_op`/`outbox_delivery` rebuild with explicit column lists including `rowid`, the `legacy_alter_table = OFF` assertion, `PRAGMA foreign_key_check`, trigger text shared as one constant with migrations 2 and 3, and a before/after `sqlite_master` object-set test. | operations §7.2 | §13.6 |
| G-10 | The Phase 7 window re-derivation for TTL ∈ {1 h, 1 d}: safety unchanged, liveness degrades to INDETERMINATE, the receipt lower bound vacuous and declared; S-F gains offline windows of 30 min, 3 h and 3 d. | operations §6.7 | §10.4, §11.5 (L7-D15) |
| G-11 | Emulator job on in-process loopback model relays, a SHA-pinned emulator action, API 29 and 35 x86_64 images, a separate `:peer` process killed with SIGKILL, real SQLCipher v3 → v4 → v5 migration, a release-variant smoke test that loads `libghost_client_net.so` and completes one in-process Olm session through `DmNative` (revised §22), and the host/Android equivalence test (RG-2). Public Tor stays manual. | operations §11.9; delivery §11.6 | §15.9 |
| G-12 | Scope cuts: cut-off rotation deferred to Phase 13 with the P1 contact backup (`card_index` stays in the card); the DropSeal outer-shape change deferred; Invite v3 deferred to Phase 13 rather than declined. | delivery §0.6; operations Q22 | §0.6, §12.5 |
| G-13 | Seeded worlds with week boundaries, one ES slot-table change, offline windows up to 40 days, backward and forward clock steps; scenarios SCN-E (recipient offline across 3 week boundaries) and SCN-H (13-week bundle-generation rotation with a PreKey message at the 91-day generation boundary and a replayed first message, revised §22). | operations §11.2–§11.3 | §15.3–§15.4 |

| G-17 | First-contact reachability after activation and restore stated (at the first relay session under Q31's default; up to the READ MISSING PRF time of ≤ 6 h otherwise) and made an owner question. | delivery §5.4 | §4.8, Q31 |

**Conflicts decided**

| # | Conflict | Decision | Why |
|---|---|---|---|
| C-1 | Session identity (the Olm identity since §22): derived or random per install | **Derived** from MESSAGING (loaded through `Account::from_dehydrated_device`, §2.2); key changes authenticated by an Ed25519 signature under the pinned identity; residue: a mnemonic thief on the official client raises no warning (L7-D18) | FR-1.2 is P0 with recovery vectors; spec Figure 2, TM:81 and `DerivationLabels.kt:19` say so; a random identity makes every restore a blocking key change at every contact |
| C-2 | Outer seal | **`salt ‖ hint ‖ AEAD` in two modes** (security); four modes since §22 (intro with an X-Wing inner layer, provisional added) | The Elligator 2 variant depends on an alpha fork crate and omits the low-order component (E-3); raw X25519 declares T9 unmet |
| C-3 | Where seal and codec live | **Rust** (`ghost-client-dm`; revised §22, which withdraws the round-9 Kotlin placement on BouncyCastle and deviation X-9) | the ratchet and the X-Wing KEM are native, and KEM secrets must not reach the JVM heap; ADR-19 and ADR-20 point 8 hold again (§2.4) |
| C-4 | Inbox namespace rotation | **Static per card, weekly credentials** (G-4) | Rotation does not break continuity under AD-1, costs read pairs and tokens at each roll, and loses all mail for recipients offline more than 3 weeks |
| C-5 | Owner inbox read | **Seed-derived READ key via `RegisterKey`** | F8 §10.7 points 2–3 and ADR-25 point 9 require it in Phase 9; the RPC exists for deposits anyway |
| C-6 | Verifier registration | **`RegisterKey`** with the `{w, w + 1}` rule against the capability week | Piggybacking on `RedeemToken` ties deposit liveness to one capability week and cannot register read keys |
| C-7 | Prekey distribution | **Sealed bundle in a card-derived namespace**; HELLO recorded as the rejected alternative with its benefits (no fetch linkability, one-time-prekey forward secrecy) | plan row 9 and Annex B; asynchronous first message without an accept round trip |
| C-8 | Relay TTL for 30 d and off | **30 d**, as ADR-27 point 11; the 7 d collapse is Q15 | approved text |
| C-9 | FRESH | **45 d**, with deadlines ≤ 7 d (SC-3); `dm_seen`, pair-key and prekey windows re-derived | follows C-8 |
| C-10 | Private key-material retention | **Derived: superseded + 91 d** (bundle validity 7 d + `UNACK_MAX` 28 d + FRESH 45 d + 2σ 6 d, §5.4; revised §22) | recomputed without libsignal constants |
| C-11 | Unread cap source | **Relay expiry through `InboundBlob.relayExpiryHour`**, clamped to `consume + bucket + 1 h` | ADR-27 C.9 literally; a sender clock ahead cannot extend the deadline |
| C-12 | Deletion wake-up | **One inexact non-exported alarm**, T20 wording amended | Doze would otherwise delay physical deletion for hours |
| C-13 | Background DM consumption | **`ConsumerTail`** (G-8) | otherwise fetched ciphertext and `FETCHED_CAP` wait for the next foreground (E-17) |
| C-14 | Key-change behaviour | **Block sending and hold new-key inbound, classified only through a signature valid under the pinned Ed25519 key** (a bundle signature or binding v3) | the variant without such a signature is spoofable (E-1); showing old-key messages alone is weaker against a mnemonic thief |
| C-15 | INDETERMINATE | **Automatic identical-bytes resend, ≤ 2** (G-7) | allowed by the Phase 7 contract; not a reflex |
| C-16 | Cut-off rotation | **Deferred to Phase 13** | needs the P1 contact backup to survive a restore |
| C-17 | Phase 8 drop outer shape | **Deferred** (Q18) | T2 NI-2/NI-3 were just closed with no exemption (F8 §19.29) |
| C-18 | Invite v3 card | **Phase 13**; ADR-05 stays open | UX work; T16 untouched in Phase 9 |
| C-19 | T32 statistical DM world | **In the Phase 9 gate**: PR scale in the `android` job, gate scale nightly | R-privacy §6, CP-10 (no claim without a test) |
| C-20 | Restore semantics | **Security residue + operations healing** (G-6) | the only combination consistent with C-1 and random pair roots |
| C-21 | Emulator relays | **Loopback model relays** (G-11); live Tor manual | a job that depends on public Tor reports "not run" too often to be gate evidence |
| C-22 | APK size limit | **Owner question, default 70 MiB** (spec), native library reported separately (Q24); no longer at risk: the DM crates add **+494 992 B (arm64-v8a) and +629 904 B (x86_64)**, measured by S0′ (V-9, 2026-09-19; the round-9b estimate of 0.2–0.5 MB and the ≈ 22 MB libsignal estimate are both withdrawn) | the release APK is 41.5 MB today (RC §7) |
| C-23 | Local content key | **Keystore-wrapped**, same authentication policy as the database key | the "no plaintext content column" rule and a meaningful T25 byte scan |
| C-24 | First contact from an unknown card holder | **Requests** with caps | asynchronous first message; no reflex on accept |
| C-25 | Revocation certificate | **v2** (minute timestamp) inside E2E | T13 alignment; it only travels in new DM code |

**Judge errors fixed**

| # | Error | Fix |
|---|---|---|
| E-1 | (operations) Any library trust refusal became a held key change without a binding: forgeable by anyone holding two cards. | §8.3: a key change requires a signature valid under the pinned Ed25519 key, checked **before** any session call (`RECV` step R6); `IDENTITY_MISMATCH` alone is a drop; mutant PM-17 and test KC5. |
| E-2 | (operations) §3.5 refused a bundle whose identity differs from the card, §10.2 treated it as a key change. | §5.5 and §8.3 say the same: a bundle whose signature verifies under the pinned Ed25519 key but names another session identity (the Olm identity since §22) is a key change; a bundle whose signature does not verify is refused. |
| E-3 | (operations) Elligator 2 recipe without the low-order component; candidate crate an alpha fork. | Not used (C-2). §6.1 records why. |
| E-4 | (security, delivery) "No AEAD crate in the lock": `ring` 0.17.14 provides ChaCha20-Poly1305 and is `required` in the `ghost-client-net` graph (`ghost/scripts/gates/rust-crypto-pins.txt:19`). | Withdrawn with the Kotlin seal (revised §22): the seal is in Rust (§2.4). The fact stays true: `ring` is transitive for `ghost-client-net` (through `rustls`, `ghost/Cargo.toml:57,62`). |
| E-5 | (delivery) `INSERT … SELECT rowid, *` supplies one value too many. | §13.6 step 1 lists every column explicitly (G-9). |
| E-6 | (delivery, operations) A new relay table was said to leave T2 unaffected. | §4.9: `keys.redb` joins the T2 export and completeness checks; `t2-exit-gate` changes (G-16, X-14). |
| E-7 | (delivery) Weekly inbox redemptions claimed equal to the Phase 8 interim. | §4.8 counts the new redemptions (1 per relay, own namespace and week) and §4.9 models them in T2. |
| E-8 | (delivery) "Offline up to a month still receives" while registrations lapse within a week. | §4.8: registrations live `start(week + 2) + 1 h`, a sender falls back one week, so the tolerance is about two weeks of absence; messages stored in that time live up to their TTL. |
| E-9 | (delivery) Random DM keys contradict FR-1.2 and Figure 2. | C-1. |
| E-10 | (security) Token accounting: WRITE MISSING only where the outbox has work; a weekly namespace roll cost two tokens. | Static namespaces (1 token per relay, own namespace and week, on the EXPIRING path); registration rows count as write work (§4.8, §13.3). |
| E-11 | (security) Deposits charged to the capability's ledger entry, which expires before the registration. | §4.5: a `DepositLedger` entry per registration, allowance credited by each registering capability, bounded by the registration's validity. |
| E-12 | (security) Bundle plaintext length. | 1 393 bytes (bundle v2, §5.3; revised §22; the libsignal-era 1 886 and 1 890 B are withdrawn). |
| E-13 | (security) Hint workload understated. | §6.2 R-S5: round 9 counted at most 9 week keys per contact plus one intro key (≤ 9 001 HMACs per blob); revised §22: ≤ 22 recognition keys per contact (pair generations and provisional keys) plus `KIO_0`, so ≤ 22 001 HMACs per unknown blob at `MAX_CONTACTS = 1 000`, computed lazily, most recent first. |
| E-14 | (security) The consumer task both never ran and ran in quiet runs. | G-8: the `ConsumerTail` runs in quiet runs after the issuer call returned and the transport closed. |
| E-15 | (delivery) Client-chosen, card-carried inbox slots (R-privacy F11). | Not used: `set(ns, w)` is a public function (§5.2). |
| E-16 | (delivery) Kotlin seal without recording the ADR-19 deviation. | Withdrawn (revised §22): the seal is in Rust, X-9 is withdrawn. |
| E-17 | (delivery) Background consumption through the listener slot only. | G-8. |
| E-18 | (all) libsignal logged untrusted identity keys in hex at warn level and a decryption-failure report at error level. | Withdrawn with libsignal (revised §22): no crate of the DM graph logs (gates G-R4, G-R8); the T3 canary scan of every output stream and the emulator logcat canary scan stay (§14.2, §15.9). |

**Corrections found during synthesis (normative)**

| # | Finding | Correction |
|---|---|---|
| SC-1 | The base's `RegisterKey` let any write-capability holder **replace** a verifier. A card holder can redeem its own token for a contact's inbox and replace the owner's deposit verifier: a targeted denial of service on every other sender. | Verifiers accumulate per (namespace, week, kind); nothing is replaced; one capability registers at most one verifier per (namespace, week, kind) (§4.4). Cut-off, the only planned use of replacement, moves to a new card index in Phase 13. |
| SC-2 | A card holder can redeem its own token for a contact's inbox and list it with that write capability (write grants read): inbound volume and timing, content sealed. No candidate declared it. | Declared L7-D16; an owner-bound read rule is a Phase 12 finding (§20 F-5). |
| SC-3 | FRESH = 45 d does not hold if a parked 30-day or timer-off op may be stored 30 days after compose (fetch up to 60 days after compose). | Deadline `author + min(bucket, 7 d)` for every DM op (§10.1), so a legitimate fetch comes at most 43 d 2 h after compose, clock skew included (§7.6). |
| SC-4 | "Widen the pair-mode window" had no bound. | A week key is erased at `start(w + 1) + FRESH`: no legitimate blob of week w can be accepted later (§6.3), which is also the T34 horizon. |
| SC-5 | The 277-character card link fits QR version 11-L (byte capacity 321), not 12-L. | §3.3. |
| SC-6 | Prekey retention depends on bundle validity, which no candidate fixed together with the 30-day TTL. | Bundle validity = its ISO week and the next; retention = superseded + 91 d (§5.4). |
| SC-7 | A consumer that runs while the transport is still open would make the relay session's close time depend on inbound volume. | The `ConsumerTail` runs after the transport closed (§13.3); NI-DM1 compares close times. |

### 0.5 Deviations from approved documents (each recorded in an ADR, "propus, aplicat")

| # | Deviation | From | ADR |
