### 0.6 What this design deliberately does not do

- **No server-certificate sealed sender, no sender certificates, no fake trust root** (§6.1). The only third-party protocol code GHOST calls is Olm's session layer; the seal, bindings, bundles and safety numbers are GHOST's (CP-09 exceptions).
- **No per-sender write capabilities, no token spent on a foreign namespace, and no fallback to them** when a recipient has not registered: the deposit waits for a credential and the op ends at its deadline.
- **No delivery or read receipts, no typing indicators, no automatic retry requests or session resets** in Phase 9. Read receipts may come in Phase 13 as an opt-in flag piggybacked on a user message, STANDARD mode only (Q8).
- **No contacting from a bare `ghost1…`.** A discoverable mode would reopen R-privacy F1 by construction (Q7).
- **No one-time keys, no attestation chain and no revocation namespace on relays.**
- **No weekly inbox rotation** (C-4) and **no cut-off card rotation** in Phase 9 (C-16; `card_index` stays 0, reserved in the card).
- **No Invite v3** (C-18) and **no change to the Phase 8 drop format** (C-17).
- **No cover traffic** (P2), no DM-specific schedule, no shorter listing interval: NFR-1 is measured and recorded, not claimed (Q23).
- **No multi-device** (P2): in P0 a "new device of a contact" is an identity-key change (RS K17).
- **No FTS index, no copied quote text, no content in notifications, no conversation "last activity" column, no persisted rejection history.**
- **No second periodic job, no exact alarm, no WorkManager, no FCM, no coroutines in GHOST code.**
- **New Rust crates only for the DM stack** (vodozemac 0.11.0 and its graph; `libcrux-kem`, `libcrux-ml-kem`, `libcrux-sha3`, `libcrux-curve25519` and their graph; ADR-35, ADR-36), **no new Gradle coordinate**, no MPL-2.0 package added, no Elligator 2 crate, no fork or vendored patch of vodozemac, no hazmat API (`low-level-api`) in the shipped graph.
- **No PQ ratchet and no quantum-resistance claim:** Olm stays classical under the X-Wing-keyed seal (PQ-R2, PQ-R7); no UI or marketing statement about quantum resistance before the model and the external review (CP-10, §9.8).
- **No public binary artifacts** (APK, AAR, `.so`, images) while the repository is public (G-L5), and no claim "open source" or "verifiable by anyone" anywhere (§2.10.6).

---

## 1. Adversary views and the declared DM leakage

### 1.1 Who sees what (effective views, not only T1 captures)

T1 constrains what an honest relay records in capture mode. AD-1 keeps everything its processes see (RP §0.1). This design reasons about the **effective view**.

| Observer | Effective view per DM-related event |
|---|---|
| Relay (AD-2) | op; namespace; blob hash; size bucket; the TTL as stored; exact arrival time; the credential presented (so its scope hash and, by construction, its kind); request id; the onion circuit (all requests of one circuit are linkable); stored bytes; `keys.redb` records; its own quota ledgers |
| AD-1 | the union over all relays, plus the issuer (Phase 8 declared leak L1–L7) |
| AD-4 / AD-5 | Tor traffic timing and volume at one or both ends |
| AD-7 (a contact) | the target's card (hence `X`, `P`, the per-slot deposit and bundle read secrets, the bundle and intro outer keys), the target's bundles (Olm identity, fallback and X-Wing public keys, week and rank), the recognisability of intro envelopes to the target (not their inner layer), the content of their own conversation, the safety number; with its own tokens, a write capability for `X` (SC-2) |
| AD-8 / AD-12 | local rows, WAL and files; a seized relay disk (blobs, `keys.redb`, `nullifiers.redb`) |

**Rule V1 (normative).** Access control on relays protects nothing against the relay itself. Every relay-stored DM byte is sealed under keys that are **never presented to a relay**. Read secrets, deposit secrets and capabilities are presented; seal keys, bundle keys, pair keys, intro keys and card secrets never are.

### 1.2 Declared DM leakage LD (the only information T32 lets through)

Per inbox namespace per relay:
- the minutes of stores, their size buckets (4/16/64 KiB) and TTL buckets (1 h / 1 d / 7 d / 30 d), and the one static deposit scope of the (relay, inbox) (§21.2);
- `check` events on the same scope;
- the owner's list schedule (T19, presence) and the number of gets per list;
- the owner's weekly redemption and `register` events at the redeem lane's renewal time (§4.8);
- bundle publications at the card's fixed weekly offset;
- bundle fetches (one read scope per bundle namespace per relay; at most three attempts per establishment, §21.7);
- the moments of activation, restore and revocation notices (L7-D10);
- the session-start co-occurrence windows of Phase 7 (LIMITE L2.1 #1);
- (§21.11) the grouping of one client's requests per circuit within a session (L7-D5), exact arrival times, the identical hash of an identical-bytes resend (L7-D9), and the result of every request, refusals included.

Everything else a relay can compute from its effective view is a T32 violation.

Card holders (AD-7) additionally see what the card opens: bundle rotations, the publication week and, through `bundle_rank`, whether a publication in that week was outside the weekly schedule (activation or restore) (§5.3, revised §22). **Corrected (§22.8.7):** a card holder that polls `P` with the shared fetch credential sees the minute each bundle first appears (the owner's first time-triggered pass after `t_pub`, hence absences, and activation and restore minutes); fetch reads return only week-coarse `uploaded_at` and expiry. **Corrected (review of S1, 2026-09-19):** "week-coarse" covers the `list` cursor too — the relay's per-namespace sequence is an upload hour and a store count, so it is sealed under the relay key before it leaves (§21.9 point 4). A card holder that spends its own token on a write capability for my inbox additionally sees every intro-envelope arrival to the minute, its `gen_id`, and which conversations I have not answered (L7-D3, L7-D16).

### 1.3 The property T32 proves (the privacy statement of the exit gate)

1. **(a) Join-freedom.** The exact searches JD1–JD9 find nothing in the effective relay view (§15.6).
2. **(b) Non-interference.** Worlds that differ only in inbound content, message type, a client's degree, the timer within a bucket, or local trust actions give identical shape views (NI-DM1–NI-DM6).
3. **(c) No learned advantage.** No attacker on the full effective view recovers conversation edges or identity mappings better than the same attacker restricted to LD (S-DM1, S-DM3 at α = 0.001). The LD attacker's success is measured and reported (S-DM2, S-DM4), not claimed away.

(a) and (b) are exact for every seed; (c) is statistical for the pinned seeds. The residue inside LD is declared in LIMITE L7 (§11.5) and in the UI copy (CP-10).

**Scope (§22.8.8).** The property, and P-1 … P-8, are proved for the design and hold against AD-1 only for the build that is published in the transparency log and reproduced by the auditors (ADR-38). Under closed-source distribution the operator of AD-1, who can publish new versions, and the vendor adversary AD-V are in practice one party; a build outside that set is AD-V and none of these guarantees applies to it.

---

## 2. DM library integration and licensing (I1; ADR-35, ADR-37 … ADR-39)

This section was rewritten by the revision of 2026-09-16 (§22). The round-9 route comparison for libsignal, its version and repository rules, API list, store rules, native-library rules, logging rules, gates G-LS1 … G-LS7, the Kotlin-seal rationale and the AGPL licence analysis are withdrawn.

### 2.1 Library decision and alternatives (normative)

**Forward note (S4b, 2026-09-20).** §22.9 items 9 and 10 record what the pickle reads of `chainPosition` and `sessionPrune` leave behind and where `chainPosition` is stricter than function 19's row.

The owner excluded libsignal and every (A)GPL component (2026-09-16). No permissively licensed library matches libsignal's maturity; the ranking of R-libs §11, re-weighted for the owner decision, is:

| Candidate | Licence (whole shipped graph) | Maturity evidence | Fit for GHOST DMs | Disposition |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **vodozemac 0.11.0 (Olm)** | Apache-2.0; graph MIT, Apache-2.0, BSD-3-Clause, `Unlicense OR MIT`, `(MIT OR Apache-2.0) AND Unicode-3.0`; **no MPL** | Least Authority audit (final report 2022-03-30; Issues I and J unresolved); deployment in Element and Element X through matrix-rust-sdk; Olm analyses by Albrecht, Dowling and Jones (IEEE S&P 2024) and by Ginesin and Nita-Rotaru (2024); releases 0.10.0 (2026-04-13) and 0.11.0 (2026-09-11) with 2026 security fixes | asynchronous 1:1 AKE from a published fallback key, per-message ratchet, no clock, no logging, no network, serialisable state, about +196 KB stripped per ABI | **chosen** (D1) |
| OpenMLS 0.9.0 as two-member groups | MIT; `hpke-rs` MPL-2.0 in the graph | SRLabs audit report v1.2 of 2026-03-11 of the core (crypto providers and the PQ suite out of scope; finding S0-1 risk-accepted, S1-3 storage atomicity left to the application); forked deployments (XMTP, Wire) | commit ordering (RFC 9420 §14) conflicts with blind unordered relays; PCS only at commits; the X-Wing suite forces every DM to 16 KiB; second- and nanosecond-precision times in state; library log sites; +2.5–3.1 MB per ABI | the documented DM fallback (Q44) and the Phase 10 library (§20 F-10) |
| mls-rs 0.56.0 | Apache-2.0 OR MIT | no third-party audit (its README); PQ only through AWS-LC | as OpenMLS | rejected |
| An own implementation of Signal's public-domain specifications | clean | none | FR-2.1 prohibits a custom Double Ratchet | rejected; used only as design input |
| libolm | Apache-2.0 | deprecated 2024-08-27, unfixed CVEs | — | rejected |
| libsignal, `spqr`, `libsignal-protocol-java`, Wire core-crypto and Proteus, SimpleX, Session, Briar, Olvid, `pq-ratchet` | AGPL-3.0 or GPL-3.0 | — | — | excluded by licence (ADR-37) |
| `wacore-libsignal` | declared MIT on crates.io, sources carry AGPL-3.0-only and Signal Messenger headers | — | — | excluded; the reason for the source-header gate G-R5 |

**Decision (ADR-35).** Olm from vodozemac 0.11.0, used unchanged through public API only, inside a GHOST crate. The residues this choice adds are declared: no PQ inside the ratchet (PQ-R2), Olm's 40-skipped-keys window (§7.7), the maturity gap to libsignal (§2.9, L7-D22) and the GHOST-built layers around Olm (CP-09 exceptions: seal, binding v3, provisional keys, session selection, dehydrated-pickle encoder, safety number).

**Corrected by S0′ (V-7, V-15, 2026-09-19; correction S0-15 of `docs/design/faza9-s0-evidence.md`).** "Used unchanged through **public API only**" has exactly one recorded exception, and it is a **deviation from this decision and from ADR-35**, not a reading of it: `chainPosition` and `sessionPrune` (§2.6 functions 19 and 20) read and write vodozemac's **serde pickle layout**. `SessionPickle`'s members, `ReceiverChain` (`pub(super)`) and `MessageKeyStore` are all private, so `/receiving_chains/inner`, `ratchet_key`, `/hkdf_ratchet/index` and `/skipped_message_keys/inner` are serde-derived names outside vodozemac's semver surface (`vz:src/olm/session/mod.rs:640-645`, `receiver_chain.rs:53-55,131-150`) — the same reason §2.2 and V-1 forbid `Account::from_pickle`. It is taken because no upstream API exposes a receiver chain's position or removes a skipped key (V-15), and no other route to the restart-proof gap hold (§7.7 point 2) or to the age bound (§7.7 point 4) exists on either pin. Its cost is bounded by three rules: the layout is pinned per vodozemac version by **G-R7** (§16.1), every drift is loud (`STATE_CORRUPT`, never a silent default, §2.6 and §7.7 point 2), and Q58's upstream contribution (a pruning API and a chain-position accessor) removes the deviation when vodozemac ships it.

### 2.2 How vodozemac's API gaps are closed (normative; public API only except the recorded pickle-layout deviation of §2.1, verified in 0.11.0 and 0.10.0)

| Gap | Resolution | Evidence |
|---|---|---|
| No constructor from given identity keys | `Account::from_dehydrated_device(ciphertext_b64, nonce_b64, key)` decrypts ChaCha20-Poly1305 and decodes the MSC3814 pickle `{version u32 = 1, private_curve25519_key [32], private_ed25519_key [32], one_time_keys Vec, opt_fallback_key}`; it is not feature-gated in 0.10.0 or 0.11.0. GHOST encodes that pickle with the seed-derived X25519 secret, a derived unused Ed25519 seed and the fallback secret of the needed generation, encrypts it under a fresh random key, and discards the `Account` after one call. A golden test decrypts `Account::to_dehydrated_device` output of an account that called `generate_fallback_key()` (so the `opt_fallback_key` branch is covered) and compares GHOST's encoder byte for byte at every pin, and checks the imported fallback secret by a session from a second account (V-1, §22.8.10 point 14). The libolm-pickle import (`libolm-compat`) is not needed. **Corrected by S0′ (V-1, 2026-09-19):** the pickle is `u32_be(1) ‖ curve25519_secret(32) ‖ ed25519_seed(32) ‖ u32_be(0) ‖ u8(1) ‖ fallback_secret(32)` (105 B; `u8(0)` and no secret without a fallback key, 73 B), confirmed byte for byte against `to_dehydrated_device` of 0.11.0 and 0.10.0 on 64 of 64 exports. Two further public construction paths exist and are **not used**: `Account::from_pickle(AccountPickle)` takes vodozemac's serde layout, which is undocumented and unversioned (the MSC3814 layout is specified and versioned), and `Account::from_decrypted_dehydrated_device` is `#[doc(hidden)]`, "public for fuzzing" (`vz:src/olm/account/mod.rs:576-594`). | `vz:src/olm/account/mod.rs:508,542,919-929`; matrix-rust-sdk `crates/matrix-sdk-crypto/src/olm/account.rs` calls `from_dehydrated_device` and `generate_fallback_key` (fetched 2026-09-16) |
| Only two fallback-key slots | GHOST stores every generation's fallback secret itself (`olm_prekey_gen`) and builds a throwaway `Account` holding exactly the generation a PreKey message names | `find_one_time_key` searches one-time then fallback keys (`vz:src/olm/account/mod.rs:221`) |
| A replayed PreKey message on a fallback key creates a new session | GHOST matches `PreKeyMessage::session_id()` against stored sessions, then refuses a `(gen_id, session_id)` already in `olm_prekey_used`, in the `RECV` transaction (D2b, §7.6) | fallback keys are not removed on use (`vz:src/olm/account/mod.rs:324`) |
| 40 skipped keys per chain, 5 receiver chains, not configurable | peek-ordered consumption, a bounded gap hold, a declared bound; an age bound evaluated in S0′ (§7.7, Q67). **Corrected by S0′ (V-7, V-15, 2026-09-19):** measured — peek ordering alone loses about 12 % of a 60-message HIGH burst for an offline recipient; the gap hold with its expected index **read from the stored session** removes every loss in 72 × 200 worlds, and the age bound is **adopted** (§7.7 points 2 and 4, Q67, Q54) | `vz:src/olm/session/receiver_chain.rs:31,50`; `vz:src/olm/session/mod.rs:55` |
| **Receiver-chain position and skipped-key eviction are not on the public API** (added by S0′, V-7 and V-15, 2026-09-19; correction S0-15) | `chainPosition` (§2.6 function 19) and `sessionPrune` (function 20) edit the serde pickle layout, pinned per vodozemac version. **Recorded deviation from §2.1/ADR-35 "public API only"**: the layout is undocumented and unversioned, so G-R7 pins both outputs per pin and any drift is `STATE_CORRUPT` — `chainPosition` returns `STATE_CORRUPT` when `/receiving_chains/inner` is a non-empty array in which no entry carries a readable 32-byte `ratchet_key`, and `NEW_CHAIN` only when the chains are readable and none matches; returning 0 on a renamed field would report every chain as new, never fire the hold, and bring the 12 % offline-burst loss back with no result code. `sessionPrune` fails with `STATE_CORRUPT` on an unreadable chain or entry | `vz:src/olm/session/mod.rs:640-645`; `receiver_chain.rs:53-55,131-150`; V-7 `chain_position_refuses_a_drifted_layout` |
| A failed decrypt is not all-or-nothing in memory | value semantics: a fresh object per call, discarded on any non-`OK` result (§7.9) | `vz:src/olm/session/double_ratchet.rs:168-191` |
| No post-quantum protection | the GHOST seal layer (§9) | — |
| `Session::from_root_key_material` builds only the sending side (`low-level-api`; the receiving `new_remote` is `pub(super)`) | not used in Phase 9; seeding Olm from a hybrid handshake is the deferred option PQ-D (§9.7) | `vz:src/olm/session/mod.rs:301-314,339` |

### 2.3 Crates and linkage (normative)

| Crate | Kind | Contents | Shipped |
|---|---|---|---|
| `client-core/dm` = `ghost-client-dm` | `lib` (rlib), `publish = false`, no default features | `derive` (`DmLabels`, `CardLabels`), `card` (v2 codec), `bundle`, `seal` (pair, intro, provisional, bundle), `codec` (L0, TLV registry), `olm` (sessions over vodozemac), `pq` (X-Wing wrapper, PQ-C), `safety` (safety number v1), `wrap` (state wrapping), `result` (codes); feature `jni-bridge` adds `jni.rs` | yes, inside `libghost_client_net.so` |
| `client-core/net` = `ghost-client-net` | existing `cdylib` | gains `ghost-client-dm = { path = "../dm", features = ["jni-bridge"] }` and invokes the export macro `ghost_client_dm::export_jni!()` in `jni_bridge.rs` (**corrected by S0′, V-5, 2026-09-19:** macro exports and a dependency's `#[no_mangle]` exports both survive linking and `strip = "symbols"` on arm64-v8a and x86_64, beside the 21 pre-existing `Java_org_ghost` exports, so the round-9 fallback "explicit wrapper functions" is not needed; the macro is kept so the closed list stays visible in `ghost-client-net` for G-R6) | yes |
| `client-core/dm-host` = `ghost-dm-host` | `cdylib`, test-only package | `ghost-client-dm` with `jni-bridge` for `x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu` and `x86_64-pc-windows-msvc`; never built for Android; excluded from `rust-client-allowlist.sh`'s shipped graph | no |

**Direct dependencies of `ghost-client-dm`** (exact pins in `Cargo.toml` and `rust-crypto-pins.txt`): `vodozemac =0.11.0` (`default-features = false`); `libcrux-kem =0.0.9` (only `XWingKemDraft06`) with `libcrux-ml-kem =0.0.10` and `libcrux-sha3 =0.0.10` — **corrected by S0′ (V-6, V-9, 2026-09-19):** `libcrux-kem` is used **only through `libcrux_kem::XWing` and the `libcrux_traits::kem::slice::Kem` trait** (typed length errors; the enum API links every KEM of the crate: +415 KB arm64-v8a, +514 KB x86_64, and it panics on short inputs), with **`libcrux-traits =0.0.8`** added as a direct dependency, and the encapsulation-key check is an explicit `libcrux_ml_kem::mlkem768::validate_public_key` call on the first 1 184 bytes, because the trait performs no ML-KEM key check; `sha2`, `hkdf`, `hmac`, `chacha20poly1305` (already reached through vodozemac 0.11.0), `ed25519-dalek` (workspace 2.2.0), `zeroize`, `serde_json` (pickle serialisation, already a vodozemac dependency), `rand`/`getrandom` (vodozemac's). vodozemac 0.11.0 also always brings `hpke 0.14.1` (not optional; §22.8.10 point 13). Dev-only: `x-wing 0.1.0` (RustCrypto, differential oracle), `vodozemac` with `low-level-api` (key-schedule oracle), `proptest` if already allowed (else seeded loops).

**Transitive crates to review in S0′ (V-6; RX-10 in §22.3).** The libcrux graph is not only the four named crates: `libcrux-kem 0.0.9` also depends on `libcrux-ecdh`, `libcrux-p256` and `libcrux-traits`, and on `rand 0.10` (the major vodozemac 0.11.0 and `ghost-client-net` already use: no new `rand` major; `rand 0.8.8` is already reached through `num-bigint-dig`; §22.8.10 point 5); `libcrux-curve25519 0.0.8` depends on **`libcrux-hacl-rs`** and `libcrux-macros`; `libcrux-p256` on `libcrux-sha2`; further `libcrux-intrinsics`, `libcrux-platform`, `libcrux-secrets`, `hax-lib` and `hax-lib-macros`. `rust-crypto-pins.txt` records the version sets of `rand` and of the three dalek crates per shipped graph (a `graph` line with a version set, §22.8.10 point 5), and the allowlist (G-R1) names every crate; if the graph review rejects the extra crates, the Q55 alternative composes X-Wing from `libcrux-ml-kem`, `libcrux-curve25519` and `libcrux-sha3` directly.

**Portable libcrux (Q55).** `.cargo/config.toml` `[env]` sets `LIBCRUX_DISABLE_SIMD128 = "1"` and `LIBCRUX_DISABLE_SIMD256 = "1"` for every client build, so only the portable ML-KEM and SHA-3 code the proofs target is linked (NEON has no proofs; RUSTSEC-2025-0133 was an unverified aarch64 fallback). Gate G-R3 checks the build-script output. **Corrected by S0′ (V-6, 2026-09-19):** the variables alone are not enough. `libcrux-ml-kem`, `libcrux-sha3` and `libcrux-intrinsics` read them in `build.rs` **without `cargo:rerun-if-env-changed`**, so setting or clearing them in an existing target directory does not re-run the build scripts and the previous SIMD cfgs are reused (measured: FAIL → FAIL → portable after a clean → still portable after the reverse change). Every client build is therefore preceded by `cargo clean -p libcrux-ml-kem -p libcrux-sha3 -p libcrux-intrinsics [--target <triple>] --release`, which also covers CI target directories restored from caches, and G-R3 reads the cfgs of the build it guards (§16.1). The Q55 combiner is **not needed**: the graph review passed (V-6).

### 2.4 Why the seal and codec are in Rust (normative; withdraws round 9's §2.8 and X-9)

1. The ratchet and the KEM are native; a Kotlin seal would have to receive the X-Wing shared secret and `k_in` on the JVM heap, where they cannot be zeroised.
2. Binding v3 covers the Olm base key and the X-Wing ciphertext, both generated inside Rust in the same operation; signing in Rust keeps one JNI call per `SEND`.
3. ADR-19 ("nicio logică de protocol în Kotlin") and ADR-20 point 8 hold again; the CP-09 exception is the construction, no longer its placement.
4. JVM tests need the host build anyway (the ratchet is native).

**What stays in Kotlin:** hint **matching** with recognition keys (§6.2; HMAC-SHA256 on BouncyCastle, under the one-attempt spy), transaction control, database rows, `:sync` calls, the Keystore wrappers, the facade.

**Ed25519 in Rust (X-21).** `ghost-client-dm` signs and verifies cards, bundles, bindings and revocation certificates v2 with `ed25519-dalek` 2.2.0 (strict verification); the DM context receives the `ghost1` Ed25519 seed from `:identity` at unlock (§2.5). BouncyCastle stays for Phase 3 identity code (the Phase 3 revocation certificate v1, invites) and for the independent Kotlin/JDK test implementation of the vectors; each artifact type has exactly one production signer. **Corrected by S0′ (V-14, 2026-09-19).** Every artifact of X-21 (card v2, bundle v2, binding v3, revocation certificate v2) is signed **and verified only in Rust** (`verify_strict`); Kotlin never verifies them. BouncyCastle stays the only signer and verifier of the Phase 3 artifacts. Signatures are identical (Ed25519 is deterministic: 64 of 64 vectors byte-identical), verification is not: BouncyCastle 1.85.2 checks the **cofactored** equation `[8]SB = [8]R + [8]kA` and differs from dalek's cofactorless `verify_strict` on 33 of 53 edge vectors — it accepts all 21 mixed-order public keys with honest signatures (strict accepts 3), all 7 mixed-order nonces and all 8 small-order nonces. Both are EUF-CMA secure and agree on every honestly generated signature, but an adversary that controls a key can build one signature the JDK accepts and Rust refuses, so "strict verification in both" is not achievable with BouncyCastle's public API (R-37 materialised).

### 2.5 DM context and state wrapping (normative)

- `DmNative.ctxOpen(messagingBranch32, identitySeed32, sessionStateKey32) → handle` derives, in native memory, the Olm identity secret, the unused Olm account signing seed, `card_secret_0`, the schedule key and the read secrets; the Kotlin caller zeroises its arrays after the call (best effort). `ctxClose(handle)` zeroises (`zeroize` on drop). Lock and wipe call it; a closed or foreign handle returns `CRYPTO_UNAVAILABLE`.
- `session_state_key`: 32 random bytes created at ACTIVATE, stored wrapped in `dm_self.state_key_wrapped` by a non-exportable Keystore key with the database-key authentication policy (the content-key pattern of §13.8). A restore creates a new one (sessions are lost anyway).
- Every secret-bearing value that Kotlin stores or passes back is an envelope `nonce(12) ‖ ChaCha20-Poly1305(session_state_key, nonce, AD = "ghost/v1/wrap/" ‖ name ‖ binding, plaintext)`, with a binding that stays the same when the value moves between rows (session id, generation id, contact id and pair-key coordinates; the table of §22.8.3; an envelope that does not open under its binding is `STATE_CORRUPT`): Olm session pickles (`olm_session.state_env`), generation secrets (`olm_prekey_gen.secrets_env` = fallback secret ‖ X-Wing seed), `reply_seed` (`olm_session.prov_env`, `dm_held.prov_env`), pair week **opening** keys and roots (`dm_pair_key.open_env`, `dm_pair_offer.root_env`, `dm_pair_offer.offer_env`). Kotlin holds in clear only **recognition keys** (§6.2), card secrets of contacts (needed for credentials) and bearer credentials.
- Rationale: the JVM heap never holds ratchet, KEM or seal opening secrets (the vodozemac audit's Issue I, for the JVM side); SQLCipher rows are doubly encrypted. Declared residue: secrets are in native memory while unlocked (L7-D19).

### 2.6 JNI surface (closed list; gate G-R6) and result codes (normative)

**Forward note (S4b, 2026-09-20).** §22.9 items 3, 4, 5, 6 and 12 win over this section for the shape of the typed facade, the slice `ownCard` and `bundleCreate` arrive in, function 14's code set (every inner decryption failure of a session creation is `UNDECRYPTABLE`), function 7's outcomes and `L1.parse`.

**Forward note (S6, 2026-09-20).** §22.11 items 1 and 2 win over this section for two output sets: function 4 (`ownReadSecret`) returns `RX_{i,s}`, `D_{i,s}` **and** `RP_{i,s}` of the same slot, and function 2 (`ownCard`) also returns the weekly publication offset of §5.4.

All functions are `static native` on `org.ghost.crypto.bridge.DmNative`; inputs are byte arrays and ints; outputs are one byte array `code(1) ‖ TLV fields` decoded by `DmResult` in Kotlin. No function performs I/O, reads a clock, logs, or keeps state between calls other than the context handle. Rust panics are caught (`catch_unwind`) **under a silent panic hook installed once per process at library load and by `ctxOpen`** (the `ghost-client-net` `jni_bridge` pattern), so no panic text reaches stderr or logcat (**added by S0′, V-5, 2026-09-19:** the JVM probe survived a libcrux panic and returned `INTERNAL`, but the **default** hook printed the panic message and its source location to stderr, which the no-logging rule forbids for the DM graph, T3), and returned as `INTERNAL`, which Kotlin turns into `IllegalStateException` (rollback, crash, poison guard). `ErrorPolicyTest` (21 JNI categories of the network surface) is unchanged; DM codes are a separate enumeration with its own exhaustive test (`DmResultCodesTest`).

| # | Function | Main outputs | Codes besides `OK` |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `ctxOpen`, `ctxClose` | handle | `CRYPTO_UNAVAILABLE` |
| 2 | `ownCard(h, cardIndex)` | card v2, `X_i`, `P_i`, recognition keys of `KIO_i` and `KB_i`, own Olm identity | — |
| 3 | `cardParse(bytes)` / `cardDerive(h, card, slot)` | fields; `X_c`, `P_c`, `D_{c,s}`, `RP_{c,s}`, recognition keys, wrapped `KB_c`/`KIO_c` | `INVALID_CARD` |
| 4 | `ownReadSecret(h, cardIndex, slot)` | `RX_{i,s}` | — |
| 5 | `bundleCreate(h, cardIndex, week, rank, genId)` | blob(4096), fallback public key, X-Wing public key, `secrets_env` | — |
| 6 | `bundleOpen(h, kbEnv, blob, expectedCard)` | verified fields (the X-Wing key validated whole, §22.8.2) | `NOT_OPEN`, `BAD_SIGNATURE`, `NON_CANONICAL`, `INVALID_KEY` |
| 7 | `sessionOutbound(h, contactId, peerOlmIdentity, peerFallback)` | `state_env`, `session_id`, `reply_seed` envelope | `INVALID_KEY` (non-contributory) |
| 8 | `encrypt(h, contactId, sessionId, stateEnv, l0)` | `l1`, `is_prekey`, new `state_env`, `acknowledged` | `OVERSIZE`, `INVALID_SESSION`, `STATE_CORRUPT` (§22.8.2, §22.8.3) |
| 9 | `sealIntro(h, ns, bucket, kioEnv, peerBundleFields, ownCard, bindingMinute, provEnv, l1)` | blob | `NO_FIT` (bug), `INVALID_KEY` (any encapsulation error, §22.8.2) |
| 10 | `sealSym(h, mode ∈ {pair, provisional}, ns, bucket, keyEnv, keyBinding, identities, l1)` | blob | `NO_FIT`, `STATE_CORRUPT` |
| 11 | `openOuter(h, blob, ns, mode, keyEnv, keyBinding)` | mode body: intro → `gen_id` and inner bytes; pair/provisional → `kind`, `l1`, Olm header (ratchet key, chain index) | `NOT_OPEN`, `NON_CANONICAL` |
| 12 | `openIntro(h, ns, bucket, genId, genSecretsEnv, genPublic, inner)` | sender card, binding fields (signature verified, freshness not), `prov_env`, `l1`, PreKey header (identity, base key, fallback key, `session_id`, inner chain index) | `NOT_OPEN` (also every decapsulation error), `BAD_SIGNATURE`, `NON_CANONICAL`, `STATE_CORRUPT` |
| 13 | `decrypt(h, contactId, current(sessionId, env)?, previous(sessionId, env)?, l1)` | slot used, L0, new `state_env`, `acknowledged` | `DUPLICATE_OR_LOST`, `GAP_TOO_BIG`, `UNDECRYPTABLE` (**corrected by S0′, V-2, 2026-09-19:** also a non-contributory ratchet key of a **normal** message, where `DoubleRatchet::advance` fails with `DecryptionError::NonContributoryKey`), `STATE_CORRUPT` |
| 14 | `sessionInbound(h, contactId, genId, genSecretsEnv, expectedPeerIdentity, l1)` | `state_env`, `session_id`, L0 | `MISSING_KEY`, `IDENTITY_MISMATCH`, `INVALID_KEY` (also a non-contributory inner ratchet key, §22.8.2), `UNDECRYPTABLE` (**corrected by S0′, V-2, 2026-09-19:** also `SessionCreationError::MismatchedSessionConfig`, a PreKey message of another session version), `STATE_CORRUPT` |
| 15 | `pairKeys(h, contactId, direction, rootGen, rootEnv, baseWeek, fromWeek, toWeek)` | opening-key envelopes and recognition keys per week | `STATE_CORRUPT` |
| 16 | `pqRootSeal(h, contactId, rootGen, peerGen, peerPk, rootRandEnv)` / `pqRootOpen(h, contactId, rootGen, genId, genSecretsEnv, tlv)` | `seal_root_pq` TLV / root envelope (§9.4) | `INVALID_KEY` (seal), `NOT_OPEN` (open, also every decapsulation error), `STATE_CORRUPT` |
| 17 | `safetyNumber(ownIdPk, ownOlm, peerIdPk, peerOlm)` | 60 digits, scannable bytes | — |
| 18 | `l0Encode` / `l0Decode` | canonical L0 | `NON_CANONICAL` |
| **19** | **`chainPosition(h, contactId, sessionId, stateEnv, ratchetKey)`** (added by S0′, V-7, 2026-09-19) | `next_index` of the receiver chain the ratchet key names, or `NEW_CHAIN` when the chains are readable and none matches; called during **peek**, on the envelope of the session the recognition key names, and writes nothing (T28) | `STATE_CORRUPT` (an unreadable `/receiving_chains/inner`: a non-empty array in which no entry carries a readable 32-byte `ratchet_key`; never a silent 0, §2.1) |
| **20** | **`sessionPrune(h, contactId, sessionId, stateEnv, today)`** (added by S0′, V-15, 2026-09-19) | new `state_env` with the **reconciled first-seen sidecar**, and the pruned count; called by SWEEP on every live session (§7.7 point 4) | `STATE_CORRUPT` (an unreadable chain or skipped-key entry) |

**Corrected by S0′ (V-7, V-15, 2026-09-19).** Functions 19 and 20 are the recorded pickle-layout deviation of §2.1; **G-R6's closed list gains both**, and G-R7 pins their outputs per vodozemac pin (§16.1). Functions **8 (`encrypt`), 13 (`decrypt`) and 14 (`sessionInbound`) gain a `today` input** (a day index supplied by Kotlin, so no function reads a clock: the first-seen sidecar of §7.7 point 4 is rebuilt on every state-storing call). `chainPosition`'s `NEW_CHAIN` is a new result value, not an error.

**Typed facade (§21.6 point 6, extended).** `:crypto-bridge` exposes GHOST types only: `L1.parse(bytes) → PreKey(OI, baseKey, fallbackKey, sessionId) | Normal(ratchetKey, chainIndex) | Invalid`; `DmSessions.decrypt(tx, contact, l1) → Ok(plaintext, slot, created, renewed) | Duplicate | UnknownGeneration | Replay | Undecryptable | Untrusted | Oversize`; `DmSessions.encrypt(tx, contact, l0) → Ok(l1, isPreKey, acknowledged) | NoSession | Untrusted | Oversize | InvalidSession | StateCorrupt`; `DmSessions.outbound(tx, contact, bundle) → Ok | Untrusted | InvalidKey | Oversize`; `decrypt` also returns `StateCorrupt` (§22.8.2, §22.8.3). Every library error has a typed code; `INTERNAL` is only a caught panic. The three signals libsignal gave only implicitly are explicit: output kind (`isPreKey`), acknowledgement (`acknowledged`) and new-state creation (`created`, decided by GHOST under R-SS1).

### 2.7 Build, size, loading, reproducibility, JVM tests (normative)

- **Android build.** No change to `build-native.sh` beyond the libcrux environment; both stacks are pure Rust without network build scripts. T12's same-path identity and `native-libs.sha256` cover the grown `libghost_client_net.so`; no third-party-built native library enters the APK.
- **Size.** Probe: vodozemac +195 632 B stripped arm64; an X-Wing probe about +150 KB more (R-libs). **Measured by S0′ (V-9, 2026-09-19):** **+494 992 B (arm64-v8a) and +629 904 B (x86_64)** for vodozemac 0.11.0 and X-Wing through the slice trait, stripped, built exactly as `scripts/build-native.sh` builds the shipped library; 0.10.0 would save only 21–52 KB; the enum API instead of the slice trait would add +415 KB (arm64-v8a) and +514 KB (x86_64) and **break the budget on x86_64** (+1.09 MiB); `precomputed-tables` costs a further 14–29 KB. The budget stays ≤ +1 MiB per ABI and `apk-size:` reports the library per ABI, so the spec's 70 MiB universal APK is not at risk (Q24).
- **dalek and `rand` majors.** vodozemac 0.11.0 brings `curve25519-dalek 5.0.0`, `x25519-dalek 3.0.0`, `ed25519-dalek 3.0.0` next to the lock's 4.1.3 / 2.0.1 / 2.2.0; `rand 0.10.2` (vodozemac 0.11.0 and `libcrux-kem 0.0.9`) and `rand 0.8.8` are both already in the `ghost-client-net` graph, so the DM stack adds no `rand` major (§22.8.10 point 5). `rust-crypto-pins.txt` lists each version set per shipped graph (a `graph` line with a version set); `ghost-client-dm`'s own Ed25519 code uses the workspace 2.2.0. vodozemac 0.10.0 avoids the dalek duplicates (the Q45 alternative).
- **Loading.** The DM context opens on the DM actor thread at the first DM use, never in `Application.onCreate`. `libghost_client_net.so` already loads lazily (`NativeLibrary.kt` pattern); a missing or unloadable library, or a closed context, maps to the facade result `CRYPTO_UNAVAILABLE`, never to a crash loop.
- **JVM tests.** The CI job `android` builds `ghost-dm-host` (`cargo build -p ghost-dm-host --release --locked`, a minute-scale build: no Arti) and passes `-Dghost.dm.native=<path>`; `DmNative` loads that path in tests and `ghost_client_net` on Android. The owner's Windows machine builds the MSVC variant (V-5). The emulator job exercises the Android build of the same crate and compares its outputs with the host build on deterministic inputs (host/Android equivalence test, §15.9 item 7, RG-2).
- **R8.** `:crypto-bridge` `consumer-rules.pro` keeps the native methods of `org.ghost.crypto.bridge.DmNative`, with a comment naming the crate (the `app/proguard-rules.pro:1-3` comment rule). GHOST classes are not obfuscated (`-keepnames class org.ghost.** { *; }`; `-dontobfuscate` takes no class filter and would disable obfuscation for the whole APK; G-DM5 asserts that `mapping.txt` maps every `org.ghost.` class to itself; ADR-38, §22.8.10 point 11).

### 2.8 Bump policy (normative)

Exact pins; no automatic bumps. A vodozemac or libcrux bump is its own PR that runs: the golden pickles, `UpgradeTest` and the dehydrated-pickle encoder check (§15.2), golden sizes, the X-Wing and ML-KEM known-answer tests, the Olm key-schedule oracle, the vodozemac test suite at the new tag (`vendor-tests`), **the PQ bump assertion PQ5** (every intro envelope and every `seal_root_pq` carries a 1 120-byte X-Wing ciphertext, `k_in` depends on the KEM secret, and a gate-variant client that seals the intro inner layer without the KEM is refused; RG-5), **the vendored clock and logging scan G-R8** (RG-3), the full `messaging-exit-gate`, `rust-crypto-pins.sh`, `rust-license-headers.sh`, and a CHANGELOG review recorded in the PR (pickle format, message format, error semantics, limits). GHSA and RustSec advisories for both are watched (cargo-deny advisories already run).

### 2.9 Maturity evidence and the declared gap (normative evidence table; ADR-35)

| Component | Audits and advisories | Deployment | Formal analysis | Maintenance |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| vodozemac (Olm) | Least Authority final report 2022-03-30: A–H resolved; **I (keys in memory) and J (8-byte MAC) unresolved**; suggestion 8 (configurable skipped-key limits) declined. CVE-2024-34063 and CVE-2024-40640 (low, fixed). Soatok disclosure 2026-02-17 and Matrix.org analysis 2026-02-18; fixes in 0.10.0 (non-contributory DH refused, strict Ed25519 mandatory) | all Element and Element X clients through matrix-rust-sdk; matrix-rust-sdk `main` pins `vodozemac = "0.11.0"` with `insecure-pk-encryption`, `experimental-session-config` and the default features (`libolm-compat`, `precomputed-tables`), a feature set GHOST forbids, so the Olm V1 code path is shared at source level, not the build configuration (`Cargo.toml:126`, fetched 2026-09-16; §22.8.10 point 15) and calls `from_dehydrated_device` and `generate_fallback_key` (`crates/matrix-sdk-crypto/src/olm/account.rs:636,759`, fetched 2026-09-16) | Olm and Megolm in Matrix: Albrecht, Dowling, Jones (IEEE S&P 2024, computational); Ginesin, Nita-Rotaru (arXiv 2408.12743, signed prekeys needed; GHOST signs every fallback key); no implementation-level verification | Matrix.org Foundation; releases 0.10.0 (2026-04-13), 0.11.0 (2026-09-11) |
| `libcrux-ml-kem` 0.0.10 | RUSTSEC-2025-0133 (unverified aarch64 fallback in `libcrux-intrinsics` ≤ 0.0.3, fixed); pre-1.0 | libsignal (PQXDH, SPQR), OpenSSH, NSS/Firefox | hax → F*: panic freedom, correctness, secret independence for the portable and AVX2 code, partial (generic `ind_cpa`, `matrix` modules unproved; NEON unverified) | Cryspen |
| `libcrux-kem` 0.0.9 (X-Wing) | GHSA-435g-fcv3-8j26 (`libcrux-ecdh` ≤ 0.0.5, low, 2026-02-12, fixed) | no deployment of this crate verified | README "pre-verification"; the X-Wing construction is proven (Barbosa et al., IACR CiC 2024) | Cryspen |
| `libcrux-curve25519` 0.0.8 | — | libcrux users | compiled from verified HACL* C (README) | Cryspen |
| GHOST seal, binding v3, provisional mode, PQ-B/PQ-C, session selection, safety number v1, dehydrated-pickle encoder, JNI | none yet | none | ProVerif model M-1 … M-8 and exhaustive state models EM-1, EM-2 (§9.5, §15.2) | GHOST |

**The declared maturity gap (L7-D22).**

| Axis | libsignal (excluded) | This stack |
|---|---|---|
| Deployment scale of the exact code path | Signal and derivatives | Element and Element X (Olm through matrix-rust-sdk); GHOST's combination is new |
| Audits of the pinned revision | continuous in-house plus public research | one external audit of a 2022 revision; post-2022 changes unaudited |
| PQ in the library | PQXDH and SPQR | none; GHOST-built PQ layer |
| Formal verification of the implementation | SPQR (hax/F*), libcrux ML-KEM | libcrux ML-KEM (partial); none for Olm |
| Out-of-order window | 2 000 skipped keys per chain | 40 per chain |

**Scope of the external audit** (plan row 15, amended by ADR-35 and ADR-38): the pinned vodozemac revision and GHOST's use of it, `hpke 0.14.1` unless V-9 shows the linker drops it (§22.8.10 point 13), the `libcrux-kem`/`libcrux-ml-kem` integration and build flags, `ghost-client-dm` in full, the Kotlin DM actor and schema, relay-blind addressing and credentials. **Corrected by S0′ (V-9, 2026-09-19):** `hpke` 0.14.1 **is dropped by the linker** — an unstripped build carries no symbol of the `hpke` crate or of `vodozemac::hpke`, and no stripped variant contains `HPKE-v1`, `HPKEXXYYZZ`, `psk_id_hash` or `MATRIX_QR_CODE_LOGIN`, while `OLM_KEYS` and `OLM_RATCHET` are present (Megolm, SAS, ECIES and `pk_encryption` are dropped too). It therefore **leaves the external audit scope** and stays only in the licence and advisory checks of the source graph, while G-R7 repeats the V-9 symbol check at every vodozemac bump.

### 2.10 Licence and closed-source distribution (normative; ADR-37 … ADR-39)

Licence statements are engineering readings, not legal advice; counsel confirms before the first commercial distribution (ADR-37 point 10).

#### 2.10.1 Licence facts for this stack

- The shipped Rust graphs of `ghost-client-net`, the relay and the issuer contain only permissive licences plus two MPL-2.0 packages reached through Arti (`option-ext` 0.2.0; `priority-queue` 2.7.0, `LGPL-3.0-or-later OR MPL-2.0` with MPL elected); `cargo deny check licenses` passes. The APK side (AndroidX and Kotlin Apache-2.0, BouncyCastle MIT, SQLCipher-android BSD-3-Clause with LibTomCrypt and SQLite inside) links no GPL, LGPL or AGPL code (R-license §0).
- vodozemac 0.11.0's graph: MIT, Apache-2.0, BSD-3-Clause, `Unlicense OR MIT`, `(MIT OR Apache-2.0) AND Unicode-3.0`; **no MPL**. The libcrux crates are Apache-2.0. The DM stack adds no copyleft and no new source-offer duty.
- Obligations that exist anyway and are unmet today: notices for every linked Rust package and the AndroidX and Kotlin set, the Apache-2.0 text, extra notice files (`ring`, `atomic-waker`, `parking`, `cesu8`, `rand`), the SQLCipher attribution in the app or its materials, and the source offer for the two MPL packages (MPL-2.0 §3.2). vodozemac and libcrux add Apache-2.0 notices (no `NOTICE` files found in these crates; V-8 re-checks at the pinned versions).
- The Rust crates have declared `MIT OR Apache-2.0` (`ghost/Cargo.toml:22`) in a public repository; counsel must confirm what that means for everything pushed before the visibility switch (at least `b2df181`; the last public SHA and the artifact inventory are recorded at the switch, §22.8.9) (R-39).

#### 2.10.2 Gates and rules added in S0′ (before the DM crates enter the build)

| Gate | Rule |
|---|---|
| G-L1 `license-notices.sh` | regenerates `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES-*` for `ghost-client-net` (both Android targets), the relay, the issuer and the APK runtime classpath; licence texts from the package or, when it ships none, from a pinned SPDX license-list-data snapshot; Apache-2.0 `NOTICE` files from a committed snapshot captured at the published commit (§22.8.10 point 3); fails on a difference, a package with no SPDX id resolvable to a text, or a dropped `NOTICE` |
| G-L2 `gradle-license-allowlist.txt` | `group:artifact → SPDX` for every release runtime component; an unknown or copyleft licence fails |
| G-L3 `apk-notices.sh` | the APK carries the notices asset whose hash matches the regenerated file |
| G-L4 `mpl-sources.sh` | every MPL-2.0 package in a shipped graph has its `.crate` in the release source bundle; `[patch]` on an MPL crate fails without a published diff |
| G-L5 `no-public-binaries.sh` | no workflow uploads an APK, AAR, `.so` or image to a public destination while the repository is public; in a private repository only the internal native hand-off artifacts with `retention-days: 1` are allowed (a fixture for each case, §22.8.9) |
| G-R5 `rust-license-headers.sh` | over the packages of `cargo tree -e normal --target <shipped triples>`: SPDX expressions evaluated (an allowed alternative passes), GPL or AGPL flagged where a file declares it as the governing licence, "Signal Messenger" copyrights (the `wacore-libsignal` trap); a committed exception file (crate, version, file, reason) starting with `priority-queue` 2.7.0 (MPL elected) and `crabgrind` 0.2.6 (never compiled) (§22.8.10 point 2) |
| `deny.toml` | named bans by exact crate name: `libsignal-protocol`, `libsignal-core`, `libsignal-net`, `libsignal-service`, `libsignal-jni`, `libsignal-ffi`, `signal-crypto`, `zkgroup`, `spqr`, `spqr-syft`, `pq-ratchet`, `libsignal-dezire`, `wacore-libsignal`, `wa-rs-libsignal` (cargo-deny takes package specs, not globs; a self-test expects "bans FAILED", §22.8.10 point 1); the licence comment at `deny.toml:37-38` re-justified by ADR-37; `[licenses.private] ignore = true` for GHOST's own crates |
| Gradle | `org.signal` removed from `libs.versions.toml:18,39` and `dependency-allowlist.txt:12`, added to `dependency-denylist.txt` |

#### 2.10.3 Documents revised (ADR-37, ADR-38)

ADR-07 (open-source clause and the "repo F-Droid" main repository), FR-8.9 first half (plan:276), FR-8.10 (plan:277), plan:202, G12 (plan:125) and G16 (plan:129), Annex D (plan:460), THREAT_MODEL AD-9 and TB-6 plus a new adversary **AD-V** (the vendor, an insider or a coerced vendor ships a malicious or targeted build), LIMITE L-SRC (= L7-D23), INVARIANTS T12 (plus T12b, auditor reproduction; the updater inclusion-proof invariant arrives in Phase 14). CP-10's text stands; its evidence must now be **published** (reports, attestations, per-release gate results). Romanian text in Appendix B.1.

#### 2.10.4 Verifiability package applied to Phase 9 (ADR-38; Q61)

- **Publishable without source** (a separate public repository with a clean history and an explicit permissive licence, Q63): the DM protocol specification (§3–§9 of this design), the vectors (`dm_seal.txt`, `dm_content.txt`, `dm_bundle.txt`, `dm_safety_number.txt`, `register.txt`, `dm_relay_set.txt`), `allowed-observables.json` v2, the ProVerif model, the T32 report format, a black-box verification kit (onion-only traffic; bucket sizes as seen through the user's own exported card and a published relay client; manifest and native-library checks; it cannot check P-1 … P-8 or T1 at relays, §22.8.8), a standalone log-inclusion verifier for first installs, and per-release native symbol files hash-bound to the shipped library.
- **Auditor access:** source under a limited-use licence; the auditor rebuilds the tagged commit in the fixed-path builder (Phase 14) and signs the hashes (T12b).
- **Before the first distribution:** the §9.5 external review report, the Phase 15 audit, a witnessed binary transparency log enforced by the updater (Phase 14; a standalone verifier for first installs, logged relay and issuer releases, declared gaps for store channels, §22.8.8), source escrow, no obfuscation of `org.ghost.**`.

#### 2.10.5 Repository and CI (owner actions; ADR-39; Q62)

Everything pushed before the visibility switch is public forever (commit history and metadata, designs, runbooks, staging onion addresses, CI artifacts): at least `b2df181`, and the 299 artifacts listed on 2026-09-16, including native libraries uploaded for `b2df181` (§22.8.9). Owner steps, in order, **before any code slice (S1–S3 included)**: delete the public binary artifacts and disable every APK, AAR and `.so` upload now; switch the repository to private and record the last public SHA and artifact inventory; proprietary `LICENSE`, Cargo `license-file` and `[licenses.private] ignore = true`; no history rewrite; rotate staging onion keys if those relays are used beyond staging; budget CI for private 2-CPU runners, re-derived in S0′ (the earlier USD 200 per month came from public 4-CPU runners; exit gates, the privacy gate and the emulator run nightly and on dispatch; §22.8.9). While the repository is still public, the native libraries are built inside each consuming job instead of being handed over as artifacts.

#### 2.10.6 UI claims (CP-10, FR-8.8)

Forbidden in addition to the existing rules: "open source", "sursă deschisă", "verificabil de oricine", "auditat" without a named report and version, "Signal protocol", "PQ ratchet", "quantum-proof", "formally verified", "100% privat". Allowed only with published evidence, next to the declared residue "Nu puteți verifica singuri codul sursă; vă bazați pe auditorii numiți și pe jurnalul public."

## 3. Identity, key hierarchy and the contact card (I5, I9; ADR-30′, ADR-33′, ADR-35)

### 3.1 Keys (normative; rewritten by §22)

**Forward note (S4b, 2026-09-20).** §22.9 item 13 adds the canonical-encoding rule for the Curve25519 keys an L1 carries.

Let `m = RootEntropy.deriveBranch(DerivationLabels.MESSAGING)` (the existing MESSAGING branch, vector `messaging_seed` in `KeyDerivationTest.kt:37`). Every DM secret derived from the seed is `HKDF-SHA256(ikm = m, salt = ∅, info = label ‖ params, L = 32)` with a label of `DmLabels` (prefix `ghost/v1/dm/`). Every card-level value is `HKDF-SHA256(ikm = card_secret_i, salt = ∅, info = label ‖ params, L = 32)` with a label of `CardLabels` (prefix `ghost/v1/card/`). Both label sets live in `ghost-client-dm` (Rust); nothing has shipped, so labels keep their prefixes and the vectors are regenerated.

| Key | Derivation / generation | Stored | Leaves the device |
|---|---|---|---|
| Ed25519 identity (`ghost1…`) | `HKDF(root, "ghost/v1/identity")` (Phase 3) | Keystore-wrapped seed; re-derived; handed to the DM context at unlock (§2.5) | the public key, in cards and bundles (to card holders only) |
| **Olm identity** `(oi, OI)`, X25519 | `oi = HKDF(m, "ghost/v1/dm/olm-identity")`, fed to vodozemac's `Account` through the dehydrated pickle (§2.2) | re-derived at unlock, native memory only; never persisted | `OI` inside bundles and PreKey messages, both sealed |
| Olm account signing seed | `HKDF(m, "ghost/v1/dm/olm-account-signing")`; required by the `Account` layout; never used to sign; never published | native memory | never |
| `card_secret_i` | `HKDF(m, "ghost/v1/dm/card" ‖ u16_be(i))`, card index i (0 in Phase 9) | re-derived | inside the card |
| inbox read secret `RX_{i,s}` | `HKDF(m, "ghost/v1/dm/inbox-read" ‖ u16_be(i) ‖ u8(s))`, s = the relay's ES slot (§21.2) | re-derived | presented to the relay of slot s in `X_i`'s set (bearer) |
| schedule key `S_i` | `HKDF(m, "ghost/v1/dm/schedule" ‖ u16_be(i))` | re-derived | never |
| card-level values | §3.2 | derived on demand | only deposit and bundle read secrets are presented to relays |
| bundle generation g: Olm fallback key `(f_g, F_g)`, X-Wing key (32-byte seed `q_g`, public `Q_g`), `gen_id_g` (u32, random, distinct from retained ones) | CSPRNG at OWNER-WEEK and ACTIVATE; `Q_g` = X-Wing key generation from the seed | `olm_prekey_gen.secrets_env` (wrapped, §2.5) | `F_g`, `Q_g`, `gen_id_g` in the sealed bundle |
| `reply_seed` per outbound session | CSPRNG 32 B at `sessionOutbound` | `olm_session.prov_env` (wrapped) until erased (§6.7) | inside every intro envelope of that session, under the X-Wing-keyed inner layer |
| pair roots and week keys | CSPRNG root (32 B) generated by the **recipient** per contact (PQ-C form `HKDF(root_rand ‖ ss)`, §9.4); week keys by ratchet (§6.3) | `dm_pair_offer.root_env` and `dm_pair_key.open_env` (wrapped; window only); recognition keys in clear | the root, once, inside E2E content to that contact |
| `session_state_key` | CSPRNG 32 B at ACTIVATE or restore | `dm_self.state_key_wrapped` (Keystore) | never |
| `seen_key` | CSPRNG 32 B at identity creation or restore | `dm_self` | never |
| content key | CSPRNG 32 B, wrapped by a Keystore key (§13.8) | `dm_self` (wrapped) | never |

Withdrawn with libsignal (§22): the libsignal identity key pair, the seed-derived X25519 **intro key** `(y_i, Y_i)` (card v2 has none, §3.3), the signed EC and last-resort Kyber prekeys, and the per-install **registration id** (which every card holder could read in bundles and which changed at restore, an undeclared restore signal of round 9).

- The derivation from `m` keeps the MESSAGING branch the source of all messaging material (FR-1.2, spec Figure 2, TM:81) and leaves room for Phase 10 MLS labels under another prefix. `DerivationLabels.MESSAGING`'s comment ("seeds the libsignal identity key pair") becomes "seeds the DM keys (Olm identity, cards, read secrets; ADR-35)" (Appendix B.1); `DERIVATION_VERSION` stays 1 because no label of `DerivationLabels` changes.
- `DmLabels` and `CardLabels` each have a prefix test (no label is a prefix of another, the `DerivationLabelsTest` rule) and vectors in `protocol/test-vectors/dm_derive.txt`, replayed by Rust and by the independent Kotlin test implementation. **Added by S0′ (V-1, 2026-09-19):** `dm_derive.txt` carries the pinned vector `olm_identity_pub` — for root entropy `000102…1f` (which reproduces the pinned `messaging_seed` `82453de0…50cf` of `:identity`, so the HKDF convention is fixed), `OI = X25519(HKDF(m, "ghost/v1/dm/olm-identity")) = d80580eca7efdcd429060d7c25bb83d731943aef3cfecd947b048b72d8b00603`, reproduced on both pins, by the JVM host run of V-5 and by a restore from the same seed (same `OI`, same safety number).
- **No derivation takes a public key, a `ghost1` string or a safety number as its key input** (T26; gate G-DM3 over the Kotlin call sites and a Rust unit test that enumerates every `derive_*` function's key-input type).
- **Random, never derived:** generation secrets, `reply_seed`, pair roots, `seen_key`, content key, `session_state_key`. They must match nothing on another device, and none can be recovered from the seed (P-7).

### 3.2 Card-level labels (`CardLabels`)

| Output | Info | Presented to relays? |
|---|---|---|
| inbox namespace `X_i` (32) | `ghost/v1/card/inbox-ns` | as the namespace id |
| bundle namespace `P_i` (32) | `ghost/v1/card/bundle-ns` | as the namespace id |
| deposit secret `D_{i,s}` (32) | `ghost/v1/card/deposit` ‖ u8(s) | yes (bearer, the relay of slot s; §21.2) |
| bundle fetch secret `RP_{i,s}` (32) | `ghost/v1/card/bundle-read` ‖ u8(s) (label unchanged) | yes (bearer, the relay of slot s; §21.2); registered and presented as kind FETCH (§22.8.7) |
| bundle seal key `KB_i` (32) | `ghost/v1/card/bundle-seal` | **never** |
| intro outer key `KIO_i` (32) | `ghost/v1/card/intro-outer` | **never** |

s is the ES slot number of the relay the secret is presented to (the slot Phase 8 binds tokens to); credentials carry no week (§21.2). Relays see `D` and `RP` but never `card_secret`, so they cannot derive `KB` or `KIO` (HKDF outputs under distinct labels are independent PRF outputs). A card holder can derive everything in this table; the inbox read secrets and the Olm identity are **not** in it. `KB_i` and `KIO_i` are held wrapped and used only inside `ghost-client-dm`; Kotlin stores and matches with their **recognition keys** `R_sel = HKDF(K_sel, "ghost/v1/dm-recognition")` only (§6.2, rule R-S6).

### 3.3 Contact card v2 (normative; replaces card v1, §22)

```
card = version(1) = 0x02 || card_index(2, BE) || identity_pk(32, Ed25519) || card_secret(32)
       || signature(64, Ed25519 over "ghost/v1/contact-card" || all previous bytes)
     = 131 bytes
link = "ghost://contact/" + z-base-32(131 bytes) = 16 + 210 = 226 characters   (QR version 9-L, byte mode, capacity 230)
```

- **Parse order (fail closed; each step has a negative test):** scheme exactly `ghost://contact/` with no other URL structure; exact-length z-base-32 decode; `version = 2` (version 1 cards were never issued and are refused as "card version unsupported", FR-7.7); `card_index = 0` in Phase 9 (another index is "card version unsupported"); signature under `identity_pk` (strict verification, §2.4); `identity_pk` ≠ own. `ghost1 = z32(SHA-256(identity_pk))` is derived, never carried.
- **One card per (identity, index), identical for all contacts.** A per-contact card would let relays re-partition senders by namespace and would be a per-contact identifier if a card leaked.
- The card carries **no relay addresses** (the relay set is a public function, §5.2), **no expiry**, **no nonce** (a replayed card re-adds the same contact), **no one-time key** (a card shown twice or photographed would make a second use fail) and, since card v2, **no intro key**: the first-contact key lives in the weekly bundle (§5.3), so nothing seed-derived opens an intro inner layer (P-7).
- It is a bearer secret for "may message me": whoever holds it can fetch the bundle, deposit (spam, quota: L7-D8), recognise intro envelopes to the owner (L7-D3) and, with its own token, list and get the inbox: its volume, the minute of every intro-envelope arrival and which conversations are unanswered (SC-2, L7-D16, §22.8.7). The owner's own card also travels inside every first message the owner sends, so every recipient of such a message holds it for good (L7-D25). A card link sent over a classical channel can be recorded for a future quantum adversary (PQ-R8). The Phase 13 UI states this when a link is shared through another app (the T16 warning pattern).

### 3.4 Binding v3 and the bundle signature (normative; replaces binding v2, §22)

- **In every bundle:** an Ed25519 signature by `identity_pk` over the whole bundle, which contains the Olm identity `OI`, the generation's fallback key `F_g` and X-Wing key `Q_g` (§5.3). The bundle signature is the identity binding for fetchers and the "signed prekey" that Olm's PCS needs (Ginesin and Nita-Rotaru).
- **In every intro envelope:** the sender's card v2, `binding_minute` (`floor_minute(now)` at sealing) and

```
binding_sig = Ed25519(identity_sk_A,
    "ghost/v1/dm-binding/v3" || OI_A(32) || u16_be(card_index_A) || recipient_namespace(32)
    || u32_be(binding_minute) || u32_be(gen_id_B) || SHA-256(xwing_ct)(32) || base_key_A(32) || OI_B(32))
```

  where `base_key_A` is the base key of the Olm PreKey message inside the same envelope, `xwing_ct` that envelope's KEM ciphertext and `OI_B` the recipient's Olm identity from its bundle. The signature and the envelope structure are verified in Rust at `openIntro`; freshness (`now − FRESH ≤ binding_minute ≤ now + 2σ`, FRESH = 45 d), the card and the identity pre-check run in Kotlin at `RECV` R5–R6, before any state change (§21.4). The recipient requires `binding.OI_A = PreKey.identity_key`, `binding.base_key = PreKey.base_key`, `binding.gen_id = envelope gen_id` and `PreKey.one_time_key = F_{gen_id}`; any mismatch is `drop(non_canonical)` with no state change.
- **What it proves (security).** A binding cannot be moved to another envelope (the ciphertext is unique, and besides the recipient only its sealer knows the KEM secret), to another Olm session (base key), recipient (namespace, `OI_B`) or time (minute). **Key-compromise impersonation, bounded.** Plain Olm 3DH on a fallback key lets anyone holding the recipient's identity and fallback secrets start a session in any sender's name with a fresh base key (all three DH values are computable from `oi_B`, `f_g` and public keys); PQXDH has the same limit for a compromised signed prekey (Bhargavan et al., USENIX Security 2024). Binding v3 blocks that: a new session in A's name needs a base key A signed, so an attacker with the recipient's complete state can act only inside a session A really started within the freshness window (for example by withholding A's genuine envelope and re-sealing other content under the same ciphertext and base key), which holding that session's state already allows. Test KC13, mutant PM-34.
- **What it does not sign (privacy).** No message content and no L1 hash (round 9's binding v2 signed `SHA-256(L1)`, a transferable signature over the first message). The recipient can prove to a third party that A's identity started a session toward the recipient's namespace at that minute; it cannot prove what A wrote, since the recipient knows the KEM secret and could re-seal any content under the same binding. Declared (L7-D20); no UI claim of deniability (CP-10). The alternatives (an HMAC binding under a static X25519-derived key, or a static Ed25519 binding without the base key) are recorded in Q51.
- Because both identities derive from one seed, an Olm identity `OI′ ≠ OI` under a valid signature by the pinned Ed25519 key happens only when the holder of that Ed25519 secret signed another Olm identity: the contact itself (including a modified client), a derivation-version change, or a seed thief. All deserve a stop (§8.3, §21.4).

---

## 4. Relay authorization: `RegisterKey`, bearer credential v3, deposit and read keys (I3, I4; D11; ADR-30)

### 4.1 Why (the security argument)

With per-sender write capabilities (Annex B literally, and the Phase 8 interim), every store into a recipient's inbox carries `capability_scope = SHA-256(capability)`, and the v2 serial makes that value distinct per sender token (`relay/crates/capability/src/lib.rs:190-195`; ADR-25 point 6). The relay then partitions the inbox by sender, counts correspondents per week (and at rest through E13 binding tags), sees each new correspondent as redeem-then-store (WRITE MISSING redeems at once, F8 §12.4), and token spend grows with DM breadth (E16). That is a sender field in substance, which Annex C T1 forbids (RP F2, RC F4, F5). The fix adapts Signal's delivery-token idea to blind relays: **the recipient authorizes deposits with one weekly secret shared by all its card holders and pays for them with its own capability.** The same RPC implements D11's shared read keys (F8 §10.7).

### 4.2 `relay.proto` additions (normative)

```proto
enum KeyKind {
  KEY_KIND_UNSPECIFIED = 0;
  KEY_KIND_READ = 1;                // list, get, check
  KEY_KIND_DEPOSIT = 3;             // store, check (2 is Write in capability headers; never used here)
  KEY_KIND_FETCH = 4;               // list, get; get returns week-coarse uploaded_at and expiry (§22.8.7)
}

enum RegisterResult {
  REGISTER_RESULT_UNSPECIFIED = 0;
  REGISTER_RESULT_OK = 1;           // recorded, or an identical record already existed
  REGISTER_RESULT_WRONG_PERIOD = 2; // period not in {cap_week, cap_week + 1}; nothing recorded
}

message RegisterKeyRequest {
  uint32 version = 1;               // MUST be 1 (proto-check.sh)
  bytes namespace_id = 2;           // 32 bytes
  uint64 period_id = 3;             // ISO week of the key (Phase 8 period)
  KeyKind kind = 4;
  bytes verifier = 5;               // 32 bytes (§4.3); the secret never reaches the relay at registration
  Capability capability = 6;        // a usable WRITE capability (v1 or v2) for namespace_id
  bytes request_id = 7;             // 16 random bytes, identical on an identical retry
}

message RegisterKeyResponse {
  RegisterResult result = 1;
  uint64 relay_period_id = 2;       // always (R9 relay-facing clock source, as RedeemToken)
  uint64 relay_minute = 3;          // always
  uint64 valid_until_unix = 4;      // OK only: when the relay forgets the record
}

service RelayService {
  // ... the existing six RPCs ...
  rpc RegisterKey(RegisterKeyRequest) returns (RegisterKeyResponse);
}
```

`StoreBlob`, `GetBlob`, `CheckBlobs` and `ListNamespace` keep their messages; their `Capability.token` may now also carry a bearer credential v3 (§4.3).

### 4.3 Bearer credential v3 and verifiers (normative; `ghost_relay_api::capability_header` gains a third layout)

```
credential v3 (67 bytes) = version(1) = 3 || kind(1) ∈ {0x01 read, 0x03 deposit, 0x04 fetch} || slot(1) || namespace(32) || secret(32)      (§21.2, §22.8.7)
verifier (32 bytes)      = SHA-256(label(kind) || u8(slot) || namespace(32) || secret(32))
label(read)              = "ghost/v1/read-verifier"        (the D11 label, F8 §10.7 point 3)
label(deposit)           = "ghost/v1/deposit-verifier"
label(fetch)             = "ghost/v1/fetch-verifier"
```

- Anyone holding the secret can build the credential; it carries no MAC because its authority is the registered verifier. It is deterministic: every presenter of the same secret at the same relay sends **identical bytes**, so `capability_scope = SHA-256(credential)` is one value per (relay, namespace, kind) for all depositors (inbox) or all fetchers (bundle), in every week (§21.2).
- The verifier binds the relay slot and the namespace (D11's `SHA-256(label ‖ secret)` refined, X-5): a verifier registered for one namespace cannot authorize another, and a relay refuses a credential naming another slot, so a relay cannot replay a secret it saw at the relays of **other slots** (§21.2). The week lives only in the registration period. **Corrected (review of S1, 2026-09-19):** the verifier names the slot number, not the serving relay, so the replay bound holds across slots, not across a slot handover: an operator that held slot s keeps deposit, read and fetch rights at the slot's successor relay for as long as the owner registers the same static secrets there (L7-D26). Binding the verifier to the relay's onion identity instead of `u8(slot)` is the alternative; it is not taken in Phase 9 because §21.2 point 2 fixes the 67-byte layout and its verifier, and a handover is a schedule change the owner controls.
- **Rights.** read → `list`, `get`, `check`; deposit → `store`, `check` only; fetch → `list`, `get` only, and a `get` with a fetch credential returns `uploaded_at` floored to its ISO week start and `expiry_unix_seconds` rounded up to the next ISO week boundary (the owner's READ keeps the exact hour expiry that C-11 needs; §22.8.7). A deposit credential presented to `list` or `get` is `rejected_capability` (contacts must not read the inbox with the card: F8 §10.7 rejected alternative "free reads").
- **`client-core` (T21).** `NamespaceClient::authorize(credential, access)` parses the v3 header before any I/O, checks the kind/operation matrix and that the header's namespace is the client's, and refuses otherwise with `invalid_argument` (0 requests, 0 connections). The refused-token matrix of `namespace_client.rs:444-694` gains the v3 rows: read at store; deposit at list and get; fetch at store and check (§22.8.7); wrong namespace; lengths 66 and 68; unknown kind 0x02 (§21.2). The circuit is the namespace's (`IsolationScope::Namespace`).

### 4.4 `RegisterKey` semantics (normative; `Relay::register_key_at(req, now)`, exactly one capture event; SC-1)

1. `version = 1`; lengths `namespace_id` 32, `verifier` 32, `request_id` 16; `kind ∈ {READ, DEPOSIT, FETCH}` → else `INVALID_ARGUMENT`, `rejected_size`.
2. The capability parses as v1 or v2, its MAC verifies (`RelayKey::verify`), its kind is Write, its namespace is `namespace_id`, and it has not expired at `now` → else `PERMISSION_DENIED`, `rejected_capability`.
3. `cap_week` = the ISO week the capability belongs to: for v2, `week(expiry − 3600 − 1)` (expiry is `start(cap_week + 1) + 3600`, F8 §10.3); for a CLI-minted v1, `week(now)`. Require `period_id ∈ {cap_week, cap_week + 1}` → else `OK` with `WRONG_PERIOD`, `rejected_period`, nothing recorded. **The relay's clock never selects the period** (G-3): a registration made late in a week with that week's capability, or early in a week with the previous week's still-valid capability, is judged by the capability.
4. **Accumulate, never replace.** In one redb write transaction, committed before the response:
   - key `namespace ‖ period ‖ kind ‖ verifier` in `bearer_keys`: absent → insert `valid_until = start(period + 2) + 3600`, `quota_bytes = capability.quota_bytes`, `serials = [serial]`; present → if `serial` is not in `serials` and `|serials| < 4`, append it and add `capability.quota_bytes` (the allowance of §4.5); otherwise unchanged. `serial` is the v2 capability's 16-byte serial, or the first 16 bytes of the v1 capability's scope hash.
   - key `namespace ‖ period ‖ kind ‖ serial` in `serial_use` → `verifier`: absent → insert with the same `valid_until`; present with the same verifier → unchanged; **present with another verifier → `PERMISSION_DENIED`, `rejected_capability`, nothing recorded** (one capability registers at most one verifier per (namespace, week, kind)).
   - Response `OK` with `valid_until_unix`.
5. **Why accumulate (SC-1).** A card holder can redeem its own token for a contact's inbox (the namespace id is in the card's derivations) and would, under a "last registration wins" rule, replace the owner's deposit verifier and lock every other sender out. Under accumulation it can only add its own verifier, whose scope is its own (it gains nothing it did not already have) and whose quota it paid for. Replacement is not needed: a cut-off moves to a new card index (Phase 13, §12.5), and the old index's registrations lapse on their own within two weeks.
6. Error messages are constants; forged, unknown, expired and conflicting registrations share one result (`rejected_capability`): no oracle.

### 4.5 Deposits, reads and the `DepositLedger` (normative; E-11)

- **`store` with a deposit credential.** Parse v3; the header's slot is the relay's own and its namespace equals the request's; compute the verifier; a `bearer_keys` record `(namespace, p, DEPOSIT, verifier)` with `now < valid_until` exists for some period p (§21.2) → else `rejected_capability`. All other store rules are unchanged (bucket size, exact TTL bucket §10.2, hash recomputation).
- **Quota.** Deposits are charged to a **`DepositLedger` entry of the matching record `(namespace, period, verifier)`**, the live one with the earliest `valid_until` that has allowance left (§21.2), not to the registering capability's `QuotaLedger` entry: that entry expires at `start(cap_week + 1) + 1 h`, and `QuotaLedger::charge` returns `Expired` once `cap.expiry_unix ≤ pruned_through` (`relay/crates/capability/src/lib.rs:205-217`), while a registration of week `cap_week + 1` lives until `start(cap_week + 3) + 1 h`. The entry's allowance is the record's `quota_bytes` (the sum credited by its registering capabilities, §4.4 point 4); its expiry is the record's `valid_until`. Over the allowance → `RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED`, capture `rejected_capability`, as for a write capability over quota. Like `QuotaLedger`, the used bytes live in memory and restart empty: a registration may be exceeded once per relay restart, bounded by its validity (E29 of ADR-25 extended, declared).
- **`list`, `get`, `check` with a read credential.** The same verification with `kind = READ`; `list` and `get` with a fetch credential verify `kind = FETCH` (§22.8.7). `check` also accepts a deposit credential (senders verify their own copies, F7:626).
- **Write capabilities keep working** for every operation as today (write grants read). A card holder that redeems its own token for a contact's inbox can list it (SC-2, L7-D16); closing that needs an owner-bound read rule, a Phase 12 item (§20 F-5, Q36).
- **List cursors** (review of S1; §21.9 point 4). `ListNamespace` answers with the blob store's per-namespace sequence sealed under the relay key and opens an incoming cursor the same way, for every caller: the sequence carries the hour of the namespace's first store and the number of stores since, which a fetch credential must not reveal. Eight opaque bytes on the wire, paging unchanged, no client change.
- **Sweep.** The existing 60-second loop deletes `bearer_keys` and `serial_use` rows with `valid_until ≤ now` in one write transaction and never runs below a persisted high-water minute (the ADR-25 point 3 clock rule).

### 4.6 `keys.redb` (normative)

- A separate `<data-dir>/keys.redb`, `SCHEMA_VERSION = 1`, `Durability::Immediate`; tables `bearer_keys` (`namespace(32) ‖ period(8) ‖ kind(1) ‖ verifier(32)` → `valid_until(8) ‖ quota_bytes(8) ‖ n(1) ‖ serial(16) × n`), `serial_use` (`namespace ‖ period ‖ kind ‖ serial(16)` → `verifier(32) ‖ valid_until(8)`), `meta` (`schema_version`, `sweep_high_water_minute`).
- **Why a separate file and not a table in `nullifiers.redb`.** Registrations are soft state that owners re-create (§4.8 healing rule), while nullifiers are money state with strict start-up rules (a missing `nullifiers.redb` next to `relay.key` refuses start, F8 §10.5). A missing `keys.redb` is created empty at start and never refuses it; `--nullifiers-reset` does not touch it; losing it is healed by owners within a session and costs senders at most the refusals until then. Registrations never enter the reconciliation of runbook R2.
- **Privacy at rest (AD-12).** Namespaces, periods, verifiers (hashes of secrets the seizer does not hold), validity instants aligned to weeks, allowances and up to four capability serials per record: no request history, no per-sender data. With the relay key also seized, a serial lets its holder test candidate nullifiers, linking a nullifier to a namespace for at most two weeks: the E13 residue, unchanged in kind. A seizer learns which namespaces had registrations in the last ≤ 2 weeks, which the blob store already implies.

### 4.7 Observables (T1; `allowed-observables.json` version 2, the "decizie de confidențialitate" rule of INV:50) and vectors

- `op` gains `"register"`. The register event carries `op`, `protocol_version`, `namespace_id`, `period_id`, `key_kind` ∈ {`read`, `deposit`, `fetch`} (a new field, on `register` only; `fetch` per §22.8.7), `capability_scope` (the hash of the registering write capability), `request_id`, `time_bucket`, `result`. The verifier never appears.
- `capability_scope`'s note becomes "SHA-256 of the presented credential: a write capability, or a bearer read or deposit key v3 (ADR-30); never the credential itself".
- `ttl_bucket_days` is replaced by `ttl_bucket_seconds` ∈ {3600, 86400, 604800, 2592000, 7776000} (§10.2). `result` values are unchanged (`rejected_ttl`, `rejected_period` and `rejected_capability` already exist).
- No other field is added in Phase 9. `circuit_id` stays allowed and unemitted (a Phase 12 item, §20 F-4); the DM world exports circuits in its effective view instead (§15.6).
- Fixtures: positive lines for `register` with each `key_kind`, for stores with `ttl_bucket_seconds = 3600`, for a store presenting a deposit scope; negative lines for `ttl_bucket_seconds = 7200`, the old `ttl_bucket_days` field, `key_kind` on a store, and a 64-hex verifier-shaped value in a field not declared as a hash.
- **Vectors `protocol/test-vectors/register.txt`** (new; `relay_semantics.txt` gets only the TTL section, §10.2), about 45 cases: register read and deposit ok; identical retry; a second capability crediting the same record (allowance doubled, no fifth credit); `cap_week + 1` accepted and `cap_week + 2`, `cap_week − 1` refused as `WRONG_PERIOD`, from a capability redeemed late in its week; no, expired, foreign-namespace and read-kind capabilities; a second verifier from the same capability refused; a second verifier from another capability accumulated; deposit store ok and charged to the record's allowance (exhaustion → `RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED`); deposit at list and get refused; read at store refused; wrong secret; credential for another week or namespace; after `valid_until`; sweep boundary; restart keeps records and empties the ledger; **added by the review of S2:** a live record at or below the deposit ledger's prune high-water is refused as `unauthorized` (`CapError::Expired`), never as `quota`. Replayed by the Rust relay (`tests/register_vectors.rs`) and by the Kotlin `ModelRelay` (`ModelRelayConformanceTest`), which keeps the model honest.

### 4.8 Owner flow, token accounting and reachability (normative; G-3, E-10, G-17)

**Registrations the owner keeps (the `REGROWS` transaction, §7.2).** For each own namespace `ns ∈ {X_0, P_0}`, each relay r of its current relay set (§5.2) and each kind it needs (`X`: DEPOSIT and READ; `P`: FETCH, §22.8.7), the owner keeps rows `(r, ns, week, kind)` in `relay_key_registration` (§13.6) for:
- `week(now)`, whether or not a capability exists (so a pending row exists to be served);
- `c` and `c + 1` for the week c of every usable write capability of `(r, ns)`.

The verifier of a row is fixed at insert (§4.3). The `:sync` work lane runs a pending row with a usable write capability of week c such that `row.week ∈ {c, c + 1}` (§13.3). Results: `OK` → `done` with `valid_until_hour`; `WRONG_PERIOD` → stays pending until the capability generation changes; transient categories → the Phase 7 backoff. Pending rows with `week < week(now) − 1` are deleted unexecuted; `done` rows are deleted after `valid_until_hour`. **Healing rule:** when the owner's READ credential for `(r, X, w)` is marked `rejected` (for example after `keys.redb` was lost), every `done` row of `(r, X, week ≥ w)` returns to pending in the same transaction and the READ credential is replaced by the write capability for reads until the rows are done again.

**Credentials the owner installs (the `KEEPER` transaction).** Once per relay r of `X_0`'s set: `Capabilities.put(r, X_0, READ, credential_v3(read, slot(r), X_0, RX_{0,slot(r)}), expires_hour = ceil_hour(now) + 8 d)`, refreshed when fewer than 7 days remain (§21.2). Reads prefer a usable READ credential and fall back to a write capability (§13.3 item 7), so the relay sees the owner's read scope for the week.

**When the relay sees it.** The owner's redemptions for `X_0` and `P_0` follow the Phase 8 planner (F8 §12.4): the EXPIRING renewal at a PRF time in the last 23 h of each week with the next week's token. The rows of the new capability's week + 1 then run in the same session on the work lane. So the weekly pattern at a relay is: one redeem per own namespace, then one `register` per missing (week, kind), at the renewal time the redeem lane already chose. Nothing about contacts, messages or fetches enters these times (T27, T31).

**Need reason of a registration-only namespace (Q31, default).** `Capabilities.needed()` treats a pending registration row like outbox work: `WRITE MISSING` where a pending row exists and no write capability does (§13.3 item 4). The planner then redeems as soon as an eligible token exists (F8 §12.4), instead of READ MISSING's PRF time within 6 h. The Phase 8 redeem hold (Q29) arms for it, which closes the Phase 8 Q34 gap for DM owners whose capabilities lapsed (§13.4). The alternative (READ MISSING, ≤ 6 h) is Q31's.

**Token accounting (T27; E-10).** Exactly **one ACCESS token per (relay, own namespace, week)** on the EXPIRING path, whatever the number of contacts or messages; at most **2 per ES slot per week** (when a relay belongs to both sets). A trial (8 per slot per week) and a pack (16) both cover it. Senders spend **0** on others' namespaces: `CapabilitySource.LOCAL` needs are never redeemed (§13.3 item 3, mutant PM-1b). There is no weekly namespace roll, so no week-p token redeems a namespace that a week-(p + 1) token renews a day later (the base's double cost). After a slot-table change, one extra token per extra union relay per week, for at most 6 weeks (§21.1).

**Reachability (G-17).**
- **After activation:** under Q31's default, a new identity's inbox is redeemed and registered in its first relay session once a token is eligible: at once for a trial in STANDARD mode; at the activation slot in HIGH mode (F8 §12.3: the next UTC day boundary after 4 h, plus U[0, 6 h], plus geometric extra days); at the activation slot for genesis. Under the alternative, up to 6 h later.
- **After a restore:** tokens are not recoverable from the seed (Phase 8 E11), but registrations are relay state. For up to two weeks after the old device's last renewal, contacts' deposits still succeed and the restored device reads its inbox with the seed-derived read key without any capability. After that it needs tokens (a trial from a new invite, or a pack).

**Offline tolerance (G-3, E-8; §21.2).** An owner online in week w registers `{w, w + 1}` and, after its renewal at the end of w, `{w + 1, w + 2}`. A sender presents its one credential for the relay; there is no week choice and no fallback. Therefore:
- a recipient whose newest write capability is of week c receives deposits until `start(c + 3) + 1 h`; about **two to three weeks** of absence cost no deposit;
- a message deposited in that time lives on the relays for its TTL (up to 30 days) and is fetched when the owner returns within it;
- beyond that, deposits wait in `wait_capability` and every DM op ends at its deadline (§10.1): at most 7 days after compose, then FAILED (no copy existed; the user may retry).

### 4.9 T2 world and gate (normative; G-16, E-6, E-7, X-14)
