The Phase 8 T2 world exports every table of the relay databases and fails on a table it does not know (F8 §19.24 point 6). Phase 9 therefore changes `t2-exit-gate`:
1. **Export and completeness.** `keys.redb`'s tables (`bearer_keys`, `serial_use`, `meta`) and the in-memory `DepositLedger` (a read-only `Relay::deposit_ledger_snapshot`, the `quota_snapshot` precedent) join the export and the completeness check; `register` capture events are counted in the relays' capture files.
2. **Client model.** Every activated world client registers its DM owner namespaces `X` and `P` at activation (redeem at once under Q31's default) and renews them at the EXPIRING PRF time, with the `register` events of §4.8. DM deposits are not modelled: they spend no token and involve no issuer (T27), which the DM world proves (JD3, JD8, NI-DM3).
3. **Report.** The `population` line gains `dm_owner_namespaces` (2 per activated client); the PR and gate baselines are re-recorded; `t2-report-check.sh` requires the new field. NI-1, NI-2, NI-3 and NI-K are unchanged in rule (no issuer input reaches the new events).
4. **Expected effect.** Two more namespaces per client in the declared attacker's relay-side features (its namespace count, F8 §19.24 point 5), identical for every client: a uniform shift, not a new signal. E16 (exhaustion-triggered purchases) cannot grow with DM breadth.

---

## 5. Addressing, relay sets and bundles (I3, I4; ADR-30′, ADR-36)

### 5.1 Namespaces (normative; G-4)

| Namespace | Registered by | `Namespaces.register` | Read | Write |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| own inbox `X_0` | owner, at activation and restore | `(X_0, DM, union set, listen = true, REDEEM)` | owner: READ credential `RX_{0,s}`, or its write capability | card holders with `D_{0,s}`; owner's capability (never used for stores) |
| own bundle `P_0` | owner, same moments | `(P_0, PREKEYS, set, listen = false, REDEEM)` | fetchers with `RP_{0,s}` | owner's write capability (bundles); owner verifies with `check` |
| contact inbox `X_c` | us, at the first send to c | `(X_c, DM, set(X_c, w), listen = false, LOCAL)` | nobody on our side | us, with `D_{c,s}` per relay slot (§21.2) |
| contact bundle `P_c` | us, during establishment only (§5.5) | `(P_c, PREKEYS, set(P_c, w), listen = true, LOCAL)`, removed after | us, with `RP_{c,s}` per relay slot (§21.2) | nobody on our side |

- Namespaces are **static per card index**; credentials rotate weekly (§3.2). Weekly namespace rotation is rejected (C-4): under AD-1 the new namespace starts in the same sessions as the old one (session co-occurrence, LIMITE L2.1 #1), so it does not break continuity; it would cost extra read pairs and tokens at every roll and lose mail for recipients offline beyond the listening window. What it would have bought, a seized relay disk showing unrelated weekly namespaces, is given up and declared (L7-D13).
- `Namespaces.remove(X_c)` when the contact is deleted and its ops are released; `P_c` is removed at the end of each establishment (§5.5).

### 5.2 Relay set of a DM namespace (normative; RP F11, G-4)

```
order(ns, w)  = the ES slots of week w sorted by HMAC-SHA256(key = ns, "ghost/v1/dm/slot-order" || u8(slot))      (no week in the HMAC, §21.1)
set(ns, w)    = the relays of the first slots in order(ns, w) until 3 are taken and they span >= 2 distinct operators
```

- Anyone who knows the namespace and the signed ES computes the set, so senders need no relay list in the card, and the set carries no information about the owner (a client choosing its own 3 of 32 slots would be a C(32, 3) = 4 960-way fingerprint across its namespaces, RP F11, E-15). Vectors `protocol/test-vectors/dm_relay_set.txt`, replayed by Kotlin (`:crypto-bridge`) and by the Rust T2 world.
- If week w offers fewer than 2 operators, `Outbox.enqueue` throws `InsufficientReplicasException` and the facade reports `NO_REPLICAS` (ADR-11 rule; rare by ES design).
- **Slot-table changes.** Writers (senders, the owner's bundle op) use `set(ns, week(now))`. The owner, as a reader, keeps `setRelays(ns, ⋃ set(ns, w′))` over the weeks `w′ ∈ [week(now) − 5, week(now)]` whose sets differ: a blob stored in week w′ may live until `w′ + 6` weeks (store ≤ compose + 7 d + 1 h, TTL ≤ 30 d + 1 h, §10.1). A relay that left the ES gets no new capability (`NO_SLOT`); the owner reads it with its still-valid READ key, and blobs there were also written to the other relays of that week's set. The `KEEPER` step recomputes the sets at each week change and calls `setRelays` only when they differ (Phase 7 repairs the deliveries, F7 §3.9).

### 5.3 Bundle v2 (normative; replaces the libsignal bundle and §21.7 point 1's `issued_hour`, §22)

```
bundle_plain = ver(1) = 0x02 || card_index(2) || bundle_week(8) || bundle_rank(2) || gen_id(4)
             || OI(32, Olm identity, X25519) || F_g(32, Olm fallback key, X25519) || Q_g(1216, X-Wing public key)
             || identity_pk(32, Ed25519) || sig(64, Ed25519 over "ghost/v1/dm-bundle/v2" || all previous bytes)
             = 1 393 bytes
blob(4096)   = salt(16) || hint(16) || ChaCha20-Poly1305(k, nonce = 0^12, AD, 0x03 || bundle_plain || zeros)      (the §6.2 layout)
k            = HKDF-SHA256(ikm = KB_i, salt = salt, info = "ghost/v1/dm-seal" || P_i, L = 32)
hint         = HMAC-SHA256(R_KB_i, "ghost/v1/dm-hint" || salt)[0..16]          (R_KB_i = the recognition key of KB_i, §6.2)
AD           = "ghost/v1/dm-seal" || 0x02 || P_i(32) || u32_be(4096) || 0x03 || 0^32
```

- **One signature binds everything** a fetcher needs: the Olm identity (the bundle signature is the identity binding), the fallback key (Olm's signed prekey, which Olm's PCS needs) and the X-Wing key. Distinct fixed lengths and positions keep X25519, X-Wing and Ed25519 encodings pairwise distinct (the key-confusion lesson of the PQXDH analysis).
- **No one-time keys** (§5.6). **No registration id and no device id**: round 9's per-install registration id let any card holder detect a reinstall or restore by polling bundles; it is gone.
- **`bundle_rank` replaces `issued_hour`** (§22.4: `issued_hour` gave every card holder the hour of the owner's first wake after `t_pub` and the restore hour, contradicting §21.9 point 4, Q43 and L7-D10). OWNER-WEEK bundles have rank 0. An ACTIVATE or restore publication in week w uses `1 + max(rank)` over the valid bundles of weeks `w − 1 … w + 1` it lists in its own `P_0` (an own-namespace read, T31), or 1 if none. Fetchers rank by `(bundle_week, bundle_rank)` descending, then the smaller blob hash. A card holder learns from the bundle "a publication outside the weekly schedule happened in week w" (activation or restore), and no hour from its bytes; by polling `P` it still sees the minute each bundle appears, and fetch reads return only week-coarse `uploaded_at` and expiry (§22.8.7, L7-D10). Mutant PM-35 (rank from sealing time) is detected by an AD-7 variant of NI-DM6.
- `dm_bundle_candidate.bundle_env` = 1 393 + 28 = **1 421** B (§13.6).
- The hint is useless for a single key but keeps one byte shape for every DM blob (T29). The X-Wing public key is 1 216 bytes (ML-KEM-768 encapsulation key 1 184 B plus the X25519 key 32 B); `Q_g` is checked by length, by libcrux's encapsulation-key validation of the ML-KEM half **and** by a contributory trial X25519 agreement on its last 32 bytes (libcrux validates only the ML-KEM half and fails encapsulation on a low-order X25519 half; `INVALID_KEY`, §22.8.2); a low-order `F_g` fails Olm's non-contributory check at session creation (`vz:src/olm/account/mod.rs:193-219`, typed `INVALID_KEY`).

### 5.4 Publication, rotation and retention (normative; C-10, SC-6; retention re-derived by §22)

- **When.** At activation and restore, at once. Then once per ISO week w at `t_pub(w) = start(w) + (HMAC-SHA256(S_0, "ghost/v1/dm/pub-offset") mod 7 d)` floored to the minute (**the 32-byte HMAC becomes an integer as its first 8 bytes, big-endian, fixed by S6, §22.11 item 2**) (a fixed offset per card, so exactly 7 days apart), at the first time-triggered actor pass at or after it (the `OWNER-WEEK` transaction, §7.2, §21.9). A missed week publishes at the next time-triggered pass. Nothing about contacts, fetches or messages enters the timing (T31, mutant PM-11).
- **What.** A new bundle generation g (`bundleCreate`, §2.6): an Olm fallback key pair `(f_g, F_g)`, an X-Wing seed `q_g` with its public key `Q_g`, and a random `gen_id_g` distinct from every retained generation; the secrets are stored wrapped in `olm_prekey_gen`; the previous generation gets `superseded_day = today` and `delete_after_day = superseded_day + 91`; `bundle_week = w`, `bundle_rank = 0`; the blob is sealed and enqueued to `P_0` with `TtlBucket.DAYS_30` and deadline `start(w + 2)` (a bundle is useless after its validity); `dm_self.bundle_week = w`. Idempotent per week (guard `bundle_week < w`) and atomic. A FAILED or INDETERMINATE bundle op is re-sealed (new salt, same generation and rank) and enqueued again at the next time-triggered pass while the week's validity lasts, tracked by `dm_self.bundle_published_week` (§21.8, §21.9).
- **Validity.** A bundle of week w is valid while `now < start(w + 2)` and `w ≤ week(now) + 1`. With weekly publication, a live owner always has a valid bundle; an owner offline for more than about two weeks has none, and new contacts cannot establish until it returns (declared, L7-D10).
- **Retention of superseded generation secrets: `delete_after_day = superseded_day + 91`.** Derivation (no library constant enters it):
  1. a fetcher can still pick generation g until its validity ends, at most 7 days after g was superseded;
  2. an initiator keeps an **unacknowledged** session built on g, and keeps sealing intro envelopes to `Q_g`, for at most `UNACK_MAX = 28 d` (§8.7; replaces libsignal's 30-day unacknowledged-session age);
  3. a peer uses an in-band announced `Q_g` (PQ-C, §9.4) only while `now < start(bundle_week_g + 5)`, that is at most 28 days after supersession;
  4. a message authored on the last such day is accepted by the recipient until `author + FRESH` = 45 days later (§7.6), and cannot be accepted after it;
  5. two clocks within σ = 3 d each add 6 days.

  7 + 28 + 45 + 6 = 86 days, rounded up to 13 weeks. After deletion (cascading to the reuse tuples, D2b) a later PreKey or intro envelope naming g is `drop(unknown_generation)` before any state change (outside CS-3's bounds by construction). A device holds at most 14 generations (92 B of wrapped secrets each). **A generation referenced by a live held row** (§8.3) is not erased before that row's `hold_until_minute` (≤ 30 d + 1 h later), so a message held near the end of the window can still create its session after the user's decision; that generation's attribution window grows by at most 31 days (declared in L7-D11).
- **Erasure.** SWEEP deletes the `olm_prekey_gen` row (cascading its reuse tuples). An intro replay older than FRESH already stops at R5 (§21.4 point 4, with the generation in place of the prekey ids).

### 5.5 Fetch (normative; T31; E-2; §21.2 and §21.7 folded in by §22)

- **When.** A bundle of contact c is fetched **only** (a) when the user sends to a contact with no usable session, (b) when the user asks to reset or reconnect (§8.6, §8.7), or (c) when a user send finds no current session, a `NO_SEAL_KEY` result (§6.8) or a stale initiator session (unacknowledged for `UNACK_MAX = 28 d`, §8.7). Never on key change, restore, inbound traffic or a schedule.
- **Mechanism (the §21.7 rules, restated in place).** In the send transaction the item stays `pending` with a `dm_pending_send` row (`fail_after_hour = ceil_hour(min(author + 7 d, deadline))`) and `EST-START` runs: `Inbox.forgetConsumed(tx, P_c)`; `Namespaces.register(P_c, PREKEYS, set(P_c, week(now)), listen = true, LOCAL)`; `Capabilities.put(READ, credential_v3(fetch, slot(r), P_c, RP_{c,slot(r)}))` (kind FETCH, §22.8.7) per relay r of the set (static per slot, §21.2); `dm_contact.establishment = fetching`, `fetch_attempts = 1`, `fetch_listen_until_minute = ceil_minute(now + 15 min)`. The next list events of those pairs fetch every live bundle blob; the `BUNDLE` transaction opens each (`bundleOpen`) and keeps the best verified candidate in `dm_bundle_candidate`; the time-triggered `EST-READY` (at the first candidate + 2 min) checks trust, pins, stores `dm_peer_pq(c, gen_id, Q_g, source = bundle)`, calls `DmSessions.outbound` (`sessionOutbound(OI_c, F_g)`, a new `current` session under R-SS0), and encrypts, seals (intro mode) and enqueues every pending item of c in one transaction; then `setListening(P_c, false)`. `EST-END` bounds the fetch at three 15-minute attempts at relay session starts at least 6 h apart; after the third the contact is `unreachable` and the pending items fail (the user may retry). A refused READ credential is re-put at most once per relay session start (§21.2).
- **Checks (all before any session call).** The blob opens under `KB_c` with the recognition hint; `ver = 2`; `card_index` = the card's; `identity_pk` = the card's; the Ed25519 signature verifies under it; validity (§5.4); no rollback of `(bundle_week, bundle_rank)` against `dm_contact.bundle_week_seen` (relays cannot delete old bundles and at most five are live). Then, on `OI`:
  - no pinned Olm identity yet → GHOST pins `OI` (`dm_contact.pinned_identity`) in `EST-READY`, before `sessionOutbound`;
  - `OI` = pinned → proceed;
  - `OI` ≠ pinned **with the signature valid under the pinned Ed25519 key** → **key change** (§8.3): no session is built, the contact goes to `changed` with `pending_identity = OI`, the candidate is kept, and after the user's decision `EST-READY` builds from it while it is valid, otherwise a new attempt starts (E-2: §5.5 and §8.3 agree);
  - signature invalid → the blob is dropped (counter `bad_binding`) and the next blob is tried.
- **In HIGH mode** the fetch is the pair's next read event like any read; the deposit follows the HIGH send delay. The Phase 7 read schedule of other pairs is unaffected (T19 "adding or removing other pairs does not change a pair's schedule").
- **Relay-visible.** Listings of `P_c` with the shared read scope of each relay (all fetchers of that card look alike), inside at most three attempt windows, then a deposit into `X_c` in the same or a later session. Under AD-1 this links `P_c` and `X_c` as one owner at establishment (declared L7-D6); a relay without the card learns nothing more.

### 5.6 One-time keys: none (normative; Q9)

- A blind store cannot hand a one-time key to exactly one fetcher: every fetcher sees all of them, two fetchers can pick the same one, and the second PreKey message fails, a message loss by construction (NFR-12). Replenishment writes would follow consumption, so the bundle namespace's write times would reveal the owner's new-contact rate (RP F15).
- Olm establishes sessions on a **fallback key** (vodozemac's `create_inbound_session` searches one-time keys, then fallback keys, `vz:src/olm/account/mod.rs:221`), exactly as matrix-rust-sdk does in production when one-time keys run out. vodozemac does not remove a fallback key on use (`:324`), so GHOST keeps the **first-flight reuse set** `olm_prekey_used(gen_id, session_id)` in the `RECV` transaction that created the session (D2b, §7.6): a replayed first message is refused while the generation lives and fails for lack of keys after it is erased.
- **Cost, declared (L7-D11, PQ-R3).** The first flight of a session has no one-time-key forward secrecy: an attacker who records it and later obtains the recipient's Olm identity key plus that generation's fallback and X-Wing secrets **before their erasure** (≤ 13 weeks after supersession) can decrypt it. The ratchet heals from the first DH ratchet step on (classically; §9.9).
- **Rejected alternative (C-7): HELLO.** Pushing a per-contact bundle with genuine one-time keys inside a signed HELLO into the recipient's inbox removes fetch linkability and restores one-time-key forward secrecy, but requires an accept round trip before the adder can write, keeps no key namespace (plan row 9 names "publicare prekeys prin relay"), and ties the first message to the card owner's consent flow. Recorded in ADR-30′ for Phase 13 reconsideration with the contact backup.

### 5.7 Attestation (ADR-14 P1): deferred, never relay-readable (Q10)

Not in Phase 9, for three reasons:
1. The identity keys are seed-derived (FR-1.2, ADR-17, C-1); Android Key Attestation covers Keystore-generated keys only, so no chain could attest the key contacts see (RS K14).
2. The chain carries OS, patch level and model family (against ADR-08 "fără model/OS în mesajele de protocol"), and with Remote Key Provisioning the certificates are per device: a cross-context identifier (RP F12).
3. Its size would put some users' bundles in a different bucket (T29).

If ever shipped (Phase 13 or later): a Keystore-generated device key signed by the identity, its chain sent inside the E2E session, opt-in, with a UI line on what it reveals. ADR-30 records the deviation (X-6).

---

## 6. The DM envelope: sealed sender without a server (I2, I4; T9; ADR-31′, ADR-36)

### 6.1 Why an outer seal, and which (the I2 decision; C-2, E-3; revised §22)

An Olm message shows its session headers in clear: a PreKey message carries the sender's Olm identity key, its base key, the recipient's one-time or fallback key and the inner normal message; a normal message carries the ratchet public key, the chain index, the ciphertext and an 8-byte MAC (`vz:src/olm/messages/pre_key.rs:97-127`, `vz:src/olm/messages/message.rs:73-97`). Stored as is, it is a sender field (T1), a per-session linkable identifier and a type marker (T9). An outer seal is mandatory, as it was over libsignal messages.

| Option | Relay sees | Recipient authenticates the sender by | Assessment |
|---|---|---|---|
| A. A server-certificate sealed-sender scheme (sender certificates signed by a trust root, self-issued without a server) | a scheme header, a raw ephemeral key, variable length, no padding: a GHOST layer is needed outside it anyway | a certificate whose validity comes entirely from GHOST's own override | **Rejected.** A fictional PKI; certificate semantics GHOST does not control; sealing to a long-term identity key (no key separation, no attribution forward secrecy) |
| B. GHOST seal around plain Olm messages | uniform bytes of one bucket | the pair or provisional key (pair and provisional modes) or the card, the X-Wing-keyed inner layer and binding v3 (intro mode), then Olm's own 3DH over both identity keys | **Chosen**, in the four-mode form below |
| C. A multi-recipient format with cleartext recipient identifiers | the recipient set, a social graph, on the relay | as A | **Rejected** |
| B′. Per-message ephemeral X25519 with Elligator 2 to one inbox key | uniform only with a correct Elligator 2 encoding | the card in the slot, then the session | **Rejected.** The candidate crate is `curve25519-elligator2` 0.1.0-alpha.2 (2024-07-26), a BSD-3-Clause fork of `curve25519-dalek`, unaudited and pre-release; the recipe omitted the random low-order component, so representatives of prime-order-subgroup keys stay distinguishable; and one long-term inbox key opens every past envelope, so a seed plus AD-1's archive attributes the whole relay history |
| B″. Raw X25519 key with a random top bit | a valid curve point in the first 32 bytes (about half of all strings are not) | as B′ | **Rejected**: T9 "indistinguishable from random" declared unmet |

**Decision.** *Pair mode* is a per-pair symmetric seal whose key is ratcheted weekly from a **root generated by the recipient** (attribution forward secrecy, P-7). *Intro mode* covers every PreKey message: a symmetric outer layer keyed by the recipient's card (uniform bytes without curve work) and an inner layer keyed by **X-Wing** to the recipient's weekly bundle generation, so other card holders cannot read it and a recording quantum adversary cannot either (P-8). *Provisional mode* covers normal messages of a session before a pair root exists, keyed from a `reply_seed` that travelled in the intro inner layer. *Bundle mode* seals bundles under the card. All four present the same `salt ‖ hint ‖ AEAD` shape, built from HKDF-SHA256, HMAC-SHA256, X-Wing and ChaCha20-Poly1305 inside `ghost-client-dm` (a CP-09 exception recorded in ADR-31′ and ADR-36).

### 6.2 L2 layout, recognition keys, AD (normative; revised §22)

```
blob(B)  = salt(16) || hint(16) || ChaCha20-Poly1305(k, nonce = 0^12, AD, P)          |P| = B − 48, B ∈ {4096, 16384, 65536}
R_sel    = HKDF-SHA256(ikm = K_sel, salt = ∅, info = "ghost/v1/dm-recognition", L = 32)   (recognition key)
hint     = HMAC-SHA256(R_sel, "ghost/v1/dm-hint" || salt)[0..16]
k        = HKDF-SHA256(ikm = K_sel, salt = salt, info = "ghost/v1/dm-seal" || namespace(32), L = 32)
AD       = "ghost/v1/dm-seal" || 0x02 || namespace(32) || u32_be(B) || mode(1) || pair_ad(32)          (not transmitted)
pair_ad  = SHA-256("ghost/v1/dm-pair-ad" || OI_sender || OI_recipient)   (pair, provisional); 0^32 (intro, bundle)
K_sel    = a pair week opening key (pair) | K_prov(dir) (provisional, §6.7) | KIO_i of the recipient's card (intro, §6.4) | KB_i (bundle, §5.3)
P        = mode(1) ∈ {0x01 pair, 0x02 intro, 0x03 bundle, 0x04 provisional} || mode-specific body || zeros
```

- **R-S1 (T9).** `|blob| = B` exactly; every length is inside P; the bytes are uniform under the PRF security of HMAC-SHA256 and the IND$-CPA security of ChaCha20-Poly1305 (salt random, hint a PRF output, key fresh per salt).
- **R-S2 (canonical).** A blob is accepted only if it opens and parses exactly: a known mode and kind byte, every length within P, **all padding bytes zero**. Otherwise it is consumed and dropped (a counter, no content). Padding cannot carry a covert channel; the format is not malleable.
- **R-S3 (binding).** AD and k bind the namespace and the bucket: a relay that copies the blob into another namespace (one relay store can hold one ciphertext under several namespaces, `relay/crates/storage/src/lib.rs:6-12`) or truncates it produces something that does not open.
- **R-S4 (nonce).** k is fresh per salt; nonce 0 never repeats under one key (a 16-byte salt repeats with probability ≈ n²/2¹²⁹).
- **R-S5 (one attempt, no oracle; E-13; candidate set amended).** The recipient computes the hints of its candidate recognition keys and opens with **at most one** key, the one whose hint matches. There is no trial decryption across keys, so the partitioning-oracle attack on the non-committing AEAD (Len, Grubbs, Ristenpart, USENIX Security 2021) does not apply. A hint match whose AEAD fails is a forgery: dropped, counted. **Candidate keys, per contact:** the in-week recognition keys of every live root generation for the weeks `w` with `now < start(w + 1) + FRESH` (at most 8) plus `K_{week(now)+1}` computed on demand (≤ 9 per generation); the `K_prov` recognition keys of its ≤ 2 sessions that still hold `prov_env` (≤ 2 per session); plus `KIO_0` of the own card. With root replacement every 4 weeks at most two root generations overlap, so **≤ 22 keys per contact** and **≤ 22 001 HMAC-SHA256 evaluations per unknown blob** at `MAX_CONTACTS = 1 000`. Hints are computed lazily, the most recent week first, contacts in an in-memory most-recently-matched order (never persisted), stopping at the first match; a forged or unknown blob costs the full set. S7 measures a 16-blob batch at 1 000 contacts on the reference mid-range device against a 1 s foreground budget, also with the 7-day PQ-C cadence of Q68 (V-13); if it misses, `MAX_CONTACTS` is lowered (Q37). A card holder flooding forged blobs spends its own deposits' allowance and the victim's CPU, bounded by `FETCHED_CAP` and the consumer budgets (L7-D8).
- **R-S6 (new): recognition vs opening.** Kotlin stores and matches with `R_sel` only; `K_sel` is stored wrapped (§2.5) and handed to Rust with the blob. A recognition key recognises but does not open; it follows the erasure schedule of its opening key, because recognition is attribution (P-7, mutant PM-36).
- **R-S7 (new): mode and identities in the AD.** The key class that matched fixes the mode the AEAD expects; `pair_ad` binds both Olm identities and the seal version (spec §7.2 "associated data binding the protocol version and both identities").

### 6.3 Pair mode (normative; SC-4; revised §22)

```
P = 0x01 (pair) || kind(1) = 0x01 (Olm normal message) || l1_len(4, BE) || L1 || zeros
```

- **Roots.** For each contact c, **I as the recipient** generate `root_{c→me}` (32 B, CSPRNG; the PQ-C form of §9.4 once c's X-Wing key is known), a base week `b = week(now)` and a generation number `root_gen` (previous + 1, starting at 0), store them wrapped in `dm_pair_offer`, and send `(root, b, root_gen)` to c inside the E2E content of my user-initiated messages to c whose Olm output is a **normal message** (L0 TLV `seal_root` or `seal_root_pq`, §7.1; never in a PreKey message, §21.3) until a pair-mode envelope from c opens under a key of that generation. A root is never derived from a seed, so no seed compromise recovers it.
- **Week keys.** `K_b = HKDF-SHA256(root, "ghost/v1/dm/pair-epoch" ‖ u64_be(b))`; `K_{w+1} = HKDF-SHA256(K_w, "ghost/v1/dm/pair-next")`. Opening keys are stored wrapped (`dm_pair_key.open_env`), recognition keys in clear (`dm_pair_key.recog_key`); both are erased together (R-S6).
  - **Sender c** (who received my root) keeps `K_{week(now)}` of the newest generation, ratchets forward at each week boundary and erases the previous key, except that it keeps the (generation, week) key of an earlier week while an item authored in that week is `pending`, `enqueued` or `failed` before its deadline (≤ 7 d; a user retry needs it, §22.8.5); it erases the root as soon as `K_b` is computed.
  - **Recipient (me)** keeps, per live generation, `K_w` for every week w with `now < start(w + 1) + FRESH` (FRESH = 45 d, §7.6) up to `week(now)`, computes `K_{week(now)+1}` on demand, and erases each `K_w` in the first sweep at or after `start(w + 1) + FRESH` (by the expiry clock, §10.3), after deriving the keys up to `week(now)` in the same sweep (§21.3). No legitimate blob sealed under `K_w` can be accepted after that instant: its author minute is before `start(w + 1)` by the sender's clock, and content older than FRESH is refused. My offered root stays in `dm_pair_offer` only so that later messages can carry it again: its secret is erased when a pair envelope under its generation opens (acknowledged) or at `start(b + 1) + FRESH`, whichever comes first (a new root is then offered on my next user message); the week keys already computed follow the window rule, and opening never needs the root. A generation stops advancing (no new week keys) once a pair envelope under a newer generation from c opens.
- **Mode rule (sender).** §6.8.
- **Replacement.** I offer a new root (a) after my restore, to every contact I reconnect (§8.6); (b) after I acknowledge or verify a key change of c (§8.3); (c) when `week(now) ≥ base_week + ROOT_REPLACE_WEEKS` for my newest root for c, to bound a stolen-state window: `ROOT_REPLACE_WEEKS = 13` without PQ-C and **4** with PQ-C (slice S13, Q49; Q68 asks for 1 week). The new root rides on my next user message to c whose output is a normal message.
- **P-7 (attribution forward secrecy).** An attacker who later seizes both devices and both seeds, and holds AD-1's archive of all blobs, cannot tell which pair-mode envelopes of a week w with `now ≥ start(w + 1) + 45 d` belong to which conversation: the keys are erased, not derivable, and the envelopes are uniform (the skipped-key exception of §21.3 point 6, now limited to live sessions, is declared). Content was already protected by Olm; this protects the **who-talked-to-whom-when history** of expired messages, which T25 alone does not.

### 6.4 Intro mode: the first message to an offline recipient (normative; replaces the X25519 intro layer, §22)

```
P        = 0x02 || gen_id(4) || xwing_ct(1120) || ChaCha20-Poly1305(k_in, 0^12, AD_in, Q)            |Q| = B − 1 189
(ct, ss) = XWing.Encaps(Q_g)  /  ss = XWing.Decaps(q_g, ct)                               (libcrux-kem XWingKemDraft06)
T_in     = SHA-256("ghost/v1/dm-intro-kem" || namespace(32) || u32_be(B) || u32_be(gen_id)
                   || Q_g(1216) || xwing_ct(1120) || OI_recipient(32) || identity_pk_recipient(32))
k_in     = HKDF-SHA256(ikm = ss, salt = T_in, info = "ghost/v1/dm-intro-inner", L = 32)
AD_in    = "ghost/v1/dm-intro" || 0x02 || T_in
Q        = kind(1) = 0x02 (Olm PreKey message) || sender_card(131) || binding_minute(4) || binding_sig(64)
           || reply_seed(32) || l1_len(4, BE) || L1 || zeros
K_sel    = KIO_i of the recipient's card (outer layer, §6.2)
```

- **The first message to an offline recipient, end to end.** A holds B's card. A's first user send creates the pending item and starts the bundle fetch (§5.5). `EST-READY` verifies B's bundle, pins `OI_B`, stores `dm_peer_pq(B, gen_id, Q_g, source = bundle)`, calls `sessionOutbound(OI_B, F_g)` (a throwaway `Account` from the seed-derived dehydrated pickle runs Olm's 3DH with a fresh base key, `vz:src/olm/account/mod.rs:193-219`) and stores the session as `current` (initiator, unacknowledged, `peer_gen_id = gen_id`, a fresh `reply_seed`). It encrypts the pending L0 into an Olm PreKey message; `sealIntro` encapsulates to `Q_g`, derives `k_in` from the transcript, signs binding v3 (§3.4) and seals under `KIO_B`. The deposit goes to `X_B` with B's deposit credential. B may stay offline up to its registration horizon (§21.2 point 9); relays store uniform bytes.
- **Every intro envelope has its own encapsulation** (a fresh `ct` and hence a fresh `k_in`), so every PreKey message of an unacknowledged session is sealed independently; `reply_seed` belongs to the session and repeats inside each inner layer. Tests: JD10 (no 16-byte window of any intro envelope's inner bytes repeats across a world) and NI-DM7 (a first flight and a re-sent unacknowledged PreKey look alike to a card holder); mutant PM-37 (RG-4).
- **Why X-Wing only** (Q55). One hybrid KEM with a proof (IND-CCA if X25519 or ML-KEM-768 holds), seed-form private keys, no negotiation, and a transcript hashing every public key and the ciphertext, as the PQXDH analysis recommends (Bhargavan et al.). Label strings carry the version; a new suite is a new label.
- **What a holder of B's card sees** (L7-D3, revised): it recognises intro envelopes to B (the `KIO` hint) and reads `gen_id` and `ct`, not the inner layer. A future quantum adversary holding B's card and the archive still cannot open the inner layer (ML-KEM). Round 9's quantum clause of L7-D3 and the intro part of R-19 are removed.
- **Attribution of first contacts (P-7, new scope).** Nothing seed-derived opens an intro inner layer any more. After B erases generation g (`superseded_day + 91`, later only while a held row references g, §5.4), no key on B's device or derivable from B's seed opens B's intro envelopes of g. Round 9's "intro envelopes stay attributable by anyone who later holds the recipient's seed" becomes "until the generation's erasure" (T34).

### 6.5 Recipient procedure (normative; runs inside the `RECV` transaction, §7.4; replaces round 9's steps, §22)

1. Peek ordering (§7.7) selects the blob; `Inbox.claimOne` claims it; `markConsumed` (R1), the poison guard (R2) and the own-namespace check (R3). `|blob| ∈ {4096, 16384, 65536}`, else drop.
2. Kotlin computes recognition hints (≤ 1 match, R-S5). No match → `drop(unknown_seal)`.
3. `openOuter` with the mode of the matched key class; canonical parse (R-S2).
4. **Intro.** Load the generation named by `gen_id` (absent → `drop(unknown_generation)`). `openIntro` decapsulates, opens the inner layer, parses card v2, verifies binding v3's signature, parses the PreKey header, and enforces `binding.OI_A = PreKey.identity_key`, `binding.base_key = PreKey.base_key`, `PreKey.one_time_key = F_{gen_id}` (else `drop(non_canonical)`). Kotlin R5: card, freshness `now − FRESH ≤ binding_minute ≤ now + 2σ`, the request path (§12.3, §21.4 point 3). R6: the identity pre-check on `OI_A` (32 B, §8.3).
5. **Pair or provisional.** Contact = the key's contact; the session identity was fixed at creation, so R6 is not needed; a PreKey L1 in these modes → `drop(non_canonical)`.
6. Continue with `RECV` R7–R12 (§7.4).

### 6.6 Sizes, `O_MAX` and bucket choice (normative; replaces the libsignal size table, §22)

| Component | Bytes (worst case) | Source |
|---|---|---|
| L2 outer (salt, hint, tag) and mode byte | 48 + 1 | §6.2 |
| intro fixed part: `gen_id` 4, `xwing_ct` 1 120, inner tag 16, kind 1, card v2 131, binding minute 4, binding 64, `reply_seed` 32, `l1_len` 4 | 1 376 | §6.4 |
| Olm PreKey message over its L0: version 1, three 34-byte key fields, message tag and length ≤ 4, the inner normal message | **≤ 179 (4 and 16 KiB), ≤ 181 (64 KiB; 3-byte length varints)** | measured on 0.11.0 and 0.10.0 over every chain-index varint length (S0′, V-3, 2026-09-19); `vz:src/olm/messages/pre_key.rs:97-127` |
| Olm normal message over its L0: version 1, ratchet key field 34, chain index field ≤ 11, ciphertext tag and length ≤ 4, AES-256-CBC PKCS#7 padding 1–16, MAC 8 | **≤ 73 (4 and 16 KiB), ≤ 74 (64 KiB)** | measured on 0.11.0 and 0.10.0 over every chain-index varint length (S0′, V-3, 2026-09-19); `vz:src/olm/messages/message.rs:73-97`; `vz:src/olm/session/message_key.rs:100-112` |
| pair or provisional body header: kind 1, `l1_len` 4 | 5 | §6.3, §6.7 |
| **intro PreKey total** | **≤ 1 606** (64 KiB) / ≤ 1 604 (4 and 16 KiB) | |
| pair or provisional total | ≤ 128 (64 KiB) / ≤ 127 (4 and 16 KiB) | |
| **`O_MAX`** | **1 664** | **frozen by S0′ (V-3, 2026-09-19)**: measured worst intro overhead 1 606 B at 64 KiB and 1 604 B at 4 and 16 KiB over 3 120 outputs per pin; margin 58 B at the tightest class, 60 B at 4 and 16 KiB; SessionConfig V2 would need 1 630 B, so a V2 move fits (+24 B). The word "provisional" is withdrawn here, in D9 and in RG-8 |

- **R-B1 (as corrected by §21.11).** `B = min{ b ∈ {4096, 16384, 65536} : L_class ≤ b − O_MAX }` with `L_class = 52 + |body| + EXT_RESERVE (160)`, computed **before** encryption, so pending TLVs and quotes never change the bucket. After sealing, the fit is asserted; a failure is a bug (`NO_FIT` → `IllegalStateException`) and rolls the transaction back before the session state commits.
- **R-B2 (floor, T29).** DMs never use 1 KiB. An intro envelope's fixed part alone is 1 425 B, and Olm sends PreKey messages until the peer answers; a 1 KiB class for established sessions would mark first contact, reset, reconnect and key change (RP F4). With R-B1, PreKey vs normal, intro vs provisional vs pair, text vs control of equal content length land in one bucket.
- **Capacities** (with `O_MAX = 1 664`): body ≤ **2 220 B** at 4 KiB; ≤ 14 508 B at 16 KiB; ≤ 63 660 B at 64 KiB. API cap `MAX_BODY_BYTES = 61 440`; longer content waits for Phase 11 media. One L0 per blob, no DM fragmentation.
- **Golden sizes (S0′ and every bump).** PreKey and normal outputs in every mode at every body length in `[C(B) − 64, C(B)]` for each bucket, with and without the PQ-C TLVs of the slack rule (§9.4), asserting the fit and bucket = f(body length), and recording the measured maximum overhead against `O_MAX`. **Fixed by S0′ (V-3, 2026-09-19):** the golden-size set re-run at every bump (G-R7) is the S0′ probe set — body lengths `0 … 64` **and** `[C(B) − 64, C(B)]` per bucket, **twelve chain-index varint classes** ({0, 127, 128, 2¹⁴, 2²¹, 2²⁸, 2³⁵, 2⁴², 2⁴⁹, 2⁵⁶, 2⁶³, 2⁶⁴ − 2}, every protobuf varint length from 1 to 10 bytes) and both output kinds. The chain index needs 10 varint bytes only past 2⁶³ messages on one chain; the bound counts them anyway.

### 6.7 Provisional mode: replies before a pair root exists (normative; new, §22)

```
P            = 0x04 || kind(1) = 0x01 (Olm normal message) || l1_len(4, BE) || L1 || zeros
sb           = SHA-256("ghost/v1/dm-session-binding" || OI_initiator || base_key || F_g || OI_responder)
K_prov(dir)  = HKDF-SHA256(ikm = reply_seed, salt = sb, info = "ghost/v1/dm/prov" || dir, L = 32)
dir          = 0x01 responder → initiator; 0x02 initiator → responder
```

- Round 9 sealed the responder's first reply in intro mode to the initiator's **seed-derived** card intro key. That key is gone (card v2). An intro reply would need the initiator's X-Wing key, which the responder holds only after a bundle fetch (a new T31 read and an L7-D6 link) or if every intro envelope carried 1 220 more bytes (−1 220 B of body capacity in every class, because `O_MAX` is uniform). The provisional key costs 32 B inside the intro inner layer and no fetch.
- `reply_seed` travels only inside `k_in`-sealed layers (PQ-B). Both sides compute `sb` from the Olm session keys they share.
- **Lifetime (amended by §22.8.5).** Each side erases `prov_env` at `prov_erase_minute = min((the minute a pair envelope under my newest root from the peer first opened) + 45 d, prov_anchor_minute + 119 d)`, or when the session is deleted; `prov_anchor_minute` is the first decryption on an initiator session and the first provisional seal on a responder session, and provisional sealing stops at `prov_anchor_minute + 28 d` and after any pair envelope was sealed on the session (§6.8). A responder that never replies, and an initiator never acknowledged, keep it until the session or request is deleted (no provisional envelope of that session exists; §21.3 point 9). Round 9b's rule alone let a side whose peer never wrote after receiving its root keep `prov_env` for the life of the session.
- **Attribution window (declared, L7-D21).** A provisional envelope becomes unattributable once both sides erased `prov_env` (each by its `prov_anchor_minute + 119 d` at the latest) **and** the recipient of the session's first flight erased that generation (which lets a seizer re-open an intro envelope and read `reply_seed`; ≤ 91 d after supersession). Round 9's responder intro replies were attributable with the initiator's seed forever.

### 6.8 Sender mode rule (normative; replaces round 9's §6.3 mode rule and SEND S6, §22)

1. Olm output `PreKey` → **intro**, to the session's `peer_gen_id` key in `dm_peer_pq`.
2. Olm output `Normal` and an out pair key of c for `week(author_minute)` exists → **pair** (the sender keeps earlier weeks' out keys while a retryable item authored in them lives, §22.8.5).
3. Olm output `Normal`, no out key, the session holds `prov_env`, no pair envelope was ever sealed on it (`pair_sent = 0`) and `now < prov_anchor_minute + 28 d` when the anchor is set → **provisional** (`dir` from the session role; §22.8.5).
4. Otherwise `NO_SEAL_KEY`: SEND rolls back, keeps the item pending and runs `EST-START` (a user-caused re-establishment). Reachable after a deliberate erasure, or when a side has sealed provisional for 28 days without receiving its peer's root (declared, L7-D6); counted.

First messages, messages after a reset or reconnect, and every message of an initiator session the peer has not answered are intro; normal messages before a pair root are provisional; everything else is pair.

---

## 7. Content, transactions, pipelines, dedup, ordering and replay (I4; T13; ADR-31′, ADR-33′)

### 7.1 L0 content `DmContent` v1 (normative)

Canonical binary, big-endian, fixed order, one encoding per value (RE §4).

| Field | Size | Meaning |
|---|---|---|
| `version` | 1 | 0x01 |
| `kind` | 1 | 0x01 TEXT; 0x02 TIMER_SET; 0x03 REACTION (rows in Phase 9, UI in Phase 13); 0x04 CONTROL (no body; TLVs only); 0x05 MEDIA (reserved for Phase 11, refused in Phase 9). **No DELIVERY or RECEIPT kind** (D10) |
| `message_id` | 16 | CSPRNG; the end-to-end idempotency key; never relay-visible |
| `author_minute` | 4 | u32 minutes since the Unix epoch at compose: seconds are zero by type (T13) |
| `minute_index` | 2 | order of the sender's own messages within `author_minute` (§7.7) |
| `timer_code` | 1 | 0 off, 1 = 5 min, 2 = 1 h, 3 = 1 d, 4 = 7 d, 5 = 30 d (ADR-27 C.7): this message's own timer |
| `timer_version` | 4 | the sender's conversation timer version (§10.5), at most `i32::MAX − 1`: the codec refuses a greater one in **both** directions, so a peer cannot write a version this device could not re-encode after the `version + 1` of its own next TIMER_SET (§22.14 item 15(c)) |
| `flags` | 1 | bit 0 `has_quote`; other bits 0 |
| `quote_ref` | 0 or 16 | `message_id` of the quoted message; never copied text |
| `body_len`, `body` | 4 + n | TEXT: UTF-8 (strict), n ≤ 61 440; TIMER_SET, CONTROL: n = 0; REACTION: target `message_id`(16) ‖ one grapheme (≤ 32 B; empty = remove) |
| `ext_len`, `ext` | 2 + m | TLVs `type(u16) ‖ len(u16) ‖ value`, strictly ascending type, each at most once; bit 15 of type = critical |

TLV registry (Phase 9):

| Type | Name | Value | Rule |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0x0001 | `seal_root` | `root(32) ‖ base_week(8) ‖ root_gen(2)` | the root the sender of this L0 wants the recipient to use for recipient → sender pair envelopes (§6.3); accepted only inside an L0 carried by a **normal Olm message** (never a PreKey message, §21.3); a `root_gen` not greater than the stored out generation is ignored, and a session renewal (R-SS1) clears that generation (§21.3) |
| 0x0002 | `card_update` | reserved (Phase 13 cut-off rotation) | non-critical; ignored in Phase 9 |
| 0x0003 | `revocation` | revocation certificate v2 (102 B, §8.5) | verified under the pinned `identity_pk` |
| 0x0004 | `session_reset` | empty | the reconnect notice (§8.6); a content-free contact event, no message row |
| 0x0005 | `seal_root_pq` | `root_rand(32) ‖ base_week(8) ‖ root_gen(2) ‖ peer_gen_id(4) ‖ xwing_ct(1120)` = 1 166 B | the PQ-C form of `seal_root` (§9.4): the root is `HKDF(root_rand ‖ ss)` with `ss` from an X-Wing encapsulation to the recipient's generation `peer_gen_id`; the `seal_root` rules apply; attached only under the slack rule R-C5 (slice S13) |
| 0x0006 | `pq_key` | `gen_id(4) ‖ bundle_week(8) ‖ xwing_pk(1216)` = 1 228 B | my current generation's X-Wing public key; stored by the recipient in `dm_peer_pq` (source `in_band`) if its week is newer; usable while `now < start(bundle_week + 5)`; attached only under R-C5 (slice S13) |
| 0x0010 | `read_upto` | reserved for Phase 13 opt-in read receipts (Q8) | non-critical; ignored in Phase 9 |
| 0x0100–0x01FF | media descriptors | Phase 11 | non-critical; ignored |

- **R-C1 (T13).** Author time exists only as `author_minute`. It is the compose minute, before any HIGH send delay (the delay applies to the store only; plan §2.3 declares author minutes visible to recipients).
- **R-C2.** No field names the sender or the recipient; the seal and the session identify the sender (§6.5).
- **R-C3 (no reflex content).** TLVs 0x0001, 0x0003, 0x0004, 0x0005 and 0x0006 are attached **only to user-initiated sends**: the next TEXT, TIMER_SET or REACTION to that contact whose Olm output is a normal message carries a pending `seal_root` or `seal_root_pq` (§21.3, §9.4); an explicit user action (reconnect, notify contacts of a revocation) enqueues a CONTROL message. No inbound blob ever causes a CONTROL message (T28).
- **Timestamps (T13, normative summary).** Every time a DM carries or stores is minute-granular or coarser: `author_minute` is a u32 count of minutes (seconds zero by type); the revocation certificate v2 carries `issued_minute`; no time value enters library state (vodozemac reads no clock and stores none, `vz:src` has no `SystemTime` or `Instant`; gate G-R8); `bundle_week` and credential weeks are ISO-week indices; local columns are `*_minute`, `*_hour` or `*_day` with `typeof` CHECKs (§13.6); the relay sees only its own minute `time_bucket`. No DM byte carries seconds or milliseconds. T13 becomes executable in Phase 9 (§14.2): `dm_content.txt` vectors, a property test on stored `author_minute`/`sort_minute`, the pickle structure test (§15.2), and mutant ME-5.
- **R-C4 (limits and strictness).** `|L0| ≤ 65 536 − O_MAX`; `ext_len ≤ 2 560` (raised from 1 024 for the PQ-C TLVs, §9.4; senders never exceed `EXT_RESERVE` with classical TLVs, §21.11); an over-read, a trailing byte, a non-ascending TLV list, an unknown critical TLV, invalid UTF-8 or a non-zero reserved bit refuses the whole L0. Vectors `protocol/test-vectors/dm_content.txt`; seeded decoder property tests (10⁶ inputs per CI run: never throws untyped, never over-reads).
- **R-C5 (slack rule for PQ TLVs; T29; new, §22).** A PQ TLV (0x0005, 0x0006) rides only on a user-initiated send, and only if `C(B) − (52 + |body| + ext_len of the classical TLVs) ≥ |PQ TLVs|` for the bucket B already chosen from the body length (R-B1). The bucket never depends on a PQ TLV (mutant ME-23). With `O_MAX = 1 664` (`C(4096) = 2 432`) and no classical TLV pending, a 4 KiB message carries `pq_key` (1 232 B with its TLV header) when its body is ≤ 1 148 B, and `seal_root_pq` (1 170 B) when its body is ≤ 1 210 B; the two together never fit a 4 KiB message and ride on separate sends there. A message without room defers its PQ TLV to a later user send; nothing is ever sent for a TLV alone (T28).

### 7.2 Transaction inventory (normative; G-1)

Every DM state change is exactly one `SyncDatabase.transaction` run by the DM actor (§13.7). Nothing outside this table writes DM state. §21.7, §21.8 and §21.12 add `EST-READY`, `OVERSIZE` and the orphan-namespace step; §21.9 restricts which triggers may run which transaction.

| Tx | Trigger | Writes (all through `tx.sql`) | After commit |
|---|---|---|---|
| **SEND** | user: `send`, `react`, `setTimer`; scheduled CONTROL due (reconnect, revocation notice) | `olm_session.state_env` of `current` (the ratchet step), `dm_outbound_item` (every kind, §21.8), `dm_message` (out; TEXT and TIMER_SET only), `dm_outbound_op` (+ retained blob), `Outbox.enqueue`, the PQ-C attachment state (`dm_contact.pq_announced_week`, `dm_pair_offer.offer_env`); or `dm_pending_send` plus `EST-START` effects when no usable session exists | `requestExpedite()` in STANDARD |
| **RESEND** | automatic for INDETERMINATE (identical bytes, §7.5; only while the contact is `unverified` or `verified`); user `retry` for FAILED (re-encrypt the same L0, re-running SEND's S1, S4, S5–S6 against `now` and S7, §22.8.5) | new op; the same blob, or a new ratchet step over the same L0 (with the kept out key of the author week) | as SEND |
| **RECV** | `SyncChange.INBOX`, consumer tail (one peek-ordered blob per transaction, §7.7) | `markConsumed`; `olm_session` (the used slot's state, R-SS1, R-SS2) and `olm_prekey_used`, or a held row; `dm_peer_pq`; `dm_seen`; message, reaction, contact, request, pair-key and `prov_erase_minute` effects | notification count |
| **BUNDLE** | `Inbox.claim(PREKEYS)` during establishment | `markConsumed`; the best verified candidate kept in `dm_bundle_candidate`; key-change classification (§21.7) | — |
| **EST-READY** | time-triggered: the first candidate + 2 min, or the attempt window's end with a usable session (§21.7) | pin; `dm_peer_pq`; `sessionOutbound` (a new `current` session, R-SS0); encrypt, intro-seal and enqueue pending items; `establishment = ready`; `setListening(P_c, false)` | `requestExpedite()` in STANDARD |
| **EST-START** | first send without a usable session, `resetSession`, reconnect due, a stale initiator session or `NO_SEAL_KEY` at a user send | `Inbox.forgetConsumed(P_c)`, `Namespaces.register(P_c, …, LOCAL)`, read credentials per relay slot (§21.2), `establishment = fetching`, `fetch_attempts`, `fetch_listen_until_minute` (§21.7; round 9's `fetch_until_day` is replaced) | — |
| **EST-END** | time-triggered: an attempt window ends without a candidate or a usable session (§21.7) | `setListening(P_c, false)`; the next attempt (≤ 3) or `establishment = unreachable` and pending items failed; `Namespaces.remove(P_c)` once no fetched row remains | — |
| **OUTCOME** | `SyncChange.OUTCOMES`, consumer tail | `Outbox.release`, message state, retained blob dropped, RESEND decision | — |
| **KEYDEC** | user: `acknowledgeKeyChange`, `verifyKeyChange`, `block` of a changed contact | trust state, pinned and pending identity, both `olm_session` rows under the old identity deleted, held rows (deleted on block), pair keys and offers | HELD posted |
| **HELD** | after KEYDEC accepted | per held row, in the order of §22.8.1 (grouped by `session_id`, by inner chain index): RECV's R7 dispatch through `DmSessions.decrypt` (a matching slot decrypts; else D2b; else `sessionInbound` with the stored `gen_id` and `prov_env` and one reuse tuple), then the RECV steps R8–R12 with the row's `relay_expiry_hour` and `consume_minute`, then delete the row; drops delete the row and count | notification count |
| **DISPLAY** | user: `markDisplayed(ids)` | `expire_at_minute`, `delete_at_minute`, state `read` | timer and alarm rescheduled |
| **OWNER-WEEK** | the first time-triggered pass at or after `t_pub(w)` (§21.9); activation | a new `olm_prekey_gen` row, `superseded_day` and `delete_after_day` of the previous one, `dm_self.bundle_week`, bundle op | — |
| **ACTIVATE** | `:entitlement` hook: activation (trial, genesis) or restore | `dm_self` (with `state_key_wrapped`), own namespaces, the first bundle generation and bundle (rank per §5.3), `accept_after_minute` on restore | — |
| **KEEPER** | session start, week change, `CAPABILITIES` hint (maintenance only, §21.9) | own READ credential; contacts' deposit and bundle read credentials per relay slot, refreshed before expiry, a refused one re-put at most once per session start (§21.2); `setRelays` on slot changes | — |
| **REGROWS** | session start, week change, `CAPABILITIES` hint, a rejected own READ credential | `relay_key_registration` rows (insert missing, return `done` to pending per the healing rule, delete stale) | — |
| **SWEEP** | in-process timer, alarm, database open, consumer tail, foreground start | expired messages (cascades), held rows, requests, pending sends, `dm_seen`, bundle generations past `delete_after_day` (with their reuse tuples; never while a live held row references them), retired `previous` sessions, `prov_env` past `prov_erase_minute`, surplus `dm_peer_pq` rows, pair keys past their window (opening and recognition keys together), `expiry_clock_minute` | `checkpointTruncate()`, notification count |

Every transaction is idempotent against re-execution after a crash: guards on expected state (OWNER-WEEK per week, ACTIVATE per identity, KEEPER puts only a differing token, REGROWS inserts only missing rows, SWEEP deletes by time).

### 7.3 `SEND` (normative; session calls re-sourced by §22)

```
send(contact c, content):                                    [DM actor; one SyncDatabase.transaction]
tx {
  S1  guard: c.trust ∈ {unverified, verified}; else KEY_CHANGED | BLOCKED | REVOKED | REQUEST_NOT_ACCEPTED
      (before any native session call; sessions exist only for the pinned identity, §8.2); DM context closed → CRYPTO_UNAVAILABLE
  S2  conv = dm_conversation(c); timer = the conversation timer (the new timer for TIMER_SET)
      L0 = encode(message_id = random16, author_minute = floor_minute(now), minute_index (§7.7), timer, TLVs per R-C3, R-C5)
      |body| > MAX_BODY_BYTES → TOO_LARGE;  B = bucketFor(L_class = 52 + |body| + EXT_RESERVE)            (R-B1, §21.11)
  S3  insert dm_message(out, 'queued', body_env = AEAD(content_key, flags ‖ quote_ref? ‖ body, AD = message_id),   (TEXT, TIMER_SET; §21.8)
                        expire_at_minute = timer ≠ off ? ceil_minute(commit_now + timer) : NULL)            (§10.3)
      insert dm_outbound_item(message_id, c, kind, l0_env, deadline_hour, 'pending')                 (every kind; §21.8)
  S4  if c has no current session, or current is a stale initiator session (§8.7):
        insert dm_pending_send(item_id, c, fail_after_hour); EST-START effects (§5.5, §21.7); return QUEUED
  S5  (l1, isPreKey, state') = DmSessions.encrypt(tx, c, L0)             (encrypt over current.state_env; state' written in tx)
  S6  mode = §6.8 (PreKey → intro; normal with an out key → pair; normal with prov_env → provisional; else NO_SEAL_KEY)
      blob = sealIntro | sealSym(mode, X_c, B, l1)                                   (fit asserted, else NO_FIT → rollback)
  S7  ensure Namespaces.register(X_c, DM, set(X_c, week), listen = false, LOCAL) and a deposit credential (KEEPER rule)
  S8  op = random16; insert dm_outbound_op(op, purpose = item, item_id, blob, deadline_hour); item state 'enqueued'   (§21.8)
      Outbox.enqueue(OutboundBlob(op, X_c, blob, ttl = ttlFor(conv, kind) (§10.1 R-T2, R-T3),
                                  deadlineEpochSeconds = author + min(bucket, 7 d)))                (§10.1)
}
after commit: SyncController.requestExpedite()                       (STANDARD only; a no-op in HIGH; no read event, T19)
```

| Result or exception inside SEND | Effect (the whole transaction rolls back first) | Facade result |
|---|---|---|
| `InsufficientReplicasException` | nothing sent, no ratchet step | `NO_REPLICAS` |
| `Untrusted` from the facade (a session call with an identity other than the pinned one) | nothing (a defence behind S1; unreachable while S1 holds) | `KEY_CHANGED` |
| `NoSession`, or `NO_SEAL_KEY` (§6.8) | a separate `EST-START` transaction keeps the message as a pending send; caused by the user's send, not by inbound traffic | `QUEUED` (re-establishing) |
| `Oversize` (a session pickle above `SESSION_STATE_MAX`, §7.9) | a bug or a format change at a bump: a separate transaction deletes the session and keeps the item pending (§21.5 handling kept as defence) | `QUEUED` |
| `StorageFullException` (the `:storage` executors' typed mapping of `SQLITE_FULL`) | nothing | `STORAGE_FULL` |
| R-B1 fit failure (`NO_FIT`) or `INTERNAL` (a caught Rust panic) | a bug: thrown as `IllegalStateException` | propagates (never caught) |
| anything else | propagates (`sync-no-catch-all.sh`) | crash |

**Why SEND is safe (CS-1).** The session state is passed into the native call as a stored envelope and comes back as a new envelope that Kotlin writes through `tx.sql` in the same transaction; no native session object survives the call (§7.9). The ciphertext leaves the device only through the outbox, which sees it only after commit. A crash before commit leaves no ratchet step, row or op; after commit, an op the engine sends. A message key is used only inside a transaction that either commits the op or leaves no trace.

### 7.4 `RECV` (normative; one transaction per blob; R4–R7 re-sourced by §22)

The actor peeks at fetched DM blobs (`Inbox.peek(Consumer.DM, 256)`, read-only) and orders them (§7.7); then, per blob in that order, `Inbox.claimOne(Consumer.DM, ns, hash)` runs its own short transaction (the offer rules of `claim` apply), followed by:

```
tx {
  R1  if !Inbox.markConsumed(ns, hash): continue                                            (exactly once)
  R2  if blob.offers ≥ 4: counter poison; return        (three earlier offers died unconsumed: consumed unprocessed, no crash loop; G-1)
  R3  ns must be an own inbox (dm_own_namespace) else drop(foreign_ns)
  R4  open per §6.5 steps 1–3 (recognition hint → at most one AEAD; canonical parse) else drop(counter)
      intro: generation gen_id present else drop(unknown_generation); openIntro (KEM, inner layer, card v2, binding v3 signature,
             PreKey header consistency) else drop(non_canonical | bad_binding)
      pair or provisional: a PreKey L1 → drop(non_canonical); pair mode: week(author_minute) must later equal the key's week (R9)
  R5  identify: pair or provisional → the contact of the matched key;
      intro → card v2 + binding v3 freshness (now − FRESH ≤ binding_minute ≤ now + 2σ), before any state change (§21.4);
              contact = dm_contact by identity_pk:
        unknown → request path (§12.3): insert dm_contact('request', card, request_until_day) if < 64 requests, else drop(request_overflow)
        blocked or revoked → drop(blocked)
  R6  identity pre-check (§8.3), before any session call, for an intro PreKey L1 (OI′ = PreKey.identity_key = binding.OI_A):
        pinned NULL          → binding valid → pin OI′                                   (first contact from their side)
        OI′ = pinned         → continue
        OI′ ≠ pinned, trust = 'request'                      → drop(request_key_mismatch); no state change   (§21.4)
        OI′ ≠ pinned, binding valid under pinned identity_pk → HOLD: dm_held(identity = OI′, l1_env, gen_id, prov_env, …)
                                                               + trust 'changed', pending = OI′; return
        OI′ ≠ pinned, binding invalid                       → drop(bad_binding); no state change            (E-1)
      trust = 'changed': a PreKey L1 under pending → hold; a normal message continues under the pinned sessions
  R7  content = DmSessions.decrypt(tx, c, l1) (§8.1, §8.2), typed results only:
        a normal message or a PreKey message whose session_id matches a slot → decrypt on that slot (R-SS2)
        a PreKey message with a new session_id: (gen_id, session_id) ∈ olm_prekey_used → drop(replay)          (D2b)
                                                 else sessionInbound(generation, pinned OI, l1) → new current (R-SS1),
                                                 insert olm_prekey_used, renewal effects (§21.3 point 2)
        Duplicate (DUPLICATE_OR_LOST) → drop(duplicate)
        UnknownGeneration (MISSING_KEY; R4 already refused absent generations) → drop(unknown_generation)
        Undecryptable (GAP_TOO_BIG, UNDECRYPTABLE, INVALID_KEY) → drop(undecryptable)
        Untrusted (IDENTITY_MISMATCH; unreachable after R6) → drop(untrusted); never a hold             (E-1)
        Oversize → rollback, then the OVERSIZE transaction (§21.5, kept as defence)
  R8  L0 = decode(content), canonical, else drop(bad_content)             (the new session state commits anyway: the key was used)
  R9  freshness (§7.6): author_minute ≥ now − FRESH(bucket(timer_code)) (§21.10); author_minute ≤ now + 2σ; author_minute ≥ dm_self.accept_after_minute;
      pair mode: week(author_minute) = the opening key's week                                          else drop(stale)
  R10 tag = HMAC-SHA256(seen_key, contact_id ‖ message_id)[0..16];  INSERT OR IGNORE dm_seen(tag, until_day)
      an existing row → drop(duplicate)
  R11 apply (§7.4.1) and TLVs: seal_root or seal_root_pq → out key (§6.3, §9.4; an unopenable seal_root_pq is ignored and counted, §22.8.6);
      pq_key → dm_peer_pq (newer week only, ≤ 3 rows, the whole key validated, else ignored and counted, §22.8.2);
      revocation (§8.5); session_reset (§8.6)
  R12 pair mode: a pair envelope under my offered root_gen → that generation is acknowledged (§6.3), prov_erase_minute set (§6.7)
}
after commit: NotificationPort.onUnreadChanged(count)                        (a constant text, no sender, §10.7)
```

- **R-P1 (commit together).** `markConsumed`, `olm_session` and `olm_prekey_used` writes, `dm_peer_pq`, `dm_seen`, message rows, pair-key and contact changes commit in one transaction (spec §7.2(b)). A crash anywhere re-offers the blob against rolled-back state, so a second attempt decrypts the same way (the stored session envelope is unchanged, §7.9).
- **R-P2 (drop is final and silent).** A `drop` is `markConsumed` plus one in-memory counter increment (`MessagingStatus`, counts only, no persisted rejection history, T33) and nothing else: no outbound blob, redeem, registration, read-schedule change or `Inbox.defer` (T28). The only kept data are a hold (§8.3) and a request (§12.3), both bounded.
- **R-P2b (a request row lives only with its message).** A request row inserted at R5 is deleted again in the same transaction when a later step drops the blob (revised §22: round 9's exception for `NoSession`/`InvalidKeyId`, the seed-openable restore healing path, is withdrawn, §8.6). A forged, replayed or undecryptable intro envelope therefore leaves no contact behind; §21.4 point 3 keeps a request only under a fresh, envelope-bound binding and an applied message.
- **R-P3 (bounded work).** One transaction handles one blob; hint work is bounded by R-S5; peeks read at most 256 rows and claims are one blob at a time (§7.7).
- **T28 (static half).** No branch of RECV, BUNDLE or HELD calls `Outbox.enqueue`, `Registrations.request`, `Capabilities.put` or `Namespaces.register` (pending sends are flushed by the time-triggered `EST-READY`, §21.7); `NoReflexTest` walks the call graph (gate G-DM2), and twin NI-DM1 checks the behaviour.

#### 7.4.1 Applying content

| Kind | Effect |
|---|---|
| TEXT | insert `dm_message(in, 'unread', sort_minute = min(author_minute, floor_minute(now)), unread_deadline_minute = timer ≠ off ? ceil_minute(min(relayExpiryHour, consume + bucket(timer) + 1 h)) : NULL)` (§10.3) |
| TIMER_SET | the timer-version rule (§10.5); an informational row `kind = TIMER_SET` with no body, subject to its own timer (ADR-27 C.8: shown to both sides) |
| REACTION | if the target exists and is not expired: insert or update `dm_reaction(target_id, from_self = 0)` (empty grapheme = delete); otherwise ignore |
| CONTROL | TLVs only; no row |
| any | `timer_version > conversation.timer_version` → adopt (§10.5) |

### 7.5 `OUTCOME` and `RESEND` (normative; G-7, C-15)

```
for o in Outbox.outcomes(DM, 64) + Outbox.outcomes(PREKEYS, 16): tx {
  if !Outbox.release(o.operationId): continue                                               (once)
  op = dm_outbound_op(o.operationId) ?: continue                        (the message expired and was swept meanwhile)
  when (o.outcome) {
    SENT, DEGRADED → message state sent | degraded; op.blob = NULL
    INDETERMINATE  → if op.resend_count < 2 AND o.resendNotAfterEpochSeconds ≠ null AND now < resendNotAfter AND now < op.deadline_hour
                         RESEND: new op id, the identical retained blob, resend_count + 1   (Phase 7 accepts identical bytes after release)
                     else message state indeterminate; op.blob = NULL
    FAILED         → bundle op: re-seal at the next time-triggered OWNER-WEEK pass while the week's validity lasts (§21.8)
                     message op: state failed; op.blob = NULL
  }
}
```

| Outcome (F7 contract) | Messaging rule | Why |
|---|---|---|
| SENT | `sent`; blob dropped | a quorum of operators verified it |
| DEGRADED | `degraded`; blob dropped; **never resent** | a copy exists; another would only be a duplicate |
| INDETERMINATE | automatic resend of the **identical blob** as a new op, at most twice, only while `now < resendNotAfter` and before the op's deadline; then `indeterminate` ("may not have been delivered") | the F7 contract (F7:1414-1416); identical bytes dedup at D1; caused by the user's own send, not by inbound traffic (T28) |
| FAILED | `failed`; the user may `retry`, which **re-encrypts the same L0** (same `message_id`, author minute, minute index, timer fields) under the next ratchet step as a new op, while `now` is before the original deadline (else `DEADLINE_PASSED`); the retry re-runs SEND's guards and seals with the out key kept for the author week (§22.8.5) | no copy ever existed, so re-encrypting is safe; D3 makes a wrongly decided FAILED harmless |

- **Retained blob.** Phase 7 wipes an op's payload when the outcome is decided (rule W, F7 §3.5), so the identical resend needs messaging's copy in `dm_outbound_op.blob`. It is E2E ciphertext no key on the sender's device opens; it is dropped at the terminal outcome and cascades with the message row at expiry (T25; L7-D7).
- **Short TTLs.** For the 1 h and 1 d buckets `resendNotAfter` is already past (§10.4), so an INDETERMINATE ephemeral message ends `indeterminate` at once; stated and correct.
- An `indeterminate` message is terminal: it has no user retry in Phase 9 (the F7 contract allows only identical bytes, which the window no longer admits). Phase 13 offers "send again" as a new message.

### 7.6 Dedup and replay: four layers, two structural bindings, freshness (normative; C-9, SC-3)

| Layer | Key | Catches | Lifetime |
|---|---|---|---|
| D0 outbox | `operation_id` (local) | repeated enqueue (`AlreadyEnqueued`) | Phase 7 |
| D1 blob | `(namespace, blob_hash)` | re-listing, several relays, identical resend | Phase 7 tombstone `ceil7(day(E) + 24)` |
| D2a Olm message keys | chain keys advance; used and evicted skipped keys are gone (`vz:src/olm/session/receiver_chain.rs:186-254`) | a second decryption of a normal message or of a known-session PreKey message → `DUPLICATE_OR_LOST` | the session (≤ 2 per contact; `previous` retired after ≤ 46 d) |
| D2b first-flight reuse set | `olm_prekey_used(gen_id, session_id)`, inserted in the RECV transaction that created the session | a replayed first flight after its session was replaced or deleted (vodozemac would create a second session, because fallback keys are not consumed, `vz:src/olm/account/mod.rs:324`) | until generation g is erased (cascade) |
| D3 content | `HMAC-SHA256(seen_key, contact_id ‖ message_id)[0..16]` | the same logical message under different bytes: re-encryption after a wrong FAILED, replay after session loss or reset, resurrection after D1 expired | `until_day = ceil7(day(author_minute) + ceil_days(FRESH(bucket)) + 1)` (§21.10) |
| B1 binding | AD binds namespace and bucket (R-S3); pair mode: `week(author_minute)` = the key's week (R9) | a blob copied into another namespace; an old L0 re-sealed under a later week key | structural |
| B2 erasure | pair week keys erased at `start(w + 1) + FRESH` (§6.3); bundle generations at `superseded + 91 d` (§5.4) | a pair-mode replay beyond its key window cannot be opened; an intro replay of a live generation opens but is refused at R5, before any state change, once older than FRESH (§21.4); an intro replay of an erased generation is `unknown_generation` | structural |

When the two-slot trial of `decrypt` returns a missing message key on one slot and a MAC or key failure on the other, the result is `DUPLICATE_OR_LOST` (a replay must not be counted as a forgery). RP4 (first-flight replay after a reset), RP5 (across a restart) and RP11 (a replayed PreKey after R-SS1 replaced its session) test D2b with every other layer disabled in turn (mutant MC-7′).

**FRESH = 45 days (normative derivation).** Let σ = 3 d, the Phase 7 per-device clock tolerance.
1. A DM op's deadline is `author + min(bucket, 7 d)` (§10.1), and Phase 7 stores it as `deadline_hour = max(ceil_hour(deadline), floor_hour(not_before) + 1 h)` (`OutboxStore.kt:179-182`) with `not_before ≤ compose + 10 min`: every store happens before `author + 7 d + 1 h`.
2. An honest relay expires the blob at `ceil_hour(store + TTL) ≤ author + 7 d + 1 h + 30 d + 1 h`; a fetch happens before expiry.
3. The sender's `author_minute` and the recipient's `now` each err by at most σ.

So a legitimate blob is accepted by `now − author_minute ≤ 37 d 2 h + 6 d = 43 d 2 h < 45 d`, and FRESH = 45 d refuses nothing legitimate. `author_minute ≤ now + 2σ` bounds the future side. A D3 tag lives while a replay could still pass freshness (`until_day` covers `author + 45 d` plus a day of rounding). A sender whose clock runs more than 45 days slow loses its messages (refused as stale; counter `stale`; Phase 13 may show "the contact's clock looks wrong"). Declared.

**The four replay cases Phase 7 leaves to the protocol layer (F7 §0.2):**
1. A relay lies about expiry or re-serves an expired blob: D1 while the tombstone lives, then B2, D2, D3, freshness; each layer is proven sufficient on its own with the others disabled (§15.5 RP2).
2. A consumer resends the same bytes as a new op long after: only our own INDETERMINATE resend does this, bounded by `resendNotAfter`; the recipient catches it with D1 or D3.
3. A namespace removed and re-added: D3 tags are keyed by contact and `message_id`, not by namespace; D2 as well.
4. A relay retired and re-added with old blobs: as case 1.

**Restore.** `dm_self.accept_after_minute` = the restore minute: content authored before the restore is refused (R9), since `dm_seen` and pair keys are gone (RE §7).

### 7.7 Ordering (normative; CS-4)

- **R-O1.** A conversation is ordered by `(sort_minute, minute_index, message_id)` (bytes ascending), a pure function of stored content: outgoing `sort_minute = author_minute`; incoming `sort_minute = min(author_minute, receive_minute)`, which stops a peer with a future clock from pinning its messages below all later ones (`receive_minute` is used inside RECV and never stored). There is no arrival-order column and no AUTOINCREMENT (RE §6.3).
- **`minute_index`** = `1 + max(minute_index)` over the sender's own live outgoing rows of the conversation with the same `author_minute`, 0 if none. No counter is persisted (T33). An expired row can make an index repeat; `message_id` breaks the tie.
- **Reorder tolerance (replaced by §22).** Olm's limits are not configurable (the audit's suggestion 8 was declined): at most **40 skipped message keys per receiver chain** with FIFO eviction, **5 receiver chains per session** (oldest evicted), a forward gap of at most **2 000** (`vz:src/olm/session/receiver_chain.rs:31,50,62-68`; `vz:src/olm/session/mod.rs:55,105-121`); libsignal kept 2 000 per chain. GHOST reorders where HIGH mode delays each store by U[0, 10 min] independently (D17), where relays are listed on independent schedules, and where a refusing or withholding relay makes one copy late. Mitigations (normative):
  1. **Peek-ordered consumption.** The actor reads up to `SORT_WINDOW = 256` fetched DM blobs with the read-only `Inbox.peek(Consumer.DM, 256)` (no offer counter change), opens each seal in memory (no state change; intro envelopes are decapsulated, and every decapsulation error is `NOT_OPEN`, never a crash, since a peek commits no offer counter, §22.8.2; a peek never writes), groups by (contact, session match or Olm ratchet key), orders each group by chain index, and then processes each blob in that order in its own RECV transaction after `Inbox.claimOne(Consumer.DM, ns, hash)`, which commits the offer counter like `claim`. The seal is opened again inside the transaction.
  2. **Bounded gap hold (enabled; rewritten by S0′, V-7, 2026-09-19; Q54's default "yes, enabled" confirmed by measurement).** A peeked blob of a session S under ratchet key R with chain index i is skipped (left `fetched`) on **every** peek pass for which i > n and some index in `[n, i)` is not in the peek set of that pass, where **n is the next chain index of S's receiver chain for R, read from S's stored state** by `chainPosition` (§2.6 function 19; 0 when S has no chain for R, reported as `NEW_CHAIN`). The hold ends at `first_seen + HOLD_MAX (12 min)`: from that moment the blob is consumed whatever the gap. The read writes nothing and sends nothing (T28), delays display by at most 12 minutes (measured worst case 10.6 min in HIGH, about 2 min elsewhere), and held blobs count against `FETCHED_CAP`. **Withdrawn:** round 9's in-memory "highest decrypted chain index per process", its "at most once per blob per process" bound and the sentence "After a restart no blob is held until one message of its chain has decrypted" — an in-memory index loses 0.5 % of HIGH bursts when every pass runs in a fresh process (57 losses in 20 of 200 worlds), while the stored session already holds the exact expected index and makes the hold restart-proof. A blob is counted once in the `held` metric however many passes held it; that is a statement about the counter, not about the policy (V-7 measured 265 132 hold passes over 9 557 held blobs in the HIGH online worlds). The read costs a dependency on vodozemac's private serde layout, the recorded deviation of §2.1.
  3. **Declared bound (CS-3; L7-D11; corrected by S0′, V-7, 2026-09-19).** Round 9's bound ("at most 40 later messages … decrypted before it") does not describe Olm: a single message 41 indices ahead loses the earlier one, and keys skipped in several steps are evicted after 40 without 40 later messages. The rule is: a message with chain index i on receiver chain C of session S is lost if, before it is processed, (a) a message of C with index greater than `i + 40` was decrypted while i had not been, (b) more than 40 keys of C were skipped after i's key was stored, (c) 5 newer receiver chains of S were created, or (d) S was deleted or retired (§8.2). (Olm's rule, `vz:src/olm/session/receiver_chain.rs:186-254`, `vz:src/olm/session/mod.rs:55`; a model of it was checked against vodozemac in every one of 72 × 200 seeded worlds, V-7.) **Measured (V-7):** with claim order 2 303 losses per 12 000 messages for an offline STANDARD recipient and 157 for an online HIGH one; with peek ordering alone 1 470 and 75; with the hold of point 2, **0 in every configuration**.
  4. **Skipped-key age bound (adopted; S0′, V-15, 2026-09-19; Q67's "prune" branch applies, RG-6).** Every call that stores a session state also stores, inside the same wrapped envelope, a first-seen sidecar `u8(chains) ‖ per chain: ratchet_key(32) ‖ u8(n) ‖ n × (u64_be chain_index ‖ u32_be first_seen_day)`. The sidecar is **rebuilt at every store as a projection of the stored pickle**: it holds exactly the `(ratchet_key, chain_index)` pairs the pickle holds, each keeping the day already recorded for it, a pair not recorded before taking the call's day. Nothing else adds or removes entries, so the keys and chains vodozemac evicts on its own (its 40-key FIFO, its 5-chain limit) leave the sidecar with them and its size is `≤ 1 + 5 × (33 + 40 × 12) = 2 566 B` **by construction** — an insert-only "log of additions" has no bound at all and was measured to outgrow the pickle. Worst pickle plus sidecar = 36 685 + 2 566 = **39 251 B**, inside `SESSION_STATE_MAX` with 26 285 B to spare. SWEEP calls `sessionPrune` (§2.6 function 20) on every live session; Rust removes from the JSON pickle (`receiving_chains.inner[].skipped_message_keys.inner[]`) every key with `today ≥ first_seen_day + FRESH + 2σ` (**51 d**), keeping the order of the others, and returns the reconciled sidecar with the new state. `decrypt`, `sessionInbound` and `encrypt` gain the `today` input. The P-7/T34 skipped-key exception is **withdrawn** and mutant MC-30 joins the gate (§15.7). RO6 asserts the projection property: after every call the sidecar's key set equals the pickle's, including across an eviction-driving run. The pickle edit is the recorded deviation of §2.1: G-R7 pins its output per pin and an unreadable chain or entry is `STATE_CORRUPT`.
- Within one minute the two directions interleave by index and id, not by causality (T13 makes the minute the clock resolution). There is no persisted **gap counter**: the hold derives its expected index from the stored session state at every pass (**corrected by S0′, V-7, 2026-09-19**; round 9's "the gap hold lives in memory only, because a persisted sequence would outlive the messages" is withdrawn — the session state is already bounded, erased with the session, and holds no time value, T33).

### 7.8 Crash points, poison and fault suites (normative; G-1)

| Crash point | State after restart | Behaviour |
|---|---|---|
| inside SEND | nothing: no ratchet step, row or op | not sent; the draft lived in memory (Phase 13) |
| after SEND, before `requestExpedite` | op pending | the next foreground or job session stores it |
| relay stored, receipt lost | Phase 7 ambiguity (`copy_hour`) | identical bytes within the Phase 7 window, or RESEND; the recipient dedups at D1 |
| inside RECV, before or after the session call | rolled back, session state included (the stored envelope is unchanged, §7.9) | the blob is re-offered and decrypted again with the same result |
| between `Inbox.peek` and `Inbox.claimOne` | nothing (peek is read-only) | the next pass peeks again |
| **native crash** (SIGSEGV) or a Rust panic (caught as `INTERNAL`, thrown as `IllegalStateException`) inside `libghost_client_net.so` during RECV | the process died or the transaction rolled back; the Phase 7 offer counter was committed by `claimOne` before the hand-off | re-offered alone; at the fourth offer (`offers ≥ 4`, three process deaths on it) RECV consumes it unprocessed (R2): no crash loop, and a double crash during recovery still processes the blob at its third offer; `CONSUMER_POISONED` in status. A crash after `sessionInbound` rolls back the session row and the reuse tuple together, so D2b stays consistent with the session set |
| after RECV, before the notification update | row committed | `onUnreadChanged` runs at every database open |
| inside OUTCOME, KEYDEC, HELD, DISPLAY, OWNER-WEEK, ACTIVATE, EST-START, EST-END, BUNDLE, KEEPER, REGROWS | atomic | redone at the next wake (ACTIVATE by the resumable check at actor start and database open, §21.12); each is idempotent (§7.2) |
| after SWEEP, before the checkpoint | rows gone, frames still in the WAL | **R-K1: `wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE)` runs at every database open**, after migration and before any read by UI or notification code |
| during the checkpoint | SQLite WAL recovery | R-K1 at the next open |
| process death with timers pending | none in memory | timers rebuilt from `idx_dm_message_delete` and the alarm; the open-time sweep runs first |
| during migration 4 or 5 | the previous version (one transaction each) | migration runs again at the next open (CS-9) |
| disk full (`SQLITE_FULL`) at any statement | the transaction rolled back | SEND → `STORAGE_FULL`; RECV → `Inbox.defer` resets the offer counter and the blob is re-offered later (§21.12); SWEEP → retried; other steps → retried at the next wake; suite SCN-J |
| power loss | `synchronous = FULL` (`Schema.kt:728-739`) | committed transactions survive; torn writes are SQLite's responsibility (not modelled, F7 §8.1) |

### 7.9 Value state and atomicity (normative; replaces the libsignal store rules, §22)

**Forward note (S4b, 2026-09-20).** §22.9 item 7 records what point 4's bound covers: pickle, sidecar and framing together.

1. **Value semantics.** Every `DmNative` call receives stored envelopes and returns new envelopes; nothing is cached across calls or transactions (MC-2 now means "a Kotlin or Rust cache of a session across transactions"). Kotlin writes returned state only inside the calling transaction, and only on `OK`.
2. **vodozemac is not all-or-nothing in memory, and GHOST does not rely on it being so.** For a message with an unknown ratchet key, `Session::decrypt` advances the sending ratchet in place: an *inactive* sending ratchet is activated with a fresh random ratchet key before the decryption can fail (`vz:src/olm/session/double_ratchet.rs:168-191`, called from `vz:src/olm/session/mod.rs:444-458`). The claim that decryption "commits only after success" holds for receiver chains, not for this activation (RX-1, §22.3). Because GHOST unpickles a fresh object per call and discards it on any non-`OK` result, stored state never contains the activation of a failed attempt. `FailedDecryptLeavesStateTest`: after a failed decrypt against an inactive ratchet the stored envelope is byte-identical and the next valid message decrypts (V-10; mutant MC-29). **Confirmed by S0′ (V-10, 2026-09-19):** the activation reproduces on 0.11.0 and 0.10.0, for **inactive ratchets only** — an active ratchet and an all-zero (non-contributory) ratchet key leave the object unchanged (`activate()` returns `None` first). Persisting the activated object does **not** stop the peer from decrypting later replies; it is forbidden because it lets a forged, relay-injectable blob change stored state (T28, the byte-identical crash runs of §15.3), which MC-29 detects. The consequence is narrower than "the session diverges": it is local state drift, not message loss.
3. **Thread.** The DM actor is the only thread that calls `DmNative` session functions (§13.7); the context handle is not shared with other threads.
