4. **Oversize.** `SESSION_STATE_MAX = 65 536` bytes of pickle, **frozen by S0′ (V-4, 2026-09-19)**: the largest JSON pickle of 0.11.0 and of 0.10.0 is **36 685 B** (5 receiver chains × 40 skipped keys, an active sending ratchet with a parent key, every 32-byte array widened to `255` and every counter to `u64::MAX`, so every byte and counter is at its longest decimal spelling), equal to the analytic bound to the byte on both pins, with a margin of 28 851 B (44.0 %). The widened pickle loads with `Session::from_pickle` and re-serialises identically, so it is a reachable encoding; the *driven* pickle length is data-dependent (about 29.7–29.9 kB over four runs) and is not a bound. The wrapped envelope `nonce(12) ‖ ciphertext ‖ tag(16)` is then at most 36 713 B, inside the `olm_session.state_env` CHECK (29 … 65 564); with the first-seen sidecar of §7.7 point 4 the worst state is 39 251 B. A peer cannot exceed it (fixed-capacity arrays, `u64` counters). `OVERSIZE` therefore signals a bug or a format change at a bump. The §21.5 handling (roll back, delete the session, `reset_needed`) stays as defence; RO6 is "the measured worst case fits with margin" plus a fault-injected oversize (mutant MC-25).
5. **Bounded local state (CS-8).** Per contact: ≤ 2 sessions (each ≤ 65 536 B of pickle, **measured worst case 36 685 B plus a ≤ 2 566 B sidecar = 39 251 B**, V-4 and V-15), ≤ 3 peer X-Wing keys, ≤ 22 recognition keys, ≤ 256 held rows. Per device: ≤ 14 bundle generations with their reuse tuples (one per inbound session created from the generation), ≤ 64 requests, registrations as in §4.8. Skipped keys: ≤ 40 × 5 per session; **age-bounded at `FRESH + 2σ` = 51 d inside a live session too (Q67's prune adopted, §7.7 point 4; corrected by S0′, V-15, 2026-09-19)**; retired with `previous` after 46 days. Worst case at `MAX_CONTACTS = 1 000`: 2 × 39 251 B per contact, about 78.5 MB (a live session is typically about 1 KB).

---

## 8. Sessions, trust, key change, safety numbers, revocation, restore (I5; ADR-33′, ADR-35)

### 8.1 Olm calls and session records (normative; replaces round 9's addresses and records, §22)

Olm receives no address, no contact identifier and no device id: a session is a wrapped pickle in a row keyed by the random local `contact_id` and a slot. Every call below runs inside one SQLCipher transaction on the DM actor.

| Moment | Call | Writes |
|---|---|---|
| establishment (`EST-READY`) | `sessionOutbound(OI_c, F_g)` | `olm_session` `current` (initiator, `acknowledged = 0`, `unack_since_day = today`, `peer_gen_id`, `prov_env`); the previous `current` handled by R-SS0 |
| send | `encrypt(current.state_env, L0)` | `current.state_env` |
| receive, normal message | `decrypt(current?, previous?, L1)` | the used slot's `state_env`; `acknowledged := 1`; `unack_since_day := NULL`; R-SS2 (with the age guards of §22.8.4) |
| receive, PreKey message, known session | `decrypt` with the slot whose `session_id` matches | the used slot's `state_env`; `peer_bind_minute` raised to the envelope's `binding_minute` (§22.8.4); R-SS2 |
| receive, PreKey message, new session | `sessionInbound(generation secrets, expected OI, L1)` | a new row per R-SS1 as amended by §22.8.4 (`peer_bind_minute = binding_minute`), or `drop(stale_session)`; `olm_prekey_used(gen_id, session_id)` when a row is created; renewal effects (§21.3 point 2) when it becomes `current` |
| held replay (`HELD`) | RECV's R7 dispatch on the stored L1 with the stored `gen_id`, in session and chain order (§22.8.1) | as the matching row above |

`sessionInbound` builds a throwaway `Account` holding exactly generation g's fallback secret and calls `create_inbound_session(SessionConfig::version_1(), expected_identity, &prekey)`, which refuses another identity (`vz:src/olm/account/mod.rs:251-262`), recomputes the 3DH and decrypts the first message before returning (`:290-326`). `sessionOutbound` builds a throwaway `Account` from the seed-derived dehydrated pickle and calls `create_outbound_session` with a fresh base key (`:193-219`). Session records, generation secrets and reuse tuples are rows in `olm_*` tables (§13.6), written only through `tx.sql` (spec §8 "dedicated representation").

### 8.2 Trust contract and session selection (normative; replaces round 9's store semantics and R-E4, §22; a CP-09 exception)

**Trust contract.**
1. GHOST pins identities in its own code before any session call (bundle §5.5, `RECV` R6).
2. `sessionOutbound` is called only with the pinned `OI`.
3. `sessionInbound` is called only with the pinned `OI`, or, at first contact, with the `OI` just pinned under a valid binding in the same transaction.
4. No session row is created for a contact in `changed`, `blocked` or `revoked`; in `changed`, new-key PreKey messages are held (§8.3); a `request` accepts only its pinned identity.
5. A session never outlives the identity it was created with: KEYDEC acceptance, block and revocation delete both rows.
6. RECEIVING is allowed for `request` contacts and, for `changed` contacts, on sessions under the old pinned identity (their normal messages were authenticated to it); SENDING only for `unverified` and `verified`.

**Session selection.** At most two rows per contact: `current` (used for every send) and `previous` (receive only, with `retire_day`).

- **R-SS0 (explicit resets).** `resetSession(c)`, `reconnect`, KEYDEC acceptance and block delete both rows before any new session. A re-establishment caused by `UNACK_MAX` or `NO_SEAL_KEY` demotes `current` to `previous` (the peer may still answer it) and deletes the old `previous`.
- **R-SS1 (a new inbound session wins, unless it is older; amended by §22.8.4).** A PreKey message whose `session_id` matches no row, whose `(gen_id, session_id)` is not in the reuse set, and whose `sessionInbound` succeeds becomes `current` (responder, `peer_bind_minute = binding_minute`); the old `current` becomes `previous` with `retire_day = today + 46`; the old `previous` is deleted. **Exception:** if `current` is a responder session with a larger `peer_bind_minute` than the new envelope's `binding_minute` (an older first flight of the same contact, sealed before its restore or reset), the new session becomes `previous` when `previous` is empty or an older responder session, and otherwise the message is `drop(stale_session)` with no session and no reuse tuple. This is a **renewal from c** (§21.3 point 2: out keys for c deleted, a new root offer inserted unless an unacknowledged one exists), an explicit GHOST decision rather than a library side effect.
- **R-SS2 (a message on `previous`; age guards added by §22.8.4).** When a message decrypts on `previous`: first, a PreKey message on a responder `previous` with a smaller `peer_bind_minute` than a responder `current`, and a normal message on an initiator `previous` whose `author_minute` is smaller than a responder `current`'s `peer_bind_minute`, never swap (stale traffic of a session the contact dropped). Otherwise: if `current` is unacknowledged, swap the slots (the peer never answered our newer session, for example because it restored and our session used its lost bundle); else, if both are acknowledged and `previous.session_id < current.session_id` bytewise, swap; otherwise keep. A swap resets the demoted row's `retire_day` to `today + 46`.
- **R-SS3 (convergence).** Simultaneous first contact: A holds `S_A` (initiator) and receives `S_B`, so R-SS1 makes `S_B` current; B symmetrically makes `S_A` current. Each side's normal replies land on the other's `previous`, which becomes acknowledged, and R-SS2's tie-break then puts the smaller session id in `current` on both sides within two round trips. Restore of B: A's unacknowledged `S_A` loses to B's new `S_B` (R-SS1) and stays demoted, because R-SS2 never swaps an acknowledged `current` for an unacknowledged `previous`. Suites RO5 and RO8 enumerate every interleaving of both flows with up to three messages per side; the exhaustive state model EM-1 (§15.2, RG-1) covers loss, duplication, reordering, process death at every step and restore.
- **R-SS4 (retirement).** SWEEP deletes `previous` at `retire_day`. 46 days exceed FRESH, so every legitimate delayed message of the retired session is already refused as stale. A retired session's skipped keys die with it: the P-7 skipped-key exception is **time-bounded for retired sessions** and count-bounded inside a live session (unless Q67's prune is adopted, §7.7) — **and S0′ adopted it (V-15, 2026-09-19), so a live session's skipped keys are now age-bounded at 51 d as well**.
- **R-SS5 (trust).** The trust contract above applies to every selection step.
- **Model obligation.** Session handling can lose FS and PCS that single-session proofs give (Cremers, Jacomme, Naska, USENIX Security 2023). The ProVerif model of §9.5 includes R-SS0 … R-SS4 with two sessions per pair (M-5), and mutant MC-19 ("newest decrypt wins") must fail EM-1, RO5 and RO8.

### 8.3 Key change (normative; FR-4.6, spec §7.2(d), plan gate "schimbare de cheie blochează"; C-14, E-1, E-2; mechanics re-sourced by §22)

**Forward note (S4b, 2026-09-20).** §22.9 item 13: a non-canonical encoding of a pinned key's point is refused at the parser, so it never reaches a pin comparison.

**Definition.** For a contact c with pinned Ed25519 `identity_pk` and pinned Olm identity `OI`:

| Event | Classification |
|---|---|
| An intro PreKey message, or a fetched bundle, carrying `OI′ ≠ OI`, **with** a signature valid under c's pinned `identity_pk` (binding v3, or the bundle signature) | **key change** |
| The same with a signature **not** valid under the pinned `identity_pk`, or with none (a PreKey L1 in a pair or provisional envelope is already dropped, R4) | **forgery**: dropped (`bad_binding` or `non_canonical`), no state change, nothing shown |
| `IDENTITY_MISMATCH` from `sessionInbound` | never a key change on its own: a drop (`untrusted`) |
| A card or intro envelope with a different `identity_pk` | a different identity: an unknown sender becomes a request (§12.3); GHOST never links two Ed25519 identities |
| An intro PreKey message with `OI′ = OI` (the contact restored, reset or reconnected) | not a key change: a new session under R-SS1; a content-free "session renewed" contact event |
| A valid revocation certificate v2 for c | **revoked** (§8.5), stronger than a key change |

Anyone who holds c's card and my card can build an intro envelope that names c's genuine card; without c's Ed25519 secret key it cannot produce a binding for another Olm identity, so it cannot move c to `changed` (E-1). A valid signature for `OI′ ≠ OI` exists only when the holder of c's Ed25519 secret signed another Olm identity: c itself (including a modified client), a derivation-version change, or a seed thief (§3.4); a genuine binding replayed from another envelope fails its namespace, minute, ciphertext or base key (§3.4, §21.4).

**State machine** (`dm_contact.trust_state`):

```
            scan card in person / compare safety number
 unverified ────────────────────────────────────────────► verified
     ▲  │ key change detected                                │ key change detected
     │  ▼                                                     ▼
     │ changed (pending_identity = OI′, held L1 rows) ◄───────┘
     │  │ acknowledgeKeyChange()          │ verifyKeyChange(scanned fingerprint) ok
     └──┘ (pinned := OI′, → unverified)   └────────────► verified (pinned := OI′)
 changed ──block()──► blocked (held rows deleted)
 any except request ──block()──► blocked ──unblock()──► deleted (card, sessions and pair keys were erased at block: add the card again)
 any ──valid revocation──► revoked (terminal; only deleteContact())
 request ──acceptRequest()──► unverified;  request ──rejectRequest() / request_until_day──► deleted;  request ──blockRequest()──► blocked
```

- **Blocking both directions (CS-5).** In `changed`: `send` returns `KEY_CHANGED` before any native session call (SEND S1); inbound intro PreKey messages under `OI′` are **held undecrypted** in `dm_held(held_id, contact_id, identity = OI′, l1_env, gen_id, prov_env, hold_until_minute, relay_expiry_hour, consume_minute)` with `l1_env` the L1 bytes under the content key, `gen_id` and the wrapped `reply_seed` needed to create the session later, and `hold_until_minute = ceil_minute(min(relayExpiryHour, consume + 30 d + 1 h))` (the held timer is unknown until decryption, so the longest DM bucket bounds it). SWEEP does not erase a generation referenced by a live held row (§5.4). Normal messages that still decrypt under the sessions of the old pinned identity are shown: they were authenticated to `OI`. At most `MAX_HELD_PER_CONTACT = 256` held rows (older ones deleted first, counted).
- **A second pending key.** A valid binding for `OI″ ∉ {OI, OI′}` while `changed` replaces `pending_identity` with `OI″` and deletes the held rows of `OI′` (the newest valid binding wins; two live devices with one seed flip-flop, declared R-20).
- **Unblock.** `acknowledgeKeyChange(c)` ("explicit confirmation", FR-4.6) or `verifyKeyChange(c, scanned)` (full verification), in one KEYDEC transaction: `pinned := OI′`, `pending := NULL`, `verified_fp` cleared (set again only by `verifyKeyChange`); both `olm_session` rows under the old identity deleted (R-SS0); the secrets of my offered roots for c (the rows stay for the generation counter, §21.3) and my out keys for c are erased and a new `seal_root` (or `seal_root_pq`) is attached to my next user normal message (a different device may now speak for c); then the held rows are replayed through RECV's R7 dispatch and R8–R12, grouped by session and in chain-index order, in HELD transactions (§22.8.1; local work, no network, no reflex). The same transaction deletes c's in-band and unreferenced bundle `dm_peer_pq` rows and discards unacknowledged PQ offers (§22.8.6).
- **Nothing reaches a relay.** Detection, holding, acknowledgement and verification produce no relay-visible event (T28, T31, NI-DM5). A PreKey message has the same bucket as any message (T29).
- **Timeouts.** A `changed` state never resolves itself. Held rows are deleted at `hold_until_minute`; the state stays `changed` until the user acts (declared, L7-D17).
- **Key-compromise impersonation at first contact (KC13).** An adversary holding B's entire state (Olm identity, every generation secret, `session_state_key`) and one genuine intro envelope from A, fresh within FRESH, that builds a new Olm PreKey message with its own base key under A's old binding and ciphertext is refused (`binding.base_key ≠ PreKey.base_key`); with a new ciphertext it is refused (signature); with a fresh base key and a fresh ciphertext under a binding it cannot sign it is refused. The declared limit is asserted too: re-sealing other content under A's own ciphertext and base key while withholding A's genuine envelope is accepted (the adversary holds that session's state). Mutant PM-34 (binding without base key and ciphertext) makes the first cases pass wrongly.

### 8.4 Safety number v1 (FR-4.6; D12; mechanics replaced by §22; a CP-09 exception with vectors)

```
own_id(party)  = identity_pk(32, Ed25519) || OI(32, Olm identity)
h_0            = SHA-512("ghost/v1/safety-number" || u8(1) || own_id)
h_{k+1}        = SHA-512(h_k || own_id)                        for k = 0 … 5 199            (5 201 SHA-512 evaluations)
digits(party)  = for j in 0 … 5: (u40_be(h_5200[5j .. 5j + 5]) mod 100 000) as 5 decimal digits      (30 digits)
display        = digits(party₁) || digits(party₂), with the lexicographically smaller 30-digit string first (60 digits)
scannable      = u8(1) || h_5200(own)[0..32] || h_5200(peer)[0..32]                        (65 bytes)
```

- **Design goal** (from the published rationale of numeric fingerprints, implemented under ADR-35's clean-room rule without AGPL source): matching one party's 30 digits costs about 10³⁰ ≈ 2⁹⁹·⁷ candidate identities × 5 201 hashes ≈ 2¹¹² SHA-512 evaluations.
- **Comparison** of a scanned code: version byte equal; scanned `own` = my `peer` part and scanned `peer` = my `own` part; otherwise `VERSION_MISMATCH` or `MISMATCH`. `verifyKeyChange` and `markVerified` use this comparison. A manual digit comparison is recorded with `markVerifiedManually(c)`; Phase 13 states what the user asserted.
- `dm_contact.verified_fp = SHA-256(displayable digits)` records what was verified; any change of the pinned key clears it. `pendingSafetyNumber(c)` computes the number with `pending_identity` while `changed`, so the user can verify before acknowledging.
- An in-person QR scan of a card sets `verified` (`source = qr_in_person`): the scanned `identity_pk` authenticates the Olm identity transitively through the bundle signature and binding v3 (§3.4), so no bundle MITM is possible for that contact. A card received as a link starts `unverified` (a substituted link is a MITM).
- Computing, displaying, scanning and comparing cause no network I/O (T31). Phase 13 marks the digits `accessibilityDataSensitive` (T24).
- Vectors `protocol/test-vectors/dm_safety_number.txt` are replayed by Rust and by an independent Kotlin test implementation on the JDK's `MessageDigest`; digits of GHOST safety numbers are never compared with any other messenger's.

### 8.5 Revocation (X-7; C-25; LIMITE L1 "Recuperare")

- **Certificate v2** (102 B): `0x02 ‖ identity_pk(32) ‖ issued_minute(u32 minutes since the epoch) ‖ reason(1) ‖ Ed25519 sig(64) over "ghost/v1/revocation" ‖ previous bytes`, signed and verified in Rust (`ghost-client-dm`, X-21). The Phase 3 v1 (`RevocationCertificate.kt:11-13`, seconds) stays a local artifact and never leaves the device.
- **Issuing** (`issueRevocation(reason)`) is local. **Propagation** is the explicit user action `notifyContactsOfRevocation()`: one CONTROL message per contact with TLV `revocation`, each due at its own `U[0, 24 h]` offset drawn at the action and persisted (`dm_scheduled`, §13.6), because a burst of k deposits in one session would show degree k (RP §3.8). Nothing is published in a namespace that contacts poll (RP F8, T31).
- **Receiving** a valid certificate for c: `trust_state := revoked`; sessions, pair keys, held rows and pending sends deleted in the same transaction; displayed messages follow their timers. A revocation can only block, so a thief holding the seed gains nothing by forging one.
- **The DM equivalent of "a revoked device does not decrypt new epochs"** (LIMITE L1): after revocation no ciphertext is produced toward c and nothing from c is shown (KC7, SCN-K).

### 8.6 Restore from seed and healing (normative; G-6, C-20; replaced by §22; Q50)

| Re-derived | Regenerated | Lost (declared, L7-D12) |
|---|---|---|
| Ed25519 identity; **Olm identity (same safety numbers)**; `card_secret_0`, inbox read secrets, schedule key; hence `X_0`, `P_0` and every credential | bundle generations, `session_state_key`, `seen_key`, content key, pair roots, `reply_seed`s | contacts and their cards, sessions, pair keys, the lost device's generations, `dm_seen`, held rows, messages, scheduled controls |

- **`Messaging.onRestored()`**, called from the Phase 8 restore path next to `Entitlement.restore` (RS K20), runs ACTIVATE: `accept_after_minute` = the restore minute; own `X_0` and `P_0` registered; a new `session_state_key`; the first relay session with a token publishes a fresh bundle (rank per §5.3) and registers keys (a relay-visible restore moment, L7-D10); a restored device has no token until it obtains a trial or a pack (§21.7 point 8). A card index other than 0 cannot exist in Phase 9.
- **Inbound after a restore.**
  - **Pair and provisional envelopes** from established contacts do not open (their keys came from roots and seeds the old device generated): counted `unknown_seal`, shown in `status()` as "unopenable envelopes since restore"; their content is lost.
  - **Intro envelopes** built on the lost device's bundles name generations whose secrets were random and are gone: they do not open either (`unknown_seal` or `unknown_generation`, counted). **A restored device therefore learns nothing about who wrote to the lost device** (round 9 rebuilt pre-restore senders as requests from seed-openable intro envelopes; that path is withdrawn because a seed-derivable first-contact key would let anyone who later seizes the seed attribute every first contact to the owner forever).
  - Intro envelopes built on the restored device's new bundles open normally and create requests or renew sessions.
- **Healing paths.** (a) The restored user re-adds cards (from the other channel or in person) and runs `reconnect(contacts)` (below); (b) a contact resets from its side once the restored device holds a token and has published a new bundle, which outranks the lost device's bundles of the same week by `bundle_rank` (§5.3, §21.7 point 8); (c) the P1 encrypted contact-card backup (Q28) restores automatic reconnect. Until then contacts' messages to the lost device's sessions are lost (declared, L7-D12). The alternative, a seed-derived X-Wing "healing" key in the bundle used only for a second inner layer carrying sender cards, keeps round 9's healing at the cost of seed-attributable first contacts forever; it is recorded in Q50.
- **`reconnect(contacts)`** (user action; Phase 13 shows the unopenable counts, not identities). For each re-added contact: a `dm_scheduled` row due at a `U[0, 48 h]` offset drawn at the action (skipped if the user sends to that contact earlier). When due (a time-triggered step, §21.9): a CONTROL item with `session_reset` waits for `EST-START` and `EST-READY` (§21.8). The contact receives an intro PreKey message from the **same** Olm identity, creates a new session under R-SS1 without a key-change warning, detects the renewal and drops its out keys for me (§21.3); new roots follow in both directions once the sessions are acknowledged, and pair envelopes then open both ways. Spreading avoids a burst of k deposits into k inboxes revealing degree k (RP §3.8).
- **Relay state after a restore.** Registrations are relay state: for up to about three weeks after the old device's last renewal, deposits still succeed and the restored device reads `X_0` with its seed-derived read key without a capability (§4.8, §21.2 point 9). Tokens are not restored (Phase 8 E11).
- **No reflex.** No revocation, reset notice, bundle fetch or registration is triggered by an inbound blob after a restore (T28, T31).

### 8.7 Session reset and staleness (normative; re-sourced by §22)

- `resetSession(c)` (user action) deletes both session rows (R-SS0) and goes through the one-shot bundle fetch (§5.5); the new session is intro mode until c answers.
- **Staleness.** An **initiator session unacknowledged since `unack_since_day` for `UNACK_MAX = 28` days** is stale: the next user send to c runs `EST-START` (one fetch, §21.7) and demotes it (R-SS0). Olm has no such rule; it replaces libsignal's 30-day soft archive. There is no time-triggered re-establishment (T28, T31). Test RS3′; Q64.
- No automatic reset, no decryption-error message, no retry request (D10).
- Two live devices with one seed (unsupported multi-device, or a thief) publish competing bundles for the same week; fetchers take the higher `bundle_rank`, then the smaller blob hash, and sessions may fail on either device (declared, R-20).

---

## 9. Post-quantum plan and residues (I6; ADR-12 amended by ADR-36; rewritten by §22)

Round 9 relied on PQXDH and SPQR as exposed by libsignal; that section is withdrawn. No permissively licensed library offers a mature post-quantum ratchet (SPQR is AGPL-3.0-only; vodozemac has no PQ), so spec §7.5 applies through its second branch: a GHOST protocol design with a model and an external review.

### 9.1 Targets (level vocabulary of Apple's PQ3 note)

| Target | Phase 9 exit | With PQ-C (slice S13) | Not provided |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Level 2**: harvest-now-decrypt-later (HNDL) confidentiality of every relay-stored DM byte (bundles, intro, provisional, pair) while no device state is compromised | **yes** (PQ-A, PQ-B; P-8) | yes | — |
| Seal-layer PQ healing after a device compromise | no | **yes**, at root-replacement granularity (§9.4) | — |
| PQ healing inside the ratchet (Level 3 proper, SPQR-class) | no | no | declared PQ-R2; ML-KEM Braid deferred (PQ-E) |
| Authentication against an active quantum adversary | no | no | PQ-R1 |
| Network metadata (Tor links, relay RPCs) | no | no | PQ-R4; Phase 12 (PQ-N, Q65) |

### 9.2 PQ-A: the X-Wing intro KEM (normative)

- **Primitive.** `libcrux-kem 0.0.9` `Algorithm::XWingKemDraft06` over `libcrux-ml-kem 0.0.10` (hax/F* proofs for the portable code, partial; the same ML-KEM code used by libsignal, OpenSSH and NSS), `libcrux-curve25519 0.0.8` (compiled from verified HACL* C, per its README) and `libcrux-sha3 0.0.10` (pre-verification). Private keys are 32-byte seeds. **Draft versions:** X-Wing draft-06 and the current draft-10 are wire-identical: draft-10's change log (Appendix G.1–G.2) lists since -06 only an ASN.1 module, the SHAKE-256 output length written in bits, implementation lists and a PEM header fix (`https://www.ietf.org/archive/id/draft-connolly-cfrg-xwing-kem-10.txt`, fetched 2026-09-16; RX-11), and `draft-irtf-cfrg-concrete-hybrid-kems-04` calls `MLKEM768-X25519` identical to X-Wing. S0′ runs the published vectors of draft-06 and draft-10 (V-6).
- **Rules.** One suite, no negotiation, no ML-KEM-1024, no pure ML-KEM, no Kyber, no standalone KEM key without a protocol role (spec §7.5; ADR-12 "niciun KEM standalone de bifat"); portable-only build (§2.3, G-R3, Q55); encapsulation randomness from the OS CSPRNG; every public key and the ciphertext in the transcript `T_in` (§6.4).
- **Tests.** X-Wing vectors (draft-06, draft-10), ML-KEM-768 known-answer tests, a differential test against RustCrypto `x-wing 0.1.0` (dev-dependency only; G-R1 keeps it out of the shipped graph), malformed lengths and ciphertexts (a malformed ML-KEM ciphertext gives a wrong `ss` by implicit rejection and the inner AEAD fails; a low-order X25519 half makes libcrux return an error, which is mapped to `NOT_OPEN`, where RustCrypto `x-wing` returns a secret; either way the blob is dropped with no state change; the whole-key and low-order vectors of §22.8.2), encapsulation and decapsulation timing on the reference low-end device (S0′; an intro open costs one decapsulation, after a hint match). **Measured by S0′ (V-6, 2026-09-19), host only:** on a portable release build, as the **median of 20 × 100-call batches under CI load**, key generation 148 µs, encapsulation 405 µs with GHOST's whole-key validation, decapsulation 193 µs; on an idle host 71 / 167 / 150 µs. **No host figure is an upper bound on a call:** the best batch of either run is 30–50 % faster than its own median, the loaded median already exceeds the fastest batch of the same table, and three further sessions spanned 104–144 µs for the best decapsulation batch alone. The design is sized against the **loaded median**, the slowest statistic recorded. The low-end device estimate (not a measurement, anchored to the loaded median, 15–25× slower single-threaded) is 2.2–3.7 ms keygen, 6.1–10.1 ms encapsulation, 2.9–4.8 ms decapsulation — far below the 1 s foreground budget of R-S5, with a factor of about 100 to spare at the high end. The measurement on the reference low-end device (§15.10 step 9) remains the **only** figure that may be treated as a bound. A SIMD build measured the same composites within noise, so the portable rule costs nothing measurable here.
- **Alternative (Q55).** If `libcrux-kem`'s extra crates (`libcrux-ecdh`, `libcrux-p256`, `rand 0.10`) fail the S0′ graph review, `ghost-client-dm` composes X-Wing from `libcrux-ml-kem`, `libcrux-curve25519` and `libcrux-sha3` (one SHA3-256 combiner call per the draft) under the same vectors. `aws-lc-rs` is the documented ML-KEM fallback provider (Q56).

### 9.3 PQ-B: PQ-only transport of seal keys (normative invariant; T35)

Every symmetric key that seals relay-stored DM bytes is one of:
1. `KB_i`, `KIO_i`: derived from a card that reached the device in person, through an out-of-band link (PQ-R8), or inside an X-Wing-keyed intro inner layer (the sender's card, §6.4; corrected by §22.8.7: GHOST does transport cards, only under keys of kind 2);
2. `k_in`: derived from an X-Wing shared secret;
3. a random secret (`reply_seed` → `K_prov`; roots → pair week keys; PQ-C roots) that was transported **only** inside envelopes sealed under keys of kind 2 or 3.

By induction over transport order, a passive adversary that breaks every X25519 instance but not ML-KEM-768 opens no DM envelope, and therefore never sees an Olm header (identity, base, fallback or ratchet public keys) of any session it could attack. **T35** enforces it in the implementation: in test builds every key object in `ghost-client-dm` carries a provenance tag {CARD(IN_PERSON | LINK | TRANSPORTED(KEM)), KEM, TRANSPORTED(tag of the carrying envelope)}; `seal*` asserts the tag of the key it uses is CARD (bundles and the intro outer layer only; LINK-sourced sealing is reported as the PQ-R8 exception, §22.8.7), KEM, or TRANSPORTED(KEM or TRANSPORTED); the DM world exercises every path, and mutants PM-32 (intro inner layer without the KEM) and PM-33 (a `reply_seed` or root sent in a classical-only layer) trip it. The model (§9.5) proves the design; T35 and the bump assertion PQ5 guard the code.

### 9.4 PQ-C: sparse PQ root re-key (normative; slice S13; Q48, Q49, Q68)

**TLVs** (registry in §7.1): `seal_root_pq` (0x0005, 1 166 B) and `pq_key` (0x0006, 1 228 B).

- **`seal_root_pq`** replaces `seal_root` whenever I hold a usable X-Wing key of c. I (the recipient of direction c → me) encapsulate to c's key, `(ct, ss) = XWing.Encaps(Q_c)`, and set `root = HKDF-SHA256(ikm = root_rand ‖ ss, salt = SHA-256("ghost/v1/dm/pq-root" ‖ OI_me ‖ OI_c ‖ u32_be(peer_gen_id) ‖ Q_c ‖ ct), info = "ghost/v1/dm/pq-root", L = 32)`. c decapsulates with its generation `peer_gen_id` in `RECV` and derives the same root. The generation-number and acknowledgement rules of `seal_root` apply.
- **`pq_key`** carries my current generation's X-Wing key; the recipient stores it in `dm_peer_pq` (source `in_band`) if its week is newer; it is usable while `now < start(bundle_week + 5)` (§5.4 step 3). It is attached on the first slack-eligible send to c in each ISO week (`dm_contact.pq_announced_week`, a period index).
- **Attachment.** Only under the slack rule R-C5 (§7.1): the bucket never depends on a PQ TLV (ME-23), and nothing is sent for a TLV alone (T28).
- **Cadence.** `seal_root_pq` is used for every root offer (§6.3 triggers: restore, key change acknowledgement, periodic replacement) when `dm_peer_pq` holds a usable key of c; otherwise the classical `seal_root` is used and the next periodic replacement retries. `ROOT_REPLACE_WEEKS = 4` with PQ-C (round 9: 13). **Q68** (RG-10) offers `seal_root_pq` at most every 7 days instead, adopted only if the V-13 hint benchmark still meets its budget with the extra overlapping root generations; the default stays 4 weeks until measured. The offered TLV bytes are stored wrapped in `dm_pair_offer.offer_env` (70 B classical, 1 194 B PQ) so that re-attachment until acknowledgement repeats identical content; a PQ offer is re-attached only while its target key is usable, a renewal from c or a KEYDEC acceptance discards unacknowledged PQ offers and c's in-band keys, and a PQ offer is used again only after a newer `pq_key` arrived (§22.8.6).
- **Healing argument (informal; model obligation M-6).** A passive adversary reads both devices' complete state at time t, records all relay traffic afterwards, owns a quantum computer, and compromises nothing more. (1) Within a week each device publishes a generation whose secrets the adversary lacks. (2) Each device announces it in-band (`pq_key`), and the peer stores it. (3) At the next root replacement (≤ 4 weeks; ≤ 1 week under Q68) each device offers a PQ root encapsulated to the other's new key; the adversary sees `root_rand` and `ct` but not `ss`, so not the root. (4) Once both new roots are acknowledged, the adversary opens neither direction's pair envelopes, so it never sees the Olm ratchet public keys that follow; knowing the Olm root key from time t does not let it compute the next DH ratchet output without them. Healing therefore takes about 5–6 weeks of ordinary two-way use at the default cadence and needs user sends in both directions (no reflex traffic). Not healed: intro and provisional envelopes addressed to generations the adversary stole (≤ 91 days); an **active** adversary after t (injection with stolen keys) is out of scope.

### 9.5 Assurance before distribution (spec §7.5; a release gate, not an implementation gate)

| # | Obligation | Reused analysis | GHOST work |
|---|---|---|---|
| M-1 | HNDL secrecy of the intro inner layer (card, binding, `reply_seed`, L1) with DH broken and ML-KEM secure; the adversary knows `KIO` (a card holder) | X-Wing proof (IND-CCA); PQXDH HNDL queries (Bhargavan et al., USENIX Security 2024) | ProVerif model of §6.4 |
| M-2 | Binding v3: agreement on (`OI_A`, base key, `ct`, namespace, minute, `OI_B`); KCI with the recipient's full state; no transfer across envelopes | PQXDH authentication queries | model and KC13 |
| M-3 | PQ-B: secrecy of `reply_seed`, roots, pair keys by induction | — | model; T35 |
| M-4 | Nesting does not weaken Olm's classical secrecy and authentication | independent-key nesting argument; Olm analyses (Albrecht–Dowling–Jones 2024, Ginesin–Nita-Rotaru 2024) | written argument |
| M-5 | Session selection R-SS0 … R-SS4: no confusion across contacts, replay refusal, convergence | Cremers–Jacomme–Naska session-handling model style | model with two sessions per pair; EM-1; RO5, RO8, RP11 |
| M-6 | PQ-C healing (§9.4) | Triple Ratchet and SCKA framing (Dodis et al., Eurocrypt 2025), not its construction | model with a compromise event |
| M-7 | P-7/T34 attribution statements as key-erasure events | — | model plus the T34 world |
| M-8 | Implementation correctness | libcrux proofs (portable), KATs | vectors, differential tests, `cargo-fuzz` targets for card, bundle, intro inner layer, L0, TLVs, Olm message parsing (nightly job) |

- **Model in CI.** A ProVerif model written by GHOST lives in `protocol/models/dm/`; job `pq-model` runs it on every change to `client-core/dm` or the model; a failing or timed-out query fails the job. ProVerif is a CI tool, never shipped. It complements, and does not replace, the exhaustive state-model enumerations EM-1 and EM-2 of the `rust` job (§15.2).
- **External cryptographic review** of the DM seal, binding v3, provisional mode, PQ composition, session selection, dehydrated-pickle encoder and their Rust implementation, **before the first distributed APK**, with a published report (ADR-38). Reviewers are chosen by the owner among teams with published work on PQXDH, Olm/Matrix or MLS (Q57).

### 9.6 PQ status per layer (normative summary)

| Layer | Primitive | Future quantum adversary with the relay archive, no device compromise | After a device compromise at t |
|---|---|---|---|
| Olm content (L1) | classical 3DH and DH ratchet | protected only through the enclosing seal | followable while the adversary can open seals; healed by PQ-C |
| Intro inner layer | X-Wing `k_in` | protected | first flights to stolen generations readable; later generations protected |
| Intro outer layer | `KIO` (card) | protected against non-card-holders | — |
| Provisional | `K_prov` from `reply_seed` (sent under X-Wing) | protected | as intro |
| Pair | week keys from roots (PQ-B; PQ-C roots) | protected | until PQ-C healing |
| Bundle | `KB` (card) | protected against non-card-holders; a quantum card holder learns public keys and could compute the Olm identity and fallback private keys, enabling active impersonation (PQ-R1), not decryption of recorded envelopes | — |
| Deposit, read and fetch credentials (§4.3) | bearer 256-bit secrets, presented to the serving relay (only the verifiers stored in `keys.redb` are hashes) | **not protected** against a quantum adversary with Tor recordings (PQ-R4): it learns the credentials, which grant deposit, read or fetch at that relay slot while registrations live, never an opening key (§22.8.7) | — |
| Ed25519 cards, bindings, bundle signatures | classical | authenticity only; active forgery (PQ-R1) | — |
| Tor links and relay RPCs | classical | **not protected** (PQ-R4) | — |

### 9.7 Deferred options (recorded, not Phase 9)

- **PQ-D, seeding Olm from a hybrid handshake** (R-pq's GPQX): when vodozemac ships a public receiving counterpart of `Session::from_root_key_material` in a release, and after the §9.5 review, a v2 handshake may seed Olm's root from the hybrid secret, keeping the nested seal. Removes PQ-R7. Rejected for Phase 9 because it needs an unreleased constructor (a vendored patch), replaces Olm's audited and deployed 3DH with GHOST handshake code (against spec §7.2), and buys the same relay-at-rest guarantee in this architecture (Q47).
- **PQ-E, ML-KEM Braid keying the pair seal** (Signal's public-domain specification): Level 3 at epoch granularity; an own implementation; incremental ML-KEM is experimental in libcrux; its own model and review; Phase 12 or later.
- **PQ-N, network layer:** HPKE with `MLKEM768-X25519` per relay session, or Tor's PQ handshake when it ships; Phase 12 (Q65).
- **PQ signatures:** none now (HNDL is the target, and ML-DSA keys and signatures would not fit the card QR); version bytes reserved (Q66).

### 9.8 Residues and CP-10 wording

| ID | Residue |
|---|---|
| PQ-R1 | Authentication is classical: an active quantum adversary can forge Ed25519 bundle signatures and bindings; before an identity is pinned (first contact, including a card verified in person before its bundle was fetched) a forgery is pinned silently. |
| PQ-R2 | No PQ inside the ratchet. After a device compromise, PQ healing exists only at the seal layer, only after a PQ-C cycle (§9.4), and only with two-way user traffic. |
| PQ-R3 | First-flight forward secrecy is bounded by generation erasure (≤ 91 days after supersession); no one-time keys through blind relays. |
| PQ-R4 | Network metadata is not HNDL-protected: Tor handshakes are classical and relay RPCs are plain HTTP/2 inside the onion stream (`ghost/client-core/net/src/relay_client.rs:45,393-399`), so a future quantum adversary with recordings of a client's Tor links could see its namespaces and credentials. |
| PQ-R5 | Primitive maturity: ML-KEM is young; libcrux is pre-1.0 and its unverified wrappers have had advisories (RUSTSEC-2025-0133, GHSA-435g-fcv3-8j26); X-Wing is an Internet-Draft. |
| PQ-R6 | The composition is GHOST-built until the model and the external review exist. |
| PQ-R7 | Olm is classical under the seal: a seal-layer implementation flaw would expose Olm to a recording quantum adversary (no defence in depth; PQ-D would add it). |
| PQ-R8 | A card link sent over a classical channel can be recorded; a later quantum adversary gets the card: it can recognise the owner's intro envelopes, deposit and fetch bundles, not read content. |

**CP-10.** Before M-1 … M-7 and the external review: no statement about quantum resistance anywhere in the UI or marketing. After them, the only allowed statement (Romanian UI text in Phase 13): "Plicurile DM păstrate pe relee combină X25519 cu ML-KEM-768 (X-Wing) împotriva înregistrării traficului pentru decriptare ulterioară. Ratchet-ul mesajelor (Olm) și autentificarea rămân clasice; metadatele de rețea nu sunt protejate post-cuantic." Forbidden: "quantum-proof", "post-quantum end-to-end encryption", "PQ ratchet", "Signal protocol", "formally verified", "100% private".

### 9.9 Forward secrecy and post-compromise security compared with libsignal (informative)

| Property | libsignal (excluded) | This stack | Net |
|---|---|---|---|
| Per-message FS (symmetric ratchet, keys deleted after use) | yes | yes (Olm chain keys) | equal |
| Skipped-key storage | 2 000 per chain, 5 chains, 40 archived states, no time bound | 40 per chain, 5 chains, ≤ 2 sessions per contact, a retired session gone after 46 d (an age bound under Q67) | smaller window (more loss under heavy reordering, §7.7); less residue on a seized device |
| Classical PCS trigger | DH ratchet at each direction change | same (Olm, `vz:src/olm/session/mod.rs:438-458`) | equal |
| PQ PCS | SPQR epochs inside the ratchet | none inside Olm; seal-layer PQ healing after a PQ-C cycle (weeks) | **weaker** (PQ-R2) |
| First-flight FS | last-resort Kyber prekey and signed prekey, bounded by prekey erasure | fallback key and X-Wing generation, bounded by erasure (91 d) | equal |
| HNDL at rest (no compromise) | PQXDH and SPQR on content; round 9's intro inner layer X25519 | every relay-stored byte under X-Wing-derived keys (nesting); Olm classical underneath | stronger for metadata inside intro envelopes; content equal against a passive recorder, weaker in depth (PQ-R7) |
| Implicit identity binding in the AKE | 3DH/4DH with XEdDSA-signed prekeys; MAC over both identity keys | 3DH with an Ed25519-signed fallback key; `pair_ad` binds both identities in the seal | equal in effect |
| KCI at first contact with the recipient's full state | PQXDH has no ephemeral-KCI resistance (Bhargavan et al.) | plain Olm 3DH has the same limit; binding v3 signs the base key and KEM ciphertext, so new sessions in a sender's name need its Ed25519 key (§3.4, KC13) | stronger, at a deniability cost (L7-D20) |
| Content deniability | no signature over content | no signature over content (round 9's binding v2 signed the L1 hash; removed) | equal to libsignal, better than round 9 |
| Inner MAC | 8 bytes | 8 bytes (Olm V1; audit Issue J) | equal; both under a 128-bit outer AEAD |
| Session-state inflation by a peer | up to ≈ 16 MB per record (§21.5) | bounded by construction (§7.9 point 4) | better |
| Time data in state | minute and day times inside records | none | better |
| Library logging of identity keys | yes, silenced by a load-bearing logger | none in the library (G-R4, G-R8) | better |

---

## 10. Ephemeral messages (I7; ADR-27, ADR-32)

### 10.1 Timer values, TTL buckets and deadlines (normative; G-5, C-8, SC-3)

| Timer (`timer_code`) | off (0) | 5 min (1) | 1 h (2) | 1 d (3) | **7 d (4, default)** | 30 d (5) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Relay TTL (`ttl_bucket_seconds`) | 2 592 000 | **3 600** | 3 600 | 86 400 | 604 800 | 2 592 000 |
| Op deadline `author + min(bucket, 7 d)` (§21.10) | author + 7 d | author + 1 h | author + 1 h | author + 1 d | author + 7 d | author + 7 d |
| Honest relay copy gone by (`deadline_hour`: R-T4) | `deadline_hour + 30 d + 1 h` | `deadline_hour + 2 h` | `deadline_hour + 2 h` | `deadline_hour + 1 d + 1 h` | `deadline_hour + 7 d + 1 h` | `deadline_hour + 30 d + 1 h` |

- **R-T1 (ADR-27 point 11, exactly).** TTL = the smallest bucket ≥ timer; timer off → 30 d, the DM store-and-forward horizon. With the bucket set {1 h, 1 d, 7 d, 30 d, 90 d} the TTL equals the timer except for 5 min. The plan row's "TTL ≤ timer" cannot hold for 5 min and is read as ADR-27 (erratum X-16). DMs never use 90 d. The collapse of 30 d and off into 7 d (fewer TTL classes, loss of mail for recipients offline 7–30 days) is only offered as Q15.
- **R-T2 (one bucket per conversation).** Every blob of a conversation uses `bucket(conversation timer)`, including TIMER_SET, REACTION and CONTROL: a control message in another bucket would stand out in the recipient's inbox.
- **R-T3.** A TIMER_SET uses the **larger** of the old and the new bucket, so a change from 7 d to 1 h reaches a recipient offline for more than an hour.
- **R-T4 (deadline; SC-3).** Every DM op carries `deadlineEpochSeconds = author + min(bucket, 7 d)` (off uses the 30 d bucket; §21.10); Phase 7 stores `deadline_hour = max(ceil_hour(deadline), floor_hour(not_before) + 1 h)` (`OutboxStore.kt:179-182`), so a 5-minute message has the same deadline as a 1-hour one and is never cancelled at expiry (§21.10). After the deadline no copy is stored anywhere and the op closes (FAILED if no copy can exist, else INDETERMINATE; F7 M3). The 7-day cap (the Phase 7 store window H) makes FRESH = 45 d hold for parked ops too (§7.6), and gives an op parked for an offline recipient a bounded life.
- **R-T5 (pending sends).** A message waiting for a bundle (§5.5) fails at `deadline_hour`: it never reaches the session layer after its store deadline.
- **Bundles** (§5.4) use the 30 d bucket, a class DMs already use, with deadline `start(bundle_week + 2)`.

### 10.2 Seconds buckets: relay, client, observables (normative change list; X-10)

| # | Where | Change |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | `relay/crates/api/src/lib.rs:21-24, 171-175` | `TTL_BUCKETS_SECONDS = [3600, 86400, 604800, 2592000, 7776000]`; `ttl_bucket_seconds(ttl) -> Option<u32>` matches **exactly** (replaces the rounding `ttl_bucket_days`) |
| 2 | `relay/crates/node/src/lib.rs:215, 236-239`; `capture.rs:25` | a store with a non-bucket TTL → `INVALID_ARGUMENT`, capture `result = rejected_ttl` (the relay persisted raw TTLs until now, RE F2); capture field `ttl_bucket_seconds` = the stored value |
| 3 | `relay/crates/storage/src/lib.rs:59-72` | unchanged: expiry `ceil_hour(now + ttl)`, so a 1 h blob lives 1–2 h; a conformance vector re-checks that a namespace's sequence seed stays monotone when 1 h memberships empty it |
| 4 | `allowed-observables.json` v2, fixtures, `capture-check` tests | `ttl_bucket_seconds` enum replaces `ttl_bucket_days`; with §4.7's `register` and `key_kind` |
| 5 | `relay.proto:27`; `relay_semantics.txt` | comment lists the five buckets; a TTL section: 3600 accepted with an hour-rounded expiry; 1, 3599, 3601, 7200, 172800 → `rejected_ttl`; list and get across the 1 h expiry; renewal; Rust and `ModelRelay` replayers |
| 6 | `client-core/net/src/relay_client.rs:260-268, 292, 736-742` | refuse a non-bucket TTL (`invalid_argument`, before any I/O) instead of snapping; add the receipt **upper** bound `expiry ≤ ceil_hour(now + ttl) + σ` (else `malformed_response`), so a relay that promises to store longer than asked is detected (T30); the lower bound is unchanged (§10.4 finding 3). **Noted in S2 (2026-09-19):** a relay answers a store over a live membership with that membership's expiry (`relay/crates/storage/src/lib.rs` `put`), so the bound also refuses an honest answer when the same ciphertext already lives in the same namespace under a **longer** TTL. No honest client reaches that: a DM blob carries a fresh salt, every op of a conversation uses its bucket (R-T2), and the Phase 7 identical-bytes resend repeats the op's own TTL. A client that did would see `malformed_response` and treat the relay as failed for that store, never a silent short storage. **Corrected by the review of S2 (2026-09-19):** what the bound achieves is that an over-promise of **more than σ** is detected; one of at most σ is tolerated for every bucket (73 h for a 1 h store, 10 d for the 7 d bucket), because the same σ covers a relay clock ahead of ours — declared as L7-D27, the mirror image of L7-D15. Its premise is `|relay now − now| ≤ σ`, which nothing enforces and which the T32 clock model of §15.6 breaks (§21.2 point 10), so that model is narrowed before S11. The accepted expiry is also clamped to `now + 90 d + σ`, the absolute bound `validate_get` enforces: `ceil_hour` rounds up, so without the clamp the 90-day bucket accepted a receipt up to one hour above it |
| 7 | `android/sync/.../api/Types.kt:46-56`; `TorRelayTransport.kt:59, 167` | `TtlBucket.HOUR_1(3600)`; every user of `days` rounds up; S3 audits each (`ownRetainDay`: `ceil(ttl / day) + 8` still covers TTL + 1 h + 2σ) |
| 8 | `Schema.kt:245` | migration 4 rebuilds `outbox_op`/`outbox_delivery` (§13.6); the v2 freeze rule is kept (no in-place amendment of a released version) |
| 9 | THREAT_MODEL §6, INVARIANTS T25 | wording: the relay observes `bucket(timer)` of the table, nothing finer (X-11) |

### 10.3 Countdowns and bounds (normative; ADR-27 C.9; C-11)

| Party / state | Start | Column | Rule |
|---|---|---|---|
| Sender, every outgoing row | the commit of SEND (S3) | `expire_at_minute = ceil_minute(commit + timer)` | set at insert; independent of the network |
| Recipient, displayed | `markDisplayed(ids)` (Phase 13 UI; the DISPLAY transaction) | `expire_at_minute = ceil_minute(now + timer)` | only if earlier than an existing value |
| Recipient, never displayed | consume (RECV) | `unread_deadline_minute = ceil_minute(min(E, consume + bucket + 1 h))` | set at insert |
| Held L1 (key change) | consume | `hold_until_minute = ceil_minute(min(E, consume + 30 d + 1 h))` | §8.3 |
| Request messages | consume | as "never displayed"; the request itself at `request_until_day` | §12.3 |

- **E** is `InboundBlob.relayExpiryHour`: the expiry the serving relay returned at `get`, validated in Rust (≤ now + 90 d + σ, `relay_client.rs:309-322`), stored in `inbox_blob.relay_expiry_hour` only while `fetched` (§13.3 item 2). An honest relay's E ≤ store + bucket + 1 h ≤ consume + bucket + 1 h, so the `min` is E, exactly ADR-27 C.9 ("cel târziu la expirarea blob-ului pe relee"). A relay lying short can only make deletion earlier (a denial of service it could cause by withholding); a relay lying long is clamped. Content-derived deadlines (`author + bucket`) are rejected: a sender clock ahead of the recipient's would extend them (C-11).
- `delete_at_minute = COALESCE(expire_at_minute, unread_deadline_minute)` (CHECK). Displaying a message may move `delete_at` later than the unread cap, because the recipient countdown starts at display (ADR-27 point 9).
- **R-T6 (only earlier).** `expire_at_minute`, `unread_deadline_minute` and `hold_until_minute` never increase once set (triggers, §13.6).
- **R-T7 (expiry clock).** `now_e = max(floor_minute(wall), dm_self.expiry_clock_minute)`; every sweep raises `expiry_clock_minute`. A clock stepped backwards never delays deletion, also across reboots; a forward step deletes early (data loss, no leak; declared).
- **Bounds (the T25 oracle).** Sender plaintext gone by `ceil_minute(commit + timer) + lag`; recipient by `ceil_minute(display + timer) + lag` or `unread_deadline_minute + lag`; held ciphertext by `hold_until_minute + lag`; the sender's retained blob with its message row, and its Phase 7 payload by the op's last possible store (≤ `deadline_hour`); honest relay copies by the §10.1 table. `lag` is the sweep lag of §10.7.

### 10.4 Phase 7 window rules for TTL ∈ {1 h, 1 d} (normative; G-10)

The re-derivation below is owned by the sync track (S3) and must keep `sync-exit-gate` green with no other change. With σ = 3 d:

1. **Resolution never applies.** `now < copy_hour + ttl − σ` is never true, so no delivery becomes resolvable and a check-before-restore never clears `copy_hour` (the resolvable-copy clause of `CapabilityStore.needed()`, `CapabilityStore.kt:141`, is empty for such TTLs). Deliveries close by the deadline (every short-TTL DM op has one) or by window expiry, and D2 then decides `indeterminate` if a `copy_hour` exists without a verified copy.
   - The **safety** invariants of F7 §8.4 do not use σ and are unchanged: `failed ⇒ never held` (FAILED needs no `copy_hour`), `sent ⇒ memberships`, and no duplicates at the recipient (stores are confined to `[F, deadline] ⊂ [F, F + H]`, and recipient tombstones live `E + 24 d`).
   - **Liveness** changes: an ambiguous short-TTL op ends `indeterminate` instead of being resolved into `failed` and re-sent. Weaker, correct ("may not have been delivered").
2. **`resendNotAfter = copy_hour + ttl − σ`** is in the past, so no identical resend happens for short TTLs; OUTCOME already expects it (§7.5).
3. **The receipt lower bound** `expiry + σ ≥ now + ttl` is vacuous for short TTLs: a relay may acknowledge with an early expiry and it is accepted. The effect is that the blob disappears early, equivalent to withholding (availability only; ADR-11 independence and recipients listing every relay of the set). Declared (L7-D15); a relay-corrected bound is a Phase 12 option.
4. **Retention** is unchanged and still safe: a contact's inbox is write-only, so there is no own tombstone; recipient tombstones are `ceil7(day(E) + 24)`; every store happens before the deadline, far inside H.
5. **Tests.** The seeded scenario S-F of the Phase 7 exhaustive suite gains TTLs of 1 h and 1 d, with and without deadlines, and offline windows of 30 min, 3 h and 3 d. `sync-exit-gate` (20 000 seeds, 17 mutants, crash counts) stays green with its report lines unchanged apart from the scenario list.
   - The **seeded worlds of §8.5 draw the new bucket too** (their TTL is uniform over `TtlBucket.entries`), so their outcome oracle carries the same re-derivation: for an op whose TTL is at most σ, OUT-1 accepts SENT, DEGRADED or INDETERMINATE, and only those — FAILED needs no copy anywhere and stays refused, and every structural invariant of F7 §8.4 is unchanged. Without it a world with a 1-hour op whose acknowledged copy expires before a check sees it (two strikes, D2 → DEGRADED) never reaches quiescence; seed 8015 is pinned as a regression in `RegressionScenarioTest`.

### 10.5 Timer changes and versions (normative; ADR-27 C.8)

- The conversation timer changes only through TIMER_SET, a message shown to both sides, with `timer_version = local + 1`. Every L0 carries its own `timer_code` and the sender's `timer_version`.
- **R-T9 (adoption).** The recipient adopts `(timer_code, timer_version)` from any L0 with a **greater** version (TIMER_SET or any message: a lost TIMER_SET is healed implicitly). Equal versions with different codes: **the shorter timer wins** (off = ∞). A lower version changes nothing, so a replayed or delayed old message cannot lengthen the timer (Signal's `expireTimerVersion` with a deterministic tie-break, RE §8.2). A version above the §9.1 bound is not adopted either, and the codec refuses the record that carries it (§22.14 item 15(c)).
- A message is always deleted by **its own** `timer_code`, whatever the conversation timer is now.

### 10.6 Deletion cascade (normative; ADR-27 C.10)

One SWEEP transaction deletes, for every row with `delete_at_minute ≤ now_e` (at most 500 per transaction):

| Item | Mechanism |
|---|---|
| `dm_message` row (`body_env`, quote reference, timers) | `DELETE` |
| its reactions | `dm_reaction` `ON DELETE CASCADE` |
| quotes of it | nothing stored: replies hold only `quote_ref` and render "unavailable" |
| its outbound items, ops and retained blobs | `dm_outbound_item` and `dm_outbound_op` cascade; **no `Outbox.cancel`**: every op runs to its outcome or deadline with E2E ciphertext no key on the device opens (§21.10) |
| a pending send | `dm_pending_send` cascade; `P_c` removed when no pending send is left (EST-END) |
| held rows past `hold_until_minute`; requests past `request_until_day` | `DELETE` (a request cascades its contact, session, pair keys and rows) |
| `dm_seen` rows past `until_day`; pair week keys past `start(w + 1) + FRESH` (opening and recognition keys together); bundle generations past `delete_after_day` (with their reuse tuples; not while a live held row references them); retired `previous` sessions at `retire_day`; `prov_env` past `prov_erase_minute`; surplus `dm_peer_pq` rows | `DELETE` |
| notification | after commit, `NotificationPort.onUnreadChanged(count)`; cancelled at 0 (§10.7) |
| media, thumbnails | `MediaPort.onMessagesDeleted(tx, ids)` (Phase 11; a no-op port in Phase 9) |
| inbox ciphertext | already NULL at `markConsumed` (Phase 7) |
| Olm skipped keys | belong to messages not yet received; stay in live session state (≤ 200 per session, wrapped; retired sessions erased after 46 d; an age bound only under Q67) (declared, L7-D7) |

### 10.7 Physical deletion, sweep scheduling, notifications (normative; C-12)

- **Checkpoint.** After a SWEEP that deleted rows: `PRAGMA wal_checkpoint(TRUNCATE)` read through `query`, **require `busy = 0`**, retry 3 times 100 ms apart, else keep `checkpoint_due` in memory and retry at the next sweep. The same result check replaces the ignored result in the Phase 7 `Gc.checkpoint` (`Gc.kt:90-92`, RE F5). **R-K1:** a checkpoint runs at every database open, after migration and before the first read. `secure_delete = ON` (never FAST).
- **Sweeps run** at: (1) the actor's in-process timer (§21.9), which includes the next `delete_at_minute`, while the process lives (lag ≤ 1 min); (2) every database open; (3) the start of every foreground session and every `ConsumerTail` run (§13.3), including quiet runs after the issuer call returned and the transport closed; (4) every read query filters `delete_at_minute IS NULL OR delete_at_minute > :now_e` (an expired row is never shown before physical deletion; a lint test runs every DAO query against an expired row); (5) **one inexact alarm**: `AlarmManager.setAndAllowWhileIdle(ELAPSED_REALTIME_WAKEUP, SystemClock.elapsedRealtime() + (next − now), pendingIntent)` (§21.10) on an explicit, **non-exported** `ExpiryAlarmReceiver` through `FLAG_IMMUTABLE`, one fixed request code, no extras, no new permission; cancelled when nothing is pending and at wipe; the receiver calls `goAsync()`, posts a sweep to the actor and finishes. Doze limits delivery to about once per 9 minutes per app. Alarms do not survive a reboot and no boot receiver exists: after a reboot the next sweep waits for the persisted periodic job (network-constrained) or the next app open (§21.10, L7-D14).
- **T20 amendment (X-11).** "One periodic job without extras" is unchanged; the messaging layer may hold this one inexact alarm with no extras. A rooted examiner can read the next expiry minute from the alarm (L7-D7).
- **Lag bound (declared, L7-D14).** With the process frozen and the alarm deferred by Doze, physical deletion waits for the next alarm window or job run; rows stay under SQLCipher and are never shown. With an authentication-bound database key (ADR-20 Q6), deletion waits for the next unlock.
- **Notifications (Q19).** `NotificationPort` exposes only `onUnreadChanged(count: Int)`; the Phase 13 implementation posts one constant text with no sender, conversation id or content, **locked or not** (Android's notification history keeps what was posted for 24 h, RE §8.4). Phase 9 ships the port and a no-op implementation; T33 tests the port contract.
- **No FTS.** No virtual table exists over DM content (`secure_delete` does not scrub FTS shadow tables); gate `dm-no-fts.sh` and schema test R-M4.

### 10.8 Interaction with Phase 7 retention (declared)

- Inbox tombstones of `X_0` keep `(namespace, hash, retain_until_day)` for `ceil7(day(E) + 24)` (ADR-20 point 6); `dm_seen` keeps one keyed 16-byte tag per received message until `ceil7(day(author) + ceil_days(FRESH(bucket)) + 1)` (§21.10). A tombstone says "a blob existed that week"; a tag also lets a seizer who knows a `message_id` (its sender, a colluding contact) confirm receipt and its week. They outlive a 1 h message by up to about two weeks and a 30 d message by up to about seven (L7-D7). A TTL-aware tombstone retention would reopen the Phase 7 retention proof: a Phase 12 option.
- Contacts' inboxes are write-only (`listen = false`): Phase 7 keeps no tombstone for them.
- Fetched but unconsumed DM ciphertext is never collected by Phase 7 GC; the DM consumer runs in every foreground session and in the `ConsumerTail` of every background and quiet run (§13.3), so `FETCHED_CAP` drains and the unread cap applies without a foreground (E-17).

### 10.9 T25 for DMs (summary; cases in §15.5 EP)

For each timer × path (sender; recipient displayed; recipient unread with an honest, a short-lying and a long-lying relay expiry; held; request; pending send): advance the virtual clock to the §10.3 bound + 1 min, sweep, then assert:
1. **logically:** no row, reaction, held row, pending row, retained blob or outbound mapping remains; the notification sink's last count is correct and every posted text equals the constant;
2. **physically:** the raw `.db` and `-wal` bytes contain neither the plaintext canary, nor the `body_env` bytes, nor the retained blob (checked at the op's outcome or `deadline_hour`, §21.10), nor the `message_id`; a logical scan decrypts every `*_env` column with the harness content key and finds no canary (§21.8);
3. the WAL is 0 bytes; a busy reader delays but does not defeat truncation;
4. a clock stepped back one day does not delay deletion;
5. every captured store TTL follows §10.1, and captures for 5 min vs 1 h timers are identical (NI-DM4).

---

## 11. Traffic and metadata (I8; ADR-09, ADR-15, ADR-20)

### 11.1 When DM traffic happens (normative)

| Traffic | STANDARD | HIGH | Depends on |
|---|---|---|---|
| Own inbox listings (`X_0` on its set) | Phase 7 schedule (T = 30 s × U[0.5, 1.5] per pair; background W = 90 s) | same | nothing DM-related (T19) |
| Gets | per new blob at list events | same | inbound volume (declared, LIMITE L2.1 #3) |
| DM deposit (store into `X_c`) | when due, right after the SEND commit (`requestExpedite`) | at a pair event after U[0, 10 min]; **DM namespaces cannot be set to `SendDelay.OFF`** (API refusal and trigger, §13.3 item 8) | user sends; scheduled CONTROL due times |
| Deposit verification (`check` with the deposit credential) | pair event ≥ ack + 60 s (F7:626) | same, at pair events | user sends |
| Automatic identical resend | when the INDETERMINATE outcome is released, within `resendNotAfter` | at pair events | the user's own earlier send (§7.5) |
| Bundle fetch of a contact | the next list events of the temporary `P_c` pairs | same | user action only (§5.5) |
| Own redeem for `X_0`, `P_0` | activation: at once (Q31); weekly: the EXPIRING PRF time in the last 23 h of the week | same, at pair events | the week; the redeem lane |
| Own `register` events | right after a capability install, on the work lane | at pair events | the capability install times |
| Own bundle publication | first session after `t_pub(w)` (fixed seed-PRF offset per card) | same | the week only |
| Reconnect resets, revocation notices | each at its own pre-drawn offset (U[0, 48 h], U[0, 24 h]) | same, then the HIGH delay | user action |
| Anything caused by an inbound blob | **nothing** (T28) | nothing | — |

No cover traffic (P2), no DM-specific schedule, no shorter listing interval: NFR-1 is measured on the gate devices and recorded, not claimed (Q23).

### 11.2 Per-event observation matrix (THREAT_MODEL §6 rows; Romanian text in Appendix B.3)

| Event | Relay (AD-2, effective view) | AD-1 adds | AD-4 / AD-5 | AD-7 (holds my card) | AD-8 (seized device) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Deposit into `X` | `store(X, hash, 4/16/64 KiB, ttl ∈ {3600, 86400, 604800, 2592000}, minute, the inbox's static deposit scope at that relay (identical for all senders, §21.2), request id, sender circuit)` | the same on the set's relays; the sender's circuit ties its stores in one session (L7-D5) | upload 3 × bucket | with AD-1: its own blobs' hashes, hence my fetch times | message row; frozen outbox ciphertext until the deadline; retained blob until the outcome |
| Receive | `list(X)` on T19 times with my static read scope at that relay (§21.2); `get` per new blob | presence, foreground/background cadence (LIMITE L2.1 #4) | download at list events | alone with its own token for my inbox, or with AD-1: my online times and total inbound volume, which blobs are intro envelopes and when each arrived (their `gen_id` and X-Wing ciphertext, never the inner layer, not even for a future quantum card holder), hence which conversations I have not answered (L7-D3, L7-D16, §22.8.7) | rows, `dm_seen` tags, tombstones, wrapped Olm state |
| Weekly owner renewal | `redeem(X)`, `redeem(P)` at the renewal PRF time; `register(X, deposit)`, `register(X, read)`, `register(P, fetch)` after them (§22.8.7) | nullifier ↔ `X`, `P` for ≤ 2 weeks (E13) | — | knows `X`, `P` | — |
| Bundle publication | `store(P, hash, 4 KiB, 2592000, minute, owner write scope)` at the card's fixed weekly offset | "this identity is alive this week" (L7-D10) | — | sees rotations, the publication week and, through `bundle_rank`, whether that week held a publication outside the schedule (activation or restore, §5.3); fetch reads return week-coarse `uploaded_at` and expiry (§22.8.7); **alone, by polling `P`**, the minute each bundle appears: the owner's first time-triggered pass after `t_pub`, absences, and restore or activation minutes (L7-D10) | bundle generations (wrapped) |
| Bundle fetch | `list/get(P)` with the card-shared read scope of that relay, a fresh circuit, in at most three attempts (§21.7) | fetch → first deposit into `X` links `P` and `X` as one owner (L7-D6); an unanswered conversation repeats it after 28 days (§8.7) | 4 KiB download | — | — |
| Key change, verification, acknowledgement, block, request accept or reject | **nothing** | nothing | nothing | nothing | trust state, held rows |
| Timer change | a user send in the larger bucket (R-T3); later blobs switch bucket | the first blob in a new bucket marks the change time (L7-D4) | — | sees the change | timer and version |
| Ephemeral expiry | nothing (TTL fixed at store) | — | — | — | deletion; tombstone and tag remain (L7-D7) |
| Restore | new registrations and a bundle from a new circuit set, same namespaces | the restore moment (L7-D10) | — | the restore week through `bundle_rank` (§5.3) | nothing about pre-restore senders: their envelopes do not open (§8.6) |
| Reconnect / revocation notice | k user-initiated sends spread over 48 h / 24 h | degree k over one or two days, weakly (L7-D10) | — | receives it | scheduled rows until due |
| A card holder redeems its own token for `X` | a redeem, lists and gets of `X` under a foreign write scope | — | — | inbound volume and timing of my inbox, the minute and `gen_id` of every intro envelope, and which conversations I have not answered (L7-D16, §22.8.7) | — |
| I send a first message, request reply or reconnect to a card | a deposit, like any message | — | — | the recipient keeps my full card for good (L7-D25) | — |

### 11.3 Consequences for the existing invariants

- **T1.** Observables v2 (§4.7, §10.2). Each new field and value is a declared privacy decision (INV:50), recorded in ADR-30 and ADR-32.
- **T19.** Unchanged and re-tested with DM activity: sends, consumption, sweeps, key change, requests, owner steps and registrations create no read event and move no pair's schedule; a temporary bundle pair does not change any other pair's schedule; registrations run on the work lane only (mutant MC-13).
- **T21.** v3 credentials are authorized by kind and namespace before I/O and use the namespace's circuit (§4.3). A DM deposit and a bundle fetch never share a circuit with another namespace.
- **T2 (Phase 8).** Deposit keys make token spend independent of DM breadth (T27), so E16 cannot grow with contacts; the T2 world models the owner namespaces (§4.9). The `ConsumerTail` runs only after a quiet run's issuer call returned and after the transport closed, so issuer-call timing and session close times cannot depend on inbound DM volume.

### 11.4 Adversary conclusions (quantitative models from RP §4; T32 measures)

- **AD-1 without cards or seeds** learns no identity (P-1, S-DM3), no sender partition inside an inbox (P-2), no message type, first contact or key change from envelope bytes and sizes (P-4), no degree of anyone (NI-DM3), and no conversation edge beyond what LD gives. It does see establishment, reset, reconnect and stale re-establishment attempts as bundle fetches linked to a deposit (L7-D6) and the activation and restore moments of an inbox (L7-D10); P-1 … P-8 hold only for the logged, auditor-reproduced build (§1.3, §22.8.8). Reply-timing SDA on LD recovers inbox ↔ inbox edges after about 6 exchanges in STANDARD and 8 in HIGH on a 10 000-inbox relay, 1–2 rounds with active delaying (L7-D1). T32 reports these numbers (S-DM2); the design does not claim them away.
- **AD-1 + AD-7** anchors one inbox to an identity, sees that identity's presence and inbound volume, recognises its intro envelopes, and, through session-start co-occurrence, the channel namespaces of the same client (L7-D2, L7-D3). No UI claim of channel-pseudonym unlinkability against relays is allowed until Phase 12 decouples session starts (CP-10, Q22).
- **AD-7 alone** can deposit (spam, quota), fetch the bundle and poll `P` for publication minutes (L7-D10), and with its own tokens list and get the inbox: inbound volume and timing, the arrival minute and `gen_id` of every intro envelope, and which conversations are unanswered (L7-D3, L7-D8, L7-D16; §22.8.7). Every recipient of my first message holds my card with the same powers (L7-D25).
- **AD-5** correlates an upload with the recipient's download at its next list event whatever the mode; only P2 cover traffic removes it (LIMITE L2).
- **AD-8** sees contacts, timers, trust states, the Olm session state (wrapped; readable with the unlocked Keystore) and residue tags (L7-D7); never expired content (T25), pair-mode history older than the T34 horizon, or intro and provisional envelopes of erased generations and seeds (P-7).

### 11.5 New LIMITE section L7 (DM residues; Romanian text in Appendix B.4)

Naming note: LIMITE **section** L7 is unrelated to the Phase 8 declared-leak **cell** L7 of T2 ("the start of a purchase after `ENTITLEMENT_NEEDED`"); DM residue ids carry the `-D` suffix.

| ID | Residue | Why it remains | Mitigation |
|---|---|---|---|
| L7-D1 | Reply-timing SDA recovers inbox ↔ inbox edges (~6 exchanges STANDARD, ~8 HIGH; 1–2 with active delay; ~2–3 for conversations with a non-default timer, L7-D4) | activity-dependent traffic, no cover | no reflexes (T28), HIGH delay, P2 cover; measured per TTL class (S-DM2, §21.11) |
| L7-D2 | Session-start co-occurrence links a client's namespaces on a relay in ~3 sessions and, with a contact, the DM identity to channel namespaces | LIMITE L2.1 #1 | Phase 12 decoupling (P1); no UI claim |
| L7-D3 | A card holder colluding with relays, **or alone with its own token for my inbox (§22.8.7)**, recognises intro envelopes in my inbox and when each arrived (first contacts, resets, reconnects, every PreKey message of a session I have not answered, and the whole of a conversation I never answer) and reads their `gen_id` and X-Wing ciphertext, not their inner layer; it sees my presence and total inbound volume; intro envelopes are attributable with my retained generation secrets until their erasure (≤ 91 d after supersession), never with my seed; a future quantum card holder reads nothing more (revised §22) | the card is a shared "may message me" secret; pair roots travel only in acknowledged sessions (§21.3) | provisional and pair modes for answered conversations; generation erasure (§5.4); card rotation (Phase 13) |
| L7-D4 | The TTL bucket (1 h / 1 d / 7 d / 30 d) partitions an inbox by conversation class and pairs the two inboxes of a conversation whose timer is not the default (reply-timing SDA then needs ~2–3 exchanges, §21.11); the first blob in a new bucket marks a timer change | TTL ≥ timer (ADR-27 point 11); both directions use the conversation's bucket | one bucket per conversation; Q15 or Q39 would reduce the classes; the Phase 13 copy states it |
| L7-D5 | Several deposits of one sender in one session share a circuit | T21 per-namespace isolation | a fresh circuit per deposit, rejected (cost) |
| L7-D6 | Establishment: the fetch of `P_c` followed by a deposit links `P_c` and `X_c` as one owner, and a relay serving the bundle chooses when that deposit happens (≥ 2 min later); up to three fetch attempts each co-occur with a session start of the fetcher (a relay set withholding bundles gets all three); an unanswered conversation repeats the fetch after 28 days unacknowledged (`UNACK_MAX`, §8.7), and a side that has sealed provisional envelopes for 28 days without receiving its peer's root does so at its next send (§22.8.5); an invitee's first DM to its inviter soon after onboarding links the two clusters | establishment needs a bundle; people message their inviter | HIGH delay; bounded attempts (§21.7); none cheap |
| L7-D7 | On the device: contacts, per-conversation timers, trust states, the Olm state of ≤ 2 sessions per contact (counters, ≤ 200 skipped keys per session) wrapped under the state key, `dm_seen` tags (≤ ~2 to ~7 weeks by bucket; with a known `message_id` they confirm receipt and its week), inbox tombstones, held ciphertext until its bound, retained E2E blobs and outbox payloads until the outcome or deadline, ≤ 14 bundle generations (≤ 13 weeks after supersession), peers' X-Wing public keys, **one cached peer bundle envelope per contact (`dm_bundle_candidate`, 1421 B, replaced at the next fetch) and a pending `reconnect` or `revocation` control with its payload and its future `due_minute` (`dm_scheduled`, until it is sent; both added in S5, §22.10 item 1)**, pair week keys (≤ FRESH after their week), `reply_seed`s until their erasure bound (§22.8.5), the minute of the newest intro envelope per responder session and each session's provisional anchor minute (§22.8.4, §22.8.5), a blocked or deleted contact's namespaces and credentials until its last op's deadline (≤ 7 d), the next expiry minute in the alarm; no time value inside library state | needed to operate | T33; minute/day floors; wrapped state (§2.5); per-bucket tag lifetimes (§21.10); content-free notifications |
| L7-D8 | The recipient pays its inbox; a card holder can exhaust a week's deposit allowance (256 MiB per relay per registering capability, ≈ 65 000 blobs of 4 KiB) and make the recipient spend CPU on hints of forged blobs; a relay that reports a short expiry can re-serve a rejected or deleted requester's genuine intro message within its freshness window and bring the request back (§21.4) | no sender-bought capabilities (T27) | allowance per registration; `FETCHED_CAP` and consumer budgets; card rotation (Phase 13) |
| L7-D9 | An identical-bytes resend after an ambiguous store links two sender sessions by hash | Phase 7 exactly-once | at most 2 resends, rare |
| L7-D10 | Weekly owner redeems, registrations and bundle publications show "this identity is alive this week"; relays see the minute of the first session after `t_pub`, hence absences, and activation and restore moments; so does a card holder that polls `P` with the shared fetch credential (fetch reads return only week-coarse times, and `bundle_rank` shows an off-schedule publication in a week; §5.3, §22.8.7); a reconnect or revocation notice shows degree weakly over one or two days; an owner offline > ~2 weeks has no valid bundle, and after ~2–3 weeks stops receiving | the inbox must be paid, registered and published | activity-independent times (T31, §21.9); `uploaded_at` floored to the week for v3 reads and the expiry rounded up to the week for fetch reads (§22.8.7); the list cursor sealed under the relay key, so a listing carries neither the namespace's first-store hour nor its store count (review of S1, §21.9 point 4); a relay-side visibility floor for fetch listings (Q69) |
| L7-D11 | No one-time keys: a recorded first flight is decryptable by whoever later obtains the recipient's Olm identity key plus that generation's fallback and X-Wing secrets before their erasure (≤ 13 weeks after supersession; ≤ 31 d longer for a generation a held row references); Olm keeps at most 40 skipped keys per receiver chain and 5 chains per session, so a message overtaken by more than 40 later messages of its chain after peek ordering and the gap hold is lost; a message whose Olm key survives as a skipped key in a **live** session on a seized device is decryptable, and if it carried a pair root it opens that generation's pair envelopes (the P-7 exception; skipped keys of retired sessions are erased after 46 d; gone if Q67's age bound is adopted) | blind relays cannot allocate one-time keys; vodozemac's limits are not configurable | weekly rotation and erasure (§5.4); peek ordering and gap hold (§7.7); roots only in acknowledged sessions (§21.3); Q67 |
| L7-D12 | A restore without a contact backup loses contacts, sessions and pair keys: every envelope sent to the lost device (pair, provisional or intro) is unopenable (counted), its content lost, and the restored device cannot tell who wrote (Q50); a reset from a contact's side fails until the restored device holds a token and publishes a bundle (they fetch the lost device's bundles meanwhile) | no contact backup in P0; bundle generations are random, not seed-derived; tokens are not restorable (Phase 8 E11) | re-added cards and `reconnect`, which needs no token (§8.6, §21.7); contact resets after the new bundle; P1 encrypted contact-card backup (Q28) |
| L7-D13 | The inbox and bundle namespaces and their credential scopes are static per card and relay: a seized relay disk links the weeks of an inbox for as long as its blobs (≤ ~6 weeks) and registrations (≤ 3 weeks) live; after a slot-table change, blobs stored only on relays that left the ES are lost once their registrations lapse | no weekly rotation (C-4, §21.1, §21.2) | card rotation (Phase 13) |
| L7-D14 | Physical deletion can lag the timer while the process is frozen (alarm deferred by Doze), the database key needs an unlock, or the device rebooted without network (no boot receiver; the periodic job needs a network; §21.10) | Android scheduling | read filter, sweeps at open and in the consumer tail, inexact alarm |
| L7-D15 | For the 1 h and 1 d buckets a relay's early-expiry receipt is accepted (the σ = 3 d tolerance makes the bound vacuous); equivalent to withholding | Phase 7 clock tolerance | recipients list every relay (ADR-11); a relay-corrected bound in Phase 12 |
| L7-D16 | A card holder can spend its own token to redeem a write capability for my inbox and list and get it, with no relay collusion: inbound volume and timing, the arrival minute (listing, or `uploaded_at` to the minute on write-capability gets), `gen_id` and X-Wing ciphertext of every intro envelope it recognises with my card, and which conversations I have not answered; content sealed (§22.8.7) | write grants read at relays; the card opens the intro outer recognition | owner-bound read rule (Q36, re-asked; Phase 12 by default) |
| L7-D17 | A key change left undecided past its hold bound (≤ relay expiry, ≤ 30 d + 1 h) loses the held messages | holding is bounded | a persistent Phase 13 prompt |
| L7-D18 | A mnemonic thief using the official client presents the same Olm identity: contacts see no key-change warning | the identity is seed-derived (FR-1.2) | revocation v2 through contacts' inboxes; safety numbers do not help against the same key |
| L7-D19 | Secrets in native memory while unlocked: the Olm identity, the `ghost1` Ed25519 seed, `session_state_key` and transient session state (the vodozemac audit's Issue I, unresolved); the JVM heap holds only wrapped state | encryption needs the keys in memory | zeroised on lock and wipe (`ctxClose`, `zeroize`) |
| L7-D20 | Binding v3 is a transferable proof, if the recipient discloses the envelope and its keys, that the sender's identity initiated a session toward the recipient's namespace at a minute; content is not proven | the signature blocks impersonation at first contact (§3.4) | no deniability claim in the UI; Q51 alternatives |
| L7-D21 | Provisional envelopes stay attributable by a seizer of the right device until both devices erased `prov_env` (each by `prov_anchor_minute + 119 d` at the latest, earlier 45 d after a pair envelope under its newest root opened) and the first flight's generation is erased (§22.8.5) | provisional keys derive from a `reply_seed` carried in the intro envelope | scheduled erasure with a peer-independent bound (§6.7, §22.8.5) |
