| L7-D22 | Maturity gap to libsignal: one vodozemac audit of a 2022 revision, later code unaudited, no implementation-level verification of Olm, a smaller reorder window, a GHOST-built PQ layer (§2.9) | libsignal is excluded (AGPL-3.0) | exact pins, vendor tests, oracles, fuzzing, the model, the external review, the Phase 15 audit |
| L7-D23 (L-SRC) | Closed source: users cannot verify that the binary matches audited source; the DM cryptography ships as stripped native code; the updater's log check does not cover a first install, updates through a store client, or relay and issuer binaries run by operators, and operator independence gives no protection against a malicious vendor build (AD-V; §22.8.8) | closed-source distribution (ADR-37) | the ADR-38 package: published audits and model, auditor-reproduced builds, a binary transparency log with a standalone verifier for first installs, logged relay and issuer releases checked by operators, per-release native symbol files, a published specification and vectors |
| L7-D25 | A first message, request reply or reconnect hands the recipient my full card for good, including recipients who never accept, block me, or published a honeypot card: it can deposit, fetch my bundle, list my inbox with its own token, recognise my intro envelopes and, colluding with relays, tie my inbox and bundle namespaces to my `ghost1` identity (§22.8.7) | the recipient needs an address to reply; card v2 has one secret per identity | Phase 13 copy before the first send to a link-sourced or request card; card rotation (Phase 13); a reduced reply card (Q70) |
| L7-D27 | The store receipt's **upper** bound tolerates an over-promise of up to σ = 3 d for every bucket (`expiry ≤ ceil_hour(now + ttl) + σ`, §10.2 point 6): a relay may declare 73 h for a 1 h store or 10 d for the 7 d bucket and be believed, which through `RetentionPolicy.expiryRetainDay` can raise the client's own tombstone day (bounded by `ceil7`). Its premise `\|relay now − now\| ≤ σ` is enforced by nothing: with the device σ behind true time and the relay ahead, an honest receipt becomes `malformed_response`, and that category is not a definite non-application, so the op is left ambiguous instead of cleanly failed (added by the review of S2) | the same σ is the device's own clock tolerance and the lower bound's, so no tighter bound exists without a relay-corrected clock | a relay-corrected bound in Phase 12; before the S11 worlds the T32 device-offset model of §15.6 (`U[−3 d, +3 d]`, §21.2 point 10) is narrowed, or the relay skew set to 0, so that honest stores are never refused there |
| L7-D26 | Credentials name the ES slot, not the serving relay (§4.3, §21.2 point 2), so after a slot handover the outgoing operator can present the deposit, read and fetch secrets it saw at the slot's successor relay, until the card is rotated (added by the review of S1) | the credential layout is static per (card, slot) and carries no relay identity (§21.2 points 1 and 2) | handovers are schedule changes the owner controls; card rotation (Phase 13); binding the verifier to the relay's onion identity is the Phase 13 alternative |
| L7-D24 | The post-quantum residues PQ-R1 … PQ-R8 (§9.8): classical authentication; no PQ in the ratchet; first-flight FS bounded by generation erasure; network metadata not PQ-protected; young primitives; a GHOST-built composition until reviewed; Olm classical under the seal; card links over classical channels | ADR-36 | PQ-C; PQ-D, PQ-E, PQ-N in later phases |
| L7-D28 | **A message lives by its sender's timer, not by mine.** §10.5 says a message is always deleted by **its own** `timer_code`, so a peer may give the message it sends me a 30-day life and a 30-day unread cap on my device (`bucket(timer_code)`, §10.3) while my own UI reads "5 minutes" for that conversation — the recipient never clamps the incoming code against the conversation timer. It is the same rule that makes a timer change apply only to what follows it, and the same rule that stops my clock from shortening a message the sender meant to last; the cost is that the conversation timer is a *shared intention*, not a guarantee I enforce. Found by the S9 review, which verified the code is right and the consequence undeclared | §10.5's "its own `timer_code`", and R-T9's convergence, which makes a divergent timer short-lived | a UI that shows the message's own timer beside the conversation's (Phase 13); a recipient-side clamp was rejected — it would delete content the sender is still showing |
| L7-D29 | **A device away for more than two years loses that conversation's pair generation, silently.** SWEEP ratchets `K_w` forward to `week(now)` whatever the gap (§21.3 point 5) and `MAX_CATCH_UP_WEEKS` bounds only the work a wild forward clock could demand, but past that bound the in-keys are erased with nothing derived to replace them: the peer's later envelopes are then dropped as `unknown_seal`, counted and nothing else, until **my own next send** re-establishes — which nothing prompts me to make. RS2's 60 days and any real absence are far inside the bound; a wall clock set years ahead is not, and `now_e` never comes back (R-T7). Recorded by the S9 review, which found the previous bound of eight weeks failing *inside* RS2 | P-7 and T34 need the erasure, and keeping an unratchetable seed would keep the week it opens readable | S12's wiring may surface "this conversation needs to be re-established" from the `unknown_seal` counter |

### 11.6 "100% privacy": what this design maximises and why 100 % is not reachable (§22)

Maximised in Phase 9 (corrected by §22.8.8): no identity or sender at relays, and no message type, first contact or key change in envelope bytes and sizes (T26–T29); relays do see establishment, reset and reconnect attempts as bundle fetches linked to a deposit (L7-D6) and activation and restore moments (L7-D10); no reflex traffic (T28); read confinement (T31); every relay-stored byte PQ-sealed, except the card-keyed layers (bundles, the intro outer layer) when the card travelled over a classical channel (PQ-R8; T35); attribution forward secrecy extended to first contacts and replies (§6.4, §6.7); no library logging, clocks or per-install identifiers; wrapped session state; bounded local residue; deniable content. Not reachable, and therefore never claimed: timing and volume correlation without cover traffic (P2) and without Phase 12 session decoupling (L7-D1, L7-D2); the classical network layer (PQ-R4); classical authentication (PQ-R1); closed source and the vendor adversary AD-V, against which no guarantee holds for a build outside the log (L7-D23, §1.3); what card holders learn with their own tokens and by polling (L7-D3, L7-D10, L7-D16) and the sender's card handed to every recipient (L7-D25); the maturity gap (L7-D22). The owner's "100% privacy" is a design goal; `docs/STATUS.md` ("Afirmații permise în marketing/UI: niciuna") and plan:41 keep it out of any UI or marketing text (CP-10).

## 12. Contact establishment, requests, block (I9; ADR-30′, ADR-33′)

### 12.1 What is exchanged, and how (normative)

| Path | Carries | Starting trust | Relay-visible |
|---|---|---|---|
| **QR in person** (`source = qr_in_person`) | my card v2 (131 B, QR 9-L) | `verified` (the scanned `identity_pk` authenticates the Olm identity through the bundle signature and binding v3, §8.4) | nothing |
| **Link** `ghost://contact/…` shared through another channel (`source = link`) | my card | `unverified`; Phase 13 warns that the channel sees the link and could substitute it (T16-style warning) | nothing |
| **Intro envelope** from someone who holds my card | the sender's card inside the sealed intro layer (§6.4) | a **request** (§12.3) unless I already hold that `identity_pk` | a deposit into my inbox, like any message |
| Invite v2 (Phase 8) | **no card** (unchanged, T16) | — | — |

### 12.2 One-scan flow

1. A scans B's card → A's contact B (`verified`, no pinned Olm identity yet, `establishment = card_only`).
2. A's first send (SEND S4) keeps the message pending and starts the fetch of `P_B` (§5.5).
3. `BUNDLE` keeps B's verified bundle; `EST-READY` (§21.7) pins `OI_B`, stores B's X-Wing key, builds the initiator session (`sessionOutbound`), encrypts the pending message into an Olm PreKey message and seals it in **intro mode** (§6.4) with A's card v2, binding v3 and the session's `reply_seed` inside, and deposits it into `X_B`; the first message carries no root (a PreKey message, §21.3).
4. B's `RECV` opens the intro envelope (recognition hint, X-Wing decapsulation with generation `gen_id`, binding v3): unknown `identity_pk` → a request with the message, and B's responder session exists (R-SS1).
5. B accepts → contact A (`unverified`, or `verified` if B also scanned A in person). B's reply is an Olm normal message from B's responder session, sealed in **provisional mode** (§6.7) under `K_prov(responder → initiator)`, with no bundle fetch, and carries B's `seal_root` for A → B.
6. A's session is now acknowledged; A's next message is pair-sealed under B's root and carries A's `seal_root`; B seals in pair mode from its next message (§21.3). Before any root exists in a direction, that direction's normal messages are provisional.

- **R-E1.** Adding a card is local: it never registers, fetches or stores anything until the user sends (T31).
- **R-E2.** A card is parsed and verified (§3.3) before any storage; an own card, an invalid signature or the `identity_pk` of a `revoked` contact is refused locally.
- **R-E3.** Re-adding a card of an existing contact: an identical card is a no-op; any other difference is refused in Phase 9 (no card rotation).
- **R-E4 (simultaneous first contact).** When both sides send first, each receives a PreKey message while its own initiator session is unacknowledged; the session-selection rules R-SS1 … R-SS3 (§8.2) make both sides converge on one session within two round trips (suites RO5, RO8; state model EM-1).

### 12.3 Requests from unknown card holders (normative; C-24, Q26)

- An intro envelope whose card and binding verify, from an `identity_pk` not in `dm_contact`, becomes a **request** if fewer than `MAX_PENDING_REQUESTS = 64` requests exist; otherwise it is dropped (`request_overflow`).
- In the RECV transaction: insert `dm_contact(trust_state = 'request', source = 'request', card_env, request_until_day = day(now) + 7)`, pin the bound Olm identity (R6), create the responder session with `sessionInbound` (RECEIVING is allowed for requests, §8.2), store the content rows as for any contact (their own timers and unread caps apply). A request whose message is dropped at any later step is deleted in the same transaction (R-P2b); round 9's `reset_needed` requests from failed decryptions are withdrawn (§8.6).
- `acceptRequest(c)` → `unverified`, sending allowed. `rejectRequest(c)` or `request_until_day` → the contact, its session, pair keys and rows deleted in one transaction. `blockRequest(c)` → `blocked` (the identity is kept, so later requests from it are dropped).
- **No reflex (T28).** Neither arrival, nor accept, nor reject, nor expiry sends anything. The requester cannot tell whether it was seen, accepted or rejected until a user reply arrives.

### 12.4 Block, unblock, delete (FR-3.6, FR-4.7: local and documented)

| Action | Local effect | Relay-visible |
|---|---|---|
| `block(c)` | `trust_state = blocked`; card, both sessions, pair keys, offers, held rows, pending sends and scheduled controls deleted; the conversation's messages kept until their timers (the UI may delete them) | nothing; c's deposits still land in my inbox (charged to my allowance) and are dropped: pair hints no longer match (`unknown_seal`), intro envelopes hit the blocked identity |
| `unblock(c)` | the blocked row is deleted; the user must add c's card again (the card was erased, so unblocking cannot silently restore an address) | nothing |
| `deleteContact(c)` | every row of c deleted; `X_c` and `P_c` recorded in `dm_orphan_namespace` and removed by SWEEP once their ops are released (§21.12); a later intro envelope from c is a new request | nothing |

The Phase 13 UI states what block does **not** do: it does not stop c from depositing, it does not tell c, and it cannot delete copies on c's device (FR-4.7).

### 12.5 Deferred in Phase 9 (G-12)

- **Cut-off (card rotation)** (C-16, Q27): stopping a card holder from depositing needs a new card index, the redistribution of the new card to the remaining contacts, and a contact backup so that a restore knows the index. Deferred to Phase 13 with the P1 encrypted contact-card backup. Phase 9 keeps `card_index` in the card, `CardLabels` indexed, and a `dm_self.card_index` column fixed at 0 by a trigger that Phase 13 drops. Old-index registrations lapse by themselves within two weeks once not renewed, so no relay replacement rule is needed (SC-1).
- **Invite v3** (C-18, Q32): the card inside an invite is Phase 13 UX; ADR-05's optional contact card stays open, not declined. An invite carries no identity by design (T16) and its drop carries exactly one blob at a pre-drawn time (ADR-24), so Phase 9 changes neither.
- **The Phase 8 drop outer shape** (C-17, Q18): drops keep the ADR-24 `DropSeal` format; aligning them with the DM shape would reopen the T2 NI-2/NI-3 twins just closed without exemption (F8 §19.29 point 7). A Phase 12 item with Elligator 2 or an equivalent.

### 12.6 Limits (NFR-14)

`MAX_CONTACTS = 1 000` (bounds the hint work, R-S5; Q37); `MAX_PENDING_REQUESTS = 64`; `MAX_HELD_PER_CONTACT = 256`; blocked rows ≤ 10 000; `MAX_BODY_BYTES = 61 440`. The API returns `LIMIT_REACHED`; nothing is silently dropped except requests and held rows beyond their caps, each counted.

---

## 13. Android and Rust modules, `:sync` and `:entitlement` extensions, schema v4 and v5, background (I10; ADR-33′, ADR-34′, ADR-35)

### 13.1 Module layout (normative)

| Module / package | Contents | Depends on |
|---|---|---|
| `client-core/dm` (Rust, `ghost-client-dm`; new, §2.3) | derivations (`DmLabels`, `CardLabels`), card v2, bundle v2, the four-mode seal, L0 codec and TLVs, Olm sessions over vodozemac (throwaway accounts, dehydrated-pickle encoder), X-Wing and PQ-C, binding v3, safety number v1, state wrapping, result codes, the JNI export behind `jni-bridge` | `vodozemac`, `libcrux-*`, RustCrypto primitives, `ed25519-dalek` (G-R1) |
| `:crypto-bridge` `org.ghost.crypto.bridge` | `DmNative` (the closed JNI surface, §2.6), `DmResult` (code decoder), `DmSessions` (the typed session facade, §2.6), `DmKeys` (DM context lifecycle: `ctxOpen`/`ctxClose`, `CRYPTO_UNAVAILABLE`), `Recognition` (hint matching with recognition keys, R-S5), `CredentialV3` builder and verifier (§4.3), `RelaySets.forNamespace` (§5.2) | `libghost_client_net.so` (the only module that references `DmNative`, G-R6); `:sync` API (`SyncTransaction`); BouncyCastle HMAC for hints |
| `:messaging` `org.ghost.messaging.api` | `Messaging` facade, value types, `MessagingListener`, `NotificationPort`, `MediaPort`, `MessagingStatus` | — |
| `…messaging.engine` | `DmActor` (single thread), the transactions of §7.2 (`SendStep`, `ReceiveStep`, `BundleStep`, `EstablishmentSteps`, `OutcomeStep`, `KeyDecision`, `HeldReplay`, `DisplayStep`, `OwnerWeek`, `Activation`, `Keeper`, `RegistrationRows`, `Sweeper`), `PeekOrder` (§7.7), `SessionSlots` (R-SS0 … R-SS5), `Requests`, `Revocation`, `Reconnect`, `DmConsumerTail` | `:crypto-bridge`, `:sync` API, `:identity` |
| `…messaging.store` | DAOs over `dm_*` and `olm_*` (every read applies the expiry filter, §10.7) | `:storage`, `:sync` API |
| `…messaging.android` | `ExpiryAlarm` + `ExpiryAlarmReceiver` (non-exported, no extras), `KeystoreContentKeyWrapper`, `KeystoreStateKeyWrapper` | Android |
| `:storage` | migrations 4 and 5 (§13.6), `Checkpoint.truncate()` (busy-checked), `StorageFullException` mapping in both executors | — |
| `:sync` | the additions of §13.3, including `Inbox.peek` and `Inbox.claimOne` | — |
| `:entitlement` | the changes of §13.4 | `:sync` |
| `client-core/net` (Rust) | credential v3 in `capability_header` and T21; `registerKey`; exact TTL refusal; the receipt upper bound; the DM JNI export (`ghost_client_dm::export_jni!()`) | `ghost-client-dm` |
| relay | `RegisterKey`, credential v3, `keys.redb`, `DepositLedger`, exact TTLs, capture | — |
| `:app` | `MessagingWiring`: `Inbox.setListener(actor hints)`, `SyncController.setConsumerTail(tail)`, activation and restore hooks with `:entitlement`, the receiver in the manifest | all |

Rules: no class in `:messaging` or `:crypto-bridge` references `org.ghost.network`, `java.net`, `javax.net`, sockets, HTTP or `kotlinx.coroutines` (gate G-DM1): the DM layer never does network I/O, and never inside a transaction (F7:1418); `ghost-client-dm` has no network, async runtime, logging or clock (G-R4, G-R8). Typed catches only (`sync-no-catch-all.sh`). No coroutines (ADR-20).

### 13.2 UI-less facade for Phase 13 (normative signatures)

```kotlin
interface Messaging {
    fun myCard(): CardExport                                   // bytes + ghost://contact/ link; no network
    fun addContact(card: ByteArray, source: CardSource, name: String?): AddContactResult
        // ADDED | INVALID_CARD | OWN_CARD | ALREADY_KNOWN | REVOKED_IDENTITY | LIMIT_REACHED
    fun contacts(): List<ContactView>                          // id, name, TrustState, Establishment, resetNeeded; no times
    fun conversation(contact: ContactId, limit: Int, before: MessageCursor?): List<MessageView>
    fun send(contact: ContactId, text: String, quote: MessageId?): SendResult
        // QUEUED | KEY_CHANGED | BLOCKED | REVOKED | REQUEST_NOT_ACCEPTED | TOO_LARGE | NO_REPLICAS
        // | LIMIT_REACHED | STORAGE_FULL | CRYPTO_UNAVAILABLE
    fun react(target: MessageId, grapheme: String?): SendResult
    fun setTimer(contact: ContactId, timer: Timer): SendResult            // OFF, MIN_5, HOUR_1, DAY_1, DAYS_7, DAYS_30
    fun markDisplayed(ids: List<MessageId>)
    fun retry(message: MessageId): SendResult                  // FAILED only; re-encrypts the same L0; DEADLINE_PASSED after the deadline
    fun safetyNumber(contact: ContactId): SafetyNumberView?    // digits + scannable bytes
    fun pendingSafetyNumber(contact: ContactId): SafetyNumberView?        // while CHANGED
    fun markVerified(contact: ContactId, scanned: ByteArray): VerifyResult
    fun markVerifiedManually(contact: ContactId): VerifyResult
    fun acknowledgeKeyChange(contact: ContactId): Boolean
    fun verifyKeyChange(contact: ContactId, scanned: ByteArray): VerifyResult
    fun resetSession(contact: ContactId): SendResult
    fun reconnect(contacts: List<ContactId>): Int               // accepted and scheduled (§8.6)
    fun acceptRequest(contact: ContactId); fun rejectRequest(contact: ContactId); fun blockRequest(contact: ContactId)
    fun block(contact: ContactId); fun unblock(contact: ContactId); fun deleteContact(contact: ContactId)
    fun issueRevocation(reason: RevocationReason); fun notifyContactsOfRevocation(): Int
    fun status(): MessagingStatus                              // counts only: drops by reason, unknown_seal, held, requests,
                                                               // reset_needed, poisoned, pending registrations
    fun setListener(listener: MessagingListener?)              // coarse, content-free change hints
    fun setNotificationPort(port: NotificationPort?)           // onUnreadChanged(count: Int) only
}
```

Every method posts to the DM actor and returns after its transaction commits; none performs network I/O; `toString()` of every value type is redacted (T3). `MessageView` holds decrypted text only in memory.

`CRYPTO_UNAVAILABLE` means "the DM context is closed (locked, wiped) or `libghost_client_net.so` could not be loaded" (§2.7); it is a result, never a crash loop.

### 13.3 `:sync` additions (normative; ADR-34, X-12)

```kotlin
enum class TtlBucket(val seconds: Int) { HOUR_1(3_600), DAY_1(86_400), DAYS_7(604_800), DAYS_30(2_592_000), DAYS_90(7_776_000) }

/** Who obtains write access. LOCAL: the consumer installs bearer credentials; never redeemed. */
enum class CapabilitySource { REDEEM, LOCAL }

interface Namespaces {
    fun register(tx: SyncTransaction, namespace: NamespaceId, consumer: Consumer, relays: Set<RelayId>, listen: Boolean,
                 sendDelay: SendDelay = SendDelay.DEFAULT, capabilitySource: CapabilitySource = CapabilitySource.REDEEM)
    // setRelays, setListening, remove: unchanged; setSendDelay refuses OFF for Consumer.DM
}

class InboundBlob(val namespace: NamespaceId, val hash: BlobHash, ciphertext: ByteArray,
                  val relayExpiryHour: Long, val offers: Int)

class CapabilityNeed(val relay: RelayId, val namespace: NamespaceId, val kind: CapabilityKind,
                     val reason: Reason, val source: CapabilitySource)

enum class KeyKind { READ, DEPOSIT, FETCH }                             // FETCH: §22.8.7
interface Registrations {
    /** In the caller's transaction; idempotent on (relay, namespace, period, kind); a different verifier throws. */
    fun request(tx: SyncTransaction, relay: RelayId, namespace: NamespaceId, periodId: Long, kind: KeyKind, verifier: ByteArray)
    /** Rows of (relay, namespace) with period >= fromPeriod return to pending with new request ids (healing rule, §4.8). */
    fun rearm(tx: SyncTransaction, relay: RelayId, namespace: NamespaceId, fromPeriod: Long)
    fun forget(tx: SyncTransaction, relay: RelayId, namespace: NamespaceId, beforePeriod: Long)
    fun pendingCount(): Int
}

/** Runs on the job thread after a BACKGROUND session's lanes and redeem hold and after a QUIET run's issuer call, both
 *  after the transport closed. Local work only; returns by the deadline. */
fun interface ConsumerTail { fun run(kind: SessionKind, deadlineMonotonicMillis: Long) }
// SyncController gains: fun setConsumerTail(tail: ConsumerTail?)   (one slot, set at wiring, like the participant)

/** Read-only snapshot of fetched rows of a consumer (no offer counter change); for ordering only (§7.7). */
class PeekedBlob(val namespace: NamespaceId, val hash: BlobHash, ciphertext: ByteArray, val relayExpiryHour: Long)
// Inbox gains: fun peek(consumer: Consumer, limit: Int): List<PeekedBlob>
//              fun claimOne(consumer: Consumer, namespace: NamespaceId, hash: BlobHash): InboundBlob?   (claims that row if still fetched)
```

1. **`TtlBucket.HOUR_1`**, schema v4 (§13.6), the §10.4 window rules, and S-F with short TTLs.
2. **`InboundBlob.relayExpiryHour` and `offers`.** `recordFetched` stores `relay_expiry_hour = floor_hour(E)` beside the ciphertext; `claim` returns it and the committed offer count; `markConsumed` sets the column to NULL in the same statement.
3. **`CapabilitySource`** on `Namespaces.register`, stored in `sync_namespace.capability_source`; `CapabilityNeed.source` reports it. The entitlement planner never redeems a LOCAL need (§13.4). A LOCAL namespace without a usable credential parks its deliveries in `wait_capability` exactly like a REDEEM one; a new credential generation re-arms them.
4. **Needs rule (Q31).** `CapabilityStore.needed()` reports WRITE MISSING also where a pending `relay_key_registration` row exists for (relay, namespace) and no write capability does, with the same "active relay in the namespace's set" joins.
5. **`Registrations` on the work lane.** A pending row is due when `inflight = 0`, `next_attempt_minute ≤ now`, and a usable, unexpired write capability of week c exists for (relay, namespace) with `period_id ∈ {c, c + 1}`; at most 8 per pass; in HIGH mode at the pair's events, in STANDARD when due. The step follows the store pattern: a lease transaction, `registerKey` on the namespace's circuit, a result transaction. `OK` → `done` with `valid_until_hour`; `WRONG_PERIOD` or `unauthorized` → pending, re-armed by the next capability generation (the M2 idea); transient categories → Phase 7 backoff and breakers; a crash inside the call → lease normalization (M1); an identical registration is idempotent at the relay. Registrations never touch the read lane (T19; `T19BasicTest` gains registrations in run (b); mutant MC-13). The RPC maps onto the existing JNI error categories (`ErrorPolicyTest` stays at 21).
6. **`ConsumerTail` (G-8, SC-7).** `SyncRuntime` calls the tail (a) after a background session's lanes ended, the Q29 redeem hold ended and the transport closed, and (b) after a quiet run's issuer call returned and its transport closed, with `deadline = job deadline − 20 s`; not in foreground sessions (the listener and the facade drive the actor there). The tail posts a drain request to the DM actor and waits for it; the actor stays the only DM writer. The tail has no transport access. Because it runs after the close, relay session close times and issuer-call times cannot depend on inbound volume (NI-DM1 compares close times; mutant PM-25). **`SessionParticipantTest` gains:** a tail that runs to its deadline does not delay the job's end past the deadline; in a quiet run the tail starts only after the issuer call returned and the transport closed; in a background session only after the lanes, the redeem hold and the transport close.
7. **Credential selection.** For `list` and `get`, a usable READ credential of (relay, namespace) is preferred and a write capability is the fallback; `store` uses the write-kind credential (a write capability, or a deposit credential stored as `kind = 'write'` for a LOCAL namespace); `check` uses the write-kind credential when present, else the READ credential. S3 verifies `CapabilityStore`'s current selection and changes it where it differs. The engine treats all tokens as opaque; `client-core` parses them (T21).
8. **`SendDelay.OFF` refused for `Consumer.DM`** by `Namespaces.setSendDelay`/`register` and by a v4 trigger (mutant PM-14).
9. **Checkpoint.** `Gc.checkpoint` uses `Checkpoint.truncate()` (busy checked, §10.7).
10. **`Inbox.peek` and `Inbox.claimOne` (revised §22).** `peek` reads up to `limit` fetched rows of a consumer in one read transaction and changes nothing; `claimOne` claims one named row if it is still `fetched`, with the offer rules of `claim` (the offer counter commits before the hand-off, a twice-offered row is returned alone). The Phase 7 exactly-once argument is unaffected: claiming and consuming keep their transactions. The Phase 7 exhaustive suite gains both calls (V-11).

### 13.4 `:entitlement` changes (normative)

- **`RedeemPlanner`** skips every need with `source = LOCAL` (counted `LOCAL_SKIPPED`, never `ENTITLEMENT_NEEDED`; mutant PM-1b).
- **Activation and restore hooks (RS K20).** The activation finalization (trial and genesis) and `Entitlement.restore` call an `IdentityActivationHook` after their own transactions; `:app` wires it to `Messaging.onActivated()` / `onRestored()`, which run ACTIVATE. This closes the F8 §8.3 step 4 gap ("prekey and inbox namespaces registered at activation").
- **Phase 8 Q34 (redeem hold for read needs), re-evaluated.** A DM owner whose capabilities all lapsed has pending registration rows for the current week (§4.8), which count as write work (§13.3 item 4), so the Q29 hold arms and the owner recovers in the background. Other listen-only namespaces (drops) keep Q34's declared behaviour.
- **NI-K** stays identical: its worlds have no DM activity. The DM world adds JD8 on an `EntHarness`-based variant (§15.1): a client's redemption and registration trace is identical with 0 and 30 contacts and any message volume.

### 13.5 State machines (summary; each has a JVM test of every legal and illegal transition)

| Machine | States | Driven by |
|---|---|---|
| Outgoing message | queued → sent / degraded / failed / indeterminate; failed → queued (user retry) | SEND, OUTCOME, RESEND, SWEEP |
| Incoming message | unread → read | RECV, DISPLAY, SWEEP |
| Contact trust | §8.3 diagram | RECV, KEYDEC, user actions |
| Establishment | card_only → fetching → ready (only through `EST-READY`, also when an inbound PreKey built the session first; §21.7); fetching → unreachable after three attempts → fetching (user retry); ready → fetching (reset, reconnect, stale session at a user send) | EST-START, BUNDLE, EST-READY, EST-END |
| Pending send | waiting → encrypted and enqueued (`EST-READY`, §21.7) / failed (`fail_after_hour`) | SEND, `EST-READY`, SWEEP |
| Key registration | pending (⇄ inflight) → done → deleted after `valid_until_hour`; done → pending (healing) | REGROWS, work lane |
| Pair roots | offered → acknowledged (root erased) → superseded (stops advancing; keys erased by window) | SEND, RECV, KEYDEC, reconnect, periodic replacement (13 weeks; 4 with PQ-C) |
| Session slots (R-SS0 … R-SS5) | none → current (outbound at `EST-READY`, or inbound new session) → previous (R-SS1 demotion, `UNACK_MAX` re-establishment) → retired at `retire_day`; current ⇄ previous (R-SS2 swap); both deleted at reset, KEYDEC acceptance, block, revocation | EST-READY, RECV, HELD, KEYDEC, SWEEP |
| Bundle generation | current → superseded (next OWNER-WEEK) → erased at `delete_after_day` (not while a held row references it) | OWNER-WEEK, ACTIVATE, SWEEP |
| Owner week | not published → published (per ISO week) | OWNER-WEEK |
| Scheduled control | due → sent (SEND) / cancelled (block, delete, user send first) | Reconnect, Revocation, SWEEP |

### 13.6 Schema v4 and v5 (normative; migration 4 = steps 0–4 in S3, migration 5 = steps 5–6 in S4b; each one transaction, append-only, fail-closed; G-9, E-5)

Migration 4 runs in the helper's transaction (R-code F1). It first asserts `PRAGMA legacy_alter_table` is 0 (off) and fails closed otherwise. **Migration 5** runs in its own transaction after migration 4 and creates only the messaging and Olm tables of steps 5 and 6, so the library-independent sync work of S1–S3 lands before the DM library code (revised §22; round 9 landed the whole DDL in migration 4, §21.13 point 3). Steps, in order:

**Step 0: guard and drop the unused v1 DM tables** (no production writer: `Repositories.kt` writes only `identity`, `invite_nonces`, `revocations`).

```sql
CREATE TABLE v4_migration_guard (row_count INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (row_count = 0));
INSERT INTO v4_migration_guard(row_count) SELECT count(*) FROM messages;
INSERT INTO v4_migration_guard(row_count) SELECT count(*) FROM conversations;
INSERT INTO v4_migration_guard(row_count) SELECT count(*) FROM contacts;
INSERT INTO v4_migration_guard(row_count) SELECT count(*) FROM signal_state;
DROP TABLE v4_migration_guard;
DROP INDEX idx_messages_conversation;
DROP INDEX idx_messages_expiry;
DROP TABLE messages;
DROP TABLE conversations;
DROP TABLE contacts;
DROP TABLE signal_state;
```

`devices` (P2), `revocations` (Phase 3 writer), `media`/`media_blobs` (Phase 11), `channels`/`channel_pseudonyms`/`mls_state`/`posts` (Phase 10) stay. S3 confirms the v1 index names from `Schema.kt:88-89`.

**Step 1: rebuild `outbox_op` and `outbox_delivery` to admit TTL 3600** (SQLite cannot alter a CHECK; the v2 CHECK is frozen, `Schema.kt:25-27, 245`).

1. `CREATE TABLE outbox_op_v4 (…)`: the v2 definition verbatim (`Schema.kt:240-253`) except `ttl_seconds IN (3600, 86400, 604800, 2592000, 7776000)`. `outbox_op` is a rowid table (no INTEGER PRIMARY KEY alias).
2. `CREATE TABLE outbox_delivery_v4 (…)`: the v2 definition verbatim (`Schema.kt:259-273`) with `REFERENCES outbox_op_v4(operation_id) ON DELETE CASCADE`; WITHOUT ROWID.
3. Copy with explicit column lists (E-5):
   ```sql
   INSERT INTO outbox_op_v4(rowid, operation_id, namespace_id, blob_hash, ciphertext, ttl_seconds, not_before_minute,
                            deadline_hour, required_operators, outcome, released)
   SELECT rowid, operation_id, namespace_id, blob_hash, ciphertext, ttl_seconds, not_before_minute,
          deadline_hour, required_operators, outcome, released
   FROM outbox_op;
   INSERT INTO outbox_delivery_v4(operation_id, relay_id, state, attempts, next_attempt_minute, inflight, lease_hour,
                                  copy_hour, ack_minute, strikes)
   SELECT operation_id, relay_id, state, attempts, next_attempt_minute, inflight, lease_hour, copy_hour, ack_minute, strikes
   FROM outbox_delivery;
   ```
   The new tables have no triggers yet, so `outbox_delivery_insert` (which refuses non-pending rows) does not fire. Rowids are preserved because the v3 guard `outbox_op_rowid` protects them.
4. `DROP TABLE outbox_delivery;` The child goes first: with `foreign_keys = ON`, dropping the parent first would cascade-delete the deliveries (an implicit DELETE runs FK actions but no triggers).
5. `DROP TABLE outbox_op;` This removes its 5 v2 triggers and the 2 v3 guards with it.
6. `ALTER TABLE outbox_op_v4 RENAME TO outbox_op; ALTER TABLE outbox_delivery_v4 RENAME TO outbox_delivery;` With `legacy_alter_table = OFF`, SQLite rewrites the FK reference in the delivery table to `outbox_op`.
7. Recreate `idx_outbox_op_open`, `idx_outbox_delivery_due`, `idx_outbox_delivery_relay`; then the 8 v2 triggers (`outbox_op_immutable`, `outbox_op_payload`, `outbox_op_outcome`, `outbox_op_release`, `outbox_op_delete`, `outbox_delivery_insert`, `outbox_delivery_state`, `outbox_delivery_copy`); then the 2 v3 guards (`outbox_op_no_replace`, `outbox_op_rowid`). Each text is one Kotlin constant shared with migrations 2 and 3, so it cannot drift.
8. `PRAGMA foreign_key_check` must return no row; otherwise the migration throws and the transaction rolls back.

**Test (G-9; corrected by §21.13).** S3 first lists every `sqlite_master` object whose SQL names `outbox_op` or `outbox_delivery`; the migration test asserts that trigger and index rows are identical by (type, name, sql), that the two tables are equal by `PRAGMA table_info`, `foreign_key_list` and `index_list` plus SQL normalized for identifier quoting, with the `ttl_seconds` CHECK as the only difference, and that every row and `outbox_op` rowid is preserved on fixtures holding ops and deliveries in every state, in-flight leases and every outcome.

**Step 2: relay expiry of fetched blobs** (§10.3).

```sql
ALTER TABLE inbox_blob ADD COLUMN relay_expiry_hour INTEGER
  CHECK (relay_expiry_hour IS NULL OR (typeof(relay_expiry_hour) = 'integer' AND relay_expiry_hour % 3600 = 0));
CREATE TRIGGER inbox_blob_relay_expiry_insert BEFORE INSERT ON inbox_blob
WHEN NEW.relay_expiry_hour IS NOT NULL
BEGIN SELECT RAISE(ABORT, 'relay expiry is kept only while fetched'); END;
CREATE TRIGGER inbox_blob_relay_expiry_update BEFORE UPDATE OF state, relay_expiry_hour ON inbox_blob
WHEN NEW.relay_expiry_hour IS NOT NULL AND NEW.state <> 'fetched'
BEGIN SELECT RAISE(ABORT, 'relay expiry is kept only while fetched'); END;
```

**Step 3: capability source and the DM send-delay rule.**

```sql
ALTER TABLE sync_namespace ADD COLUMN capability_source TEXT NOT NULL DEFAULT 'redeem'
  CHECK (capability_source IN ('redeem', 'local'));
CREATE TRIGGER sync_namespace_dm_delay BEFORE UPDATE OF send_delay ON sync_namespace
WHEN NEW.consumer = 'dm' AND NEW.send_delay = 'off'
BEGIN SELECT RAISE(ABORT, 'DM namespaces cannot turn the send delay off'); END;
CREATE TRIGGER sync_namespace_dm_delay_insert BEFORE INSERT ON sync_namespace
WHEN NEW.consumer = 'dm' AND NEW.send_delay = 'off'
BEGIN SELECT RAISE(ABORT, 'DM namespaces cannot turn the send delay off'); END;
```

**Step 4: key registrations.** **Corectură (review of S2, 2026-09-19):** `kind` admite și `'fetch'`. §22.8.7 a introdus tipul FETCH pentru namespace-ul bundle (`P`: FETCH, §4.8) și l-a implementat capăt-la-capăt în S2 (`BearerKind::Fetch`, `KeyKind.FETCH`), dar lista de secțiuni corectate în §22.8.7 nu conținea §13.6, așa că `CHECK (kind IN ('read', 'deposit'))` interzicea exact rândurile pe care migrarea 4 trebuie să le păstreze. Orice mapare de `kind` scrisă de S3 folosește cele trei valori.

```sql
CREATE TABLE relay_key_registration (
  relay_id            INTEGER NOT NULL REFERENCES relay_directory(relay_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  namespace_id        BLOB NOT NULL REFERENCES sync_namespace(namespace_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  period_id           INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(period_id) = 'integer' AND period_id >= 0),
  kind                TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (kind IN ('read', 'deposit', 'fetch')),
  verifier            BLOB NOT NULL CHECK (length(verifier) = 32),
  request_id          BLOB NOT NULL CHECK (length(request_id) = 16),
  state               TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (state IN ('pending', 'done')),
  inflight            INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0 CHECK (inflight IN (0, 1)),
  lease_hour          INTEGER CHECK (lease_hour IS NULL OR (typeof(lease_hour) = 'integer' AND lease_hour % 3600 = 0)),
  attempts            INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0 CHECK (typeof(attempts) = 'integer' AND attempts >= 0),
  next_attempt_minute INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(next_attempt_minute) = 'integer' AND next_attempt_minute % 60 = 0),
  valid_until_hour    INTEGER CHECK (valid_until_hour IS NULL OR (typeof(valid_until_hour) = 'integer' AND valid_until_hour % 3600 = 0)),
  CHECK ((state = 'done') = (valid_until_hour IS NOT NULL)),
  CHECK (inflight = 0 OR lease_hour IS NOT NULL),
  CHECK (state = 'pending' OR inflight = 0),
  PRIMARY KEY (relay_id, namespace_id, period_id, kind)
) WITHOUT ROWID;
```

**Step 5 (migration 5): messaging and Olm tables.** Every table: random keys (WITHOUT ROWID) or a random generation id, no created or last-activity column, time columns `*_minute` (Unix seconds, `% 60 = 0`), `*_hour` (`% 3600 = 0`) or `*_day` (day index) with `typeof = 'integer'`; content columns are envelopes `nonce(12) ‖ ChaCha20-Poly1305(content_key, AD = the row key)` (28 bytes of overhead); secret-bearing session columns (`*_env` of `olm_*`, `dm_pair_key.open_env`, `dm_pair_offer.root_env` and `offer_env`, `dm_held.prov_env`) are envelopes under `session_state_key` (§2.5). The round-9 `sig_*` tables and `dm_self.registration_id` are not created.

```sql
CREATE TABLE dm_self (
  id                  INTEGER PRIMARY KEY CHECK (id = 1),
  card_index          INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(card_index) = 'integer' AND card_index BETWEEN 0 AND 65535),
  seen_key            BLOB NOT NULL CHECK (length(seen_key) = 32),
  content_key_wrapped BLOB NOT NULL CHECK (length(content_key_wrapped) BETWEEN 40 AND 256),
  state_key_wrapped   BLOB NOT NULL CHECK (length(state_key_wrapped) BETWEEN 40 AND 256),                   -- §2.5
  bundle_week         INTEGER CHECK (bundle_week IS NULL OR (typeof(bundle_week) = 'integer' AND bundle_week >= 0)),
  expiry_clock_minute INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(expiry_clock_minute) = 'integer' AND expiry_clock_minute % 60 = 0),
  accept_after_minute INTEGER CHECK (accept_after_minute IS NULL
                                     OR (typeof(accept_after_minute) = 'integer' AND accept_after_minute % 60 = 0)),
  bundle_published_week INTEGER CHECK (bundle_published_week IS NULL
                                       OR (typeof(bundle_published_week) = 'integer' AND bundle_published_week >= 0))   -- §21.8
);

CREATE TABLE dm_own_namespace (
  namespace_id BLOB PRIMARY KEY NOT NULL CHECK (length(namespace_id) = 32),
  role         TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (role IN ('inbox', 'bundle')),
  card_index   INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(card_index) = 'integer' AND card_index BETWEEN 0 AND 65535),
  UNIQUE (role, card_index)
) WITHOUT ROWID;

CREATE TABLE dm_contact (
  contact_id        BLOB PRIMARY KEY NOT NULL CHECK (length(contact_id) = 16),
  identity_pk       BLOB NOT NULL UNIQUE CHECK (length(identity_pk) = 32),
  card_env          BLOB CHECK (card_env IS NULL OR length(card_env) = 159),              -- card v2 (131) + 28
  card_index        INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(card_index) = 'integer' AND card_index BETWEEN 0 AND 65535),
  pinned_identity   BLOB CHECK (pinned_identity IS NULL OR length(pinned_identity) = 32),   -- Olm identity
  pending_identity  BLOB CHECK (pending_identity IS NULL OR length(pending_identity) = 32),
  bundle_week_seen  INTEGER CHECK (bundle_week_seen IS NULL OR (typeof(bundle_week_seen) = 'integer' AND bundle_week_seen >= 0)),
  trust_state       TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (trust_state IN ('request', 'unverified', 'verified', 'changed', 'blocked', 'revoked')),
  source            TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (source IN ('qr_in_person', 'link', 'request')),
  establishment     TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (establishment IN ('card_only', 'fetching', 'ready', 'unreachable')),
  fetch_attempts    INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0 CHECK (fetch_attempts BETWEEN 0 AND 3),                                -- §21.7
  fetch_listen_until_minute INTEGER CHECK (fetch_listen_until_minute IS NULL
                                           OR (typeof(fetch_listen_until_minute) = 'integer' AND fetch_listen_until_minute % 60 = 0)),
  reset_needed      INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0 CHECK (reset_needed IN (0, 1)),
  verified_fp       BLOB CHECK (verified_fp IS NULL OR length(verified_fp) = 32),
  name_env          BLOB CHECK (name_env IS NULL OR length(name_env) BETWEEN 29 AND 284),
  request_until_day INTEGER CHECK (request_until_day IS NULL OR (typeof(request_until_day) = 'integer' AND request_until_day >= 0)),
  pq_announced_week INTEGER CHECK (pq_announced_week IS NULL
                                   OR (typeof(pq_announced_week) = 'integer' AND pq_announced_week >= 0)),    -- PQ-C, §9.4
  CHECK ((trust_state = 'changed') = (pending_identity IS NOT NULL)),
  CHECK ((trust_state = 'request') = (request_until_day IS NOT NULL)),
  CHECK (trust_state IN ('blocked', 'revoked') OR card_env IS NOT NULL),
  CHECK (trust_state <> 'changed' OR pinned_identity IS NOT NULL),
  CHECK (pending_identity IS NULL OR pending_identity IS NOT pinned_identity),
  CHECK ((establishment = 'fetching') = (fetch_listen_until_minute IS NOT NULL))
) WITHOUT ROWID;

CREATE TABLE dm_pair_key (           -- in: keys I accept (window); out: the key I seal with
  contact_id BLOB NOT NULL REFERENCES dm_contact(contact_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  direction  TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (direction IN ('in', 'out')),
  root_gen   INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(root_gen) = 'integer' AND root_gen BETWEEN 0 AND 65535),
  week       INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(week) = 'integer' AND week >= 0),
  recog_key  BLOB NOT NULL CHECK (length(recog_key) = 32),                                  -- R_sel, clear (R-S6)
  open_env   BLOB NOT NULL CHECK (length(open_env) = 60),                                   -- K_w under session_state_key
  PRIMARY KEY (contact_id, direction, root_gen, week)
) WITHOUT ROWID;

CREATE TABLE dm_pair_offer (         -- my root for c → me, kept only until its first key is computed and acknowledged
  contact_id   BLOB NOT NULL REFERENCES dm_contact(contact_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  root_gen     INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(root_gen) = 'integer' AND root_gen BETWEEN 0 AND 65535),
  root_env     BLOB CHECK (root_env IS NULL OR length(root_env) = 60),                     -- NULL once erased
  offer_env    BLOB CHECK (offer_env IS NULL OR length(offer_env) IN (70, 1194)),           -- the offered TLV (seal_root or seal_root_pq)
  base_week    INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(base_week) = 'integer' AND base_week >= 0),
  acknowledged INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0 CHECK (acknowledged IN (0, 1)),
  PRIMARY KEY (contact_id, root_gen)
) WITHOUT ROWID;

CREATE TABLE dm_conversation (
  conversation_id BLOB PRIMARY KEY NOT NULL CHECK (length(conversation_id) = 16),
  contact_id      BLOB NOT NULL UNIQUE REFERENCES dm_contact(contact_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  timer_code      INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 4 CHECK (timer_code BETWEEN 0 AND 5),          -- 7 d (ADR-27 point 7)
  timer_version   INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0 CHECK (typeof(timer_version) = 'integer' AND timer_version >= 0)
) WITHOUT ROWID;

CREATE TABLE dm_message (
  message_id             BLOB PRIMARY KEY NOT NULL CHECK (length(message_id) = 16),
  conversation_id        BLOB NOT NULL REFERENCES dm_conversation(conversation_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  direction              TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (direction IN ('in', 'out')),
  kind                   INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (kind IN (1, 2)),                           -- TEXT, TIMER_SET notice
  author_minute          INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(author_minute) = 'integer' AND author_minute % 60 = 0),
  sort_minute            INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(sort_minute) = 'integer' AND sort_minute % 60 = 0 AND sort_minute <= author_minute),
  minute_index           INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (minute_index BETWEEN 0 AND 65535),
  timer_code             INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (timer_code BETWEEN 0 AND 5),
  body_env               BLOB CHECK (body_env IS NULL OR length(body_env) BETWEEN 30 AND 61485),   -- flags ‖ quote_ref? ‖ body; no quote_ref column (§21.8)
  state                  TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (state IN ('queued', 'sent', 'degraded', 'failed', 'indeterminate', 'unread', 'read')),
  expire_at_minute       INTEGER CHECK (expire_at_minute IS NULL OR (typeof(expire_at_minute) = 'integer' AND expire_at_minute % 60 = 0)),
  unread_deadline_minute INTEGER CHECK (unread_deadline_minute IS NULL
                                        OR (typeof(unread_deadline_minute) = 'integer' AND unread_deadline_minute % 60 = 0)),
  delete_at_minute       INTEGER CHECK (delete_at_minute IS NULL OR (typeof(delete_at_minute) = 'integer' AND delete_at_minute % 60 = 0)),
  CHECK ((direction = 'out') = (state IN ('queued', 'sent', 'degraded', 'failed', 'indeterminate'))),
  CHECK (direction = 'in' OR sort_minute = author_minute),
  CHECK ((kind = 1) = (body_env IS NOT NULL)),
  CHECK (timer_code <> 0 OR (expire_at_minute IS NULL AND unread_deadline_minute IS NULL)),
  CHECK (timer_code = 0 OR direction = 'in' OR expire_at_minute IS NOT NULL),
  CHECK (timer_code = 0 OR direction = 'out' OR unread_deadline_minute IS NOT NULL),
  CHECK (state <> 'read' OR timer_code = 0 OR expire_at_minute IS NOT NULL),
  CHECK (delete_at_minute IS (CASE WHEN expire_at_minute IS NOT NULL THEN expire_at_minute ELSE unread_deadline_minute END))
) WITHOUT ROWID;
CREATE INDEX idx_dm_message_order  ON dm_message(conversation_id, sort_minute, minute_index, message_id);
CREATE INDEX idx_dm_message_delete ON dm_message(delete_at_minute) WHERE delete_at_minute IS NOT NULL;

CREATE TABLE dm_outbound_item (       -- every outgoing L0, all kinds (§21.8)
  item_id       BLOB PRIMARY KEY NOT NULL CHECK (length(item_id) = 16),                     -- the L0 message_id
  contact_id    BLOB NOT NULL REFERENCES dm_contact(contact_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  message_ref   BLOB REFERENCES dm_message(message_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,                  -- TEXT, TIMER_SET: itself; REACTION: target
  kind          INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (kind BETWEEN 1 AND 4),
  control       TEXT CHECK (control IS NULL OR control IN ('reconnect', 'revocation', 'reset')),
  l0_env        BLOB CHECK (l0_env IS NULL OR length(l0_env) BETWEEN 29 AND 65564),
  deadline_hour INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(deadline_hour) = 'integer' AND deadline_hour % 3600 = 0),
  state         TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (state IN ('pending', 'enqueued', 'sent', 'degraded', 'failed', 'indeterminate')),
  CHECK ((kind = 4) = (control IS NOT NULL)),
  CHECK (kind = 4 OR message_ref IS NOT NULL)
) WITHOUT ROWID;

CREATE TABLE dm_outbound_op (
  operation_id  BLOB PRIMARY KEY NOT NULL CHECK (length(operation_id) = 16),
  purpose       TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (purpose IN ('item', 'bundle')),                         -- §21.8
  item_id       BLOB REFERENCES dm_outbound_item(item_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  bundle_week   INTEGER CHECK (bundle_week IS NULL OR (typeof(bundle_week) = 'integer' AND bundle_week >= 0)),
  blob          BLOB CHECK (blob IS NULL OR length(blob) IN (4096, 16384, 65536)),          -- retained for RESEND
  resend_count  INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0 CHECK (resend_count BETWEEN 0 AND 2),
  deadline_hour INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(deadline_hour) = 'integer' AND deadline_hour % 3600 = 0),
  CHECK ((purpose = 'item') = (item_id IS NOT NULL)),
  CHECK ((purpose = 'bundle') = (bundle_week IS NOT NULL))
) WITHOUT ROWID;
CREATE INDEX idx_dm_outbound_op_item ON dm_outbound_op(item_id) WHERE item_id IS NOT NULL;

CREATE TABLE dm_pending_send (
  item_id         BLOB PRIMARY KEY NOT NULL REFERENCES dm_outbound_item(item_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,   -- §21.8
  contact_id      BLOB NOT NULL REFERENCES dm_contact(contact_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  fail_after_hour INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(fail_after_hour) = 'integer' AND fail_after_hour % 3600 = 0)
) WITHOUT ROWID;

CREATE TABLE dm_bundle_candidate (    -- the best verified bundle of an attempt (§21.7)
  contact_id   BLOB PRIMARY KEY NOT NULL REFERENCES dm_contact(contact_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  bundle_env   BLOB NOT NULL CHECK (length(bundle_env) = 1421),                             -- bundle v2 (1 393) + 28
  bundle_week  INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(bundle_week) = 'integer' AND bundle_week >= 0),
  bundle_rank  INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(bundle_rank) = 'integer' AND bundle_rank BETWEEN 0 AND 65535)   -- §5.3
) WITHOUT ROWID;

CREATE TABLE dm_orphan_namespace (    -- namespaces of blocked or deleted contacts, removed by SWEEP (§21.12)
  namespace_id BLOB PRIMARY KEY NOT NULL CHECK (length(namespace_id) = 32)
) WITHOUT ROWID;

CREATE TABLE dm_scheduled (
  sched_id   BLOB PRIMARY KEY NOT NULL CHECK (length(sched_id) = 16),
  contact_id BLOB NOT NULL REFERENCES dm_contact(contact_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  what       TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (what IN ('reconnect', 'revocation')),
  payload    BLOB CHECK (payload IS NULL OR length(payload) = 102),                         -- revocation certificate v2
  due_minute INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(due_minute) = 'integer' AND due_minute % 60 = 0),
  CHECK ((what = 'revocation') = (payload IS NOT NULL)),
  UNIQUE (contact_id, what)
) WITHOUT ROWID;

CREATE TABLE dm_held (
  held_id           BLOB PRIMARY KEY NOT NULL CHECK (length(held_id) = 16),
  contact_id        BLOB NOT NULL REFERENCES dm_contact(contact_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  identity          BLOB NOT NULL CHECK (length(identity) = 32),                           -- the pending Olm identity
  l1_env            BLOB NOT NULL CHECK (length(l1_env) BETWEEN 29 AND 65564),
  gen_id            INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(gen_id) = 'integer' AND gen_id BETWEEN 0 AND 4294967295),
  prov_env          BLOB CHECK (prov_env IS NULL OR length(prov_env) = 60),                -- the wrapped reply_seed
  hold_until_minute INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(hold_until_minute) = 'integer' AND hold_until_minute % 60 = 0),
  relay_expiry_hour INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(relay_expiry_hour) = 'integer' AND relay_expiry_hour % 3600 = 0),   -- §21.10
  consume_minute    INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(consume_minute) = 'integer' AND consume_minute % 60 = 0)
) WITHOUT ROWID;

CREATE TABLE dm_reaction (
  target_id    BLOB NOT NULL REFERENCES dm_message(message_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  from_self    INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (from_self IN (0, 1)),
  grapheme_env BLOB NOT NULL CHECK (length(grapheme_env) BETWEEN 29 AND 60),
  PRIMARY KEY (target_id, from_self)
) WITHOUT ROWID;

CREATE TABLE dm_seen (                 -- D3: a keyed tag and its day, nothing else
  tag       BLOB PRIMARY KEY NOT NULL CHECK (length(tag) = 16),
  until_day INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(until_day) = 'integer' AND until_day >= 0 AND until_day % 7 = 0)
) WITHOUT ROWID;

-- Olm and bundle-generation state (wrapped; §2.5, §8.1)
CREATE TABLE olm_session (
  contact_id        BLOB NOT NULL REFERENCES dm_contact(contact_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  slot              TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (slot IN ('current', 'previous')),
  session_id        BLOB NOT NULL CHECK (length(session_id) = 32),
  role              TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (role IN ('initiator', 'responder')),
  acknowledged      INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (acknowledged IN (0, 1)),
  state_env         BLOB NOT NULL CHECK (length(state_env) BETWEEN 29 AND 65564),           -- pickle ≤ SESSION_STATE_MAX (§7.9)
  prov_env          BLOB CHECK (prov_env IS NULL OR length(prov_env) = 60),
  prov_erase_minute INTEGER CHECK (prov_erase_minute IS NULL
                                   OR (typeof(prov_erase_minute) = 'integer' AND prov_erase_minute % 60 = 0)),
  peer_gen_id       INTEGER CHECK (peer_gen_id IS NULL
                                   OR (typeof(peer_gen_id) = 'integer' AND peer_gen_id BETWEEN 0 AND 4294967295)),
  unack_since_day   INTEGER CHECK (unack_since_day IS NULL OR (typeof(unack_since_day) = 'integer' AND unack_since_day >= 0)),
  retire_day        INTEGER CHECK (retire_day IS NULL OR (typeof(retire_day) = 'integer' AND retire_day >= 0)),
  peer_bind_minute  INTEGER CHECK (peer_bind_minute IS NULL
                                   OR (typeof(peer_bind_minute) = 'integer' AND peer_bind_minute % 60 = 0)),        -- §22.8.4
  prov_anchor_minute INTEGER CHECK (prov_anchor_minute IS NULL
                                   OR (typeof(prov_anchor_minute) = 'integer' AND prov_anchor_minute % 60 = 0)),    -- §22.8.5
  pair_sent         INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0 CHECK (pair_sent IN (0, 1)),                                        -- §22.8.5
  CHECK ((role = 'responder') = (peer_bind_minute IS NOT NULL)),
  CHECK (role = 'initiator' OR acknowledged = 1),
  CHECK ((role = 'initiator' AND acknowledged = 0) = (unack_since_day IS NOT NULL)),
  CHECK ((role = 'initiator') = (peer_gen_id IS NOT NULL)),
  CHECK ((slot = 'previous') = (retire_day IS NOT NULL)),
  CHECK (prov_erase_minute IS NULL OR prov_env IS NOT NULL),
  PRIMARY KEY (contact_id, slot),
  UNIQUE (contact_id, session_id)
) WITHOUT ROWID;

CREATE TABLE olm_prekey_gen (          -- own bundle generations
  gen_id           INTEGER PRIMARY KEY NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(gen_id) = 'integer' AND gen_id BETWEEN 0 AND 4294967295),
  bundle_week      INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(bundle_week) = 'integer' AND bundle_week >= 0),
  fallback_pub     BLOB NOT NULL UNIQUE CHECK (length(fallback_pub) = 32),
  secrets_env      BLOB NOT NULL CHECK (length(secrets_env) = 92),              -- f_g(32) || q_g seed(32), wrapped
  superseded_day   INTEGER CHECK (superseded_day IS NULL OR (typeof(superseded_day) = 'integer' AND superseded_day >= 0)),
  delete_after_day INTEGER CHECK (delete_after_day IS NULL OR (typeof(delete_after_day) = 'integer' AND delete_after_day >= 0)),
  CHECK ((superseded_day IS NULL) = (delete_after_day IS NULL)),
  CHECK (delete_after_day IS NULL OR delete_after_day = superseded_day + 91)
) WITHOUT ROWID;
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX idx_olm_prekey_gen_current ON olm_prekey_gen((superseded_day IS NULL)) WHERE superseded_day IS NULL;

CREATE TABLE olm_prekey_used (         -- D2b first-flight reuse set
  gen_id     INTEGER NOT NULL REFERENCES olm_prekey_gen(gen_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  session_id BLOB NOT NULL CHECK (length(session_id) = 32),
  PRIMARY KEY (gen_id, session_id)
) WITHOUT ROWID;

CREATE TABLE dm_peer_pq (              -- peer X-Wing keys: the establishment generation and the newest in-band one
  contact_id  BLOB NOT NULL REFERENCES dm_contact(contact_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
  gen_id      INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(gen_id) = 'integer' AND gen_id BETWEEN 0 AND 4294967295),
  bundle_week INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(bundle_week) = 'integer' AND bundle_week >= 0),
  source      TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (source IN ('bundle', 'in_band')),
  xwing_pk    BLOB NOT NULL CHECK (length(xwing_pk) = 1216),
  PRIMARY KEY (contact_id, gen_id)
) WITHOUT ROWID;
```

**Step 6 (migration 5): triggers and guards; `CURRENT_VERSION = 5`; `expectedTables` and `expectedTriggers` updated.**

| Trigger | Rule |
|---|---|
| `dm_self_fixed` | `id` and `seen_key` immutable; `card_index` immutable in Phase 9 (Phase 13 drops this trigger for cut-off rotation) |
| `dm_self_monotonic` | `bundle_week` and `expiry_clock_minute` never decrease |
| `dm_contact_identity` | `contact_id`, `identity_pk` immutable |
| `dm_contact_pin` | `pinned_identity` changes only from NULL (pin), or to `OLD.pending_identity` while `OLD.trust_state = 'changed'` (KEYDEC) |
| `dm_contact_trust` | only the transitions of §8.3 and §12; `revoked` is terminal |
| `dm_message_identity` | no update of any column other than `state` and the three expiry columns (no edits in Phase 9) |
| `dm_message_state` | `queued → sent, degraded, failed, indeterminate`; `failed → queued`; `unread → read`; nothing else |
| `dm_message_expiry`, `dm_held_expiry` | `expire_at_minute`, `unread_deadline_minute` and `hold_until_minute`, once non-NULL, only decrease (NULL-safe, R-T6); `delete_at_minute` follows its CHECK and may move later at display (§21.10) |
| `dm_conversation_timer` | `timer_version` never decreases |
| `dm_held_limit` | at most 256 rows per contact (an insert beyond aborts; RECV deletes the oldest first, counted) |
| `dm_pair_offer_root` | `root_env` and `offer_env` may only become NULL; `acknowledged` only 0 → 1 |
| `olm_session_identity` | `session_id`, `role`, `peer_gen_id` immutable; `acknowledged` only 0 → 1; `unack_since_day` only to NULL; `prov_env` only to NULL; `peer_bind_minute` only increases; `prov_anchor_minute` write-once; `pair_sent` only 0 → 1 (§22.8.4, §22.8.5); slot changes are delete-and-insert in one transaction (the envelope bytes move unchanged, §22.8.3) |
| `olm_prekey_gen_fixed` | `gen_id`, `fallback_pub`, `secrets_env`, `bundle_week` immutable; `superseded_day` and `delete_after_day` write-once |
| `olm_prekey_used_append_only` | no update; delete only by cascade |
| `dm_peer_pq_limit` | at most 3 rows per contact |
| `relay_key_registration_state` (migration 4) | `verifier`, `kind`, `period_id` immutable; `pending → done`, `done → pending` (healing) only |
| `<table>_no_replace` | REPLACE guards (Phase 8 §19.21 point 4) for `dm_self`, `dm_own_namespace`, `dm_contact` (both keys), `dm_conversation` (both keys), `dm_message`, `dm_outbound_item`, `dm_outbound_op`, `dm_pending_send`, `dm_bundle_candidate`, `dm_orphan_namespace`, `dm_scheduled`, `dm_held`, `dm_reaction`, `dm_pair_offer`, `olm_session`, `olm_prekey_gen`, `olm_prekey_used`, `dm_peer_pq` (migration 5) and `relay_key_registration` (migration 4) (§21.14; revised §22: the `olm_*` and `dm_peer_pq` guards replace round 9's `sig_*` guards). `dm_seen` inserts with `INSERT OR IGNORE` (which deletes nothing); `dm_pair_key` and `dm_peer_pq` are written with UPDATE-then-INSERT pairs; `olm_session` slot changes are delete-and-insert. No `REPLACE`, `INSERT OR REPLACE` or UPSERT anywhere in DM code (gate G-DM4). SWEEP never deletes an `olm_prekey_gen` row whose `gen_id` a live `dm_held` row references (§5.4) |

**R-M3 (introspection; T20, T33).** The exact set of time columns added by migration 4 is `inbox_blob.relay_expiry_hour` and `relay_key_registration.{lease_hour, next_attempt_minute, valid_until_hour}` (the sync set grows from 11 to 15 columns); migration 5 adds `dm_self.{expiry_clock_minute, accept_after_minute}`; `dm_contact.{fetch_listen_until_minute, request_until_day}`; `dm_message.{author_minute, sort_minute, expire_at_minute, unread_deadline_minute, delete_at_minute}`; `dm_outbound_item.deadline_hour`; `dm_outbound_op.deadline_hour`; `dm_pending_send.fail_after_hour`; `dm_scheduled.due_minute`; `dm_held.{hold_until_minute, relay_expiry_hour, consume_minute}`; `dm_seen.until_day`; `olm_session.{prov_erase_minute, unack_since_day, retire_day, peer_bind_minute, prov_anchor_minute}` (the last two from §22.8.4 and §22.8.5); `olm_prekey_gen.{superseded_day, delete_after_day}`. Period indices (`bundle_week` of `dm_self`, `dm_bundle_candidate`, `dm_outbound_op`, `olm_prekey_gen`, `dm_peer_pq`; `bundle_week_seen`; `bundle_published_week`; `pq_announced_week`; `week`; `base_week`; `period_id`), the rank `dm_bundle_candidate.bundle_rank` and the random ids `gen_id`, `peer_gen_id` are integers with `typeof` CHECKs (§21.14). A test lists every column of every table and fails on any other time-like name. **Library state holds no time at all** (vodozemac reads no clock and stores none): round 9's record-timestamp decode test is replaced by a **pickle structure test** that decodes a wrapped pickle in a test build and fails on any field outside the pickle schema pinned for the vodozemac version. **R-M4:** no `CREATE VIRTUAL TABLE` (gate `dm-no-fts.sh`). **R-M1:** after every message of a conversation has expired, a full dump shows only the contact, its trust and establishment state, the conversation's timer code and version, its wrapped session state, and unlinked `dm_seen` tags.

**Migration tests (CS-9).** Every upgrade path (v1, v2, v3 → v4 → v5) on populated fixtures (outbox ops in every delivery state, in-flight leases, every outcome, `ent_*` rows); an interruption at every statement of migrations 4 and 5 (`FaultySqlExecutor`), after which the reopened database is the previous version and migrates; after migration identical rows and rowids, the exact trigger set, an empty `foreign_key_check`, `integrity_check = ok`; the guard aborts when any v1 DM table holds a row; `legacy_alter_table` on → refused; `ReplaceGuardsTest` extended to every new guard. The emulator job repeats v3 → v4 → v5 on real SQLCipher (§15.9).

### 13.7 Threads, background, wiring (normative)

- **DM actor.** One process-wide single-thread executor (`ghost-dm`) owns every `:messaging` transaction and every `DmNative` session call (§7.9). It is woken by: listener hints (`SyncChange.INBOX`, `OUTCOMES`, `CAPABILITIES`, `TOPOLOGY`), facade calls, the in-process expiry timer, the alarm receiver, the database open, and the `ConsumerTail`. Hints carry no payload and the actor re-reads state, so a lost or duplicated hint is harmless.
- **No work on lane threads.** `Inbox.setListener` (one slot, unused until Phase 9, `Inbox.kt:26`) fires on the committing thread, which can be a lane thread (`SyncDatabase.kt:16-19`); the listener only posts a coalesced flag to the actor (test: "the listener returns without touching the database"). Phase 10 will turn the slot into a fan-out in `:app`; the API stays unchanged.
- **Actor pass** (split by §21.9). Every pass (until empty or the deadline): SWEEP → OUTCOME loop → BUNDLE claims (`Consumer.PREKEYS`) → HELD replays (§22.8.1) → peek ordering and RECV claims (`Inbox.peek`, then `Inbox.claimOne` one blob at a time, §7.7; none while the native library or the DM context is unavailable, §21.12) → reschedule the timer and the alarm. Only a **time-triggered** pass (the actor timer, database open, a relay session start) also runs OWNER-WEEK and re-seals, the week-change parts of KEEPER and REGROWS, scheduled controls due, `EST-READY`, `EST-END` and expired pending items.
- **Background.** The Phase 7 JobScheduler job (one periodic job, no extras, ADR-20), the `ConsumerTail` in background sessions and quiet runs (§13.3 item 6), plus the inexact alarm (§10.7). No FCM, no WorkManager, no coroutines, no foreground service.
- **Wiring.** `MessagingWiring` (in `:app`) creates the actor after the sync controller, sets the listener and the tail at process start, wires the `IdentityActivationHook` (§13.4), and runs a sweep, R-K1 and the resumable ACTIVATE check (§21.12) at the first database open. Lock and wipe close the DM context (`ctxClose` zeroises the native keys, §2.5) and zeroize the Kotlin-side key material; wipe also cancels the alarm.

### 13.8 Keys at rest (normative; C-23)

- **Never stored:** the Olm identity secret, the Olm account signing seed, own `card_secret`s, inbox read secrets, the schedule key (all re-derived at unlock from the Keystore-wrapped seed and held only in native memory by the DM context; zeroised on lock and wipe, L7-D19).
- **Stored in SQLCipher, wrapped under `session_state_key`:** Olm session pickles, bundle-generation secrets, `reply_seed`s, pair opening keys, roots and offered root TLVs (erased on schedule, §5.4, §6.3, §6.7, T34). **Stored in clear inside SQLCipher:** recognition keys (erased with their opening keys), `seen_key`, bearer credentials; contacts' card secrets inside `card_env` (content key).
- **Content key:** 32 random bytes, stored wrapped (`nonce(12) ‖ AES-256-GCM(32) ‖ tag(16)`, 60 bytes on the reference provider) by a non-exportable Android Keystore key created with **the same authentication policy as the database-key wrapper** (not authentication-bound by default, ADR-20 Q6). `body_env`, `card_env`, `name_env`, `grapheme_env`, `l1_env` are ChaCha20-Poly1305 under it with the row's key as AD. Purpose: the "no plaintext content column" rule (`Schema.kt:7`), a meaningful T25 byte scan, and no plaintext in SQLite freed pages even before `secure_delete` runs. It adds no secrecy against AD-8 with the database unlocked. JVM tests use an in-memory wrapper port.
- **State key (§2.5).** `session_state_key`: 32 random bytes, stored wrapped in `dm_self.state_key_wrapped` by a non-exportable Keystore key with the same authentication policy as the content key; unwrapped only into the native DM context at unlock. Purpose: the JVM heap never holds ratchet, KEM or seal opening secrets, and SQLCipher rows of session state are doubly encrypted. It adds no secrecy against AD-8 with the database unlocked and the Keystore available. JVM tests use an in-memory wrapper port.

---

## 14. Invariants (new T26–T35; changes to T1, T3, T9, T12, T13, T19, T20, T21, T25)

### 14.1 New invariants (normative; Romanian rows for INVARIANTS.md in Appendix B.2; T26, T29, T32–T34 amended and T35 added by §22)

| ID | Invariant | Verification | Executable from | Code |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **T26** Relay-blind DM addressing | No relay-visible value (namespace, verifier, credential, stored byte, capture field) is computable from public identity material (`ghost1`, Ed25519 key, Olm identity key, safety number) without a card or the seed: every DM derivation takes `m`, a `card_secret` or a random secret as its key input; relay sets are `set(ns, w)`; every relay-stored DM byte is sealed under keys never presented to a relay (Rule V1); no Olm header byte string (identity, base, fallback or ratchet key, chain index) and no X-Wing public key appears outside the seal | derivation vectors; static check of every `DmLabels`/`CardLabels` call site and a Rust test over every `derive_*` key-input type (gate G-DM3); JD1, JD2, JD9; the model relay tries to open every stored blob with every secret it was shown (must open 0); S-DM3; mutants PM-3, PM-13, PM-20, PM-24 | Phase 9 | `client-core/dm`, `:crypto-bridge`, `:messaging`, DM world |
| **T27** Sender-indistinguishable deposits; DM-independent tokens | Every store into one inbox namespace at one relay with a deposit credential presents the same credential scope, in every week and whatever the sender's clock or earlier refusals (§21.2); no honest client redeems or registers for a namespace it does not own (LOCAL needs are never redeemed); a client's redemptions and registrations (count, minute, relay, namespace) are identical in worlds that differ only in its DM activity, below quota exhaustion; a registration never replaces another | JD3, JD8; NI-DM3; `register.txt`; the planner test for LOCAL; NI-DM6; mutants PM-1, PM-1b, PM-21, PM-23, PM-26, PM-27, PM-28 | Phase 9 | relay, `:sync`, `:entitlement`, `:messaging` |
| **T28** No reflex traffic | Processing any inbound blob (valid, malformed, duplicate, replayed, reordered, undecryptable, forged, key-changed, request, probe, poison) enqueues nothing, redeems nothing, registers nothing, changes no read schedule and does not change the relay session's close time; every DM store maps to a user action, a scheduled user action, the owner's weekly bundle, or an automatic identical resend of the user's own INDETERMINATE op; PQ TLVs never cause a send (R-C5) | `NoReflexTest` (call graph, gate G-DM2); NI-DM1 (with session close times); JD6; mutants PM-2, PM-6, PM-25 | Phase 9 | `:messaging`, `:sync`, DM world |
| **T29** Uniform, type-hiding blobs | DM envelopes and bundles are exactly 4, 16 or 64 KiB, with the bucket a function of the body length only (never 1 KiB, never a PQ TLV); all lengths and zero padding inside the AEAD; non-canonical blobs refused; bytes uniform (salt, PRF hint, AEAD output); every intro envelope has a fresh X-Wing encapsulation, so no byte window repeats between a first flight and a re-sent PreKey envelope | property test over kinds × lengths × modes (pair, intro, provisional) × session states; byte-frequency χ² per offset on 10 000 blobs; golden sizes; NI-DM2 (intro vs provisional vs pair, with and without PQ TLVs); JD10; NI-DM7; mutants PM-4, PM-12, PM-37, ME-1…ME-4, ME-22, ME-23 | Phase 9 | `client-core/dm`, DM world |
| **T30** TTL mapping and bounded relay lifetime | Relay TTL = the §10.1 table; the relay accepts exactly the five buckets (`rejected_ttl` otherwise) and captures the stored bucket in seconds; every DM op has the deadline `author + min(bucket, 7 d)` and no copy is stored after it; an honest relay's copy is gone by `deadline_hour + bucket + 1 h`; a relay promising storage **more than σ = 3 d longer** than asked is detected at the receipt (an over-promise of at most σ is tolerated, L7-D27) | relay and client vectors; capture-check fixtures (3600 positive; 7200 and the old field negative); JD5; client receipt upper-bound test; mutants PM-5, PM-9, ME-14, ME-15 | Phase 9 | relay, `client-core/net`, `:sync`, `:messaging` |
| **T31** Read confinement; activity-independent owner writes | A client lists and gets only its own namespaces (inbox, drops) and channels, plus one bundle fetch (at most three 15-minute attempts, §21.7) per establishment or explicit reset or reconnect; owner redemptions, registrations and bundle publications happen at times independent of contacts and activity; revocation and resets travel through recipients' inboxes as user sends; safety-number work, verification, acknowledgement, block and request decisions cause no network I/O | JD7; static check: `Namespaces.register(listen = true)` from `:messaging` only for own namespaces or the establishment steps; NI-DM3, NI-DM5; mutants PM-7, PM-8, PM-11, PM-31 | Phase 9 | `:messaging`, DM world |
| **T32** DM unlinkability world | Exact joins JD1–JD10 find nothing; twins NI-DM1–NI-DM7 give identical shape views (NI-DM7: identical card-holder views); no learned attacker on the full effective relay view beats the attacker restricted to LD on conversation edges or identity mappings (S-DM1, S-DM3 at α = 0.001); S-DM2, S-DM4 measured and reported against a recorded baseline | §15.6 | Phase 9 (PR scale in `android`; gate scale in `dm-privacy-gate.yml`) | `android/messaging/src/test/.../dmworld/`, `dm-report-check.sh` |
| **T33** Local DM forensic minimality | DM tables keep only `*_minute`/`*_hour`/`*_day` time columns with `typeof` CHECKs (R-M3) and period indices; no conversation activity time or counter; no persisted rejection history; random WITHOUT ROWID keys; no virtual table; content columns are envelopes under the content key and session columns envelopes under the state key; notifications carry a constant text only; **no time value enters library state** (vodozemac reads no clock and stores none; **clarified by S0′, V-15, 2026-09-19:** the first-seen sidecar of §7.7 point 4 holds `u32_be` **day** indices, is GHOST state carried beside the pickle inside the same wrapped envelope — never inside vodozemac's own state — and is supplied a `today` argument rather than reading a clock) | schema introspection; the pickle structure test (§13.6); notification-port contract test; the vendored clock scan G-R8; mutants PM-10, ME-12, ME-13, ME-18, ME-24 | Phase 9 (JVM); device checklist | `:messaging`, `:storage`, `client-core/dm` |
| **T34** Attribution forward secrecy of DM envelopes | For an ISO week w with `now ≥ start(w + 1) + 45 d`, no key stored on either device and nothing derivable from either seed opens a pair-mode envelope of week w: roots are random, travel only in acknowledged sessions and are erased, week keys (opening and recognition) are ratcheted and erased by the window; declared exception: a root carrier whose Olm key survives as a skipped key in a **live** session (§21.3; none for retired sessions; none if Q67's prune is adopted — **and S0′ adopted it, V-15, 2026-09-19, so the exception is withdrawn: the prune at `FRESH + 2σ` = 51 d removes such keys from live sessions too, and the T34 world gains an age case asserting it, MC-30**); no seed-derived key opens an intro inner layer or a provisional envelope: intro envelopes open only while their generation lives, provisional envelopes only while a `prov_env` or that generation lives | seizure scenario (§15.6): dump both databases, state keys and seeds at day 70 of a world, try every stored key, every derivation, Olm decryption with every retained session, throwaway accounts over every retained generation, X-Wing decapsulation and every retained `prov_env` on every archived blob; expectations: pair-mode envelopes of old weeks opened = the skipped-key ground truth of live sessions (0 for retired sessions, 0 in the lossless variant); intro and provisional envelopes opened only for generations and `prov_env`s not yet erased (ground truth); erasure unit tests at week and generation boundaries; mutants PM-19, PM-29, PM-36 | Phase 9 | `client-core/dm`, `:messaging`, DM world |
| **T35** PQ confinement (new, §22) | Every symmetric key that seals relay-stored DM bytes is card-derived (bundle, intro outer layer), X-Wing-derived (intro inner layer), or transported only inside envelopes sealed under such keys (`reply_seed`, pair roots, PQ-C roots); no DM envelope opens with every classical secret but without the ML-KEM secrets | provenance-tagged key objects in `ghost-client-dm` test builds across the DM world (PQ2); PQ1 (one decapsulation per intro open, `k_in` depends on `ss`); PQ5 at every vodozemac or libcrux bump; ProVerif M-1 and M-3 in the `pq-model` job; mutants PM-32, PM-33 | Phase 9 | `client-core/dm`, `:messaging`, `protocol/models/dm` |

| ID | Change |
|---|---|
| T1 | `allowed-observables.json` v2: `op += register`; `key_kind` on `register`; `ttl_bucket_seconds` replaces `ttl_bucket_days`; `capability_scope` covers read and deposit keys |
| T3 | canaries add: the Olm identity secret, `session_state_key`, unwrapped session pickles (test builds export root and chain keys through the `low-level-api` oracle), bundle-generation secrets, `reply_seed`s, card secrets, pair keys, read and deposit secrets, inbox and bundle namespaces, safety-number digits, message plaintext; scanned in public strings, exception messages, `toString` of every DM type, Rust error strings (which never cross JNI) and every output stream of the process (revised §22: the libsignal logger clause is withdrawn; the DM graph logs nothing, G-R4, G-R8) |
| T9 | executable for DMs in Phase 9: bucket from body length, DM floor 4 KiB, uniform bytes (T29) |
| T12 | unchanged for GHOST's own builds; **T12b** (ADR-38): an auditor rebuilds the tagged commit in the fixed-path builder and signs the hashes of the APK, the native libraries and the relay and issuer binaries; the updater inclusion-proof invariant arrives in Phase 14 |
