## 15. Test plan and exit-gate argument (I11; amended by §22)

### 15.1 Infrastructure

- **`DmWorld`** (`android/messaging/src/test/.../harness/`), built on the Phase 7 harness through the shared-test-source pattern of `:entitlement` (`srcDir` of `storage/src/testShared` and the `:sync` harness, `friendPaths`, `excludeTestsMatching("org.ghost.sync.*")`; harness `-D` properties declared as test inputs; RC §8).
  - **Clients:** 2 to 6 per world (up to 600 in T32), each a real `:messaging` + `:crypto-bridge` + `:sync` stack on its own SQLite **file** (`JdbcSqlExecutor`, production pragmas; both `journal_mode = WAL` and `DELETE`), migrated to v5, with the real engine lanes driven by the `DeterministicDriver`.
  - **The real ratchet** through `DmNative` loading the host build `ghost-dm-host` (built by the `android` job before Gradle tests and passed as `-Dghost.dm.native`; Linux in CI, MSVC on the owner's Windows machine; **S0′ blocking check V-5**: if the host build cannot be loaded, the session-dependent suites move to the emulator job with fewer seeds and the exit-gate argument is re-stated before S7. **Resolved in part by S0′ (V-5, 2026-09-19):** Windows MSVC is verified — a 1 479 680 B `cdylib` with exactly three JNI exports loads with `System.load` on JDK 17.0.20.1 and runs the real ratchet and X-Wing (`OI = d80580ec…0603`, reply decrypted; 1 216-byte X-Wing key, equal secrets) and survives a libcrux panic as `INTERNAL` with the JVM alive. **Linux was not verified on this host:** the first `android` job run on the ubuntu-24.04 runner image executes `client-core/dm-spike/scripts/v5-host-load.sh` before S4b starts; if it fails, the §17.1 re-plan applies). The seal, codec and PQ layer are in the same native library, so every DM world runs the production cryptography.
  - **Randomness (replaced by §22.8.10 point 7).** All DM randomness is drawn inside Rust, ciphertexts decide blob hashes and Phase 7 orders fetches by blob hash, so GHOST-level orders are not reproducible unless the native randomness is. The `ghost-dm-host` test build uses getrandom 0.4's custom backend (`--cfg getrandom_backend="custom"`) backed by a ChaCha20 stream keyed by the world seed and reset per world, every other `getrandom` major of the graph is routed through the same stream, and a world's native calls run only on actor threads that world created; ciphertexts, blob hashes and orders are then identical run to run, and crash runs share the recording run's prefix. G-R2 fails if the cfg reaches an Android build. A failure prints the seed and the transcript (message ids, orders, results). Round 9b's record/replay switch is withdrawn.
  - **Two capability variants:** (a) the *core world*, where a harness "redeem fairy" installs own write capabilities at the planner's times (fast: crash enumeration, reorder, replay, key change, T25); (b) the *entitlement world* on `EntHarness`, where the real redeem lane redeems trial tokens and the real work lane registers keys (JD8, week-boundary renewals, the registration healing rule).
  - **`ModelRelay` extensions**, conformance-pinned by `relay_semantics.txt` (TTL section) and `register.txt`, which the Rust relay also replays: `RegisterKey`, v3 credentials, verifier accumulation, the `DepositLedger`, exact TTL buckets with hour-rounded expiry, sealed list cursors (§21.9 point 4; the Phase 7 harness reads the model's raw sequence today, so this slice either seals it or keeps it out of the effective view and says so), circuit labels (the effective view, RP F10). Hostile modes: the existing REWIND, REPEATS, WITHHOLD, WRONG_BYTES, STALL, SHORT_EXPIRY, plus **RESURRECT** (re-serve after the recipient's tombstone GC), **COPY_NAMESPACE**, **TRUNCATE**, **SHUFFLE**, **DELAY_RELEASE**, **LONG_EXPIRY**, **WITHHOLD_NEWEST_BUNDLE**, **LOSE_KEYS** (`keys.redb` lost), **ATTACKER_REGISTERS** (a card holder redeems and registers its own verifier), **REFUSE_ONE_SENDER**, **WITHHOLD_ALL_BUNDLES** (§21.14).
  - **Faults:** `FaultySqlExecutor` (a crash before every statement, before and after commit, and `SQLITE_FULL` injection mapped to `StorageFullException`); `SimulatedNativeCrash` thrown from the `DmNative` port during RECV (the process-death model of a SIGSEGV or an uncaught native abort; a caught Rust panic is the `INTERNAL` code); `HarnessDriver` interleavings of actor passes between lane items; a virtual clock with steps.
  - **Oracles:** `oracle_sent(message_id, outcome)` written in OUTCOME; `oracle_displayed(device, message_id)` with a primary key that catches a duplicate at the event that caused it; `oracle_user_action(op)` written by every facade call and by scheduled controls, so JD6 maps stores to actions; a recording `Outbox` wrapper that fails on an enqueue inside RECV, BUNDLE or HELD; a recording counter on `DmNative.encrypt` per contact trust state; a session-slot event log (created, renewed, swapped, retired) for the R-SS assertions.
- **Debug key-change hook** (test sources and the `gate` debug source set only): a DM context whose Olm identity differs from the derived one but is bound by its real Ed25519 key (the "stolen seed on a modified client" scenario). Gate G-DM5 fails if the hook class, or any harness package, is in the release APK.

### 15.2 Unit, vector and property tests

**Forward note (S4b, 2026-09-20).** §22.9 items 1, 16 and 17 win over this section for the enforced EM-1 and EM-2 bounds, the seeded-input counts of the S4b parsers and the mutant that makes EM-1's loss property falsifiable.

- **Rust (`cargo test -p ghost-client-dm`, `rust` job):** derivation vectors for every `DmLabels` and `CardLabels` output and the Olm labels, label prefix tests; card v2 codec (round trip; every single-byte mutation and every length refused; alphabet; own card; index ≠ 0; version 1 refused); bundle v2 build and open; seal vectors `dm_seal.txt` for pair, intro, provisional and bundle at every bucket, recognition hints included; L0 and TLV vectors `dm_content.txt` including 0x0005, 0x0006 and the slack rule; binding v3 vectors; safety-number vectors `dm_safety_number.txt`; X-Wing (draft-06, draft-10) and ML-KEM-768 known-answer tests; the differential X-Wing test against RustCrypto `x-wing`; `FailedDecryptLeavesStateTest` (§7.9); the dehydrated-pickle encoder check; golden pickles and `UpgradeTest`; the Olm key-schedule oracle (below); property tests (10⁶ seeded inputs per CI run) for every parser: never panics, never over-reads; provenance-tag assertions (T35); `DmResultCodesTest`.
- **Exhaustive state-model enumerations (RG-1; `rust` job).** An abstract model of the DM session and seal layers, enumerated exhaustively with at most 6 user sends per side, message loss, duplication, reordering, process death at every step and a restore:
  - **EM-1 (sessions):** the `current`/`previous` slots under R-SS0 … R-SS4, the D2b reuse set, `UNACK_MAX` re-establishment and simultaneous initiation; properties: convergence to one session per pair, no decryption on a session of another identity, no second session from a replayed first flight, no lost message outside the declared bound of §7.7;
  - **EM-2 (seal modes):** the intro → provisional → pair transitions per direction, root offers and acknowledgements, renewals, and `prov_env` and generation erasure; properties: the sender never seals with a key the recipient cannot hold, no root travels in a PreKey message, every erasure schedule is met.
  Mutant MC-19 ("newest decrypt wins") must fail EM-1. The model complements the ProVerif obligations M-1 … M-8 (§9.5), which check secrecy and authentication rather than state reachability.
- **Kotlin (`android` job):** an independent JDK implementation replays `dm_seal.txt` (pair, provisional, bundle, the intro outer layer, recognition hints), `dm_content.txt`, `dm_safety_number.txt` and **the Ed25519 signing vectors and the verification-class expectations of V-14** (corrected by S0′, 2026-09-19: the JDK replays signatures in full — 64 of 64 byte-identical — and verification only as documented expectations per class, BouncyCastle's accept/reject recorded beside dalek's `verify_strict`, so a BouncyCastle bump that changes a class fails the test; X-Wing is covered by Rust's differential test, since the JDK 17 provider set has no ML-KEM); `DmResult` codec exhaustiveness; credential v3 and verifier vectors (shared with the Rust capability crate); `dm_relay_set.txt` (Kotlin and Rust); **one-attempt test:** for any blob at most one AEAD open is attempted (a counting spy on recognition matching), whatever the number of contacts (PM-18); the hint-cost benchmark at 1 000 contacts with ≤ 22 recognition keys per contact (reported; V-13); schema v4 and v5 migration tests (§13.6), triggers (legal and illegal transitions), REPLACE guards, R-M1, R-M3, R-M4, the pickle structure test and the expired-row lint over every DAO query; the trust-contract tests (§8.2: every rule, including refusal of a session call with an unpinned identity); `:sync` tests: `CapabilitySource` planner filter, the registration need rule, the work-lane registration step, credential selection (§13.3 item 7), `ConsumerTail` cases in `SessionParticipantTest`, T19 run (b) with registrations, `peek` and `claimOne` (offers unchanged by peek; `claimOne` of a consumed row returns null).
- **Substitutes for official vectors** (Olm has none; REQ-A6):
  1. **vodozemac's own test suite at the pinned tag**, in the test-only job `vendor-tests` (its dev-dependencies include libolm bindings, built only there; nothing reaches the product graph; V-12). **Specified by S0′ (V-12, 2026-09-19; the suite could not run on the S0′ host, no CMake):** the job runs `client-core/dm-spike/scripts/v12-vendor-tests.sh` (moved to `scripts/ci/` in S4b) for the pinned version — the checksum-verified crates.io package at the recorded tag commit (`db1b3482…` for 0.11.0, `bb39ec65…` for 0.10.0, from `.cargo_vcs_info.json`), then `cargo test --locked` with default features and with `--all-features`. The packages carry their unit tests in `src/` (169 `#[test]` functions in 0.11.0, 152 in 0.10.0) and **no `tests/` directory**, so those unit tests are the only tests available without a git checkout; if upstream integration tests exist outside the package, the job checks out the recorded tag commit instead. The job needs crates.io access, CMake and a C++ compiler (`olm-sys` builds libolm; the ghost-runner image has both, `docs/ci-self-hosted.md` §2.1), and its dev-dependency lock is checked by `cargo deny` in the job.
  2. **Golden pickles and transcripts** per pin under `client-core/dm/tests/golden/vodozemac-<version>/`: sessions after N messages each way with skipped keys, a responder and an initiator side, and full transcripts; `UpgradeTest` loads the previous pin's pickles with the new pin and continues both ways (G-2, re-sourced). **Extended by S0′ (V-7, V-15, 2026-09-19):** each pin's directory also holds **pruned pickles with their sidecars** and a pinned session pickle whose expected `chainPosition` outputs (per ratchet key, including an unknown key and a renamed field) and `sessionPrune` outputs (removed count and resulting key set) are asserted at every bump by G-R7; `UpgradeTest` loads the previous pin's **pruned** pickles and continues both ways (R-41's mitigation, drilled on both pins in V-15).
  3. **Olm key-schedule oracle:** a test-only implementation of Olm's documented schedule (`OLM_ROOT`, `OLM_RATCHET`, the HMAC chain, AES-256-CBC with HMAC-SHA-256 truncated to 8 bytes; the Olm specification) decrypts golden transcripts given the keys exported through vodozemac's `low-level-api` in the dev graph. It catches configuration misuse (V1 vs V2, a wrong session) that the library's own tests cannot see.
  4. **Dehydrated-pickle encoder check (amended by §22.8.10 point 14):** an `Account::new()` that called `generate_fallback_key()`, exported with `to_dehydrated_device(key)`, decrypted and re-encoded by GHOST's encoder must be byte-identical (so the `opt_fallback_key` branch is covered); an account built from a GHOST-encoded pickle must report the expected Curve25519 identity, and its fallback secret is checked behaviourally: an outbound session from a second account to `(OI, F_g)` opens with `create_inbound_session`, and a session to another F fails with `MissingOneTimeKey` (`Account::fallback_key()` lists only unpublished keys, and the MSC3814 import marks the key published) (V-1).
- **Removed with libsignal (§22):** `SilentLoggerTest`, the T3 libsignal log canary, `LibsignalUpgradeTest` and the golden libsignal records, PQ-3 (session version and `pq_ratchet` field), the record-timestamp decode test, the libsignal store-semantics tests.

### 15.3 Crash enumeration (CS-1, CS-2; the F7 §8.3 method)

For each scenario: run it without faults and record K events (every `exec`, `execUpdate` and `query`, pre-commit and post-commit, and the model relay's before-send, after-apply and after-response events); for every k crash at k, reboot (drop the uncommitted connection, reopen the same file, migrate, `verifyIntegrity`, R-K1, build a new client: engine, actor, DM context), check the structural invariants, run to quiescence, check everything. Every relay event also runs as "timeout after apply". Double crashes (the second during recovery) for SCN-A, SCN-B, SCN-F and SCN-I.

| Scenario | Content |
|---|---|
| **SCN-A** send | A → B: 3 messages; 3 relays over 2 operators, one refusing; STANDARD and HIGH |
| **SCN-B** receive | 6 messages reordered across relays (SHUFFLE, REPEATS), one RESURRECTed after tombstone GC, one COPY_NAMESPACE'd; a variant with an injected unknown ratchet key against an inactive sending ratchet (MC-29) |
| **SCN-C** establishment | card → fetch with WITHHOLD_NEWEST_BUNDLE → `sessionOutbound` → intro PreKey messages → the recipient's request and accept → provisional reply carrying a root → pair mode on both sides |
| **SCN-D** key change | the debug hook changes B's Olm identity with a valid binding: A holds and moves to `changed`, A's send refused; acknowledge → HELD replays; a block variant; a **forged** variant (a third client with its own identity sends an intro envelope naming B's genuine card and a PreKey message under another identity): dropped, no state change (E-1) |
| **SCN-E** offline recipient | B offline across 3 week boundaries: deposits succeed with its static per-slot credential while any registration lives, then wait in `wait_capability` (§21.2 point 9; round 9's "then the fallback" is withdrawn); B returns, renews, registers; waiting ops of A delivered before their deadlines or FAILED after; a LOSE_KEYS variant exercising the healing rule |
| **SCN-F** ephemeral | 5 min, 1 h, 7 d; displayed, unread (honest, short and long E), held; a sweep with a busy reader; a wall clock stepped back |
| **SCN-G** outcomes | an ambiguous store → INDETERMINATE → identical resend (≤ 2); every relay refusing → FAILED → user retry (re-encryption; the recipient dedups by D3); a default-timer FAILED retry across a week boundary and one after the peer's `prov_erase_minute`: no bundle fetch, no PreKey message, displayed once (§22.8.5) |
| **SCN-H** bundle generations | weekly publication across 13 weeks; a PreKey message accepted at the 91-day generation boundary and refused after; a replayed first flight (D2b); a generation kept past its boundary while a held row references it |
| **SCN-I** restore | B restores from seed: pre-restore pair, provisional and intro envelopes counted (`unknown_seal`, `unknown_generation`), no request created; B re-adds A's card and reconnects (a scheduled reset waiting for A's bundle, §21.8); A creates a new session under R-SS1 without a key-change warning and drops its out keys for B; later pair envelopes open in both directions (§21.3); a variant in which A resets from its side after B published a new bundle; variants in which B's pre-restore first flight, PreKey re-sends or normal replies reach A after B's reconnect intro (§22.8.4) |
| **SCN-J** faults | `SQLITE_FULL` injected at every statement of SEND, RECV, BUNDLE and SWEEP; `SimulatedNativeCrash` during RECV: re-offered alone, consumed as poison at the fourth offer (a declared loss); single and double SQL crashes never reach the threshold; a caught Rust panic (`INTERNAL`) rolls back and crashes like any `IllegalStateException` |
| **SCN-K** revocation | A notifies its revocation (scheduled); B drops the sessions, refuses sending, drops later blobs |
| **SCN-L** migration | a v3 database with a live outbox and inbox migrates to v4 and v5, then SCN-A traffic |

**Structural invariants (after every reboot; RG-7):** `integrity_check` ok, `foreign_key_check` empty, every expected trigger present; every `queued` outgoing message has an unreleased outbox op or a pending send; every `dm_outbound_op` of purpose `item` references a live outbox op or a released outcome; every `pending` or `enqueued` item has a pending send or an unreleased op; held rows exist only for `changed` contacts; **at most 2 `olm_session` rows per contact, with exactly one `current` whenever any exists; every `previous` row has a `retire_day`; every `olm_prekey_used` row references a live generation; at most 14 generations; at most 3 `dm_peer_pq` rows per contact; no session row for a `revoked`, `blocked` or `changed` contact other than sessions under the pinned identity of a `changed` contact**; the `delete_at` CHECK holds; after SWEEP and R-K1 no row has `delete_at_minute ≤ now_e` and the WAL is 0 bytes; every registration row is consistent with its capability; every F7 §8.4 structural invariant holds.

**At quiescence:** CS-2 (`oracle_displayed` at most one row per device and `message_id`); CS-3 under the stated premises; CS-4 (the rendered order is equal on both devices and across all crash runs); CS-5 (no encrypt to a `changed` contact; no held content displayed before KEYDEC); T28 (every store maps to an action); session liveness (one more message each way decrypts); the F7 OUT-1…IN-2 invariants. The job summary prints `dm crash classes: <n> singles, <m> doubles` per scenario (report-checked).

### 15.4 Seeded worlds (G-13)

1 000 seeds per PR (`android` job), 20 000 in `messaging-exit-gate.yml`; plain JUnit loops with `SplittableRandom`; a failure message carries its seed and the recorded transcript (§15.1). Each world draws: 2–6 clients, 2–4 relays over 2–3 operators, both modes; 0–40 messages per pair with a timer mix; displays at random times; **offline windows from 1 minute to 40 days**; **week boundaries** crossed by every world; **one ES slot-table change** in a quarter of the worlds; **clock steps** backwards up to 1 h and forwards up to 2 d while READY, large jumps while not READY; key changes (1 %), restores with reconnect (1 %), blocks and requests; one hostile relay per set; a crash probability per event and a `SQLITE_FULL` probability.

### 15.5 Functional suites

| Suite | Cases |
|---|---|
| **RO reorder** (CS-4) | RO1 all 720 orders of 6 messages each way: identical rendering on both clients; RO2 50 messages in random orders over 3 relays with HIGH delays; **RO3′** gaps up to 40 skipped keys per chain decrypt, beyond that the declared loss of §7.7 point 3 **as corrected by S0′ (V-7, 2026-09-19)** — a single message more than 40 indices ahead, more than 40 keys skipped after a stored key, 5 newer chains, or a deleted or retired session — gaps > 2 000 refused; RO4 several unacknowledged PreKey messages reordered; **RO5** simultaneous first contact converges (R-SS3); **RO6** the measured worst-case pickle fits `SESSION_STATE_MAX` with margin, and a fault-injected oversize is a typed outcome with a working later reset (§7.9 point 4); **RO7** a HIGH burst of 60 messages with a withholding relay: loss 0 with peek ordering and the gap hold — **extended by S0′ (V-7, 2026-09-19):** RO3′ and RO7 use the V-7 worlds (60-message HIGH bursts without replies, an offline recipient, a withholding relay and a late relay, **a fresh process for every consumer pass**) and require **zero loss under the hold rule §7.7 point 2 states**, re-run whenever that rule changes; **RO8** every interleaving of restore-driven and simultaneous initiation with up to three messages per side converges |
| **RS restart** | RS1 process death at every lane item and actor step on seeded interleavings; RS2 a client offline 60 days (blobs past TTL lost and declared, no duplicates, sessions usable or reset by the user; pair envelopes open after the absence, §21.3 point 5); **RS3′** a 28-day unacknowledged initiator session: the next send re-establishes, the old session is demoted |
| **RP replay** (CS-7) | RP1 re-listing within the tombstone (D1); RP2 RESURRECT after tombstone GC with each other layer disabled in turn (B2, D2a, D2b, D3, freshness each sufficient); RP3 COPY_NAMESPACE (B1: does not open); RP4 first-flight replay after session resets (D2b, then D3); RP5 PreKey replay across a restart (the reuse tuple persisted); RP6 re-encryption after a wrongly reported FAILED (D3); RP7 an old L0 re-sealed under a later week key (B1 week binding); RP8 pre-restore content (`accept_after_minute`); RP9 TRUNCATE and byte flips (does not open, no counter difference that could be an oracle); RP10 a blob of contact A presented as contact B's (hint mismatch); **RP11** a replayed PreKey message after R-SS1 replaced its session |
| **KC key change** (CS-5) | KC1 B's Olm identity changes (debug hook): A `changed`, `send` → `KEY_CHANGED`, the session envelope and the outbox byte-identical before and after the attempt; KC2 new-key messages held, not displayed, no plaintext in DB or WAL, held rows gone at their bound; KC3 acknowledge: held messages displayed once in order, sending allowed, a new root offered, `verified_fp` cleared, old sessions deleted; **KC3b** (§22.8.1) two and more than 40 held messages of one new session in random `held_id` order, and a RECV-created session before HELD: displayed once, in order, no constraint abort; KC4 a wrong scanned fingerprint keeps `changed`, the right one → `verified`; KC5 **forged binding or no binding**: dropped, no state change (E-1); KC5b a genuine binding replayed with another recipient namespace, another ciphertext or base key, or a stale minute → no state change; KC6 key change found in a fetched bundle before any encrypt (E-2); KC7 revocation: nothing produced toward B, nothing shown from B; KC8 same-identity PreKey (B restored): a new session under R-SS1, no block, out keys reset; KC9 old-session messages of B shown while `changed`; KC10 a second pending key replaces the first; KC11 NI-DM5 on the whole suite; KC12 a requester sending two identities → no crash, no state change; **KC13** key-compromise impersonation at first contact (§8.3) |
| **CB contacts** | requests (limit, expiry, accept, reject, block, no reflex), block, unblock, delete, `MAX_CONTACTS`, R-E1…R-E4; a request-cap flood by RESURRECT after generation erasure and after a restore → no request |
| **RE restore** | same safety numbers; `accept_after_minute`; unopenable counts for pre-restore pair, provisional and intro envelopes and no request from them (§8.6); `reconnect` after re-added cards, spread over 48 h and cancelled by an earlier user send; reads with the seed-derived key before any token; the lost device's bundle in the same and the previous week, a restored device without tokens, and a contact-initiated reset after the restored bundle (outranking by `bundle_rank`) |
| **EP ephemeral** (CS-6, T25) | the §10.9 cases for every timer × path; timer versions (concurrent equal versions converge to the shorter timer; a lost TIMER_SET healed; a replayed old version ignored); R-T3 bucket on TIMER_SET; the checkpoint busy-reader case; a clock step back; the alarm contract (non-exported receiver, no extras, one request code, cancel at wipe); a sender clock 5 days ahead does not extend the unread deadline (ME-19); HIGH mode 5-minute store after expiry (ME-21); held replay with 1 h and 30 d timers; a stale `markDisplayed` |
| **OS owner schedule** | registrations for `{c, c + 1}` from a capability redeemed late in its week and from one redeemed early; `WRONG_PERIOD` from a stale capability; the healing rule after LOSE_KEYS; ATTACKER_REGISTERS changes nothing for other senders (SC-1); publication at the PRF offset with and without sessions nearby; generation rotation and erasure at 91 d; `bundle_rank` after activation and restore; pair-key and `prov_env` erasure at their windows; a FAILED and an INDETERMINATE publication re-sealed; owner offline across week boundaries (the bundle gap declared, registrations catch up) |
| **RK relay** | `register.txt` and TTL vectors (Rust relay, `ModelRelay`); `keys.redb` restart, sweep and loss; the `DepositLedger` after restart; capture fixtures v2; `two_nodes.rs` capture with `register`, deposits and 3600 TTLs validated by `capture-check`; a credential replayed at another slot refused |
| **PQ (new, §22)** | PQ1 every intro open performs exactly one decapsulation and `k_in` depends on `ss` (spy); PQ2 T35 provenance over the DM world; PQ3 PQ-C healing world: both devices' state leaked at day t, the adversary given all classical secrets and no post-t ML-KEM secrets opens no pair envelope after the healing cycle and does before it (ground truth); PQ4 slack-rule attachment never changes the bucket (an NI-DM2 variant); **PQ5 bump assertion (RG-5)**, run at S0′ and by every vodozemac or libcrux bump PR: every intro envelope and every `seal_root_pq` of a scripted world carries a 1 120-byte X-Wing ciphertext, `k_in` depends on `ss`, and a `gate`-variant client whose intro inner layer is sealed without the KEM is refused by an honest recipient (PM-32 as a bump gate); **PQ6** (§22.8.6) a restored and a reset peer with a stored PQ offer and an old in-band key: pair mode from the peer resumes within one round trip after its next `pq_key` or classical root, no `NO_SEAL_KEY`; the low-order and invalid-key vectors of §22.8.2 (typed codes, no crash, no state change, also through peek) |

### 15.6 DM privacy world (T32) and the seizure scenario (T34)

**Forward note (S11, 2026-09-22).** §22.16 records what the S11 slice built of this section — the
world at the PR scale, JD1–JD7 and JD9, NI-DM1 and NI-DM3, S-DM3, the seizure world without its one-reply
variant, `dm-privacy-gate.yml` — and names every part it did not.

- **World.** N = 120 clients over 14 virtual days (PR, in the `android` job), N = 600 over 42 days (gate, `dm-privacy-gate.yml`, nightly and on dispatch, 10 seed pairs, STANDARD and HIGH); 3 model relays, all AD-1, exporting the effective view (exact times, circuits, raw TTLs, stored bytes, `keys.redb` records, credential kinds). Behaviour: degree ~ Poisson(6); bursty conversations with LogNormal reply delay (median 2 min); the Phase 7 app-session and job model; timers 60 % 7 d, 10 % 30 d, 5 % off, 10 % 1 d, 10 % 1 h, 5 % 5 min; 2 % key changes (debug hook), 1 % restores with reconnect, 3 % requests rejected, 10 % of clients onboard by invite and message their inviter; one ES slot-table change; 10 % of devices with a clock offset U[−3 d, +3 d] (§21.2) — **narrowed by the review of S2 (2026-09-19)** to `U[−σ/2, +σ/2]` unless the relay skew of the seed is 0, because the store receipt's upper bound (§10.2 point 6) refuses an honest receipt once `relay now − now > σ`, which a device at −3 d and a relay ahead of true time reach (L7-D27); the clock-offset twin NI-DM6 keeps its purpose at that range; hostile modes REFUSE_ONE_SENDER and WITHHOLD_ALL_BUNDLES in a quarter of the seeds; the PQ-C cadence and slack-rule attachments in the behaviour model. Ground truth: identities, cards, namespaces, sessions, edges, the user action behind each store, timers.
- **Exact joins (must find 0).**
  - JD1 identity dictionary (every `ghost1` hash, Ed25519 key, Olm identity key in its 32-byte form and base64 forms, safety-number bytes, and for each SHA-256, SHA-256 with every label, HKDF with every label) scanned over every captured field, circuit-level byte, stored blob and `keys.redb` record;
  - JD2 Olm header strings (identity keys, base keys, fallback keys, ratchet keys, 32-byte prefixes of X-Wing public keys and ciphertexts, `gen_id` values in context) absent;
  - JD3 per (relay, inbox, week) the deposit-credential store scopes ⊆ {the deposit scope}, and no redeem or register by an honest client for a namespace it does not own;
  - JD4 `size_bucket` = class(body length) for every DM store;
  - JD5 TTL = the §10.1 table, no store after the deadline, every membership expiry ≤ the T30 bound;
  - JD6 every store maps to an `oracle_user_action`, a scheduled action, a bundle publication or an identical resend;
  - JD7 every list or get is on own namespaces, except one bundle fetch per establishment, reset or reconnect (≤ 3 attempts, list events only inside attempt windows);
  - JD8 redemptions and registrations depend only on own namespaces and the week;
  - JD9 every DM namespace's relay set equals `set(ns, w)` or the declared 6-week union;
  - **JD10 (RG-4)** no 16-byte window of any intro envelope's inner bytes (after the outer AEAD, as a card holder sees them) repeats anywhere in the world: every re-sent PreKey envelope has a fresh X-Wing ciphertext.
- **Twins** (identical shape views `(op, relay, ns, size, ttl, minute, scope, key_kind, batch_count, result, circuit open/close minute)`): NI-DM1 inbound content (valid, malformed, replays, probes, forged, key-changed, requests) with identical user actions; NI-DM2 message type and session state at equal body length (first vs later, intro vs provisional vs pair, text vs TIMER_SET vs CONTROL, with and without pending PQ TLVs); NI-DM3 degree (k vs k′ idle contacts); NI-DM4 timer within a bucket (5 min vs 1 h; 30 d vs off); NI-DM5 local trust actions (verify, acknowledge, block, request decisions); NI-DM6 inbound arrival times, unrelated send times, sender clock offsets and one relay's refusals (owner, scheduled and registration store minutes; deposit-scope multisets per (relay, inbox); §21.9), with an AD-7 variant over bundle contents and every field of every get response (PM-35, PM-38) and a reported (not gated) polling list view of `P` (§22.8.7); **NI-DM7 (RG-4)** card-holder views (the outer layer opened with `KIO`: `gen_id`, ciphertext and inner bytes as a card holder sees them) are identical in shape between twin worlds that differ only in a first flight versus a re-sent unacknowledged PreKey message.
- **Statistics.** S-DM1 (gating): conversation-edge attackers on the full view (reply-timing survival, co-occurrence plus fetch → store lag, logistic regression over every effective-view feature) vs the same family restricted to LD (§1.2): full-view AUC − LD AUC not significantly > 0 (stratified permutation test, 999 permutations, α = 0.001). S-DM2 (reported): LD-attacker AUC and rounds-to-single-out per mode and per TTL class (§21.11); the report check fails on a regression above the recorded baseline + 0.02. S-DM3 (gating): an attacker holding every `ghost1` but no card maps namespaces to identities within the 99.9 % binomial interval of chance; a control with a colluding subset's cards maps exactly that subset's contacts. S-DM4 (reported): mutual information between an inbox's TTL-bucket sequence and the conversation label. **AD-7 with a token (reported, §22.8.7):** a card holder alone that redeems one token per relay for a target inbox, lists, gets and matches `KIO` hints; the report gives, against ground truth, the intro envelopes it recognised with their minutes and the unanswered conversations it inferred (the declared L7-D16 residue, measured). Floors as in F8 §19.24 point 6: a statistical check with n < 100 fails rather than passes.
- **T34 seizure scenario** (a separate world: 10 clients, 70 virtual days, daily conversations): at day 70 dump two clients' databases, state keys and seeds; try every stored key, every derivation, Olm decryption with every retained session, throwaway accounts over every retained generation, X-Wing decapsulation and every retained `prov_env` on every archived blob: pair-mode blobs of weeks with `start(w + 1) + 45 d ≤ day 70` opened = the skipped-key ground truth of live sessions (0 for retired sessions, 0 in the lossless variant); intro and provisional blobs opened only for generations and `prov_env`s not yet erased (ground truth); recognition keys retained beyond their opening keys = 0 (PM-36); a one-reply conversation variant (the peer never writes after receiving my root; the world runs 130 virtual days and is dumped at day 125) asserts that both devices erased `prov_env` by `prov_anchor_minute + 119 d` (PM-39, §22.8.5).
- **Report.** `scripts/gates/dm-report-check.sh` (the `t2-report-check.sh` pattern) requires every line and refuses a PASS on an empty sample.

### 15.8 Exit-gate evidence and argument

Gate: *"2 dispozitive fizice: reorder/restart/replay; schimbare de cheie blochează; T25 verde"* (plan:311), with spec p.23 and the FR-2.1 (as amended by ADR-35), FR-4.1, FR-4.4, FR-4.5, FR-4.6 acceptance criteria.

| Gate item | What proves it on the JVM (exhaustive, the real ratchet through `ghost-dm-host`, the real `:sync`) | What the emulator job adds (real Android, SQLCipher, Keystore, JobScheduler, process death, the Android build of `ghost-client-dm`) | What the two physical devices add (signed checklist) |
|---|---|---|---|
| reorder | RO1–RO8, CS-4 oracle, SCN-B, seeded SHUFFLE and HIGH delays, EM-1 | the SCN-B script on SQLCipher, order digests equal | vendor schedulers, real Tor latency, airplane toggles; order digests equal (step 2) |
| restart | SCN-A…SCN-L crash enumeration with doubles and structural invariants, native-crash and Rust-panic poison, `SQLITE_FULL`; RS1, RS2, RS3′ | the `:peer` process killed with SIGKILL at scripted points; emulator reboot | `am kill`, force-stop and reboot during sends and receives (step 3) |
| replay | RP1–RP11 with each layer isolated; SCN-B, SCN-G, SCN-H | the replay scripts over the loopback model relay | identical-bytes re-stores and debug-hook resurrection against staging relays (step 4) |
| key change blocks | KC1–KC13, SCN-D (with the forged variant), PM-15…PM-17, PM-34, MC-6 | the debug hook on the peer, blocking and acknowledgement | the same through the `gate` variant (step 5) |
| T25 green | EP suite, logical and physical scans, SCN-F, ME-6…ME-24; the `NotificationPort` contract | SQLCipher WAL at 0 bytes after sweep; the alarm scheduled and cancelled | exported database and WAL hold no canary; staging capture shows 3600 (step 6); notification evidence is Phase 13's (§21.10) |
| privacy (not in the gate text; required by CP-10 before any claim) | T32 (JD1–JD10, NI-DM1–7, S-DM1/S-DM3 gating), T34, **T35**, 40 privacy mutants, the `pq-model` job green | logcat holds no canary key; host/Android equivalence (§15.9 item 7) | staging capture: `register`, deposit scopes, `ttl_bucket_seconds`, validated by `capture-check` (step 8) |
| restore (FR-1.4, C-05; the checklist names it) | SCN-I, RE suite | — | restore, re-added card and reconnect on a real device (step 7) |

**Argument.** Every correctness property of the gate is a property of transactions, value-state session calls and state machines that the JVM world exercises exhaustively with the real session library and the real sync engine against a relay model whose semantics are pinned by vectors the real relay replays, and that EM-1 and EM-2 enumerate at the abstract level. The emulator job removes the JVM-specific assumptions: SQLCipher instead of plain SQLite, Keystore wrapping, JobScheduler, alarm delivery, process death, the Android build of `ghost-client-dm` inside `libghost_client_net.so` and R8, and it checks by the equivalence test that the Android build and the host build produce byte-identical outputs on deterministic inputs. The physical devices are what the gate names: they add vendor power management, real notification history, real flash, real Tor and staging relays, and they are where NFR-1, NFR-4 and NFR-5 are measured. **The gate is closed only when all three columns are green on the same commit.**

**What the proof assumes:** vodozemac implements Olm correctly at the pinned version (TM A1 as amended by ADR-35; evidence in §2.9); the host build of `ghost-client-dm` behaves like its Android build (same Rust source and pins; checked on deterministic inputs by the host/Android equivalence test, RG-2; the emulator and devices exercise the Android build); libcrux X-Wing matches the published vectors; SQLite and SQLCipher durability with `synchronous = FULL`; the Phase 7 exactly-once proof (`sync-exit-gate` stays green, including `peek` and `claimOne`).

### 15.9 Emulator substitute (`dm-emulator.yml`; G-11, C-21)

- **Runner.** `reactivecircus/android-emulator-runner` pinned by commit SHA (the `ci.yml:5` rule for third-party actions, like `android-actions/setup-android`; X-17), on ubuntu with KVM; **API 29 and API 35 `x86_64`** AOSP images without Google services.
- **Suite.** The instrumented `DeviceGateSuite` in `:messaging`'s `androidTest`:
  1. Client B runs in a separate `:peer` process (a service declared only in the `androidTest` manifest, so the app's merged manifest, T15m, is unaffected), killed with **SIGKILL** at scripted points.
  2. **Real SQLCipher:** a v3 fixture database migrates to v4 and v5 (CS-9 on the real engine), then the SCN-A, SCN-B, SCN-D and SCN-F scripts.
  3. **Real native library:** `libghost_client_net.so` (x86_64) with the DM symbols; a **release-variant smoke test**: the R8-shrunk APK starts, loads the library, opens a DM context and completes one in-process Olm session between two in-memory state sets through `DmNative`, with no transport (§21.13); the loopback-relay session runs in the debug `androidTest` variant.
  4. **In-process loopback model relays** over a test-only transport; no Tor. `live-tor` stays manual.
  5. **T25 on SQLCipher:** WAL at 0 bytes after `TRUNCATE`; the alarm is scheduled and cancelled (notification checks move to Phase 13, §21.10) (`dumpsys alarm` through `UiAutomation`).
  6. **Logcat scan:** during a scripted key change, no line holds a canary key (the libsignal tag clause is withdrawn with libsignal; no crate of the DM graph logs).
  7. **Host/Android equivalence test (RG-2; V-16).** The same deterministic inputs run on `ghost-dm-host` (in the `android` job, outputs recorded as a fixture) and on the emulator's x86_64 **test library** (the same source, pins and toolchain as `libghost_client_net.so` plus the `test-vectors` feature, packaged only in the `androidTest` APK; the shipped library never carries the entry point, §22.8.10 point 6). **Input set fixed by S0′ (V-16, 2026-09-19)** — the deterministic case set of `protocol/test-vectors/dm_equivalence.txt` (`id ‖ kind ‖ hex input`): HKDF derivations and the card v2 codec; the MSC3814 encoder and the identity keys of `Account::from_dehydrated_device`; X-Wing key generation from seeds and `encapsulate_derand`/decapsulation (the draft vectors plus 32 seeded cases); every seal mode with fixed salts, nonces and KEM randomness, and their openings; decryption of the golden transcripts from their stored pickles; normal-message encryption from a fixed **active** pickle (deterministic once the sending ratchet is active); the pickle JSON after each of those steps; the prune of V-15 on a fixed pickle and sidecar; safety numbers; L0 and TLV codecs including refusals (result codes are outputs too). **Excluded, because they draw randomness:** PreKey creation and outbound session creation. The `android` job records `id ‖ SHA-256(output) ‖ hex output` as a job-output fixture and `dm-emulator.yml` replays the same file on the emulator's x86_64 test library, comparing byte for byte and printing the first differing case. **Controls (required):** a self-test case whose input differs between the two runs by one byte must be reported as different, so the comparator is not vacuous; the report line `dm-equivalence: N cases, N identical, control detected` is required by `dm-report-check.sh`; a job that cannot boot the emulator reports "not run". **Same build inputs:** one commit, one `Cargo.lock`, Rust 1.98.1, the portable libcrux cfgs (G-R3 on both builds) and the same feature set plus `test-vectors`; the fixture header records the commit and both build-script cfg lists, and the comparison refuses fixtures from another commit. **Not covered, declared:** arm64 execution (the emulator image is x86_64; arm64 is exercised by the release smoke test on the physical devices, §15.10), CPU-dependent backends (curve25519-dalek selects its x86_64 backend at run time: outputs are equal by definition, the code is not), and anything randomised. The outputs must be byte-identical; a difference fails the job.
- **Triggers (amended by §22.8.9).** Nightly on `main` and on dispatch; pull requests touching `android/{messaging,crypto-bridge,storage,sync,network}` or `client-core/dm` run one API level with the reduced script set, budgeted in S0′'s CI plan. While the repository is public the job builds its native libraries itself (no artifact hand-off).
- **Status.** A regression net and gate evidence for the Android-specific parts, not the physical gate; a run that cannot start the emulator reports **not run**, never green. It also closes the Phase 7/8 "device-only" items that fit an emulator (SQLCipher migration, JNI on x86_64). The job uploads no artifact to a public destination (G-L5).

### 15.10 Physical-device checklist (manual, signed; G-15)

**Setup.** Two phones: A a mid-range arm64 device on API 29–31, B a recent arm64 device on API 35 or later; the release candidate APK and its `gate` debug variant (never released) for the steps marked †, which exposes `Messaging` to an instrumentation runner and includes the debug key-change hook and the debug-only order digest export; live Tor; 3 staging relays over 2 operators with the stagenet ES; a host orchestrator `scripts/device/dm-gate.sh` that drives both phones through adb and **records digests, never content**. The APKs are built locally or in a job that uploads nothing and are installed only on the gate devices (G-L5, ADR-39).

1. † **Establishment:** each phone's card is injected into the other by adb as `addContact(card, qr_in_person)` (no camera, no new permission; §21.13); both `verified`; safety numbers equal.
2. † **Reorder:** 30-message bursts each way, HIGH mode on A, airplane-mode toggles on B (`cmd connectivity airplane-mode`); the order digests (SHA-256 of the rendered `message_id` sequence) are equal on both phones.
3. † **Restart:** `am kill`, `am force-stop` and a reboot during 20 sends on A and during receives on B; after quiescence the multiset digest shows no duplicate and nothing missing after its relay quorum.
4. † **Replay:** the host re-store tool (§21.13) re-stores 5 delivered blobs with B's deposit credential (identical bytes, D1); † A's debug hook re-encrypts a delivered L0 after a forced FAILED (D3); † B's debug hook clears its Phase 7 tombstones for its inbox and re-lists (D2a, D2b, D3, freshness). Nothing shown twice; the drop counters rise.
5. † **Key change blocks:** B's debug hook switches to a bound new Olm identity and sends to A; A shows `changed`, A's send is refused, held messages are invisible; after acknowledgement they appear once, in order, and both directions work.
6. † **T25:** a 5-minute timer both ways; both locked for 20 minutes; unlocked: the messages are gone; database and WAL exported through `run-as`; the host scan finds no canary and no `body_env` bytes (notification history is Phase 13 evidence, §21.10).
7. † **Restore and reconnect (G-15):** B is wiped and restored from its mnemonic; A's earlier messages are counted unopenable on B and create no request (§8.6); B re-adds A's card (adb injection as in step 1) and reconnects A (the scheduled reset is forced due through †); A's session renews with no key-change warning; A's later messages are delivered to B.
8. † **Staging capture** (S1 relays deployed to staging, §21.13): the 5-minute message stored with `ttl_bucket_seconds = 3600`; `register` events with `key_kind`; one deposit scope per inbox per week; validated by `capture-check`.
9. **Measurements, recorded and not gated:** NFR-1 (30 min of 4 KiB messages in the foreground, p50/p95/p99), NFR-4 (APK size universal and per ABI, `libghost_client_net.so` reported separately with the DM crates' share measured in S0′), NFR-5 (idle battery over 6 h with the screen off, if time allows); the portable ML-KEM decapsulation time on A (V-6).

**Evidence.** `docs/reviews/<date>-faza9-devices.md`: device models and Android versions, build and relay commit hashes, orchestrator logs, digests; signed off by the owner.

---

## 16. Gates, CI and dependencies (I1, I11; rewritten by §22)

### 16.1 Gates (each change has a negative fixture and a `self-test.sh` hit count)

**Forward note (S4b, 2026-09-20).** §22.9 item 15: G-R2 is implemented in `rust-feature-policy.sh`; G-R4's dependency-graph half and G-R7 are deferred to the S0′ gate slice with G-R3 and G-R8.

**Forward note (S6, 2026-09-20).** §22.11 items 8 and 9: **G-DM2** is implemented in `dm-enqueue-callers.sh` and runs in `run-all.sh`, with four self-test fixtures; its behavioural half (`NoReflexTest`) is S7's. The scope widening of this table's last rows — `sync-no-catch-all.sh`, `no-logging.sh`, `anti-placeholder.sh` and `kotlin-clearnet.sh` over `android/messaging`, `android/crypto-bridge` and `client-core/dm`, and `messaging-no-network.sh` (G-DM1) — stays with the S0′ gate slice, which must first decide what to do about `DmSessions.call`'s deliberate `UnsatisfiedLinkError` catch.

| Gate | Rule | Supports |
|---|---|---|
| **G-R1** `rust-client-allowlist.sh`, `client-core/rust-dependency-allowlist.txt`, `rust-crypto-pins.txt` | the new crate names of vodozemac 0.11.0 (`aead`, `block-padding`, `cbc`, `chacha20poly1305`, `hpke`, `matrix-pickle`, `matrix-pickle-derive`, `poly1305`, `serde_bytes`, `serdect`, `vodozemac`) and of libcrux (`libcrux-kem`, `libcrux-ml-kem`, `libcrux-sha3`, `libcrux-intrinsics`, `libcrux-platform`, `libcrux-secrets`, `libcrux-traits`, `libcrux-curve25519`, **`libcrux-hacl-rs`**, `libcrux-ecdh`, `libcrux-p256`, `libcrux-macros`, `libcrux-sha2`, `hax-lib`, `hax-lib-macros`; the final list generated from `cargo tree -e normal` on both shipped triples in S0′, V-6, RX-10, §22.8.10 point 8); exact versions of vodozemac, `hpke`, every libcrux crate, and the version sets `curve25519-dalek {4.1.3, 5.0.0}`, `x25519-dalek {2.0.1, 3.0.0}`, `ed25519-dalek {2.2.0, 3.0.0}`, `rand {0.8.8, 0.10.2}` per shipped graph (both `rand` majors are already in `ghost-client-net`; `rust-crypto-pins.sh` gains version sets on `graph` lines with a self-test, §22.8.10 point 5); `x-wing` and `vodozemac/low-level-api` only in dev graphs; `ghost-dm-host` excluded from the shipped graph. **Confirmed by S0′ (V-6, 2026-09-19):** the names are exactly this list (11 vodozemac + 15 libcrux) on **both** shipped triples, generated by `cargo tree -e normal`; vodozemac 0.11.0 adds no further name, while 0.10.0 would add `opaque-debug` and not `hpke` or `serdect`. `libcrux-traits 0.0.8` becomes a direct dependency (§2.3). The nine **new versions of existing names** are identical on both triples (`base16ct 1.0.0`, `base64 0.23.1`, `curve25519-dalek 5.0.0`, `ed25519 3.0.0`, `ed25519-dalek 3.0.0`, `hkdf 0.13.0`, `hmac 0.13.0`, `signature 3.0.0`, `x25519-dalek 3.0.0`); `chacha20poly1305 0.11.0` is a **new name**, not a new version (the unpatched graph holds none), and `curve25519-dalek-derive 0.1.1` is already in the unpatched x86_64 graph and is added by neither triple. No `cc`, `cmake`, `bindgen`, `clang-sys`, `crabgrind` or `libloading` in the normal+build graph of either triple, and no new `links` key: the DM stack adds **no C code** | T7, I1 |
| **G-R2** `rust-feature-policy.sh` | vodozemac in the shipped graph without `libolm-compat`, `low-level-api`, `experimental-session-config`, `insecure-pk-encryption` (**`precomputed-tables` stays off** — decided by S0′, V-9, 2026-09-19: it costs +28 816 B arm64-v8a and +13 616 B x86_64 and speeds up only vodozemac-internal key generation and Ed25519 signing, which GHOST does not use for identities); `ghost-client-dm` default features empty | ADR-35 |
| **G-R3** `libcrux-portable.sh` | **Rule replaced by S0′ (V-6, 2026-09-19).** The libcrux build scripts do not re-run when `LIBCRUX_DISABLE_SIMD*` change (no `cargo:rerun-if-env-changed`), so **every** client build — host and both Android triples — is preceded by `cargo clean -p libcrux-ml-kem -p libcrux-sha3 -p libcrux-intrinsics [--target <triple>] --release`, which also covers target directories restored from caches (`Swatinem/rust-cache`). `libcrux-portable.sh` then fails if a `cargo:rustc-cfg` line of those three packages carries `feature="simd128"` or `feature="simd256"`, **or if it finds no such build at all**: for host builds it reads the `build-script-executed` messages of the guarded `cargo build --message-format=json`; for cargo-ndk builds, which print no cargo messages, it reads the single `build/<package>-<hash>/output` file each package has after the clean. Fixture: a target directory first built without the variables and not cleaned (the S0′ sequence measured FAIL → FAIL → portable → portable). `.cargo/config.toml` `[env]` still sets both variables, but it is no longer the mechanism the gate trusts | ADR-36 |
| **G-R4** `dm-rust-no-logging.sh` | **Rule corrected by S0′ (V-8, 2026-09-19)**, because `jni` 0.21.1 depends on `log` and the round-9 rule failed as soon as `jni-bridge` was on: `ghost-client-dm`'s normal graph **without the `jni-bridge` feature** contains no `log`, `tracing` or `env_logger`; **with `jni-bridge`, `log` is reached only through `jni`** (checked with `cargo tree -i log`), and no crate of either graph installs a logger (the Rust side never calls `log::set_logger`); its sources contain no `println!`, `eprintln!`, `dbg!`. No `tracing` in either graph | T3 |
| **G-R5** `rust-license-headers.sh` | §2.10.2 | ADR-37 |
| **G-R6** `dm-jni-surface.sh` | the exported `Java_org_ghost_crypto_bridge_DmNative_*` symbols of the shipped `libghost_client_net.so` equal the closed list of §2.6 (**which gained `chainPosition` and `sessionPrune` in S0′, 2026-09-19; V-7, V-15**), and those of `ghost-dm-host` and the emulator test library equal the list plus the `test-vectors` entry (checked with `llvm-nm -D --defined-only`, since release builds strip `.symtab`; §22.8.10 point 6); only `android/crypto-bridge/src/{main,gate}` references `DmNative`; `:messaging` references only the typed facade | I1, ADR-35 |
| **G-R7** `dm-golden-lock.sh` | at a vodozemac or libcrux pin change, the golden directories of the new and the previous version exist, the encoder check and PQ5 pass (the first pin is exempt). **Extended by S0′ (2026-09-19):** it also pins (a) the **golden sizes** of the V-3 probe set — body lengths `0 … 64` and `[C(B) − 64, C(B)]` per bucket, twelve chain-index varint classes, both output kinds (§6.6, S0-3); (b) a **pinned session pickle per pin whose expected `chainPosition` outputs** (per ratchet key, including an unknown key and a renamed field) **and `sessionPrune` outputs** (removed count and resulting key set) are asserted, because both read vodozemac's private serde layout (§2.1, S0-15); (c) the **V-9 symbol check** that the linker still drops `hpke` (§2.9); and (d) a **compile-time exhaustive `match`** over `SessionCreationError`, `DecryptionError`, `EncryptionError` and libcrux-kem's `Error` (none is `#[non_exhaustive]` in 0.11.0 or 0.10.0), so a variant added at a bump fails the build (§15.2 `DmResultCodesTest`; V-2) | ADR-35, ADR-36 |
| **G-R8** `dm-vendored-clock-log.sh` (RG-3; rule replaced by §22.8.10 point 4) | over the compiled library code (no `build.rs`, tests, benches, examples, `cfg(test)` modules or comments) of every crate of the shipped `ghost-client-dm` graph from `cargo metadata`, the sites matching `SystemTime`, `Instant::now`, `std::time`, `chrono`, `time::`, `log::`, `tracing::`, `println!`, `eprintln!` and `dbg!`, with `cargo:` directives matched separately, must equal a committed baseline whose every entry is justified; an addition fails (the pinned sources already match, so "empty" could not hold); runs on every pin change and in `rust`; it counts mechanically. **Defined and frozen by S0′ (V-8, 2026-09-19):** the scan walks the **module tree of each package's `lib` target** with `syn`, removes every item, impl item, statement and arm whose `#[cfg]` is false for the target (`test`, `debug_assertions`, disabled features, other architectures and operating systems; unknown predicates are kept), follows `mod` declarations including `#[path]` and `cfg_attr(…, path)`, and scans the remaining tokens **including macro invocations and `macro_rules!` bodies** (comments are not tokens); proc-macro and build-time-only crates are labelled and kept in the report; the **network names** `std::net`, `TcpStream`, `TcpListener`, `UdpSocket`, `ToSocketAddrs` join the pattern list. Measured baseline: 100 packages over both triples (74 linked, 10 proc-macros, 16 build-time), **39 sites — 31 linked, 8 build-time — and 0 sites in vodozemac, hpke, matrix-pickle and every libcrux crate**; the linked sites are all in crates GHOST already ships (`jni` 23 × `log::`, `memchr` 2 inside a disabled-feature macro body, `serde_core` 4 re-exports, `rand` 1, `libc` 1), with seven justification classes and two `include!` notes (`thiserror`, `hax-lib`). The baseline starts from `client-core/dm-spike/baseline/g-r8-baseline.txt` (176 lines, 0 unjustified) and moves to `ghost/scripts/gates/dm-vendored-clock-log.baseline` in S4a; any unjustified entry fails the gate. A token scan counts names, not calls, so every re-export needs a justification | T3, T33 |

### 16.2 CI jobs

**Forward note (S4b, 2026-09-20).** §22.9 item 11 adds the nightly `dm-state-models` job.

| Job | Change |

| `static-gates` | G-L5, the source-only G-DM gates and every fixture (the job has no Rust toolchain or build outputs, `ci.yml:34-46`, `run-all.sh:2`; §22.8.10 point 10) |
| placement of the build-dependent gates | G-R1, G-R2, G-R4, G-R5, G-R7, G-R8 and the Rust part of G-L1 in `rust`; G-R3 and G-R6 (Android) in `android-native`, G-R6 (host) where `ghost-dm-host` is built; the Gradle part of G-L1, G-L2 and G-L3 in `android` or `reproducible` |
| `rust` | relay `RegisterKey` unit, restart and loss tests; `register.txt` and TTL vector replayers; `two_nodes.rs` capture with `register`, deposits and 3600; `client-core` T21 v3 matrix, TTL refusal, receipt upper bound; the T2 PR variant with the §4.9 changes; **`ghost-client-dm`** unit, vector, KAT, differential and property tests, provenance assertions, and the exhaustive state models EM-1 and EM-2 |
| **`vendor-tests`** (new; nightly and on pin changes) | vodozemac's test suite at the pinned tag; the X-Wing and ML-KEM vector suites. **Requirements recorded by S0′ (V-12, 2026-09-19):** crates.io access (or a vendored dev-dependency set), **CMake and a C++ toolchain** (`olm-sys` builds libolm; both are on the ghost-runner image, `docs/ci-self-hosted.md` §2.1); it runs `v12-vendor-tests.sh <version>`, which verifies the `.crate` against its crates.io SHA-256 and runs `cargo test --locked` with default features and with `--all-features` against the package's own lock; the package carries only its `src/` unit tests (169 in 0.11.0, 152 in 0.10.0), so a tag checkout is used if upstream integration tests exist outside it; the job's dev-dependency lock is checked by `cargo deny` (§15.2) |
| **`dm-fuzz`** (new; nightly) | `cargo-fuzz` targets (card, bundle, intro inner layer, L0, TLVs, Olm message parsing) for a fixed budget, on a dated pinned nightly (`cargo +nightly-YYYY-MM-DD fuzz`); `client-core/dm/fuzz` is excluded from the workspace with its own lock file, checked by cargo-deny (§22.8.10 point 9) |
| **`pq-model`** (new; on changes to `client-core/dm` or `protocol/models/dm`) | the ProVerif queries M-1 … M-7 (tool version pinned by hash) |
| `android-native` | builds `libghost_client_net.so` with the DM crate twice at the same path (T12); publishes the sums as a job output (text); uploads no `.so` while the repository is public, and afterwards only a hand-off artifact with `retention-days: 1` (§22.8.9) |
| `android` | **JDK 17 (no move to 21)**; builds `ghost-dm-host` first; DM unit, vector, schema and property tests; the PR `DmWorld` variant (1 000 seeded worlds, crash singles for SCN-A and SCN-B, 97 mutants on a small world (S0′, 2026-09-19), T32 at N = 120 over 14 days with one seed pair per mode); records the host outputs of the equivalence fixture; `merged-manifest-lint.sh`; builds the native libraries itself and compares them with `android-native`'s sums while the repository is public; its time budget on the private 2-CPU runner is measured in S0′ (§22.8.9) |

## 18. Risks

Rows R-1 … R-5, R-7, R-21, R-22 and R-24 were libsignal risks; the revision of 2026-09-16 withdraws them and keeps one-line records (§22). R-25 … R-41 are new; R-6, R-8, R-14 and R-19 are amended.

| Risk | Impact | Mitigation |
|---|---|---|
| R-1 (withdrawn, §22) libsignal "unsupported outside Signal" and its API churn | — | replaced by R-25 and R-29 |
| R-2 (withdrawn, §22) dormant `libsignal-net`/TLS code in a third-party native library | — | removed: no crate of the DM graph has network code (V-8) |
| R-3 (withdrawn, §22) a native library built by a third party | — | removed: GHOST builds every native byte from source (T12) |
| R-4 (withdrawn, §22) the JDK 21 move | — | removed: JDK stays 17 |
| R-5 (withdrawn, §22) desktop natives of a third-party JAR do not load on the JVM | — | replaced by R-32 (the host build) |
| R-6 `O_MAX` underestimated (Olm varints, the X-Wing encoding, a format change at a bump) | a message does not fit its bucket | measurement probe before S4a (V-3); golden sizes near every boundary with and without PQ TLVs; the runtime fit assertion rolls back before the session state commits; rerun at every bump |
| R-7 (withdrawn, §22) library logging of identity keys | — | removed: G-R4 and G-R8 keep the DM graph free of logging; the T3 canary and logcat scans stay |
| R-8 The GHOST seal (pair, intro, provisional, bundle), binding v3 and the PQ composition have a design flaw; a CP-09 exception whose weight grows because the seal now also carries the post-quantum claim (P-8) | metadata, authenticity or HNDL confidentiality loss | standard primitives only; transcript binding; one AEAD attempt per blob; canonical parsing; vectors plus the JDK cross-implementation; T29, T34, T35; EM-2; the ProVerif model in CI (§9.5); ADR-31′ and ADR-36 record the exception; the external review of §9.5 before the first distributed APK, and the Phase 15 audit |
| R-9 Relay protocol changes (`RegisterKey`, accumulation, the ledger, exact TTLs) carry bugs | refused deposits, stale verifiers, wrong expiry | vectors replayed by the Rust relay and the Kotlin model; restart, sweep and loss tests; capture fixtures; the owner healing rule |
| R-10 The recipient pays its inbox: 1 token per relay, own namespace and week, for every activated identity | exhaustion means the user cannot receive; a cost for users who never DM | constant in contacts (T27); `ENTITLEMENT_NEEDED`; trials and packs cover it; measured in the T2 world |
| R-11 Card-only establishment (no contacting by `ghost1`) hurts adoption | fewer contacts | QR and link UX in Phase 13; a discoverable mode would be a separate ADR whose residue is RP F1 by construction (Q7) |
| R-12 The key-change hold blocks a legitimate change or confuses users | friction | rare by construction (seed-derived keys); acknowledgement path; Phase 13 copy; held messages kept until their bound |
| R-13 Restore without a contact backup loses inbound messages of established contacts until reconnect | message loss after restore | declared (L7-D12); re-added cards and `reconnect`, contact resets after the restored device publishes a bundle (§8.6; round 9's intro healing is withdrawn by §22); P1 encrypted contact-card backup (Q28) |
| R-14 Hint computation at 1 000 contacts (≤ 22 recognition keys per contact) on low-end devices | slow consumption | lazy, most recent first; measured in S7 (V-13); `MAX_CONTACTS` lowered if needed (Q37); Q68's faster PQ-C cadence only if the budget holds |

| R-26 No PQ inside the ratchet | a quantum adversary with device state follows Olm until PQ-C heals | PQ-A and PQ-B at exit; the PQ-C slice; PQ-D and PQ-E recorded; declared PQ-R2, PQ-R7 |

| R-29 vodozemac changes pickle or message formats, limits or error semantics at a bump | stored sessions unreadable, silent semantic change | one PR per bump with golden pickles, `UpgradeTest`, the encoder check, PQ5, G-R8 and a CHANGELOG review; no automatic bumps |

| R-31 Olm's 40-key window loses reordered messages | user-visible loss in HIGH bursts | peek ordering, the gap hold, RO7 and the V-7 measurement; the declared bound (§7.7) |

| R-34 Portable ML-KEM too slow on low-end phones | slow intro opens | one decapsulation per intro open, after a hint match; S0′ timing (V-6); NEON only after it is verified (Q55) |

| R-37 Two Ed25519 implementations (BouncyCastle in `:identity`, dalek in `ghost-client-dm`) diverge on edge cases | a card or binding verifies on one side only | **materialised in S0′ (V-14, 2026-09-19): BouncyCastle 1.85.2 verifies cofactored and differs from `verify_strict` on 33 of 53 edge vectors, so "strict verification in both" is not achievable with its public API.** Mitigation: **one verifier per artifact type, never two** (X-21 artifacts verified only in Rust, Phase 3 artifacts only in BouncyCastle); the vector files carry each case's `verify_strict` result, and the independent JDK implementation replays signatures in full and verification only as documented expectations per class (BouncyCastle's accept/reject recorded beside dalek's), so a BouncyCastle bump that changes a class fails the test |

| R-41 The skipped-key age bound (Q67) relies on an upstream API or a pinned-schema pickle edit | a pickle edit breaks at a bump or drops a legitimate key | **adopted in S0′ (V-15, 2026-09-19): neither pin has an upstream API, so the pickle edit ships — a recorded deviation from §2.1/ADR-35 (S0-15).** The layout (`receiving_chains.inner[].skipped_message_keys.inner[]` with `key` and `index`) is identical on 0.10.0 and 0.11.0, pruned pickles load across the pins (drilled both ways), FIFO order survives a prune, and an unreadable chain or entry is `STATE_CORRUPT`, never a silent drop. Golden pickles with sidecars and `UpgradeTest` cover it, G-R7 pins the output per pin, MC-30 is in the gate |

## 19. Owner questions (the proposed default applies unless the owner objects; revised §22)

**Removed and void (§22).** **Q1** (the client licence under AGPL-3.0) is **removed**: the owner excluded libsignal and decided closed-source distribution on 2026-09-16; its number is retired, and no question is excluded from the default rule any more. **Q2** (libsignal integration route), **Q3** (Signal's Maven repository and JDK 21) and **Q21** (Kyber1024 fallback) are void and their numbers retired. Q4, Q5, Q9, Q13, Q24 and Q37 are amended below; **Q44 … Q68 are new**; the review of the revision (§22.8) re-asks Q36, amends Q61 and Q62 and adds **Q69 … Q71**. The closed-source verifiability question is **Q61**; the licence policy is Q59 and Q60, the repository Q62 and Q63.

| **Q61** | **Closed-source verifiability.** Users can no longer check source against binary. Adopt the verifiability package as release gates: published full external audit reports with retests; the published DM model; the external review of the DM composition; source access for auditors and vetted researchers under a limited-use licence and a bug bounty; builds reproduced and signed by the auditor (T12b); a witnessed binary transparency log that the updater enforces; a separate public repository with the protocol specification, vectors, `allowed-observables.json`, the model and a black-box verification kit; published per-release evidence (threat model, guarantee matrix, residual limits, SBOM, gate results); no obfuscation of GHOST classes; source escrow with public release conditions; the UI residue "Nu puteți verifica singuri codul sursă"; **amended (§22.8.8):** a standalone log-inclusion verifier for first installs, relay and issuer releases logged and checked by operators, per-release native symbol files hash-bound to the shipped library, and P-1 … P-8 stated only for the logged build? Or open-source only the security core after the first audit (contrary to the owner decision, kept as an option to revisit)? | **the package, as release gates** (ADR-38), with the §22.8.8 additions; no partial open-sourcing now | Fără sursă publică: audituri și modele publicate, build reprodus de auditor, jurnal de transparență impus de actualizator, specificație și vectori publici, escrow; toate sunt condiții de lansare |

## 20. Findings outside Phase 9 (proposed as separate tasks)

- **F-1.** `docs/STATUS.md` is stale: the Phase 8 row still says "următoarea", and ADR-22 … ADR-27 statuses are incomplete (RS K23). Updated in S14 at the latest (S13 before the revision of §22).
- **F-2.** NFR text conflicts: NFR-1 has three values (spec, plan, ADR-20) and NFR-4 two (70 or 80 MiB); the owner fixes the text (RS K7, K8; Q23, Q24).
- **F-3.** ADR-27 names an observable `ttl_bucket` that does not exist (the field is `ttl_bucket_days`); corrected by ADR-32 and Appendix B.
- **F-4.** The T1 capture does not emit `circuit_id` although it is allowed; real captures under-estimate linkability (RP F10). The DM world exports circuits; the relay capture should too (Phase 12).
- **F-5.** Owner-bound read rule (SC-2, L7-D16, Q36): a relay could refuse `list`/`get` with a write capability on a namespace whose owner registered a READ key with an ownership proof; needs a proof unknown to card holders (for example a seed-derived preimage of the namespace carried in the card). Phase 12.
- **F-6.** Session-start co-occurrence now links DM identities to channel namespaces through a contact (L7-D2); the P1 decoupling belongs in Phase 12.
- **F-7.** The Phase 7 receipt lower bound is vacuous for TTLs below σ; a relay-corrected bound is a Phase 12 item (L7-D15).
- **F-8.** TTL-aware tombstone retention for ephemeral blobs (RP §3.9 item 5) would shorten the AD-8 trail; it reopens the Phase 7 retention proof. Phase 12.
- **F-9.** Phase 8 drops keep a raw X25519 key first (`DropSeal.kt:14-20`), distinguishable from uniform bytes (RP F9); aligning drops with a uniform shape is a Phase 12 item (C-17, Q18).
- **F-10 (RG-11). Carry the OpenMLS work of round 9b into the Phase 10 (channels) design, not Phase 9:** the snapshot and change-set storage adapter with its scope test (it neutralises the SRLabs storage-atomicity finding S1-3); the day rewrite of OpenMLS epoch timestamps and a mechanical clock-site gate (the G-R8 pattern); a log-site gate that counts call sites mechanically at the pin (the hand counts of 229 and 190 `log::` calls disagree; RX-12); the single `rayon` worker check; the probe measurements with the X-Wing suite (KeyPackage 2 830 B, Welcome 4 521 B, commit 4 127 B, which force 16 KiB with that suite); the `hpke-rs` MPL-2.0 decision; the SRLabs report **v1.2 of 2026-03-11** (not v1.1; RX-3), whose finding **S0-1 (unbounded allocations, DoS of group members) is risk-accepted, not mitigated** (RX-4). File an ADR amending spec Appendix A's MLS row "OpenMLS 0.8.x baseline via reviewed Rust-to-Android bridge" (spec.txt:1086) when Phase 10 pins 0.9.x (RX-7).
- **F-11. Upstream vodozemac requests (Q58):** a documented `Account` constructor from caller-chosen keys (today served by the MSC3814 dehydrated-device path, R-30); a public receiving counterpart of `Session::from_root_key_material` (PQ-D); an API to drop or age-bound receiver-chain skipped keys (Q67).
- **F-12. Repository history and operator images (ADR-39, R-39):** everything pushed before the visibility switch is public (at least `b2df181` and the CI artifacts listed then, §22.8.9); the owner actions of §2.10.5 and counsel's review of the published `MIT OR Apache-2.0` declaration and of distributing operator images are outside the phase's code but gate the first distribution.

## 21. Normative corrections after review

**Revision note (2026-09-16).** This section records the review of the libsignal-based round. §22 revises it for the removal of libsignal: points that named libsignal APIs, records or signals are re-sourced in place and marked "(revised §22)"; §21.5 and §21.6 points 1–5 are withdrawn and replaced by the rules they point to. Where §22 and this section disagree, §22 wins.

The adversarial review of this design (four lenses: cryptography, metadata privacy, correctness, implementability; 49 findings) was checked against this document, the five research notes, the libsignal v0.102.2 sources copied during the review (`session.rs`, `session_management.rs`, `protocol.rs`, `core/src/address.rs`, `storage.proto`, `SessionCipher.java`) and the repository at `e833250` (`InboxStore.kt`, `DirectoryStore.kt`, `Namespaces.kt`, `OutboxStore.kt`, `MigrationRunner.kt`, `RestoreScan.kt`, `EngineTestKit.kt`, `AndroidManifest.xml`, `MainActivity.kt`, `app/build.gradle.kts`, `ci.yml`, `build-native.sh`, `issuer/crates/service/tests/t2/world.rs`, `infra/issuer/RUNBOOK.md`, `relay.proto`, `relay/crates/node/src/lib.rs`; the repository is public and has no licence file). **No finding was rejected as wrong.** Four pairs are duplicates and are merged (CR-2 = CR-04, CR-6 = CR-06, PRIV-11 = CR-10, CR-15 = BUILD-10), and CR-16 is resolved by the §21.2 credential rule. For nine findings (CR-2, CR-3, CR-4, CR-5, CR-6, CR-02, PRIV-1, PRIV-4, PRIV-12) the proposed fix is replaced, narrowed or only partly adopted, each with its reason in the subsection. Two claims inside findings are narrowed: an oversize session write rolls back, so a later SEND crashes only when its own write crosses the bound (CR-6); `ReusedBaseKeyException extends InvalidMessageException` (CR-7 c) was not in the copied sources and is an S0 check. **This section is normative: where it and an earlier section disagree, this section wins.** Earlier sections were corrected in place where the change is local; those places cite "(§21.n)".
