### 21.1 Relay sets do not depend on the week (CR-01)

**Defect.** §5.2 put `u64_be(w)` into the slot-order HMAC, a leftover of the base design's weekly namespaces. With static namespaces (C-4) every DM namespace got a fresh relay set each week: owners registered on last week's relays, senders deposited on this week's, readers unioned up to 18 relays, and `setRelays` failed in-flight deliveries at every boundary.

**Rules.**
1. `order(ns, w)` = the ES slots valid in week w sorted by `HMAC-SHA256(key = ns, "ghost/v1/dm/slot-order" || u8(slot))`; `set(ns, w)` = the relays of the first slots in that order until 3 are taken and they span ≥ 2 operators. With an unchanged slot table the set is identical in every week; it changes only when the slot numbers valid in a week, or the operator behind a slot among the first ones, change.
2. `KEEPER` recomputes the set at each week change and calls `setRelays` only on a difference (unchanged, now rare).
3. **Reader union after a real change.** The owner reads the relays of `set(ns, w′)` for `w′ ∈ [week(now) − 5, week(now)]`. `REGROWS` keeps READ rows for every union relay that still holds an ES slot, and DEPOSIT rows only on the current set; the registration need rule (§13.3 item 4) then redeems there. Cost: one extra ACCESS token per extra union relay per week, for at most 6 weeks after a slot-table change; §4.8's accounting and the T2 client model (§4.9) count it. A union relay that left the ES gets no capability; its last registrations lapse by themselves (§21.2 point 9), it then leaves the union, and blobs stored there are lost only if every relay of their week's set left too (declared in L7-D13).
4. **Tests.** `dm_relay_set.txt` gains: the same table in weeks w and w + 1 gives identical sets; a change of a slot outside the first three gives an identical set; a change inside changes it. Mutant **MC-22** (order keyed by the week) is detected by JD9 and by SCN-E (deposits refused after a week boundary).

### 21.2 Credentials: static per (card, relay slot, kind), bound to the slot (PRIV-1, CR-5, CR-16)

**Defects.** (1) The deposit credential's week was chosen by the sender's own clock, KEEPER timing and a relay's refusal (the fallback), while relays accepted three weeks at once: a sender with a clock offset switched scope at a device-specific minute in every inbox it wrote to, a malicious relay could tag a refused sender across sessions, and P-2 was false. (2) v3 credentials named no relay: a relay that saw a deposit or read secret could replay it at the other relays and operators of the set, exhausting the recipient's allowance there or listing the owner's inbox, against ADR-11 independence. (3) The fallback had no stop rule and could alternate refused stores without bound.

**Why not the reviewer's fixes.** "Always present week(now) − 1" and per-store random weeks near boundaries still select by the device clock, so the switch minute still leaks the offset, only diluted. The week in a card-derived secret protects nothing: every card holder derives every week's secret, the namespace is static (C-4), and relay binding (point 2) covers replay. The week is therefore removed from credentials and kept only in registrations, which lapse.

**Rules.**
1. **Secrets** (no week): `D_{i,s} = HKDF(card_secret_i, "ghost/v1/card/deposit" || u8(s))`, `RP_{i,s} = HKDF(card_secret_i, "ghost/v1/card/bundle-read" || u8(s))`, `RX_{i,s} = HKDF(m, "ghost/v1/dm/inbox-read" || u16_be(i) || u8(s))`, where s is the ES slot of the relay (the slot number Phase 8 already binds access tokens to, F8 D2). New vectors replace the weekly ones.
2. **Credential v3 (67 bytes)** = `version(1) = 3 || kind(1) ∈ {0x01 read, 0x03 deposit} || slot(1) || namespace(32) || secret(32)`; **verifier** = `SHA-256(label(kind) || u8(slot) || namespace(32) || secret(32))`. Every presenter at one relay sends identical bytes, so `capability_scope` is one value per (relay, namespace, kind) for all depositors or fetchers, in every week.
3. **Relay.** A relay without a configured slot (the Phase 8 `--slot` flag) refuses every v3 credential; a header whose slot is not the relay's own is `rejected_capability` before any verifier is computed (constant error, no oracle). `client-core` (T21) parses the 67-byte layout, checks the kind/operation matrix and the namespace, and refuses lengths 66 and 68; the slot check is the relay's.
4. **`RegisterKey` keeps its shape and the period rule** (`period_id ∈ {cap_week, cap_week + 1}`, judged by the capability, G-3; accumulation, SC-1). Records stay keyed `namespace || period || kind || verifier` with `valid_until = start(period + 2) + 1 h`; the owner registers the same verifier for each period.
5. **Authorization.** `store`/`check` with a deposit credential, and `list`/`get`/`check` with a read credential, need a record `(namespace, p, kind, verifier)` with `now < valid_until` for some p; the relay looks at `p ∈ [week(now) − 3, week(now) + 1]` by its own clock (internal to the relay, not observable).
6. **`DepositLedger`** entries are per record; a deposit is charged to the live matching record with the earliest `valid_until` that has allowance left; none → `RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED`. Restart semantics unchanged (§4.5).
7. **Senders.** One deposit credential per (contact, relay slot); no week choice and no fallback. `KEEPER` installs it with `expires_hour = ceil_hour(now) + 8 d` and refreshes it when fewer than 7 days remain; it never re-puts a credential on a listener hint. A credential refused by a relay (Phase 7 `unauthorized` → `wait_capability`) is re-put as a new generation **at most once per relay session start per (relay, namespace)**, so refused store attempts are bounded by one retry per session (CR-16).
8. **Owner.** The READ credential of `(r, X_0)` is installed once per relay and refreshed like point 7. Healing rule (§4.8) restated: when it is refused, every `done` row of `(r, X_0)` with `period ≥ week(now)` returns to pending, and reads use the write capability until a row is done again.
9. **Offline tolerance, restated.** Deposits are accepted while any registration lives: an owner whose newest write capability is of week c receives deposits until `start(c + 3) + 1 h`. An owner last online at its end-of-week-w renewal (capability w + 1) receives until `start(w + 4) + 1 h`, about three weeks; one last online earlier in week w until `start(w + 3) + 1 h`. §4.8's "about two weeks" and the fallback bullets are replaced.
10. **P-2 restated.** Every store into one inbox namespace at one relay with a deposit credential presents the same credential scope, in every week, whatever the sender's clock, session timing or earlier refusals. JD3: per (relay, inbox) at most one deposit scope over the whole world. The T32 world gains the F8 T2 clock model (10 % of devices offset U[−3 d, +3 d], narrowed to `U[−σ/2, +σ/2]` beside a non-zero relay skew, §15.6, L7-D27) and the hostile mode **REFUSE_ONE_SENDER**; twin NI-DM6 (§21.9).
11. **Declared.** A relay sees one scope per inbox for as long as the card lives, which the static namespace already implies (L7-D13). F8 §10.7's "shared read keys per (namespace, epoch)" are realized per relay slot, with the epoch carried by the registration period (X-5, ADR-30, Q38). **Added by the review of S1 (2026-09-19):** point 2's replay bound is per slot, not per relay. When the operator behind a slot changes (§21.1 point 1), the namespace keeps the slot number and therefore the same secrets, so the outgoing operator can present the deposit, read and fetch secrets it already saw at the slot's successor relay, for as long as the owner registers them there — until the card is rotated (Phase 13). Declared as L7-D26; no code refuses it, and the T32 worlds do not claim otherwise.
12. **Tests and mutants.** `register.txt`: a credential replayed at another slot refused; a relay without a slot refuses v3; lengths 66 and 68; records of periods p and p + 1 authorize the same credential; the ledger charges the earliest record. T21 matrix rows. **PM-27** (weekly credential selected by the device clock) is detected by JD3 in the clock-offset world; **PM-28** (credential without slot) by the RK cross-slot replay case; **MC-15** is redefined as "a refused LOCAL credential re-put on every `CAPABILITIES` hint", detected by the SCN-E bound on refused stores per session.

### 21.3 Pair roots travel only in acknowledged sessions; renewal reset; P-7 restated (CR-1, CR-2 = CR-04, CR-13, PRIV-12)

**Defects.** (1) The first root of every conversation rode inside PreKey messages, which the recipient's seed-derived identity plus its retained prekey records (kept until `superseded + 91 d`) decrypt later: a seizure at day 70 recovered the root and opened week-0…2 pair envelopes, contradicting P-7 and T34. (2) `root_gen` restarted at 0 after a restore (or delete and re-add), the peer ignored a generation "not greater" than its stored one, and nothing cleared the peer's out keys on a session renewal: the peer kept sealing under a root the restored device no longer had. (3) A recipient that did not run for about 52 days erased its newest in-key in the first sweep before ratcheting forward. (4) Unanswered conversations stay intro-mode for good, which L7-D3 did not declare.

**Rules.**
1. **Roots only in normal Olm messages** (revised §22). A `seal_root` or `seal_root_pq` TLV is attached only when Olm's output for that send is a normal message (the session has received a message, `vz:src/olm/session/mod.rs:377-401`). A root TLV carried in a PreKey message is ignored on receipt (counter `root_in_prekey`). The one-scan flow (§12.2) becomes: A's first message is intro without a root; B's reply (a normal message from the responder session) is provisional-sealed (§6.7) and carries B's root; A's second message is pair-sealed under B's root and carries A's root; B's second message is pair-sealed. (Round 9 sealed B's reply in intro mode, one more intro envelope per new conversation; Q40.)
2. **Session renewal** (revised §22). A `RECV` or `HELD` decryption of a PreKey message from contact c that creates a new session under R-SS1 (§8.2) is a *renewal from c*; GHOST decides it explicitly (round 9 inferred it from libsignal's `markKyberPreKeyUsed` call). Once the decryption returned and the blob passed R9 and R10, the same transaction (a) deletes every out key for c, so the next root TLV from c is accepted whatever its generation, and (b) inserts a new offer for c unless an unacknowledged one exists. Causality makes this safe: c's roots travel only in normal messages, which exist only after c received my reply to the renewal.
3. **Generations never repeat on a device that kept the contact.** `dm_pair_offer` rows are kept while the contact exists; KEYDEC, acknowledgement and the periodic replacement (`ROOT_REPLACE_WEEKS`, §6.3) set `root_env` to NULL; SWEEP may delete a row with `root_env = NULL` only if a row of a higher generation exists. The next generation is `1 + max(root_gen)` over c's rows. A restore, delete and re-add, or unblock and re-add start again at 0, and the peer's floor is cleared by point 2 (my first message in the new state is a PreKey). §8.3's "my offered roots are erased" means their secrets.
4. **Stale roots from a demoted session** (revised §22; a delayed normal message of c's `previous` session arriving after the renewal) follow R-SS2: it decrypts on `previous`, and the slots swap only if `current` is unacknowledged; a conversation that still diverges needs a reset from either side, exactly as without pair mode (declared with L7-D12).
5. **Ratchet before erasure (CR-13).** SWEEP first derives and stores, for every live in-generation, `K_w` for every week up to `week(now)` from the newest stored key, then erases keys past `start(w + 1) + FRESH`, in one transaction. RS2 asserts that pair envelopes open after a 60-day absence; mutant **MC-21** (erase before ratcheting) is detected by RS2.
6. **P-7 and T34 restated** (revised §22). For an ISO week w with `now ≥ start(w + 1) + 45 d`, no key on either device and nothing derivable from either seed opens a pair-mode envelope of week w, **except** through a root-carrying message whose Olm message key is still stored, as a skipped key, in a **live** session of that root's recipient (a copy lost or reordered in transit). Olm keeps up to 40 skipped keys per receiver chain and 5 chains per session; an active conversation evicts such a chain after five ratchet turns, a `previous` session keeps it until its `retire_day` (≤ 46 d), and Q67's age bound removes the exception if S0′ adopts it. Declared in L7-D11. **Corrected by S0′ (V-15, 2026-09-19): the age bound is adopted**, so SWEEP's `sessionPrune` removes every skipped key older than `FRESH + 2σ` = 51 d from live sessions as well; the exception is withdrawn and L7-D11 narrows to the ≤ 51-day window before the next prune sweep.
7. **T34 seizure attacker extended** (revised §22). It also runs Olm decryption on every archived blob it can open, with the seed-derived identity, throwaway accounts over every retained generation, X-Wing decapsulation, every retained session and every retained `prov_env`. Expectation: pair-mode envelopes of old weeks opened = the ground-truth set of generations whose root carrier has a stored skipped key in a live session, and 0 in a lossless variant. Mutant **PM-29** (root in a PreKey message) opens week-0…2 envelopes at day 70.
8. **Tests.** SCN-I asserts that pair envelopes open in both directions after the reconnect; KC8 asserts the same after a same-key renewal; mutant **MC-18** (out-key floor kept across a renewal) is detected by SCN-I.
9. **Unanswered conversations (PRIV-12), declared.** When the recipient never sends a user message, every envelope of that conversation is intro-mode (recognisable by a holder of the recipient's card colluding with relays; attributable with the recipient's retained generation secrets until their erasure, no longer with the seed, revised §22), and the sender re-fetches a bundle after 28 days unacknowledged (`UNACK_MAX`, §8.7; revised §22), repeating the L7-D6 fetch→deposit link monthly. L7-D3 and L7-D6 are extended; P-7 covers answered conversations only; Phase 13 copy states it.

### 21.4 Intro bindings are bound to their envelope and fresh (CR-3, CR-4, CR-05)

**Defects.** (1) The binding signature named no recipient, time or message, so anyone who ever received an intro envelope from C could replay C's card and binding: to move C to `changed` with a historical key, to create "C tried to reach you" requests or `reset_needed` with a bogus `signed_pre_key_id` (the round-9 session library refused an unknown prekey id before any key agreement; revised §22: an unknown generation is `drop(unknown_generation)` at R4, before identification), or to make a new contact pin a historical key. (2) Intro envelopes stay openable forever and freshness ran after decryption, so a relay re-serving old intro PreKeys after prekey erasure or a restore brought back deleted, rejected or pre-restore-blocked identities as requests and filled the request cap; §7.6's B2 claim that such replays "cannot even be opened" was false. (3) A requester sending a second key drove `request → changed`, which no trigger or CHECK allows: an untyped abort crashed the process three times per blob.

**Rules.**
1. **Layout** (binding v2 of round 9, replaced by **binding v3**, §3.4 and §6.4; revised §22). `Q = kind(1) || sender_card(131) || binding_minute(4) || binding_sig(64) || reply_seed(32) || l1_len(4, BE) || L1 || zeros`, with `binding_minute` = `floor_minute(now)` at sealing (u32 minutes since the epoch) and `binding_sig = Ed25519(identity_sk_A, "ghost/v1/dm-binding/v3" || OI_A(32) || u16_be(card_index_A) || recipient_namespace(32) || u32_be(binding_minute) || u32_be(gen_id_B) || SHA-256(xwing_ct)(32) || base_key_A(32) || OI_B(32))`. The intro fixed part of §6.6 is 1 376 B inside the outer layer; `O_MAX` is 1 664 B. The minute is sealing time, not `author_minute`, because a pending send may be sealed days after compose. Binding v2's `SHA-256(L1)` is not signed any more (content deniability, L7-D20); the base key and the ciphertext bind the binding to its envelope and its session instead.
2. **R5, before any state change.** Card valid; binding v3 valid over the blob's own namespace, `binding_minute`, the envelope's X-Wing ciphertext and the PreKey message's base key and identities (revised §22; round 9's binding v2 covered the L1 bytes); `now − FRESH ≤ binding_minute ≤ now + 2σ` (FRESH = 45 d, the timer being unknown before decryption). Otherwise `drop(stale)` or `drop(bad_binding)` with no state change.
3. **State before decryption.** Only two changes may precede R7, both under a binding that passed point 2: pinning a NULL `pinned_identity`, and a hold (§8.3). A request row inserted at R5 is kept only if the message is applied (R7–R10 pass), and every drop deletes it in the same transaction (revised §22: the `NoSession`/`InvalidKeyId` exception is withdrawn with the seed-openable restore path, §8.6). A genuine envelope that decrypts again after its requester was rejected or deleted (a new `contact_id`, so D3 does not match) is refused at R9 once older than the freshness of its bucket (§21.10), which an honest relay's D1 tombstone outlives; a relay that reports a short expiry and re-serves the envelope after the tombstone expired can bring back that requester's genuine message as a request within the window (declared, L7-D8). `reset_needed` on an existing contact needs the same fresh binding. (Revised §22: intro envelopes carry only PreKey messages, so round 9's rule for intro `SignalMessage`s from unknown identities is withdrawn.)
4. **Replays** (revised §22). Within FRESH, a genuine intro PreKey message hits a matching session (`DUPLICATE_OR_LOST`), the reuse set D2b (`drop(replay)`) or, after a restore, an erased generation (`drop(unknown_generation)` at R4). Beyond FRESH it stops at R5. Bundle generations are erased at `superseded + 91 d`, later than `binding_minute + FRESH` for every bundle they served, so no replay after erasure passes R5. §7.6 B2 now reads: "a pair-mode replay beyond its key window cannot be opened; an intro replay of a live generation opens but is refused at R5, before any state change, once older than FRESH; an intro replay of an erased generation is `unknown_generation`".
5. **Requests (CR-05).** For `trust_state = request`, a PreKey L1 with `OI′ ≠ pinned` under a valid binding is `drop(request_key_mismatch)` with no state change; a `request → changed` transition does not exist. After the request is rejected or expires, a later intro creates a fresh request. Every trust-state precondition of RECV, HELD, KEYDEC, `EST-*` and SEND is a Kotlin pre-check; a trigger or CHECK abort inside a DM transaction is a bug, never an expected path.
6. **Reasoning corrected (§3.4, §8.3).** A valid binding for `OI′ ≠ OI` (round 9: `K′ ≠ K`) exists whenever the holder of c's Ed25519 secret signs one: c itself (including a modified client), a derivation-version change, or a seed thief. A key change therefore means "c's identity asserts a new Olm identity" (revised §22).
7. **Tests.** KC5b: a genuine binding replayed with another recipient namespace, another ciphertext or base key, or a stale minute → no state change. KC12: a requester sending two identities → no crash, no state change. CB: a request-cap flood by RESURRECT after generation erasure and after a restore → no request. Mutants **MC-20** (binding without namespace, minute, ciphertext or base key) and **MC-24** (`request → changed` attempted) (revised §22).

### 21.5 Session state bound (CR-6 = CR-06; withdrawn and replaced by §22)

**Round 9's defect.** A libsignal session record could grow to about 16 MB (skipped keys capped per receiver chain, 5 chains per state, 40 archived states), so any peer with a session could push a victim's record past the 1 MiB CHECK and cause an untyped constraint error inside RECV. Round 9 bounded it with `SESSION_RECORD_MAX = 1 MiB`, a typed `SessionRecordOversizeException` and an `OVERSIZE` transaction.

**Now (§7.9 point 4).** Olm session state is bounded by construction (5 receiver chains × 40 skipped keys, ≤ 2 sessions per contact); `SESSION_STATE_MAX = 65 536` bytes of pickle is frozen from the S0′ measurement probe (V-4). **Measured by S0′ (V-4, 2026-09-19):** the largest JSON pickle of either pin is **36 685 B** — the analytic bound to the byte — leaving 28 851 B (44.0 %) of margin, 26 285 B once the ≤ 2 566 B first-seen sidecar of §7.7 point 4 is added. A peer cannot exceed it; `OVERSIZE` signals a bug or a format change at a bump. The round-9 handling is kept as defence: **RECV and HELD** roll back, then an `OVERSIZE` transaction runs `markConsumed` (RECV), deletes the contact's `olm_session` rows, sets `reset_needed = 1` and counts `oversize`; **SEND, RESEND, `EST-READY`** roll back, then a separate transaction deletes the sessions and keeps the item pending (result `QUEUED`, the §5.5 re-establishment path). **Tests:** RO6 asserts that the measured worst case fits with margin and that a fault-injected oversize is a typed outcome with a later working reset. Mutant **MC-25** (oversize as an untyped exception) is detected by RO6.

### 21.6 Session API, addresses, store exceptions, module boundary (CR-7, CR-19, BUILD-4; points 1–5 withdrawn by §22)

1. **API list, addresses, catch order, logging citations, `markKyberPreKeyUsed` (round-9 points 1–5): withdrawn.** They described libsignal's `SessionCipher` constructors and `decrypt` signatures, `SignalProtocolAddress` names that never parse as a ServiceId, the catch order of the Kyber reuse refusal, the logging lines of `session_management.rs` and the store exceptions. Their replacements: the closed JNI surface and result codes (§2.6); no address, contact identifier or device id reaches Olm (§8.1); typed result codes instead of library exceptions (§2.6, `DmResultCodesTest`); no logging in the DM graph (G-R4, G-R8); the first-flight reuse set D2b and the explicit renewal decision R-SS1 (§7.6, §8.2).
2. **Module boundary (BUILD-4; point 6, kept and extended).** `:messaging` references no native type. `:crypto-bridge` exposes GHOST types only: `L1.parse(bytes) → PreKey(OI, baseKey, fallbackKey, sessionId) | Normal(ratchetKey, chainIndex) | Invalid`; `DmSessions.decrypt(tx, contact, l1) → Ok(plaintext, slot, created, renewed) | Duplicate | UnknownGeneration | Replay | Undecryptable | Untrusted | Oversize`; `DmSessions.encrypt(tx, contact, l0) → Ok(l1, isPreKey, acknowledged) | NoSession | Untrusted | Oversize`; `DmSessions.outbound(tx, contact, bundle) → Ok | Untrusted | InvalidKey | Oversize`. G-R6 allows `DmNative` only in `crypto-bridge/src/main` (and `crypto-bridge/src/gate`, §21.13 point 7). The golden pickles and `UpgradeTest` live in `client-core/dm/tests/golden/vodozemac-<version>/`.

### 21.7 Establishment: forgotten tombstones, candidates, `EST-READY`, bounded attempts, restore without tokens (CR-02, CR-03, CR-09, PRIV-4, PRIV-11 = CR-10)

**Defects.** (1) Phase 7 keeps consumed-blob tombstones across `remove` and re-register (`Namespaces.kt:5`, `InboxStore.kt:118-131`), so a reset, a key-change re-establishment or a re-added contact could not see bundles consumed earlier and waited up to a week. (2) After a restore, contacts fetched the lost device's bundles (the restored device has no tokens to publish, and equal-week ties were a coin flip), so contact-initiated resets failed silently. (3) BUNDLE enqueued without the exact deadline (`OutboxStore.enqueue` requires `deadline > now`) or a trust check (`UntrustedIdentityException` in `process` for a contact that became `changed`). (4) A missing bundle kept `P_c` listening for 7 days, co-occurring with the fetcher's session starts and linkable by AD-1 withholding bundles. (5) T28's static rule forbade the enqueue that §5.5 and G-DM2 required, and nothing flushed pending sends when an inbound PreKey built the session first.

**Why not the reviewers' fixes in full.** A per-contact long-lived bundle cache was not adopted: forgetting tombstones gives the same availability without keeping a deleted contact's keys. A separate short transport session per fetch does not exist in Phase 7; attempts are bounded instead.

**Rules.**
1. **Issuance marker** (revised §22). Round 9 added `issued_hour(4)` to `bundle_plain`, which gave every card holder the hour of the owner's first wake after `t_pub` and the restore hour (contradicting point 4 of §21.9, Q43 and L7-D10). It is replaced by `bundle_rank(2)` (§5.3): rank 0 for OWNER-WEEK bundles, `1 + max(rank)` over the own valid bundles for an activation or restore publication. Bundles are ranked by `(bundle_week, bundle_rank)` descending, then the smaller blob hash; bundle v2 is 1 393 bytes.
2. **`EST-START`** calls the new `:sync` operation `Inbox.forgetConsumed(tx, namespace)`, which deletes the consumed tombstones of a namespace whose consumer is `PREKEYS` (refused for any other consumer; re-consuming a bundle is idempotent), then registers `P_c` listening, installs the `RP_{c,s}` credentials (§21.2), and sets `fetch_attempts = 1`, `fetch_listen_until_minute = ceil_minute(now + 15 min)`.
3. **BUNDLE** (one consumed blob): open and verify as in §5.5, then keep the better candidate in `dm_bundle_candidate(contact_id, bundle_env, bundle_week, bundle_rank)`; nothing else. A key change found in a bundle (`OI′ ≠ pinned` under a valid bundle signature; revised §22) still moves the contact to `changed` and keeps the candidate; after the user's decision `EST-READY` builds from that candidate while it is valid, otherwise a new attempt starts (with `forgetConsumed`).
4. **`EST-READY`** (time-triggered, §21.9: at the first candidate's insert + 2 min, or at `fetch_listen_until_minute` if a usable session exists without a candidate; the 2-minute delay lives in memory, so after a restart an existing candidate is processed at the next time-triggered pass): require `trust_state ∈ {unverified, verified}`, else keep the pending items and the candidate and stop; fail every pending item whose exact deadline is ≤ now + 1 min; pin a NULL identity; store the candidate's X-Wing key in `dm_peer_pq`; `DmSessions.outbound(best candidate)` (`sessionOutbound`, a new `current` under R-SS0) unless a usable session already exists (an inbound PreKey built it, R-SS1; revised §22); encrypt, intro-seal and enqueue every remaining pending item; `establishment = ready`; `setListening(P_c, false)`; delete the candidate. Typed results: `Untrusted` → keep pending; `InvalidKey` → drop the candidate, count, and let the attempt continue; `Oversize` → §21.5 (as revised); `StorageFullException` → retried at the next time-triggered pass.
5. **`EST-END`** (time-triggered at `fetch_listen_until_minute` with no candidate and no usable session): `setListening(P_c, false)`; if `fetch_attempts < 3`, the next attempt starts at the first relay session start at least 6 h later with a new 15-minute window; after the third, `establishment = unreachable` and the pending items fail (the user may retry). `Namespaces.remove(P_c)` runs once no fetched row remains (SWEEP retries).
6. **Declared (L7-D6 extended).** A listening `P_c` co-occurs with at most three session starts per establishment; a relay set that withholds bundles gains those samples; a relay that serves a bundle chooses when `EST-READY` runs (≥ 2 min later). Hostile mode **WITHHOLD_ALL_BUNDLES**; a JD7 variant requires ≤ 3 attempts and `P_c` list events only inside attempt windows; mutant **PM-31** (unbounded attempts).
7. **T28 static half (PRIV-11 = CR-10).** RECV, BUNDLE and HELD never call `Outbox.enqueue`, `Namespaces.register`/`setListening`/`remove`, `Registrations` or `Capabilities.put`. G-DM2's allowed callers: SEND, RESEND, `EST-START`, `EST-READY`, `EST-END`, OWNER-WEEK, ACTIVATE, KEEPER, REGROWS, and SWEEP (`Namespaces.remove` only). The recording Outbox wrapper of §15.1 stands; JD6 maps each `EST-READY` store to the user action of its item. §13.5's establishment row: `fetching → ready` only through `EST-READY`.
8. **Restore (CR-03).** A restored device has no tokens (Phase 8 E11), so it can neither register nor publish until it gets tokens (a trial from a new invite, or a pack). Meanwhile contacts' fetches return the lost device's bundles (valid up to two weeks, stored up to 30 days), whose PreKey messages name generations the restored device does not hold (`unknown_generation`, counted, no request; revised §22, §8.6). Healing therefore starts from the restored side: `reconnect` needs no token (it fetches the contact's bundle and deposits with the contact's credential). §8.6's "their `resetSession` fetches my new bundle" holds only after the restored device has published; with tokens, its week-w bundle outranks the old device's by `bundle_rank` (revised §22). L7-D12 extended. RE cases: the old device's bundle in the same and the previous week, a restored device without tokens, and a contact-initiated reset in each.
9. **Schema.** `dm_contact.fetch_until_day` is replaced by `fetch_attempts INTEGER NOT NULL DEFAULT 0 CHECK (fetch_attempts BETWEEN 0 AND 3)` and `fetch_listen_until_minute` (minute column, `CHECK ((establishment = 'fetching') = (fetch_listen_until_minute IS NOT NULL))`); new table `dm_bundle_candidate` (`contact_id` PK, cascade; `bundle_env` 1 421 B (revised §22; round 9: 1 918 B); `bundle_week`; `bundle_rank`) with a REPLACE guard. The table lands in migration 5 (§13.6).

### 21.8 Durable outbound items; FAILED controls and bundles; quote reference inside the envelope (CR-07, CR-17, BUILD-5)

**Defects.** CONTROL messages and reactions had no row a pending send, an outcome or a retry could use (`dm_message.kind IN (1, 2)`, `dm_pending_send` and `dm_outbound_op` referenced `dm_message`), so a reconnect could not wait for a bundle and a FAILED control vanished; a user retry could not rebuild the L0 (timer version and TLVs were not stored). A FAILED bundle op could not be re-sealed because OWNER-WEEK's week guard had already advanced. `dm_message.quote_ref` was a plaintext column equal to a quoted `message_id`, which fails the T25 physical scan legitimately, while content copied into `body_env` is invisible to a byte scan.

**Rules.**
1. **`dm_outbound_item`** holds every outgoing L0:
   ```sql
   CREATE TABLE dm_outbound_item (
     item_id       BLOB PRIMARY KEY NOT NULL CHECK (length(item_id) = 16),            -- the L0 message_id
     contact_id    BLOB NOT NULL REFERENCES dm_contact(contact_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,
     message_ref   BLOB REFERENCES dm_message(message_id) ON DELETE CASCADE,         -- TEXT, TIMER_SET: itself; REACTION: target; CONTROL: NULL
     kind          INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (kind BETWEEN 1 AND 4),
     control       TEXT CHECK (control IS NULL OR control IN ('reconnect', 'revocation', 'reset')),
     l0_env        BLOB CHECK (l0_env IS NULL OR length(l0_env) BETWEEN 29 AND 65564),
     deadline_hour INTEGER NOT NULL CHECK (typeof(deadline_hour) = 'integer' AND deadline_hour % 3600 = 0),
     state         TEXT NOT NULL CHECK (state IN ('pending', 'enqueued', 'sent', 'degraded', 'failed', 'indeterminate')),
     CHECK ((kind = 4) = (control IS NOT NULL)),
     CHECK (kind = 4 OR message_ref IS NOT NULL)
   ) WITHOUT ROWID;
   ```
   `l0_env` is the exact L0 under the content key. It becomes NULL at SENT, DEGRADED or INDETERMINATE and at the deadline; a FAILED item keeps it until the deadline. `dm_pending_send` is keyed by `item_id`; `dm_outbound_op.purpose ∈ {'item', 'bundle'}` with `item_id` for items and `bundle_week` for bundles. REPLACE guard and state trigger added.
2. **SEND** writes an item for every kind; `dm_message` rows exist only for TEXT and TIMER_SET, own reactions in `dm_reaction(from_self = 1)`.
3. **Retry** of a FAILED TEXT, TIMER_SET or REACTION item re-encodes the stored L0 with identical `message_id`, author minute, minute index, timer code, timer version and body, and the TLVs pending now (§21.3 point 1 applies to the new output); D3 dedups by `message_id`.
4. **FAILED controls.** A reconnect or revocation item that ends FAILED is rescheduled as a new item (new `message_id` and author minute) at a fresh `U[0, 24 h]` offset, at most 3 times, then counted `control_failed` in `status()`. A `reset` control (from `resetSession`) and a REACTION end `failed` and may be retried by the user. INDETERMINATE items behave as messages (§7.5).
5. **Bundles (CR-17).** `dm_self` gains `bundle_published_week`. OUTCOME sets it to the op's `bundle_week` at SENT or DEGRADED. OWNER-WEEK rotates when `bundle_week < w`, and re-seals the current bundle (new salt, same generation and `bundle_rank`; revised §22) when `bundle_published_week < bundle_week` and `now < start(bundle_week + 2) − 1 h`. An OS case covers a FAILED and an INDETERMINATE publication.
6. **Quote reference (BUILD-5).** `dm_message.quote_ref` is removed; `body_env = AEAD(content_key, flags(1) || quote_ref(0 | 16) || body, AD = message_id)`. The T25 physical scan keeps looking for expired `message_id`s. ME-12 is detected by a logical scan that decrypts every `*_env` column with the harness content key and searches for the canary, in addition to the byte scan.
7. **Structural invariants and scenarios.** Every `pending` or `enqueued` item has a pending send or an unreleased op; SCN-I gains a variant in which the reconnect CONTROL waits for the contact's bundle; SCN-K gains a FAILED revocation that is rescheduled.

### 21.9 Activity-independent triggers; coarse upload times; consumer cost model (PRIV-3, PRIV-9, PRIV-7)

**Defects.** Bundle publications, scheduled reconnect and revocation controls and re-seals ran at the first actor wake after their time, and wakes include `INBOX` hints (fetched inbound blobs) and the user's own sends: a card holder with AD-1 could deposit a probe during the target's session and watch its due controls go out a minute later into its contacts' inboxes. Relays return `uploaded_at` to the minute, so any card holder saw the owner's first session after `t_pub` each week. PM-25 could not be detected, because the harness charges virtual time only for relay calls (`EngineTestKit.kt:241-242`).

**Rules.**
1. **Time-triggered steps** (OWNER-WEEK, re-seals, the week-change parts of KEEPER and REGROWS, due scheduled controls, `EST-READY`, `EST-END`) run only from: the actor timer set to `min(next delete_at_minute, t_pub, next due_minute, next week boundary, next EST timer)`; database open; and a relay session start (foreground or background, a Phase 7 event).
2. **Hint- and facade-triggered passes** run only SWEEP, RECV, BUNDLE, HELD, OUTCOME/RESEND, DISPLAY, KEYDEC, user SEND, and KEEPER/REGROWS maintenance for a capability just installed (`CAPABILITIES`). §13.7's pass order is split accordingly.
3. **Twin NI-DM6.** Worlds that differ only in inbound arrival times, in the send times of unrelated contacts, in sender clock offsets and in REFUSE_ONE_SENDER give identical minutes of bundle stores, scheduled-control stores and registrations, and identical deposit-scope multisets per (relay, inbox). Mutant **PM-30** (time-triggered steps on any wake) is detected by NI-DM6.
4. **Coarse upload time (PRIV-9).** For `get` authorized by a v3 read credential, a relay returns `uploaded_at_unix_seconds` floored to the start of its ISO week (the proto already allows relay-policy granularity; a `relay_semantics.txt` case and a capture-independent node test). §11.2's AD-7 cell for bundle publication reads: "sees rotations and the publication week; with a relay, the owner's first session after `t_pub` each week, absences and the restore or activation minute (L7-D10)", extended by §22 with the `bundle_rank` signal (an off-schedule publication in a week, with no hour in the bundle). **Corrected by §22.8.7:** the relay's `expiry_unix_seconds` still gave card holders the upload hour and polling gives the minute; fetch reads now also return a week-coarse expiry, and polling is declared (Q69). Q43. **Corrected by the review of S1 (2026-09-19):** `ListNamespace`'s `next_cursor` was the blob store's per-namespace sequence, whose high 32 bits are the UTC hour of the namespace's first store and whose low 32 bits count the stores since, so one `list` with the free shared fetch secret gave a card holder the bundle namespace's activation or restore hour and its publication count, and a chosen cursor was an oracle for the same hour. **Rule:** a relay answers with, and accepts, the sequence sealed under its own key — `HMAC-SHA256(relay key, "ghost/v1/list-cursor" || round(1) || namespace(32) || u32_be(half))` as the round function of a six-round Feistel permutation of the 64-bit sequence, so the cursor keeps its eight opaque bytes (`relay.proto` already calls it opaque), paging is unchanged (the map is a bijection), a cursor of one namespace names an unrelated sequence at another, and no sequence reaches any caller, credential or capability. The relay is the only party that has to read a cursor. Pinned by `relay/crates/node/tests/list_cursor.rs` and the `ghost-relay-capability` unit test; the **NI-DM6 AD-7 variant compares every field of every `get` **and** `list` response**, so PM-38's family covers a relay that answers with the raw sequence as well as one that answers with an exact expiry. The Kotlin `ModelRelay` still answers with its raw model sequence (its cursor is read by the Phase 7 harness invariants and by `ListStepTest`); the slice that builds `DmWorld` seals the model's cursor the same way, or excludes the model's cursor from the effective view and states why — otherwise the AD-7 list comparison measures the model, not the relay (§15.1).
5. **PM-25 (PRIV-7).** Detected by a direct ordering assertion in `SessionParticipantTest` (the tail starts only after the issuer call returned and the transport closed) and by NI-DM1 with a `DmWorld` consumer cost model: virtual time per claimed blob (1 ms per 1 000 HMACs, 1 ms per AEAD open, 5 ms per Olm session call (re-measured on the host build, revised §22), 1 ms per row write; a forged blob costs its full hint set), plus an NI-DM1 variant with equal blob counts and a different valid/forged mix, comparing session close minutes.

### 21.10 Ephemeral: bucket deadlines without cancel, freshness per bucket, held expiry, display guard, alarm, reboot, notifications (PRIV-2, PRIV-8, CR-11, CR-14, BUILD-13, BUILD-9, BUILD-6)

1. **Deadline (PRIV-2).** Every DM item carries `deadline = author + min(bucket(timer), 7 d)` (off → 7 d). Only 5-minute messages change: their store deadline is `author + 1 h`, like 1-hour messages, so NI-DM4 compares equal inputs and a HIGH-mode send delay of up to 10 minutes can no longer outlive the deadline. §10.1's deadline row and R-T4, D15 and T30 read accordingly; the honest-relay bound for 5 minutes becomes that of 1 hour. Q41.
2. **No cancel at expiry.** SWEEP never calls `Outbox.cancel` for a DM op: ops run to their outcome or deadline. The message row, its item and the retained blob cascade at expiry (T25 logical); the Phase 7 op payload (the same E2E bytes, which no key on the device opens) is bounded by the op's outcome or `deadline_hour`, and the T25 physical scan for blob bytes uses that bound. EP case: HIGH mode, a 5-minute message whose `not_before` falls after its expiry is stored. Mutant **ME-21** (`Outbox.cancel` at expiry) is detected by that case and NI-DM4.
3. **Freshness per bucket (PRIV-8).** `FRESH(b) = min(b, 7 d) + b + 2 h + 2σ`: 1 h → 6 d 4 h; 1 d → 8 d 2 h; 7 d → 20 d 2 h; 30 d and off → 43 d 2 h, kept at 45 d. R9 uses `FRESH(bucket(timer_code))` of the decrypted L0; D3's `until_day = ceil7(day(author_minute) + ceil_days(FRESH(b)) + 1)`. The pair-key window, the bundle-generation retention (§5.4, revised §22) and R5's binding freshness keep FRESH = 45 d. HELD evaluates R9 at the held row's consume minute.
4. **Declared, corrected.** A seizer who knows candidate `message_id`s (their sender, a colluding contact) can confirm from `dm_seen` that the device received them and in which week, until `until_day` (§10.8, R-M1 and L7-D7 no longer say "nothing about content or sender"). ME-6 is detected by a dictionary test that recomputes keyed and unkeyed tags of the world's `message_id`s over the dump after expiry.
5. **Held messages (CR-11).** `dm_held` gains `relay_expiry_hour` (hour) and `consume_minute` (minute), both in R-M3. HELD's R9 and R11 use them; a replayed message whose computed unread deadline is ≤ `now_e` is not inserted (counted `expired_while_held`). EP cases: hold, acknowledge and replay with 1 h and 30 d timers, deletion by the original `min(E, consume + bucket + 1 h)`.
6. **DISPLAY (CR-14)** ignores ids whose `delete_at_minute ≤ now_e`. Trigger `dm_message_expiry` names `expire_at_minute` and `unread_deadline_minute` only; `delete_at_minute` follows its CHECK and may move later at display (ADR-27 point 9). EP case: a stale `markDisplayed` after the unread cap.
7. **Alarm call (BUILD-13).** `setAndAllowWhileIdle(ELAPSED_REALTIME_WAKEUP, SystemClock.elapsedRealtime() + (next − now), pendingIntent)`; a unit test checks the computed trigger time.
8. **Reboot (BUILD-9).** No boot receiver exists or is added (G-DM7). After a reboot the next sweep waits for the persisted periodic job, which has a network constraint, or the next app open; an offline rebooted device keeps expired rows (encrypted, never shown) until then. The §10.7 sentence about `RECEIVE_BOOT_COMPLETED` is withdrawn; L7-D14 is extended.
9. **Notifications (BUILD-6).** Phase 9 posts no notification (a no-op port; no `POST_NOTIFICATIONS`). ADR-27's notification rules are proven in Phase 9 only by the `NotificationPort` contract test; the `getActiveNotifications()` check (§15.9 item 5), the notification-history check (§15.10 step 6) and the corresponding §15.8 evidence are removed and move to Phase 13 with its permission ADR and device check.

### 21.11 Declared leakage, TTL-class pairing, size classes, PQ wording (PRIV-6, PRIV-5, PRIV-10, CR-8)

1. **LD (PRIV-6)** gains everything the honest protocol shows relays and the design already declares: the grouping of one client's requests per circuit within a session (L7-D5); exact arrival times; the identical hash of an identical-bytes resend (L7-D9); the `result` of every request, refusals included; the one static credential scope per (relay, namespace, kind) (§21.2); the bounded bundle fetch attempts (§21.7). S-DM1's LD attacker receives the same features; the baselines are recomputed.
2. **TTL-class pairing (PRIV-5).** Both directions of a conversation use its bucket, so a non-default timer pairs the conversation's two inboxes: with the T32 mix (1-hour bucket 15 %), reply-timing SDA isolates such a pair in about 2–3 exchanges instead of ~6–8. L7-D1 and L7-D4 say so; S-DM2 and rounds-to-single-out are reported per TTL class and per mode; S-DM4 adds the cross-inbox pairing measure; ADR-32's consequences and the Phase 13 copy (CP-10) state that non-default timers make a conversation easier to link at relays. Q39 offers fewer DM TTL classes as an ADR-27 deviation (default: keep ADR-27).
3. **Size classes (PRIV-10).** `B = min{ b : L_class ≤ b − O_MAX }` with `L_class = 52 + |body| + EXT_RESERVE`, where 52 B are the fixed L0 fields with the quote reference always counted and `EXT_RESERVE = 160 B` (a `seal_root` TLV 46, a revocation TLV 106, `session_reset` 4, 4 spare). Senders refuse classical TLVs beyond `ext_len = 160` in Phase 9; PQ TLVs ride only under the slack rule R-C5 (receivers accept up to 2 560; revised §22). Body capacities with `O_MAX = 1 664` (revised §22): 4 KiB → 2 220 B; 16 KiB → 14 508 B; 64 KiB → 63 660 B (`MAX_BODY_BYTES = 61 440` unchanged; round 9 with `O_MAX = 2 560`: 1 324 B, 13 612 B, 62 764 B). NI-DM2 and the property test use body lengths at the class boundaries with and without pending TLVs and quotes. Mutant **ME-22** (bucket from the full L0 length) is detected by NI-DM2.
4. **PQ wording (CR-8; superseded by §22).** Round 9's intro inner layer was X25519, so a future quantum adversary holding my card and AD-1's archive could open recorded intro envelopes and learn each sender's full card. Revised §22: the intro inner layer is X-Wing (§6.4), so that clause of L7-D3 and R-19 is removed (§9.6). The signature clause stays: while no Olm identity is pinned (first contact, including a card verified in person before its bundle was fetched), a forged binding or bundle signature by an active quantum adversary is pinned silently (PQ-R1, R-19).

### 21.12 Poison guard, resumable activation, orphan namespaces (CR-08, CR-12, CR-18)

1. **Poison guard (CR-08).** `Inbox.claim` commits the offer counter before the hand-off (`InboxStore.kt:306-335`), so non-fatal failures used to count as process deaths. Rules: the actor claims no `DM` or `PREKEYS` blob while the native library or the DM keys are unavailable; the `ConsumerTail` claims one blob at a time and stops claiming when fewer than 2 s of its deadline remain; a RECV or BUNDLE transaction that rolls back with `StorageFullException` is followed by `Inbox.defer(ns, hash, 60 s)` in its own transaction, which resets the counter (`InboxStore.kt:347-354`; a local re-offer time, no relay-visible effect, so not a T28 reflex), and if that also fails an in-memory storage-full latch stops DM claims until a write transaction succeeds. R2's threshold then counts process deaths and native crashes only. SCN-J gains a persistent `SQLITE_FULL` over four passes and a deadline-truncated tail, asserting no loss.
2. **Resumable activation (CR-12).** At every actor start and database open: if an activated identity exists and `dm_self` does not, ACTIVATE runs. Restore semantics apply when the Phase 8 restore state (`ent_state.restore_scan_root`, written before the seed is persisted, `RestoreScan.kt:48-61`) commits to the stored identity; `accept_after_minute` is then `floor_minute(now)` of the resumed ACTIVATE (a later minute only refuses more pre-restore content). A migrated v3 database with an activated identity is activated at the first actor start (SCN-L). Crash-enumeration cases between identity persistence and the ACTIVATE commit, for activation and for restore.
3. **Orphan namespaces (CR-18).** `block` and `deleteContact` insert `dm_orphan_namespace(namespace_id)` rows for `X_c` and `P_c` in their transaction (REPLACE guard; no time column); SWEEP calls `Namespaces.remove` for each and deletes the row when it returns true (no op, no fetched row left; `remove` also deletes the credentials, `DirectoryStore.kt:187-205`). The residue lasts at most until the last op's deadline (≤ 7 d) and is declared in L7-D7; R-M1 and a T25 assertion cover the `:sync` tables after block and delete plus that bound.

### 21.13 Licence and CI artifacts, NDK and sums, slices, device checklist, smoke test, T2 export, gate variant, runbook, migration test (BUILD-1, BUILD-2, BUILD-3, BUILD-7, BUILD-8, BUILD-11, BUILD-12, BUILD-14, CR-15 = BUILD-10)

1. **Licence and conveying (BUILD-1; revised §22).** The repository is public and `ci.yml:198-203` uploads `app-release-unsigned.apk` on every push and pull request. Round 9's reason (an APK containing AGPL libsignal while Q1 was open) is withdrawn with libsignal and Q1. The rule stays for a new reason: under closed-source distribution, CI artifacts, emulator images and staging uploads from a public repository **publish** closed binaries without third-party notices. **S0′, before any DM crate enters the build**, removes every public APK and `.so` artifact upload (release and debug), enforced by G-L5 while the repository is public (ADR-39). A release-checklist item (S14, required before the first distribution): the proprietary `LICENSE`, the generated `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES-*` and the APK's licence screen (Phase 13), the MPL-2.0 source offer, and the verifiability package of ADR-38.
2. **NDK and sums (BUILD-2; revised §22).** `:app` pins `android.ndkVersion` to the CI `NDK_PACKAGE` version; the `android`, `reproducible` and `dm-emulator` jobs install that NDK. Round 9's `ghost/libsignal-natives.sha256` for a third-party `libsignal_jni.so` is withdrawn: the only native library is GHOST's `libghost_client_net.so`, whose hashes `build-native.sh` regenerates in `native-libs.sha256`.
3. **Slices (BUILD-3; revised §22).** Migration 4 lands in S3 with steps 0–4 of §13.6 only (the sync and entitlement work), `CURRENT_VERSION = 4`, `expectedTables` and `expectedTriggers`, so every slice leaves a consistent schema that `MigrationRunner.verifyIntegrity` accepts; migration 5 (steps 5–6: the `dm_*` and `olm_*` DDL with its triggers, `CURRENT_VERSION = 5`) lands in S4b. S5 is the v5 trigger transition tests, the introspection tests (R-M1, R-M3, R-M4, the pickle structure test) and the DAO lint. Round 9's G-LS3 logger clause and G-LS4 first-pin exemption are withdrawn; G-R7 exempts the first vodozemac and libcrux pins.
4. **Device checklist (BUILD-7).** Every step that adds a contact, sends, displays or sets a timer is † and runs through the `gate` variant's instrumentation; step 1 injects the scanned card by adb as `addContact(card, qr_in_person)` (no camera, no new permission). S12 gains the `gate` build type, `scripts/device/dm-gate.sh`, the digest export and a host re-store tool (a `client-core` test binary that stores captured blobs over Tor with a deposit credential), each with a self-test.
5. **Release smoke test (BUILD-8; revised §22).** On the release variant: load `libghost_client_net.so`, open a DM context and run one in-process Olm session between two in-memory state sets through `DmNative`; no transport. The loopback-relay session runs in the debug `androidTest` variant only. The release APK gains no transport-injection seam and no equivalence-test entry point (G-DM5 extended).
6. **T2 export (BUILD-11).** The T2 world enumerates every `*.redb` in each relay data directory and fails on an unknown file or table (`world.rs:4068-4085` iterates two named files today); the `keys.redb` export and completeness check land in S1 with the relay change.
7. **Gate variant (BUILD-12).** ADR-34 records the `gate` variant: built locally or in a job that uploads nothing, signed with a throwaway key, installed only on the gate devices, never distributed. The debug key-change hook lives in `crypto-bridge/src/gate` (a G-R6 exception for that source set; revised §22); G-DM5 checks source-set membership of the release variant and R8's `mapping.txt`, not obfuscated class names; `anti-placeholder.sh` scans `*/src/gate/*`.
8. **Runbook and staging (BUILD-14).** §16.4 adds `ghost/infra/issuer/RUNBOOK.md` (the O1 expected listings now include `keys.redb`, created at start; its loss and healing) and an S12 item deploying S1 relays to staging (`infra/relay/docker-compose.staging.yml`) in capture mode before checklist step 8.
9. **Migration test (CR-15 = BUILD-10).** Trigger and index rows naming `outbox_op`/`outbox_delivery` are compared by (type, name, sql) exactly; the two tables by `PRAGMA table_info`, `foreign_key_list` and `index_list` equality plus normalized SQL (identifier quoting normalized), asserting the `ttl_seconds` CHECK as the only difference; row and rowid preservation are unchanged. MC-17 relies on this test.

### 21.14 Tests, mutants, counts, schema delta, questions

**New and changed mutants.** Privacy: PM-27 (§21.2), PM-28 (§21.2), PM-29 (§21.3), PM-30 (§21.9), PM-31 (§21.7); PM-25 detection per §21.9. Correctness: MC-15 redefined (§21.2), MC-18 (§21.3), MC-20 (§21.4), MC-21 (§21.3), MC-22 (§21.1), MC-23 (EST-START without `forgetConsumed`: RS/KC cases of a reset and a key-change acknowledgement in the bundle's week), MC-24 (§21.4), MC-25 (§21.5). Ephemeral and envelope: ME-21 (§21.10), ME-22 (§21.11); ME-6 and ME-12 detection per §21.10 and §21.8. **Counts (revised §22):** round 9 stated 79 but defined 78 (MC-19 did not exist). The revision defines MC-19 and adds PM-32 … PM-37, MC-26 … MC-29 (and MC-30 if Q67's prune is adopted), ME-23 and ME-24, re-expresses PM-4, PM-6, PM-13, PM-15, PM-16, PM-17, PM-29, MC-6, MC-7′, MC-11, MC-18, MC-20, MC-25 and ME-2, and counts **38 privacy, 29 correctness, 24 ephemeral and envelope**: `dm mutants: 91 run, 91 detected` (§15.7); amended to 96 (40, 32, 24) by §22.8.11.

**New cases.** KC5b, KC12, RO6, the RE old-bundle and no-token cases, OS FAILED and INDETERMINATE publications, EP (HIGH 5-minute store, held replay, stale display), SCN-I and SCN-K variants, SCN-J persistent disk-full and truncated tail, CB request flood, the reset and key-change establishment within the bundle's week (MC-23). Twin NI-DM6; hostile modes REFUSE_ONE_SENDER and WITHHOLD_ALL_BUNDLES; the T32 clock-offset population.

**Schema delta (migration 5 since §22; round 9: migration 4, S3).** New tables `dm_outbound_item`, `dm_bundle_candidate`, `dm_orphan_namespace`. `dm_contact`: `fetch_until_day` → `fetch_attempts`, `fetch_listen_until_minute`. `dm_message`: `quote_ref` removed. `dm_outbound_op`: `purpose ∈ {'item', 'bundle'}`, `item_id`, `bundle_week` (replacing `message_id` and `contact_id`). `dm_pending_send`: keyed by `item_id`. `dm_held`: `relay_expiry_hour`, `consume_minute`. `dm_self`: `bundle_published_week`. REPLACE guards for every new table (round 9 also guarded `sig_signed_prekey` and `sig_kyber_prekey`; revised §22: the `olm_*` and `dm_peer_pq` tables instead). R-M3's time-column set: minus `dm_contact.fetch_until_day`, plus `dm_contact.fetch_listen_until_minute`, `dm_held.{relay_expiry_hour, consume_minute}`, `dm_outbound_item.deadline_hour`; period indices plus `dm_self.bundle_published_week`, `dm_bundle_candidate.bundle_week`, `dm_outbound_op.bundle_week` (revised §22: `dm_bundle_candidate.issued_hour` is replaced by the rank `bundle_rank`; these DM tables land in migration 5). `:sync` API (X-12, ADR-34) gains `Inbox.forgetConsumed`.

**Vectors.** `dm_relay_set.txt` (§21.1), `register.txt` and the credential and derivation vectors (§21.2), `dm_seal.txt` intro cases with binding v3 (§21.4, revised §22), bundle v2 layout with `bundle_rank` (§21.7, revised §22), `dm_content.txt` with `EXT_RESERVE` class cases (§21.11), `relay_semantics.txt` coarse `uploaded_at` (§21.9).

**Owner questions.** Round 9 amended Q1 (excluded from the default rule; no APK artifacts while open); revised §22: Q1 is removed. New in the review: Q38 (static per-slot credentials), Q39 (DM TTL classes), Q40 (roots only in acknowledged sessions), Q41 (5-minute store deadline of one hour), Q42 (bounded bundle fetch attempts), Q43 (coarse `uploaded_at` for v3 reads). ADR-30′ … ADR-34′ in Appendix A carry these corrections.

## 22. Revision 2026-09-16: no libsignal, closed source

**This section is normative. It lists every change of the revision, states which earlier text each change overrides, and wins over §0–§21 and over the appendices wherever they disagree.** The earlier sections were rewritten or edited in place; places changed in place say "(revised §22)" or cite a §22 subsection. The §21 corrections of round 9 stay in force unless a row below withdraws or re-sources them.

### 22.0 Owner decision (normative input) and precedence

1. **No libsignal.** Its AGPL-3.0 licence is unacceptable; this excludes `spqr` and every AGPL or GPL component (R-libs §1, §8).
2. **Closed source.** The client, the relay and the issuer are distributed closed source.
3. **Maturity.** Use the alternative closest to libsignal's cryptographic maturity: audits, production deployment, formal analysis, maintenance.
4. **Privacy.** Look for every way to maximise metadata and content privacy, and declare every residue; CP-10 still forbids unverifiable claims in the UI.

Precedence: §22 > §21 > §0–§20 > summary tables; within a section, "(normative)" text wins over a summary table.

### 22.1 The choice

Round 9b compared two complete deltas against the requirement catalogue of R-delta §3: a Double Ratchet stack (**Olm from vodozemac 0.11.0, unchanged and through public API, nested inside the GHOST seal, whose first-contact inner layer uses X-Wing**) and OpenMLS 0.9.0 two-member groups with the X-Wing suite. The judge chose the vodozemac stack (scores: maturity 6 vs 5, privacy 8 vs 7, PQ 6 vs 8, feasibility 7 vs 4):

- **Maturity of the code that runs.** Olm's 3DH and Double Ratchet (V1) run the same source as in Element and Element X (matrix-rust-sdk `main` pins 0.11.0 and calls `from_dehydrated_device` and `generate_fallback_key`; its build enables `insecure-pk-encryption`, `experimental-session-config` and the default features, which GHOST forbids, so the feature configuration differs, §22.8.10 point 15); one public audit (2022), two protocol analyses, 2026 fixes. The OpenMLS route needed a GHOST turn rule and merge-at-send profile that break RFC 9420 §14, so neither the MLS analyses nor the server-ordered deployments cover the protocol GHOST would run; its PQ suite, libcrux provider and `hpke-rs` were outside the SRLabs audit, and `hpke-rs` had a High advisory on 2026-02-13 (GHSA-g433-pq76-6cmf).
- **Privacy.** The 4 KiB DM floor stays (OpenMLS with X-Wing forces 16 KiB, four times the Tor volume); no clock, logging or per-install identifier in the library; wrapped session state; binding v3 blocks key-compromise impersonation at first contact; intro and provisional envelopes stop being attributable once their keys are erased.
- **Post-quantum.** OpenMLS heals inside the library at every commit, which the vodozemac route cannot. The vodozemac route reaches Level 2 at the exit, heals only at the seal layer after a PQ-C cycle, and keeps Olm classical underneath; that gap is declared (PQ-R2, PQ-R7), testable (T35, the model) and has upgrade paths (PQ-D, PQ-E).
- **Feasibility.** About +0.2–0.5 MB per ABI (**measured by S0′, V-9, 2026-09-19: +495 KB arm64-v8a, +630 KB x86_64, both inside the +1 MiB budget**), no MPL code, JDK 17; the two load-bearing library facts (in-place activation of an inactive ratchet on a failed decrypt, fallback-key replay) are handled by value semantics and D2b.

OpenMLS stays the documented DM fallback (Q44) and the Phase 10 library; the MLS integration work of the round carries into the Phase 10 design (§20 F-10).

### 22.2 Grafts adopted (RG-1 … RG-11)

| # | Graft | Where |
|---|---|---|
| RG-1 | Exhaustive abstract state-machine models in the `rust` job (at most 6 user sends per side, loss, duplication, reordering, process death at every step, restore): EM-1 over the session slots R-SS0 … R-SS4 and the D2b reuse set, EM-2 over the intro → provisional → pair transitions and erasures; mutant MC-19 ("newest decrypt wins") must fail EM-1 | §15.2, §8.2, §16.2, §17.1 (S4b) |
| RG-2 | Host/Android equivalence test: the same deterministic inputs (golden-transcript decryption, seal and open with fixed salts, nonces and KEM randomness, normal-message encryption from a fixed pickle, safety numbers, L0 and TLV codecs) on `ghost-dm-host` and on the emulator's x86_64 build, outputs byte-identical | §15.9 item 7, §15.8 assumptions, §2.7, V-16 |
| RG-3 | A clock and logging gate at every pin change: `cargo vendor` sources of vodozemac, `libcrux-*` and `hpke` scanned for `SystemTime`, `Instant::now`, `log::`, `tracing::`, `println!`; fail on an addition to a justified baseline (rule replaced by §22.8.10 point 4) | G-R8 (§16.1), §2.8, V-8 |
| RG-4 | Card-holder indistinguishability: JD10 (no 16-byte window of any intro envelope's inner bytes repeats in a world) and NI-DM7 (first flight vs re-sent unacknowledged PreKey give identical card-holder views); mutant PM-37 (reused ciphertext) | §15.6, §6.4, T29, T32 |
| RG-5 | A PQ assertion at every vodozemac or libcrux bump: PQ5 (1 120-byte X-Wing ciphertext in every intro envelope and every `seal_root_pq`, `k_in` depends on `ss`, a `gate`-variant client sealing the intro inner layer without the KEM is refused) | §15.5 PQ5, §2.8, G-R7 |
| RG-6 | An age bound for seizure-exposed skipped keys, evaluated in S0′ (an upstream API or a pinned-schema pickle edit), adopted only if golden pickles and `UpgradeTest` cover it; otherwise the count-bounded exception stays declared | §7.7 point 4, Q67, V-15, R-41, MC-30 |
| RG-7 | Structural invariants after every crash-enumeration reboot: ≤ 2 `olm_session` rows per contact with exactly one current; `previous` rows have `retire_day`; every reuse tuple references a live generation; ≤ 14 generations; ≤ 3 `dm_peer_pq` rows per contact; no session for a revoked, blocked or changed contact (other than under the pinned identity) | §15.3 |
| RG-8 | A measurement probe before S4a: a scratch crate on the real toolchain measures worst-case Olm PreKey and normal overheads and the worst-case JSON pickle for 0.11.0 and 0.10.0, so `O_MAX` and `SESSION_STATE_MAX` are frozen from measurement. **Done (S0′, V-3 and V-4, 2026-09-19): `O_MAX` = 1 664 B (worst measured intro overhead 1 606 B) and `SESSION_STATE_MAX` = 65 536 B (worst measured pickle 36 685 B) are frozen; "provisional" is withdrawn from both** | §17.1 S0′, §6.6, §7.9 point 4, V-3, V-4 |
| RG-9 | The deniable MAC binding and the static Ed25519 binding recorded as documented alternatives to binding v3, with their trade-off | Q51, §3.4 |
| RG-10 | A PQ-C cadence question: `seal_root_pq` at most every 7 days instead of 4 weeks, only if the hint benchmark holds; default 4 weeks until measured | Q68, §9.4, §6.2 R-S5, V-13 |
| RG-11 | The OpenMLS work carried into the Phase 10 design (storage adapter, timestamp rewrite, clock-site and log-site gates, `rayon` check, probe sizes, `hpke-rs` MPL decision, SRLabs S0-1 risk-accepted finding, the spec Appendix A MLS-row ADR) | §20 F-10 |

### 22.8 Review of the revision (round 9c): findings and normative corrections

**This subsection is normative and wins over the rest of §22, §21, §0–§20 and the appendices wherever they disagree.** The adversarial review of round 9b (three lenses: cryptography CRY-1 … CRY-10, privacy PH-1 … PH-13, implementability IMPL-01 … IMPL-17; 40 findings) was checked against this document, the pinned crate sources in the local cargo registry (vodozemac 0.11.0 and 0.10.0, libcrux-kem 0.0.9, libcrux-ecdh 0.0.8, libcrux-curve25519 0.0.8, libcrux-hacl-rs 0.0.5, x-wing 0.1.0, hpke 0.14.1, priority-queue 2.7.0), the repository at `b2df181` (`relay.proto`, `relay/crates/{node,storage,capability,api}`, `Cargo.toml`, `Cargo.lock`, `deny.toml`, `rust-crypto-pins.{txt,sh}`, `ci.yml`, `run-all.sh`, `proguard-rules.pro`, `InboxStore.kt`, `ModelRelay.kt`), the GitHub API and documentation (fetched 2026-09-16) and a cargo-deny 0.20.2 probe over the DM graph. **No finding was rejected as wrong.** Four overlaps are merged: CRY-5 = PH-7, PH-10 = IMPL-15, PH-12 = IMPL-06, and CRY-9 (a) is folded into IMPL-07. For seven findings the proposed fix is replaced or narrowed, each with its reason below: PH-1 (a separate FETCH credential kind instead of coarsening every v3 read, so C-11 and ADR-27 C.9 keep the exact relay expiry), CRY-4 (a same-sender age order limited to stale first-flight and pre-renewal traffic; the confirmation alternative deadlocks a restore interleaving), CRY-7 (associated data built from session ids and generation ids, so no re-wrap call is needed), PH-3 (declared and re-asked; the owner-bound read rule is sketched in Q36, not implemented), PH-6 (per-session anchors instead of "FRESH after the own root was sent", which would lose in-flight replies), IMPL-01 (text job outputs instead of a committed sums file, which would change on every Rust edit) and PH-11 (per-release symbol files published as part of the ADR-38 package). Places corrected in place cite "(§22.8.n)".

#### 22.8.1 Held replays dispatch like RECV, in chain order (CRY-1)

**Defect.** HELD called `sessionInbound` on every held row in `held_id` order. A contact whose Olm identity changed sends several PreKey messages of one new session before the user decides. vodozemac does not consume fallback keys (`vz:src/olm/account/mod.rs:324`) and decrypts a later chain index by skipping keys, so a second `sessionInbound` on the same PreKey data succeeds and returns the same `session_id`: the insert breaks `UNIQUE (contact_id, session_id)` or the `olm_prekey_used` primary key, an abort that §21.4 point 5 classes as a bug (a crash loop, since HELD has no offer counter). `held_id` is random, so replay order was arbitrary against Olm's 40-skipped-keys window, and a RECV pass that ran before HELD could create the session first.

**Rules.**
1. **Order.** HELD reads every live held row of the contact, parses each L1 (the facade's `L1.parse` returns the inner chain index for PreKey messages too), groups rows by `session_id`, orders the groups by their smallest `consume_minute`, and orders rows inside a group by the inner chain index (ties by `held_id`).
2. **Dispatch.** Each row runs in its own HELD transaction through `DmSessions.decrypt`, exactly RECV's R7: a `session_id` that matches a slot decrypts on that slot; otherwise `(gen_id, session_id)` in `olm_prekey_used` is `drop(replay)`; otherwise `sessionInbound` with the row's `gen_id` and `prov_env`, one reuse tuple, the selection rules of §22.8.4 and the renewal effects. Then R8–R12 with the row's `relay_expiry_hour` and `consume_minute`, and the row is deleted. Every drop deletes the row and counts; no constraint abort is reachable.
3. **Pass order.** §13.7's actor pass runs HELD before the peek and RECV claims; rule 2 keeps either order correct.
4. **Tests and mutant.** KC3b: two held messages of one session; more than 40 held messages of one session inserted in random `held_id` order; a RECV-created session before HELD runs; all displayed once, in order. Mutant **MC-31** (every held row through `sessionInbound` in `held_id` order) is detected by KC3b (a constraint abort or a lost message).

#### 22.8.2 Low-order and invalid peer keys: typed codes, never a crash (CRY-2, CRY-3)

**Defects.** (1) `Session::new_remote` stores the PreKey message's inner ratchet key without a Diffie-Hellman (`vz:src/olm/session/mod.rs:339-351`); the first message decrypts with the 3DH chain key, and the victim's first `encrypt` then activates the ratchet and fails with `EncryptionError::NonContributoryKey` (`vz:src/olm/session/double_ratchet.rs:52-55,71-78`; `vz:src/types/curve25519.rs:52-56`). `encrypt` had only `OVERSIZE`, so the error became `INTERNAL`, an `IllegalStateException`, and a crash loop for scheduled CONTROL sends. Any card holder can do this through a request; any contact through R-SS1. (2) `XWingKemDraft06PublicKey::decode` validates only the ML-KEM half (`libcrux-kem-0.0.9/src/kem.rs:242-258`), and encapsulation and decapsulation return an error, not a pseudo-random secret, when the X25519 output is all zero (`kem.rs:360,500`; `libcrux-ecdh-0.0.8/src/x25519.rs:111-118`; `libcrux-curve25519-0.0.8/src/impl_hacl.rs:13-18`; `libcrux-hacl-rs-0.0.5/src/curve25519_51.rs:331-342`). RustCrypto `x-wing 0.1.0` never fails (`src/lib.rs:110-127,207-218`). A contact could publish a bundle or an in-band `pq_key` with a low-order X25519 half and make `sealIntro` or `pqRootSeal` fail untyped in the time-triggered `EST-READY`; a sender could put a low-order `ct_X` into an intro envelope and make decapsulation fail inside a **peek**, which commits no offer counter, so even the poison guard would not stop the loop. §9.2's "implicit rejection gives a wrong `ss`" was wrong for libcrux.

**Rules.**
1. **`sessionInbound`** checks, before creating anything, that a trial X25519 agreement of a fresh random scalar with the PreKey message's inner ratchet key is contributory (the base key and identity are already covered by the 3DH check, `vz:src/olm/account/mod.rs:290-296`); otherwise `INVALID_KEY` with no state change (`drop(undecryptable)`).
2. **`encrypt`** gains the code **`INVALID_SESSION`** (every library encryption error). SEND, RESEND and `EST-READY` roll back; a separate transaction deletes the contact's sessions, keeps the item pending and runs `EST-START` (the `OVERSIZE` path of §21.5 as revised), counted `invalid_session`. Facade: `DmSessions.encrypt → … | InvalidSession`.
3. **X-Wing keys are validated whole.** `bundleOpen` and R11 (`pq_key`) accept a 1 216-byte key only if the ML-KEM half passes libcrux's encapsulation-key validation **and** a trial X25519 agreement of a fresh scalar with the last 32 bytes is contributory; otherwise `INVALID_KEY` (the candidate is dropped, counted `bad_pq_key`; an in-band key is ignored and counted).
4. **Every KEM error is typed.** `sealIntro` and `pqRootSeal` map any encapsulation error to `INVALID_KEY` (`EST-READY` drops the candidate and the attempt continues; a PQ offer falls back to the classical `seal_root` and the `dm_peer_pq` row is deleted). `openIntro`, `pqRootOpen` and every peek-time opening map any decapsulation error to `NOT_OPEN` (`drop(non_canonical)`; an unopenable `seal_root_pq` is handled by §22.8.6). `INTERNAL` remains only for caught panics; `DmResultCodesTest` enumerates every variant of vodozemac's `SessionCreationError`, `DecryptionError`, `EncryptionError` and libcrux-kem's `Error` and requires a code for each.
5. **Tests.** Negative vectors with the all-zero key and each small-order X25519 point as (a) the PreKey inner ratchet key, (b) the base key, (c) `pk_X` of a bundle and of `pq_key`, (d) `ct_X` of an intro envelope (also through peek) and of `seal_root_pq`; an ML-KEM half that fails validation. The differential test against `x-wing` expects the documented difference: libcrux returns an error where `x-wing` returns a secret, and GHOST must refuse those inputs.

**Forward note (S0′ evidence, 2026-09-19).** The round-9c text above is the record of that review and is not rewritten; three facts measured afterwards extend it, and the extended wording lives in the sections named, which win for these three points:
- *Rule 3, call path.* X-Wing is reached **only** through `libcrux_kem::XWing` and `libcrux_traits::kem::slice::Kem`, after an explicit `libcrux_ml_kem::mlkem768::validate_public_key` on the first 1 184 bytes (the trait performs no ML-KEM key check) and the contributory X25519 trial. The **enum API is not used**: `PublicKey::decode(XWingKemDraft06, ·)` panics on fewer than 1 184 bytes and `encapsulate_derand` on a seed shorter than 64 bytes (`libcrux-kem-0.0.9/src/kem.rs:246,574`; V-6), and it links every KEM of the crate (§2.3, §2.7, V-9). A fuzz target covers every libcrux entry point.
- *Rule 4, exhaustiveness.* `DmResultCodesTest` is a **compile-time exhaustive `match` without a wildcard** over `SessionCreationError`, `DecryptionError`, `EncryptionError` (none is `#[non_exhaustive]` in 0.11.0 or 0.10.0) and libcrux-kem's `Error`, so a variant added at a bump fails the build (a G-R7 drill, §16.1); its runtime half checks the mapping table. Two variants the §2.6 table did not name are now mapped there: `MismatchedSessionConfig` at `sessionInbound` and a decrypt-time `NonContributoryKey` (V-2).
- *Rule 4, ratchet-key check.* Rule 1's contributory pre-check fires **before** the library: V-2 confirmed on both pins that `create_inbound_session` accepts and decrypts a PreKey message whose inner ratchet key was replaced with a small-order point and re-authenticated by the sender's chain key, and that the failure then surfaces at the victim's first `encrypt` (`NonContributoryKey` → `INVALID_SESSION`); GHOST returns `INVALID_KEY` first.

#### 22.8.3 Wrapping associated data stable across slot moves (CRY-7)

**Defect.** §2.5 bound every wrapped secret to "label ‖ row key", but no JNI function received a row key, and several flows change or lack the row key: slot swaps (`PRIMARY KEY (contact_id, slot)`), `dm_held.prov_env` becoming `olm_session.prov_env`, `openIntro` returning `prov_env` before any row exists, and `pairKeys` output without a contact.

**Rule.** The AD of an envelope is `"ghost/v1/wrap/" ‖ name ‖ binding`, with a binding that never changes when a value moves and that Rust receives or computes:

| Envelope | `name` | Binding |
|---|---|---|
| `olm_session.state_env` | `olm-session` | `contact_id(16) ‖ session_id(32)` |
| `olm_session.prov_env`, `dm_held.prov_env` | `reply-seed` | `session_id(32)` (from the PreKey header, or from `sessionOutbound`) |
| `olm_prekey_gen.secrets_env` | `generation` | `u32_be(gen_id) ‖ fallback_pub(32)` |
| `dm_pair_key.open_env` | `pair-key` | `contact_id ‖ direction(1) ‖ u16_be(root_gen) ‖ u64_be(week)` |
| `dm_pair_offer.root_env`, `offer_env` | `pair-root`, `pair-offer` | `contact_id ‖ u16_be(root_gen)` |
| transient `kbEnv`, `kioEnv` | `card-key` | `identity_pk(32)` of the card and a kind byte |

JNI inputs gained (§2.6 edited): `bundleCreate` takes the `genId` Kotlin chose (distinct from retained ones); `sessionOutbound` and `encrypt` take `contactId` (and `sessionId` for `encrypt`); `decrypt` takes `contactId` and `(sessionId, env)` per slot; `openIntro`, `sessionInbound` and `pqRootOpen` take `genId`; `sessionInbound` takes `contactId`; `pairKeys` takes `contactId`, `direction`, `rootGen`; `pqRootSeal` and `pqRootOpen` take `contactId` and `rootGen`; `sealSym` and `openOuter` take the binding of the key envelope they receive (pair: `contactId`, `direction`, `rootGen`, `week`; provisional: `sessionId`). A slot change is a delete-and-insert of the same envelope bytes; a held row's `prov_env` moves unchanged. An envelope that does not open under its binding returns the new code **`STATE_CORRUPT`** (handled like `OVERSIZE`: the sessions are deleted, `reset_needed = 1`, counted). **Test** `WrapBindingTest`: swaps and held-to-session moves open; a `state_env` copied to another `contact_id`, secrets to another `gen_id`, a pair key to another week return `STATE_CORRUPT`.

#### 22.8.4 Session age in the selection rules (CRY-4)

**Defect.** R-SS1 promoted every new inbound session, and responder sessions were acknowledged by CHECK, so R-SS2 could never undo a wrong promotion. A contact's older first flight (sealed before its restore or explicit reset), processed after its newer reconnect or reset intro, became `current`; the user's replies went to a session the contact no longer had until the contact's `UNACK_MAX` re-establishment, a loss outside CS-3. The same happens when a late PreKey re-send of the old session decrypts on `previous` and the session-id tie-break swaps it back, and when a late pre-restore normal reply acknowledges my old initiator session.

**Why not the reviewer's confirmation alternative.** Treating a responder session as unacknowledged until a normal message arrives deadlocks a restore in which the contact's pre-restore reply to my initiator session arrives after its new intro: my acknowledged old session would stay `current` and the contact's PreKey messages on the new one would never confirm it. Any tie-break between two sessions the contact still holds must also stay symmetric (both sides compare the same session ids), or an `UNACK_MAX` re-establishment could retire a session the contact keeps using. The age order therefore applies only to traffic that proves the contact no longer answers on the older session: first-flight (PreKey) traffic, and replies authored before the newer session was sealed.

**Rules.**
1. **Schema.** `olm_session` gains `peer_bind_minute` (responder sessions: the largest `binding_minute` of the intro envelopes decrypted on it; NULL for initiator sessions; it only increases). Responder sessions stay acknowledged by CHECK.
2. **R-SS1 (amended).** A new inbound session becomes `current` unless `current` is a responder session whose `peer_bind_minute` is greater than the new envelope's `binding_minute` (both written by the contact's clock); in that case it becomes `previous` if `previous` is empty or a responder session with a smaller `peer_bind_minute` (the replaced row is deleted), and otherwise the message is dropped (`drop(stale_session)`, no session, no reuse tuple; counted). The renewal effects of §21.3 point 2 apply only when the new session becomes `current`.
3. **R-SS2 (amended).** When a message decrypts on `previous`, before the existing rules: (a) a **PreKey** message on a responder `previous` whose `peer_bind_minute` is smaller than a responder `current`'s never swaps; (b) a **normal** message on an initiator `previous` whose L0 `author_minute` (decoded in the same transaction, R8) is smaller than a responder `current`'s `peer_bind_minute` never swaps. Otherwise the existing rules apply unchanged (an unacknowledged `current` is swapped; between two acknowledged sessions the smaller `session_id` wins), so live sessions keep the symmetric tie-break.
4. **Tests.** EM-1 adds a contact that restores or resets while its earlier first flight, PreKey re-sends or normal replies are still in transit, in every processing order, with and without an `UNACK_MAX` re-establishment and with simultaneous initiation; RO5 and RO8 are re-run under the amended rules; SCN-I gains the late pre-restore first-flight and late pre-restore reply variants. Mutant **MC-32** (R-SS1 and R-SS2 without the age rules) is detected by those SCN-I variants and EM-1.
5. **Declared.** The age rules compare two minutes of the contact's clocks; a contact restored onto a device whose clock is behind by more than the time between its old and new sends can still leave the old session selected until its `UNACK_MAX` re-establishment (CS-3's restore clause covers it).

#### 22.8.5 Out keys for retries; RESEND guards; bounded provisional lifetime (CRY-5 = PH-7, PH-6)

**Defects.** (1) A user retry of a FAILED item re-encodes the same L0 with its original `author_minute` (§21.8 point 3), but the sender kept only `K_{week(now)}`, and R9 requires the key's week to equal `week(author_minute)`. Deadlines reach 7 days, so every retry of a default-timer message that failed at its deadline crosses a week boundary; §6.8 then fell to provisional mode, which the peer may already have erased (each side's `prov_erase_minute` runs on its own clock and events: a silent loss while the sender records an outcome), or to `NO_SEAL_KEY`, a bundle fetch and a new session (a relay-visible establishment and a recognisable intro envelope). (2) RESEND did not re-run SEND's trust and staleness guards. (3) A side that never opens a pair envelope under its newest root (the peer never wrote after receiving that root) kept `prov_env` for as long as the session existed, so P-7 and L7-D21's bound for provisional envelopes was false.

**Rules.**
1. **Out keys kept for retries.** Besides `K_{week(now)}` of the newest generation, the sender keeps the out key (generation, week) of every earlier week w for which an item of c authored in w is `pending`, `enqueued` or `failed` with `deadline_hour > now` (at most 7 days); SWEEP erases it at that deadline. The recipient still holds that key (its window is `start(w + 1) + FRESH`). A retry keeps its author minute.
2. **RESEND guards.** A user retry re-runs SEND's S1 (trust), S4 (no or stale session → pending plus `EST-START`), S5–S6 (the §6.8 rule against `now`) and S7. The automatic identical-bytes resend runs only while the contact is `unverified` or `verified`; otherwise the item ends `indeterminate`.
3. **§6.8 rule 3 (amended).** Provisional sealing also requires `olm_session.pair_sent = 0` (no pair envelope was ever sealed on that session; set to 1, never back, when SEND seals pair mode) and `now < prov_anchor_minute + 28 d` when the anchor is set; otherwise rule 4.
4. **Anchors and lifetime.** `prov_anchor_minute` (write-once) is, for an initiator session, the minute of the first decryption on it (its acknowledgement), and for a responder session, the minute of its first provisional seal.
